“按法律标准，再清楚不过。我们手头有三位名医的意见书——我猜是他自己收着的，以防万一——每一份都声明他从领口往上一切健全。不过你知道，一个人离谱到相当地步，在法律上却未必算疯；老纳特科姆——这么说吧，我们姑且称他任性乖张。他似乎是在正常与怪癖之间走之字形路线的人物。

===Original===
“His only surviving relatives appear to be a nephew and a niece. The nephew dropped out of the running two years ago when his aunt, old Nutcombe’s wife, who had divorced old Nutcombe, left him her money. This seems to have soured the old boy on the nephew, for in the first of his wills that I’ve seen﻿—you remember I told you I had seen three﻿—he leaves the niece the pile and the nephew only gets a hundred dollars. Well, so far there’s nothing very eccentric about old Nutcombe’s proceedings. But wait!

“Six months after he had made that will he came in here and made another. This left a hundred dollars to the nephew as before, but nothing at all to the niece. Why, I don’t know. There was nothing in the will about her having done anything to offend him during those six months, none of those nasty slams you see in wills about: ‘I bequeath to my only son John one shilling and sixpence. Now perhaps he’s sorry he married the cook.’ As far as I can make out he changed his will, just as he did when he left the money to you, purely through some passing whim. Anyway he did change it. He left the pile to support the movement those people are running for getting the Jews back to Palestine.

“He didn’t seem, on second thoughts, to feel that this was quite such a brainy scheme as he had at first, and it wasn’t long before he came trotting back to tear up this second will and switch back to the first one﻿—the one leaving the money to the niece. That restoration to sanity lasted till about a month ago, when he broke loose once more and paid his final visit here to will you the contents of his stocking. This morning I see he’s dead after a short illness, so you collect. Congratulations!”
===Chinese===
“他活着的至亲，似乎只有一个侄儿一个侄女。侄儿两年前就出了局：他的婶母——也就是老纳特科姆的妻子，已同老纳特科姆离了婚——把自己的钱留给了他。这一着似乎叫老家伙对侄儿寒了心，因为在我见过的他头一份遗嘱里——你记得我说过见过三份——整份家产归了侄女，侄儿只得一百美元。好，到这里为止，老纳特科姆的行事还没有什么出奇的地方。可是等着瞧！

“那份遗嘱立过六个月，他又上这儿来立了一份。这一份照旧给侄儿一百美元，侄女却分文皆无。为什么，我不知道。遗嘱里没有说她在这六个月里干过什么开罪他的事，也没有遗嘱里常见的那种恶毒的挖苦，比如：‘今遗赠我独生子约翰一先令六便士。这下他该后悔娶了那个厨娘了吧。’依我看，他改遗嘱——同他把钱留给你那一回一样——纯粹出于一时心血来潮。反正遗嘱他改了。他把整份家产留下来，资助那班人正在奔走的运动：让犹太人重返巴勒斯坦。

“再往深处一想，他似乎又觉得这主意不像起初想的那么高明了，没多久便小跑着回来，撕掉这第二份遗嘱，改回第一份——留钱给侄女的那一份。这一次神志复原，撑到大约一个月前，他又挣脱了缰绳，最后一次光临此地，把他袜筒里的家当遗嘱给了你。今天早上我见报，他缠绵数日，已经故去。所以，你都明白了。恭喜恭喜！”

===Original===
Lord Dawlish had listened to this speech in perfect silence. He now rose and began to pace the room. He looked warm and uncomfortable. His demeanor, in fact, was by no means the accepted demeanor of the lucky heir.

“This is awful!” he said. “Good Lord, Jerry, it’s frightful!”

“Awful﻿—being left five million dollars?”

“Yes, like this, I feel like a bally thief.”
===Chinese===
达利什勋爵听这篇宏论，自始至终一声没吭。这会儿他站起身，开始在屋里踱来踱去。他看上去浑身燥热，很不自在。说实在的，他这副神气，同幸运继承人约定俗成的那副神气，丝毫不沾边。

“这太可怕了！”他说，“天哪，杰里，简直吓死人！”

“可怕——平白得了五百万美元的遗产？”

“是啊，这么着，我觉得自己活像个十足的贼。”

===Original===
“Why on earth?”

“If it hadn’t been for me, this girl﻿—what’s her name?”

“Her name is Boyd﻿—Elizabeth Boyd.”

“She would have had the whole five millions if it hadn’t been for me. Have you told her yet?”

“She’s over in America. I was writing her a letter when you came in﻿—informal, you know, to put her out of her misery. If I had waited for the governor to let her know in the usual course of red tape we should never have got anywhere. Also one to the nephew, telling him about his hundred dollars. I believe in humane treatment on these occasions. The governor would write them a legal letter with so many ‘hereinbefores’ in it that they would get the idea that they had been left the whole pile. I just send a cheery line, saying, ‘It’s no good, old top. Abandon hope,’ and they know just where they are. Simple and considerate!”
===Chinese===
“这到底是为了什么？”

“要不是因为我，这位姑娘——她叫什么来着？”

“她姓博伊德——伊丽莎白·博伊德（Elizabeth Boyd）。”

“要不是因为我，那整整五百万就都是她的了。你通知她了没有？”

“她在美国那边。你进来的时候，我正给她写信——非正式的，你知道，好叫她早点解脱，不必再悬着。要是等着老爷子按寻常那套繁文缛节去知会她，这事到猴年马月也办不成。另有一封写给那个侄儿的，告诉他那一百美元的事。这种场合，我信奉人道待遇。换了老爷子，他会给他们写一封法律信函，里头‘鉴于前述’之类的字眼密密麻麻，他们八成当自己承袭了整份家产。我呢，只写一行快活的短信：‘没指望了，老伙计，死了这条心吧。’他们立刻心里有底。简洁而体贴！”

===Original===
A glance at Bill’s face moved him to further speech.

“I don’t see why you should worry, Bill. How, by any stretch of the imagination, can you make out that you are to blame for this Boyd girl’s misfortune? It looks to me as if these eccentric wills of old Nutcombe’s came in cycles, as it were. Just as he was due for another outbreak he happened to meet you. It’s a moral certainty that if he hadn’t met you he would have left all his money to a Home for Superannuated Caddies or a Fund for Supplying the Deserving Poor with Niblicks. Why should you blame yourself?”

“I don’t blame myself. It isn’t exactly that. But﻿—but, well, what would you feel like in my place?”

“A two-year-old.”
===Chinese===
他朝比尔的脸瞥了一眼，又接着说下去。

“我实在看不出你有什么可愁的，比尔。你纵然极尽想象之能事，又怎么能把这博伊德姑娘的不幸，算到你自己头上？依我看，老纳特科姆这些怪遗嘱，仿佛是周期性发作的。正赶上他该再发作一场的当口，他碰巧遇上了你。那是准而又准的事：他要没遇上你，就会把全部家当留给一所退休球童之家，或者一个替值得受济的穷人置办挖起杆（niblick）的基金会。你何苦怪自己？”

“我不怪自己。不完全是那么回事。可是——可是，唉，换了你处在我这地步，你会是什么滋味？”

“跟个两岁娃娃似的。”

===Original===
“Wouldn’t you do anything?”

“I certainly would. By my halidom, I would! I would spend that money with a vim and speed that would make your respected ancestor, the Beau, look like a village miser.”

“You wouldn’t﻿—er﻿—pop over to America and see whether something couldn’t be arranged?”

“What!”

“I mean﻿—suppose you were popping in any case. Suppose you had happened to buy a ticket for New York on tomorrow’s boat, wouldn’t you try to get in touch with this girl when you got to America, and see if you couldn’t﻿—er﻿—fix up something?”
===Chinese===
“难道你就什么都不做？”

“我当然要做。以我的圣物起誓，我一定做！我会把那笔钱花出去，那股劲头、那个速度，管保叫您那位可敬的祖先‘美男子’达利什看上去像个乡下吝啬鬼。”

“你不会——呃——跑一趟美国，看看能不能安排点什么？”

“什么！”

“我是说——假使你横竖要跑这一趟。假使你碰巧买了一张明天开往纽约那班船的票，你到了美国，会不会设法同这位姑娘接上头，看看能不能——呃——商量出一个办法？”

===Original===
Jerry Nichols looked at him in honest consternation. He had always known that old Bill was a dear old ass, but he had never dreamed that he was such an infernal old ass as this.

“You aren’t thinking of doing that?” he gasped.

“Well, you see, it’s a funny coincidence, but I was going to America anyhow tomorrow. I don’t see why I shouldn’t try to fix up something with this girl.”

“What do you mean﻿—fix up something? You don’t suggest that you should give the money up, do you?”

“I don’t know. Not exactly that, perhaps. How would it be if I gave her half, what? Anyway I should like to find out about her, see if she’s hard up, and so on. I should like to nose round, you know, and﻿—er﻿—and so forth, don’t you know. Where did you say the girl lived?”
===Chinese===
杰里·尼科尔斯望着他，实实在在地惶骇了。他一向知道老比尔是个可爱的老笨蛋，却从没梦想到他竟是一个这等无可救药的老笨蛋。

“你不是当真想这么干吧？”他倒抽一口气。

“唉，说来也巧，横竖我明天就要动身去美国。我看不出为什么不该同这位姑娘商量商量。”

“你说的商量出一个办法，是什么意思？你不见得是想把钱让出去吧？”

“说不好。也许不完全是那样。我把一半分给她，你看怎么样，嗯？反正我想摸一摸她的底，看她手头紧不紧，如此等等。我想四处打探打探，你知道的，还有——呃——诸如此类，你懂的。你说那姑娘住在哪儿来着？”

===Original===
“I didn’t say, and I’m not sure that I shall. Honestly, Bill, you mustn’t be so quixotic.”

“There’s no harm in my nosing round, is there? Be a good chap and give me the address.”

“Well, with misgivings﻿—Brookport, Long Island.”

“Thanks.”
===Chinese===
“我没说过，恐怕也不打算说。说真的，比尔，你可千万不能这样堂吉诃德。”

“我打探打探，总没有什么坏处吧？做个好人，把地址给我。”

“唉，怀着满腹疑虑——长岛（Long Island）布鲁克波特（Brookport）。”

“多谢。”

===Original===
“Bill, are you really going to make a fool of yourself?”

“Not a bit of it, old chap. I’m just going to﻿—er﻿—”

“To nose round?”

“To nose round,” said Bill.
===Chinese===
“比尔，你当真要出自己的洋相？”

“哪有的事，老伙计。我不过是打算——呃——”

“去打探打探？”

“去打探打探，”比尔说。

===Original===
Jerry Nichols accompanied his friend to the door, and when he had closed it turned to the boy Perkins, who was eating a sandwich and reading a handy pocket edition of *Dillingwater on Torts*.

“Perkins,” said Jerry.

“Yes, sir?”

“That was Lord Dawlish who just went out.”

“Yes, sir.”
===Chinese===
杰里·尼科尔斯把朋友送到门口，掩上门，转身面向勤杂少年珀金斯——他正一面嚼三明治，一面研读一部便于携带的袖珍版《迪林沃特论侵权法》（Dillingwater on Torts）。

“珀金斯，”杰里说。

“是，先生？”

“方才出去的那位，是达利什勋爵。”

“是，先生。”

===Original===
“He’s a fool.”

“Yes, sir?”

“But I wish to heaven there were a few more like him in this weary world.”

“Yes, sir?”
===Chinese===
“他是个傻瓜。”

“是，先生。”

“可我对天起誓，但愿这个叫人厌倦的尘世上，像他这样的人再多几个。”

“是，先生？”

===Original===
Jerry regarded his young assistant thoughtfully.

“Don’t you ever say anything except ‘Yes, sir,’ Perkins?”

“Yes, sir,” said the stripling with a touch of surprise in his voice. Jerry surveyed him a few moments longer, then with a resigned shrug of his shoulders picked up his hat and went out to lunch. The boy Perkins took another bite out of his sandwich and resumed his study of *Dillingwater on Torts*.

Peace reigned in the offices of Nichols, Nichols, Nichols and Nichols.
===Chinese===
杰里若有所思地打量着这位小助手。

“珀金斯，你就说不出一句‘是，先生’以外的话么？”

“是，先生，”小家伙说，声音里带着几分诧异。杰里又把他端详了片刻，随即无可奈何地耸耸肩，抓起帽子出去吃午饭。小家伙珀金斯又咬了一口三明治，接着攻读他的《迪林沃特论侵权法》。

尼科尔斯、尼科尔斯、尼科尔斯与尼科尔斯律师事务所里，一派太平。

===Original===
The time of a man who has at a moment’s notice decided to leave his native land for a sojourn on foreign soil is necessarily taken up with a variety of occupations; and it was not till the following afternoon, on the boat at Liverpool, that Bill had leisure to write to Claire, giving her the news of what had befallen him. He had booked his ticket by a Liverpool boat in preference to one that sailed from Southampton, because he had not been sure how Claire would take the news of his sudden decision to leave for America. There was the chance that she might ridicule or condemn the scheme, and he preferred to get away without seeing her. Now that he had received this astounding piece of news from Jerry Nichols he was relieved that he had acted in this way. Whatever Claire might have thought of the original scheme, there was no doubt at all what she would think of his plan of seeking out Elizabeth Boyd with a view to dividing the legacy with her.

He was guarded in his letter. He mentioned no definite figures. He wrote that Ira Nutcombe, of whom they had spoken so often, had most surprisingly left him in his will a large sum of money, and eased his conscience by telling himself that half of five million dollars undeniably was a large sum of money.

The addressing of the letter called for thought. She would have left Southampton with the rest of the company before it could arrive. Where was it that she said they were going next week? Portsmouth, that was it. He addressed the letter Care of The Girl and the Artist Company, to the King’s Theater, Portsmouth.
===Chinese===
一个接到通知便当即决断、抛下故土去异邦小住的人，光阴必然被五花八门的营生占满；直到第二天下午，在利物浦（Liverpool）港的船上，比尔才腾出工夫给克莱尔·芬威克（Claire Fenwick）写信，把她遭逢的变故告诉了她。他订的是利物浦开出的那班船的票，舍南安普顿（Southampton）启航的那班而不取，因为他拿不准克莱尔会怎样看待他突然决意远赴美国这件事。说不定她会讥笑这个计划，或者加以斥责；他宁可不见她一面就走。如今从杰里·尼科尔斯那里听到了这桩惊人的消息，他倒庆幸自己先前的做法了。原先那个计划，克莱尔无论作何感想都还无妨；至于他要去找伊丽莎白·博伊德、打算同她分那份遗产的打算，她会作何感想，那是半点疑问也没有的。

信他写得含而不露，没有提到确切的数目。他写道，他们俩谈论过多少回的艾拉·纳特科姆，出人意料地在遗嘱里留给他一大笔钱；随后又宽慰自己的良心：五百万美元的一半，无可否认是一大笔钱。

信封上怎么写，颇费斟酌。信寄到之前，她早该随全团人马离开了南安普顿。她说过来着，他们下个星期去哪儿？朴次茅斯（Portsmouth），正是。他把信写给《姑娘与艺术家》剧团收转，寄朴次茅斯国王剧院（King’s Theater）。

## IX / 第九章

===Original===

Elizabeth entered Nutty’s room and, seating herself on the bed, surveyed him with a bright, quiet eye that drilled holes in her brother’s uneasy conscience. This was her second visit to him that morning. She had come an hour ago, bearing breakfast on a tray, and had departed without saying a word. It was this uncanny silence of hers even more than the effects﻿—which still lingered﻿—of his revels in the metropolis, that had interfered with Nutty’s enjoyment of the morning meal. Never a hearty breakfaster, he had found himself under the influence of her wordless disapproval physically unable to consume the fried egg that confronted him. He had given it one look, then, endorsing the opinion which he had once heard a character in a play utter in somewhat similar circumstances﻿—that there was nothing on earth so homely as an egg﻿—he had covered it with a handkerchief and tried to pull himself round with hot tea, He was now smoking a sad cigarette and waiting for the blow to fall.

Her silence had puzzled him. Though he had tried to give her no opportunity of getting him alone on the previous evening when he had arrived at the farm with Lord Dawlish, he had fully expected that she would have broken in upon him with abuse and recrimination in the middle of the night. Yet she had not done this, nor had she spoken to him when bringing him his breakfast. These things found their explanation in Elizabeth’s character, with which Nutty, though he had known her so long, was but imperfectly acquainted. Elizabeth had never been angrier with her brother, but an innate goodness of heart had prevented her falling upon him before he had had rest and refreshment. She wanted to massacre him, but at the same time she told herself that the poor dear must be feeling very, very ill and should have a reasonable respite before the slaughter commenced.

It was plain that in her opinion this respite had now lasted long enough. She looked over her shoulder to make sure that she had closed the door, then leaned a little forward and spoke.

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）走进纳蒂（Nutty）的房间，在床沿上坐下，用一双明亮而沉静的眼睛打量着他——那道目光在她哥哥那颗七上八下的良心上钻出一个又一个窟窿。这是她这天早晨第二回进他屋里来了。一个钟头以前，她端着放早餐的托盘来过一趟，一言未发便走了。正是她这股瘆人的沉默，比他在大都会狂欢作乐的余波——那余波至今还没有散尽——更叫纳蒂的早饭吃得败兴。他本就不是个胃口健旺的早餐客，这会儿在她那份无声的谴责底下，面对眼前那枚煎蛋，竟连咽也咽不下去了。他看了那蛋一眼，随即赞同了曾听某位戏剧人物在相近情境里道出的高见——天底下没有比一枚鸡蛋更家常的东西了——便拿手帕把它盖上，想靠热茶把自己缓过来。此刻他正凄凄然抽着一支香烟，静候那一记打击落下来。

她的沉默叫他摸不着头脑。昨天傍晚，他同达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）一道回到农场，当时他处处留神，不肯给她单独逮住自己的机会，心里却认定她半夜准会破门而入，把他痛骂数落一场。可她没有来，端早饭来时也没同他说一句话。这一切的谜底都埋在伊丽莎白的性格里，而那性格，纳蒂虽同她相识了这么多年，认识得却并不到家。伊丽莎白生平没有对哥哥生过这么大的气，然而天生一副好心肠拦住了她：总得让这可怜的人先歇一歇、缓一缓，再动手开刀问斩不迟。她是想把他就地正法的，可她同时又对自己说，这可怜的宝贝这会儿准病得很有两分了，屠戮开场之前，理应容他喘息片刻。

显然，照她看来，这段缓刑如今已经到头了。她回头望了一眼，确信房门已经关严，然后微微向前俯过身来，开了口。

===Original===

“Now, Nutty!”

The wretched youth attempted bluster.

“What do you mean﻿—‘Now, Nutty!’ What’s the use of looking at a fellow like that and saying, ‘Now, Nutty!’ Where’s the sense﻿—”

His voice trailed off. He was not a very intelligent young man, but even he could see that his was not a position where righteous indignation could be assumed with any solid chance of success. As a substitute he tried pathos.

===Chinese===

“好了，纳蒂！”

那苦命的青年试图虚张声势一番。

“你这是什么意思——‘好了，纳蒂！’那么个瞪法瞪着一个大活人，嘴里念叨‘好了，纳蒂！’，顶什么用？有什么道理可讲——”

他的声音渐渐泄了气。他本算不得个怎么聪明的青年，可连他也看得分明：眼下这个处境，摆出一副义正词严的架势，实在没有几成得胜的把握。于是他改弦更张，试起了可怜相。

===Original===

“Oo-oo, my head does ache!”

“I wish it would burst,” said his sister unkindly.

“That’s a nice thing to say to a fellow!”

“I’m sorry. I wouldn’t have said it﻿—”

“Oh, well!”

“Only I couldn’t think of anything worse.”

===Chinese===

“哎——哟，我的头好痛哟！”

“但愿它炸开来才好，”他妹妹口下毫不留情。

“这话亏你说得出口！”

“对不起。我本来不打算说这话的——”

“哼，那又怎么样！”

“只是一时想不出比这更难听的了。”

===Original===

It began to seem to Nutty that pathos was a bit of a flivver too. As a last resort he fell back on silence. He wriggled as far down as he could beneath the sheets and breathed in a soft and wounded sort of way. Elizabeth took up the conversation.

“Nutty,” she said, “I’ve struggled for years against the conviction that you were a perfect idiot. I’ve forced myself against my better judgment to try to look on you as sane, but now I give in. I can’t believe you are responsible for your actions. Don’t imagine that I am going to heap you with reproaches because you sneaked off to New York. I’m not even going to tell you what I thought of you for not sending me a thirty-cent telegram, letting me know where you were. I can understand all that. You were disappointed because Uncle Ira had not left you his money, and I suppose that was your way of working it off. If you had just run away and come back again with a headache, I’d have treated you like the Prodigal Son. But there are some things which are too much, and bringing a perfect stranger back with you for an indefinite period is one of them. I’m not saying anything against Mr. Chalmers personally. I haven’t had time to find out much about him, except that he’s an Englishman; but he looks respectable. Which, as a friend of yours, is more or less of a miracle.”

She raised her eyebrows, as a faint moan of protest came from beneath the sheets.

===Chinese===

纳蒂渐渐觉得，可怜相这条路看来也有点砸锅。万般无奈，他退守到沉默里去。他扭着身子往被单底下能钻多深钻多深，呼吸放得又轻又委屈。伊丽莎白接着把话头提了起来。

“纳蒂，”她说，“这许多年来，我一直强压着‘你是个十足的白痴’这个判断。我强迫自己违背更靠得住的见识，努力把你当个神志健全的人看，如今我认输了。我不能相信你还要对自己的行为负责。你别以为，就为你偷偷溜去纽约这一桩，我就要把你数落个底朝天。你连一封三十美分的电报都舍不得打给我，让我知道你在哪儿——就连这一条我也不打算说你。这些都懂。舅舅艾拉没把钱留给你，你失望了，我猜这一手就是你的排遣法子。你要是只顾自己跑出去，回来再添上一场头痛，我会把你当回头的浪子那样接待。可有些事就太不像话了，领回来一个素不相识的生人，住期还没有个准谱，便是其中之一。查默斯（Chalmers）先生本人，我倒没有什么好说的。我还没来得及多打听他，只知道他是个英国人；不过看上去倒还体面。就凭他是你的朋友这一层，这也多半算得上是个奇迹了。”

她扬了扬眉毛，因为被单底下发出了一声微微的呻吟，算是抗议。

===Original===

“You surely,” she said, “aren’t going to suggest at this hour of the day, Nutty, that your friends aren’t the most horrible set of pests outside a penitentiary? Not that it’s likely after all these months that they are outside a penitentiary. You know perfectly well that while you were running round New York you collected the most pernicious bunch of social gangsters that ever fastened their talons into a silly child who ought never to have been allowed out in a big city without his nurse.”

After which complicated insult Elizabeth paused for breath, and there was silence for a space.

“Well, as I was saying, I know nothing against this Mr. Chalmers. Probably his fingerprints are in the Rogues’ Gallery, and he is better known to the police as Jack the Blood or something, but he hasn’t shown that side of him yet. My point is that, whoever he is, I do not want him or anybody else coming and taking up his abode here while I have to be cook and housemaid too. I object to having a stranger on the premises spying out the nakedness of the land. I am sensitive about my honest poverty. So, darling Nutty, my precious Nutty, you miserable simp, you poor boneheaded muddler, will you kindly think up at your earliest convenience some plan for politely ejecting this Mr. Chalmers of yours from our humble home, because if you don’t I’m going to have a nervous breakdown.”

===Chinese===

“事到如今，”她说，“纳蒂，你总不至于还想暗示，说你的朋友并不是监狱高墙之外顶可怕的一窝祸害吧？再说，闹了这几个月，他们多半也未必还在高墙之外了。你心里透亮：你在纽约满城乱跑的那阵子，结识的正是天底下顶歹毒的一帮上流匪棍——他们把爪子扣进一个傻孩子的肉里，那样的孩子，没有保姆领着，本就不该放他进大城市去。”

这番结构复杂的辱骂发表完毕，伊丽莎白停下来缓了口气，一时屋里寂静无声。

“嗯，接着说方才的话：这位查默斯先生，我手里并没有他什么把柄。他的指纹多半已经进了警察局的惯犯照片馆（Rogues’ Gallery），警方那边的熟人都管他叫什么‘嗜血杰克’（Jack the Blood）之类的名号，只是他还没露出这一面来罢了。我的意思是，管他是谁，我不乐意他也好旁人也好，跑来这里安营扎寨，害得我又当厨娘又当侍女。我反对家里驻进一个生人，把我们这块穷家当的底细打探个精光。我这人对我这一身清白的穷很敏感。所以，亲爱的纳蒂，我的宝贝纳蒂，你这个可怜的蠢货，你这个榆木脑袋的糊涂虫，劳你大驾，越早越好，想出个什么法子来，客客气气地把你这位查默斯先生从我们这个寒碜家里请出去——因为你要是办不到，我可要精神崩溃了。”

===Original===

And, completely restored to good humor by her own eloquence, Elizabeth burst out laughing. It was a trait in her character which she had often lamented, that she could not succeed in keeping angry with anyone for more than a few minutes on end. Sooner or later some happy selection of a phrase of abuse would tickle her sense of humor, or the appearance of her victim would become too funny not to be laughed at. On the present occasion it was the ridiculous spectacle of Nutty cowering beneath the bedclothes that caused her wrath to evaporate. She made a weak attempt to recover it. She glared at Nutty, who at the sound of her laughter had emerged from under the clothes like a worm after a thunderstorm.

“I mean it,” she said. “It really is too bad of you! You might have had some sense and a little consideration. Ask yourself if we are in a position here to entertain visitors. Well, I’m going to make myself very unpopular with this Mr. Chalmers of yours. By this evening he will be regarding me with utter loathing, for I am about to persecute him.”

“What do you mean?” asked Nutty, alarmed.

===Chinese===

一番雄辩下来，伊丽莎白的心境已然彻底回春，噗嗤一声笑了出来。她性格里有一样东西，她常常引为憾事：无论对谁，她的气总撑不过连续几分钟。或早或晚，总有一句骂得妙的词儿会挠着她的痒处，要么就是挨骂那位的尊容变得滑稽得叫人不能不笑。眼下这一回，叫她的怒气化于无形的，正是纳蒂缩在被窝里的那副狼狈相。她还勉强试着把怒气收回来。她狠狠瞪着纳蒂——他听见她笑，早已像雷雨过后的一条蚯蚓，从被子底下钻出来了。

“我说真的，”她说，“你这一手实在太过分了！你总该有点脑子，稍许替旁人想一想。你扪心自问，我们这儿这副家底，配款待客人吗？好，我打定主意，要叫你这位查默斯先生把我讨厌到骨子里。到今天晚上，他看我的眼神里就只剩下深仇大恨了，因为我就要去迫害他了。”

“你这是什么意思？”纳蒂慌了神，问道。

===Original===

“I am going to begin by asking him to help me open one of the hives.”

“For heaven’s sake!”

“After that I shall﻿—with his assistance﻿—transfer some honey. And after that﻿—well, I don’t suppose he will be alive by then. If he is I shall make him wash the dishes for me. The least he can do, after swooping down on us like this, is to make himself useful.”

A cry of protest broke from the appalled Nutty, but Elizabeth did not hear it. She had left the room and was on her way downstairs.

===Chinese===

“我要先请他帮我开一个蜂箱。”

“看在老天爷分上！”

“然后我要——在他的协助下——转移一些蜂蜜。再然后嘛——我估计到那时候他已经不在人世了。要是还在，我就叫他替我洗碗碟。人家这样从天而降扑到我们头上，出把力总还是最起码的。”

吓坏了的纳蒂失声发出抗议，可伊丽莎白没有听见。她已经出了房间，正在下楼。

===Original===

Lord Dawlish was smoking an after-breakfast cigar in the grounds. It was a beautiful day, and a peaceful happiness had come upon him. He told himself that he had made progress. He was under the same roof as the girl he had deprived of her inheritance, and it should be simple to establish such friendly relations as would enable him to reveal his identity and ask her to reconsider her refusal to relieve him of a just share of her uncle’s money. He had seen Elizabeth for only a short time on the previous night, but he had taken an immediate liking to her. There was something about the American girl, he reflected, which seemed to put a man at his ease, a charm and directness all her own. Yes, he liked Elizabeth, and he liked this dwelling place of hers. He was quite willing to stay on here indefinitely.

Nature had done well by Flack’s. The house itself was an ordinary frame house, more pleasing to the eye than most of the houses in those parts, owing to the black and white paint which decorated it and an unconventional flattening and rounding of the roof. But Nature had made so many improvements that the general effect was unusually delightful. From where Bill stood linden trees, chestnut trees, locust trees and a solitary blue fir, the aristocrat of the garden, met his eye. The porch that ran round two sides of the house was almost hidden by masses of roses of Sharon. There were hydrangeas on the turf beyond the sandy drive, and more roses. To the left, shaded by a little regiment of apple trees, stood the beehives. The sun shone, a gentle breeze blew up from the bay, and the air was full of the soothing murmur of bees and the cheerful gossiping of crickets. Assuredly the lines were fallen unto him in pleasant places.

He perceived Elizabeth coming toward him from the house. He threw away his cigar and went to meet her. Seen by daylight she was more attractive than ever. She looked so small and neat and wholesome, so extremely unlike Miss Daisy Leonard’s friend. And such was the reaction from what might be termed his later Riegelheimer’s mood, that if he had been asked to define feminine charm in a few words, he would have replied without hesitation that it was the quality of being as different as possible in every way from the Good Sport. Elizabeth fulfilled this qualification. She was not only small and neat, but she had a soft voice to which it was a joy to listen.

===Chinese===

达利什勋爵正在园子里抽饭后的雪茄。天气好极了，一种安宁的幸福感落到他身上。他对自己说，事情有了进展。如今他同那被他夺去了继承权的姑娘同在一个屋檐底下了，只要把交情处到那一步，他就能亮明身份，请她重新考虑，莫再拒绝接下她叔叔钱财里理应归他的那一份，好卸掉他这副担子——这应当不难。昨天晚上他同伊丽莎白只匆匆见了一面，却一眼就喜欢上了她。美国姑娘身上有种东西，他暗自寻思，似乎天生就让人自在松泛，一种她们独有的魅力和爽直。是啊，他喜欢伊丽莎白，也喜欢她这处居所。要他在这儿长住下去，他一百个乐意。

造化待弗拉克家（Flack’s）着实不薄。房子本身是一座普普通通的木板屋，比那一带多数房子养眼些，全凭黑白两色的油漆装点，再加上屋顶一反常规地又压扁又收圆。可造化添置的改良这样多，整体的效果便出奇地宜人。从比尔（Bill）站的地方望出去，椴树、栗树、洋槐，还有一株孤零零的蓝杉——庭园里的贵族——尽收眼底。绕屋两面的游廊几乎教大丛大丛的木槿没了顶。沙土车道那边的草皮上开着绣球，还有更多的蔷薇。左边，在一小队苹果树的荫庇底下，立着那些蜂箱。太阳照着，一阵柔风从海湾那边吹上来，空气里满是蜜蜂安抚人心的嗡嗡和蟋蟀们快活的闲磕牙。诚然，用绳量给他的地界，正坐落在佳美之处。

他望见伊丽莎白从屋里出来，朝这边走。他扔掉雪茄，迎上前去。白日底下看她，比昨晚更动人。她显得那样小巧、利落、康健，同黛西·伦纳德（Daisy Leonard）小姐的那位朋友真是一个天上一个地下。而他此际正从里格尔海默餐厅（Riegelheimer’s）那场心境里反拨过来，倘若这会儿有人请他用几句话界定何为女性魅力，他会不假思索地答道：就是在方方面面都同爽快姑娘（the Good Sport）相反得越远越好的那种气质。伊丽莎白样样合格。她不只小巧利落，还有一副柔和的嗓音，听来叫人心悦。

===Original===

“I was just admiring your place,” he said.

“Its appearance is the best part of it,” said Elizabeth. “It is a deceptive place. The bay looks beautiful, but you can’t bathe in it because of the jellyfish. The woods are lovely, but you daren’t go near them because of the ticks.”

“Ticks?”

“They jump on you and suck your blood,” said Elizabeth carelessly. “And the nights are gorgeous, but you have to stay indoors after dusk because of the mosquitoes.” She paused to mark the effect of these horrors on her visitor. “And then, of course,” she went on, as he showed no signs of flying to the house to pack his bag and catch the next train, “the bees are always stinging you. I hope you are not afraid of bees, Mr. Chalmers?”

“Rather not. Jolly little chaps!”

===Chinese===

“我正在观赏你们这地方呢，”他说。

“外表是它顶好的一部分，”伊丽莎白说，“这地方专会骗人。海湾看着美，你可下不去水，水母闹的。树林子看着可爱，你可不敢挨近，蜱虫闹的。”

“蜱虫？”

“它们往你身上一蹦，就吸你的血，”伊丽莎白说得轻描淡写。“夜色倒是美极了，可黄昏一过你就得待在屋里，蚊子闹的。”她顿住话头，好觑看这些恐怖故事在客人身上起了什么效力。“还有呢，自然啦，”她接着说——他分明毫无飞奔回屋收拾提包、赶下一班火车之意——“蜜蜂还老蜇人。你不会是怕蜜蜂吧，查默斯先生？”

“怎么会怕。多可爱的小家伙！”

===Original===

A gleam appeared in Elizabeth’s eye.

“If you are so fond of them perhaps you wouldn’t mind coming and helping me open one of the hives?”

“Rather!”

“I’ll go and fetch the things.”

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白眼里闪过一道亮光。

“你既然这么疼它们，那你也许不介意过来帮我开一个蜂箱吧？”

“乐意之至！”

“我这就去取家伙。”

===Original===

She went into the house and ran up to Nutty’s room, waking that sufferer from a troubled sleep.

“Nutty, he’s bitten.”

Nutty sat up violently.

“Good Lord; what by?”

“You don’t understand. What I meant was that I invited your Mr. Chalmers to help me open a hive, and he said ‘Rather!’ and is waiting to do it now. Be ready to say goodbye to him. If he comes out of this alive his first act, after bathing the wounds with ammonia, will be to leave us forever.”

===Chinese===

她进屋上楼，直奔纳蒂的房间，把这位受难者从一场不安稳的瞌睡里摇醒了。

“纳蒂，他上钩了。”

纳蒂猛地弹坐起来。

“我的老天爷；叫什么咬了？”

“你不懂。我的意思是，我请你那位查默斯先生帮我开一个蜂箱，他说了声‘乐意之至！’，这会儿正等着动手呢。准备好同他道别吧。他要是能活着闯过这一关，头一件事——拿氨水洗完伤口之后——就是永远离开我们。”

===Original===

“But look here, he’s a visitor﻿—”

“Cheer up! He won’t be much longer.”

“You can’t let him in for a ghastly thing like opening a hive. When you made me do it that time I was picking stings out of myself for a week.”

“That was because you had been smoking. Bees dislike the smell of tobacco.”

===Chinese===

“可是，你瞧，他是客人——”

“高兴点儿！他做不长久了。”

“你可不能叫他遭开蜂箱这样的活罪。上回你逼我干那趟，我往自己身上挑蜂刺就挑了一个礼拜。”

“那是因为你抽过烟。蜜蜂不喜欢烟草味。”

===Original===

“But this fellow may have been smoking.”

“He has just finished a strong cigar.”

“For heaven’s sake!”

“Goodbye, Nutty, dear, I mustn’t keep him waiting.”

===Chinese===

“可这位爷说不定也抽了烟。”

“他刚抽完一支劲头很大的雪茄。”

“看在老天爷分上！”

“再见了，亲爱的纳蒂，我不能让他久等。”

===Original===

Lord Dawlish looked with interest at the various implements which she had collected when she rejoined him outside. He relieved her of the stool, the smoker, the cotton waste, the knife, the screwdriver and the queen clipping cage.

“Let me carry these for you,” he said, “unless you’ve hired a van.”

Elizabeth disapproved of this flippancy. It was out of place in one who should have been trembling at the prospect of doom. She threw her mind back to the first occasion on which she had opened a hive. Only a firm conviction that the bee-moth had been at work inside it had given her the courage to go through the ordeal. She could still recall the sensations attendant on taking out her first brood frame.

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白回到外头他身边时，达利什勋爵正饶有兴致地端详她收集来的各色家什。凳子、喷烟器、废棉纱、刀、螺丝刀、蜂王剪翅笼，他一样一样从她手里接了过去。

“这些让我替你拿，”他说，“除非你们还雇了一辆搬运车。”

伊丽莎白不满意这份轻浮。在一个本该听到大祸临头就浑身打颤的人身上，这股劲儿未免不对路。她把心思抛回自己头一回开蜂箱的那一天。全靠一口咬定里头有蜡螟在作祟，她才有那股勇气熬过那场酷刑。头一回往外抽巢框时的百般滋味，她至今记忆犹新。

===Original===

“Don’t you wear a veil for this sort of job?”

As a rule Elizabeth did. She had reached a stage of intimacy with her bees which rendered a veil a superfluous precaution, but until today she had never abandoned it. Her view of the matter was that, though the inhabitants of the hives were familiar and friendly with her by this time and recognized that she came among them without hostile intent, it might well happen that among so many thousands there might be one slow-witted enough and obtuse enough not to have grasped this fact. And in such an event a veil was better than any amount of explanations, for you cannot stick to pure reason when quarreling with bees. But today it had struck her that she could hardly protect herself in this way without offering a similar safeguard to her visitor and she had no wish to hedge him about with safeguards.

“Oh, no,” she said brightly; “I’m not afraid of a few bees. Are you?”

===Chinese===

“干这种活儿，你们不戴防蜂面纱的吗？”

平日里伊丽莎白是戴的。她同她的蜂群早已亲厚到面纱纯属多余的地步，可直到今天，她还从来没有弃用过。她对这事的看法是：蜂箱里的居民虽则如今同她厮熟而友善，也认得她出入其间并无相残之意，可是几千几万只里头，难保没有一只愚钝透顶、冥顽不化的，偏就没领会这一层。真到那步田地，一顶面纱胜过千言万语的解释，因为同蜜蜂拌嘴，是讲不得纯粹理性的。可今天她忽然想到：她若这样护住自己，却不同客人也备上一份同样的保障，未免说不过去；而她可无意拿保障把他层层围裹起来。

“哦，不怕，”她欢欢喜喜地说，“几只蜜蜂，我才不怕呢。你呢？”

===Original===

“Rather not!”

“You know what to do if one of them flies at you?”

“Well, it would anyway, what? What I mean to say is, I could leave most of the doing to the bee.”

Elizabeth was more disapproving than ever. This was mere bravado. She did not speak again until they reached the hives.

===Chinese===

“怎么会怕！”

“要是有一只朝你扑过来，你知道该怎么办吗？”

“这个嘛，它反正横竖要扑过来的，是不是？我是说，怎么办的问题，大半可以留给蜜蜂去办。”

伊丽莎白越发不以为然了。这纯粹是逞英雄。直到蜂箱跟前，她再没有开过口。

===Original===

In the neighborhood of the hives a vast activity prevailed. What, heard from afar, had been a pleasant murmur became at close quarters a menacing tumult. The air was full of bees﻿—bees sallying forth for honey, bees returning with honey, bees trampling on each other’s heels, bees pausing in midair to pass the time of day with rivals on competing lines of traffic. Blunt-bodied drones whizzed to and fro with a noise like miniature high-powered automobiles, as if anxious to convey the idea of being tremendously busy without going to the length of doing any actual work. One of these blundered into Lord Dawlish’s face, and it pleased Elizabeth to observe that he gave a jump.

“Don’t be afraid,” she said, “it’s only a drone. Drones have no stings.”

“They have hard heads though. Here he comes again!”

===Chinese===

蜂箱左近，一片大繁忙的景象。远听原是悦耳的嗡嗡，到了近前，就成了气势汹汹的喧腾。满空中都是蜜蜂——出征采蜜的蜜蜂，载蜜凯旋的蜜蜂，彼此踩着脚后跟的蜜蜂，悬在半空同对面航线上的对头寒暄问好的蜜蜂。身躯粗钝的雄蜂呜呜地横冲直撞，响动活像一台台小号的大马力汽车，一副急于表明自己忙得不可开交、却又绝不肯当真动手干活的架势。其中一只一头撞上了达利什勋爵的脸，伊丽莎白眼见他蹦了一下，心里大为受用。

“别怕，”她说，“不过是只雄蜂。雄蜂没有螫针。”

“它们脑袋可硬得很。瞧，它又来了！”

===Original===

“I suppose he smells your tobacco. A drone has thirty-seven thousand eight hundred nostrils, you know.”

“That gives him a sporting chance of smelling a fifteen-cent cigar, what? I mean to say, if he misses with eight hundred of his nostrils he’s apt to get it with the other thirty-seven thousand.”

Elizabeth was feeling annoyed with her bees. They resolutely declined to sting this young man. Bees flew past him, bees flew into him, bees settled upon his coat, bees paused questioningly in front of him, as who should say, “What have we here?” but not a single bee molested him. Yet when Nutty, poor darling, went within a dozen yards of the hives he never failed to suffer for it. In her heart Elizabeth knew perfectly well that this was because Nutty, when in the presence of the bees, lost his head completely and behaved like an exaggerated version of Lady Wetherby’s Dream of Psyche, whereas Bill maintained an easy calm; but at the moment she put the phenomenon down to that inexplicable cussedness which does so much to exasperate the human race, and it fed her annoyance with her unbidden guest.

===Chinese===

“我想它是闻见了你身上的烟味。你要知道，一只雄蜂有三万七千八百个鼻孔呢。”

“那么闻见一支十五美分的雪茄，总算有几分胜算了，是不是？我是说，就算八百个鼻孔落了空，剩下那三万七千个里头，总有一个能撞上的。”

伊丽莎白在生她的蜜蜂们的气。它们断然不肯蜇这位青年。蜜蜂从他身边掠过，蜜蜂撞进他怀里，蜜蜂落在他的外套上，蜜蜂在他面前疑疑惑惑地悬停着，仿佛在说：“这是哪来的家伙？”可就没有一只蜜蜂招惹他。而可怜的宝贝纳蒂，只要挪到离蜂箱十来码之内，就没有不遭殃的时候。伊丽莎白心里其实透亮：这是因为纳蒂一见到蜜蜂就方寸大乱，举止活脱脱是韦瑟比夫人（Lady Wetherby）《普绪克之梦》（Dream of Psyche）的夸张重演，而比尔却能从容自若；可眼下她把这现象归咎于那股没来由的邪性——人间多少恼火都拜它所赐——这份邪性又给她那不请自来的客人添了一层气。

===Original===

Without commenting on his last remark she took the smoker from him and set to work. She inserted in the fire chamber a handful of the cotton waste and set fire to it; then with a preliminary puff or two of the bellows to make sure that the conflagration had not gone out, she aimed the nozzle at the front door of the hive.

The results were instantaneous. One or two bee policemen, who were doing fixed-post duty near the opening, scuttled hastily back into the hive; and from within came a muffled buzzing as other bees, all talking at once, worried the perplexed officials with foolish questions, a buzzing that became less muffled and more pronounced as Elizabeth lifted the edge of the cover and directed more smoke through the crack. This done, she removed the cover, set it down on the grass beside her, lifted the supercover and applied more smoke, and raised her eyes to where Bill stood watching. His face wore a smile of pleased interest.

Elizabeth’s irritation became painful. She resented his smile. Nutty, on the famous occasion when she had induced him to help her open a hive, had wabbled with pure terror. She hung the smoker on the side of the hive.

===Chinese===

他方才那句话，她不加置评，从他手里接过喷烟器，动起手来。她往火室里塞了一把废棉纱，点上火；先鼓它一两下风箱，确证火头没有熄掉，然后把喷嘴对准了蜂箱的正门。

效力立竿见影。在巢门口站固定岗的一两名蜜蜂警察，慌忙缩回箱里去；接着里头传出一阵瓮声瓮气的嗡嗡——别的蜜蜂七嘴八舌，拿愚蠢的问题纠缠那几位丈二和尚摸不着头脑的官员；待伊丽莎白掀起箱盖的一角，顺着缝往里再灌一阵烟，那嗡嗡便不那么瓮了，愈发响亮起来。这套做完，她把盖子挪开，搁在身边的草地上，掀起继箱盖，又熏上一阵，然后抬起眼睛，望向站着观望的比尔。他脸上挂着一副又高兴又好奇的笑。

伊丽莎白的火气这会儿烧得她自己都难受了。她恨他那个笑。当初她哄纳蒂帮忙开蜂箱的那桩有名的旧事里，纳蒂可是抖得不成个样子，纯纯粹粹吓的。她把喷烟器挂上蜂箱的侧沿。

===Original===

“The stool, please, and the screw driver.”

She seated herself beside the hive and began to loosen the outside section. Then taking the brood frame by the projecting ends she pulled it out and handed it to her companion. She did it as one who plays an ace of trumps.

“Would you mind holding this, Mr. Chalmers?”

This was the point in the ceremony at which the wretched Nutty had broken down absolutely, and not inexcusably, considering the severity of the test. The surface of the frame was black with what appeared at first sight to be a thick, bubbling fluid of some sort, pouring viscously to and fro as if some hidden fire had been lighted beneath it. Only after a closer inspection was it apparent to the lay eye that this seeming fluid was in reality composed of mass upon mass of bees. They shoved and writhed and muttered and jostled, for all the world like a collection of home-seeking New Yorkers trying to secure standing room on a subway express at half-past five in the afternoon.

Nutty, making this discovery, had emitted one wild yell, dropped the frame, and started at full speed for the house, his retreat expedited by repeated stings from the nervous bees. Bill, more prudent, remained absolutely motionless. He eyed the seething frame with interest but without apparent panic.

===Chinese===

“劳驾，凳子，还有螺丝刀。”

她在蜂箱旁坐下，动手松动外层那节箱体。然后捏住巢框两头探出的框耳，把它拔了出来，递到同伴手里。那做派，俨然是打出一张王牌。

“麻烦您端着这个，查默斯先生，介意吗？”

典礼进行到这一步，正是那苦命的纳蒂当日彻底崩溃之处——鉴于这场考验的严酷程度，倒也情有可原。巢框表面黑压压的一片，乍一看像是某种稠得直冒泡的流体，黏黏糊糊地来回涌动，仿佛底下点着一炉看不见的火。非得凑近细看，外行人的眼睛才看得出：这疑似流体之物，其实是一层压着一层的蜜蜂叠成的。它们推搡、蠕动、嘁嘁低语、摩肩接踵，那光景活脱脱是一群归心似箭的纽约客，下午五点半钟，正在地铁快车上抢站位。

纳蒂当日一有这个发现，便发出一声狂叫，撒手扔了巢框，拔腿全速往屋里跑，那几只受了惊的蜜蜂连番下针，倒替他把脚程催得更急。比尔稳重些，站在原地纹丝不动。他兴味盎然地注视着那面沸腾的巢框，脸上却不见怎么惊慌。

===Original===

“I want you to help me here, Mr. Chalmers. You have stronger wrists than I have. I will tell you what to do. Hold the frame tightly.”

“I’ve got it.”

“Jerk it down as sharply as you can to within a few inches of the door, and then jerk it up again. You see that shakes them off.”

“It would me,” agreed Bill cordially, “if I were a bee.”

===Chinese===

“这地方我要请您搭把手，查默斯先生。您的腕子比我有力。我告诉您怎么办。把巢框攥紧了。”

“攥住了。”

“照您使得出的最大猛劲儿，把它往下一顿，顿到离巢门只有几寸的地方，再猛地往上一抬。您瞧，这就把它们抖下去了。”

“换了我也会的，”比尔由衷地附和道，“要我是只蜜蜂的话。”

===Original===

Elizabeth had the feeling that she had played her ace of trumps and by some miracle lost the trick. If this grisly operation did not daunt the man, nothing, not even the transferring of honey, would. She watched him as he raised the frame and jerked it down with a strong swiftness which her less powerful wrists had never been able to achieve. The bees tumbled off in a dense shower, asking questions to the last, then sighting the familiar entrance to the hive they bustled in without waiting to investigate the cause of the earthquake.

Lord Dawlish watched them go with a kindly interest.

“It has always been a mystery to me,” he said, “why they never seem to think of manhandling the Johnny who does that to them. They don’t seem able to connect cause and effect. I suppose the only way they can figure it out is that the bottom has suddenly dropped out of everything, and they are so busy lighting out for home that they haven’t time to go to the root of things. But it’s a ticklish job for all that, if you’re not used to it. I know when I first did it I shut my eyes and wondered whether they would bury my remains or cremate them.”

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白只觉得，她那张王牌打了出去，却不知撞了什么邪，这一墩竟输了。这么骇人的操作都吓不住这个人，那就再没有什么吓得住他了，连转移蜂蜜也算不上数。她看着他提起巢框，又猛往下一顿，那腕子又狠又快，是她那双弱腕从来不曾办到的。蜜蜂稠雨般哗啦啦坠落，喋喋打问到最后一刻，随后望见了蜂箱那扇熟悉的正门，便一窝蜂忙忙碌碌钻了进去，地震打哪儿来，压根懒得查究。

达利什勋爵和和气气、兴味盎然地目送它们进去了。

“有一桩事我一直纳闷，”他说，“它们怎么从来想不到，把这么作弄它们的那个家伙痛揍一顿。它们似乎不大会把因果两下里联系起来。我猜它们唯一能想通的讲法就是：天底下突然掉了底，它们又都忙着落荒逃家，顾不上去刨根问底。不过话说回来，这活儿要是生手来干，也够提心吊胆的。我记得我头一回干的时候，眼睛闭得铁紧，心里直琢磨：它们事后是给我收尸下葬呢，还是把我一把火烧了。”

===Original===

“When you first did it?” Elizabeth was staring at him blankly. “Have you done it before?”

Her voice shook. Bill met her gaze frankly.

“Done it before? Rather! Thousands of times. You see, I spent a year on a bee farm once, learning the business.”

===Chinese===

“你头一回干的时候？”伊丽莎白直勾勾地瞪着他，“你以前干过？”

她的声音都变了调。比尔坦然迎住她的目光。

“以前干过？那还用说！干过几千回了。是这样的，我当年在一个养蜂场上待过一年，学这一行。”

===Original===

For a moment mortification was the only emotion of which Elizabeth was conscious. She felt supremely ridiculous. For this she had schemed and plotted﻿—to give a practiced expert the opportunity of doing what he had done a thousand times before!

And then her mood changed in a flash. Nature has decreed that there are certain things in life which shall act as hoops of steel, grappling the souls of the elect together. Golf is one of these; a mutual love of horseflesh another; but the greatest of all is bees. Between two beekeepers there can be no strife. Not even a tepid hostility can mar their perfect communion. The petty enmities which life raises to be barriers between man and man and between man and woman vanish, once it is revealed to them that they are linked by this great bond. Envy, malice, hatred and all uncharitableness disappear, and they look into each other’s eyes and say “My brother!”

The effect of Bill’s words on Elizabeth was revolutionary. They crashed through her dislike, scattering it like an explosive shell. She had resented this golden young man’s presence at the farm. She had thought him in the way. She had objected to his becoming aware that she did such prosaic tasks as cooking and washing up. But now her whole attitude toward him was changed. He could stay there as long as he liked, the longer the better.

===Chinese===

有那么一瞬，羞臊是伊丽莎白唯一的知觉。她觉得自己荒唐到了极点。她机关算尽、处心积虑，原来是为了给一位熟练的老师傅提供机会，去做他早已做过几千遍的事！

接着，她的心境霎时转了个向。造化早已注定：人生在世，有几种东西要充任钢箍，把天选之人的灵魂牢牢箍作一处。高尔夫是其中之一；对马的同好又是一项；而至大的一项，是蜜蜂。两个养蜂人之间，容不得任何争斗。连不冷不热的敌意也败坏不了他们圆满无间的相通。人生砌在人与人、男人与女人之间的那些小肚鸡肠的藩篱，一旦揭晓他们原是被这条伟大的纽带连在一起的，便都消融了。嫉妒、怨毒、仇恨和一切不仁不慈尽皆退散，他们四目相望，唤一声“我的兄弟！”

比尔这番话对伊丽莎白起了翻天覆地的效力。它们轰然击穿她的厌恶，像一颗开花弹把它炸得四散。她原本嫌恶这位金光灿烂的青年驻在农场，觉得他碍手碍脚，又不愿意让他察觉自己竟操持烧饭洗碗这等凡俗家务。可如今她对他的整个态度都变了。他愿意住多久就住多久，越久越好。

===Original===

“You have really kept bees?”

“Not actually kept them, worse luck; I couldn’t raise the capital. You see, money was a bit tight﻿—”

“I know,” said Elizabeth sympathetically. “Money is like that, isn’t it?”

“The general impression seemed to be that I should be foolish to try anything so speculative as beekeeping, so it fell through. Some very decent old boys got me another job.”

===Chinese===

“你真的养过蜂？”

“说真的养倒没养上，倒霉就倒霉在这儿；本钱凑不出来。是这样的，手头有点紧——”

“我懂，”伊丽莎白满心同情地说，“钱就是这副德行，是不是？”

“周遭众口一词，都觉得我去尝试养蜂这种投机营生未免犯傻，于是这事就黄了。几位顶厚道的老前辈替我另谋了一份差事。”

===Original===

“What job?”

“Secretary of a club.”

“In London, of course?”

“Yes.”

“And all the time you wanted to be in the country keeping bees!”

Elizabeth could hardly control her voice, her pity was so great.

===Chinese===

“什么差事？”

“一家俱乐部的秘书。”

“自然是在伦敦？”

“对。”

“可你从头到尾一心想的，是在乡下养蜜蜂！”

伊丽莎白的怜惜汹涌如潮，嗓音都几乎把持不住了。

===Original===

“I should have liked it,” said Bill wistfully. “London’s all right, but I love the country. My ambition would be to have a whacking big farm, a sort of ranch miles away from anywhere﻿—”

He broke off. This was not the first time he had caught himself forgetting how his circumstances had changed in the past two weeks. It was ridiculous to be telling hard-luck stories about not being able to buy a farm, when he had the wherewithal to buy dozens of farms. It took a lot of getting used to, this business of being a millionaire.

“That’s my ambition too,” said Elizabeth eagerly. This was the very first time she had met a congenial spirit. Nutty’s views on farming and the Arcadian life generally were saddening to an enthusiast. “If I had the money I should get an enormous farm, and in the summer I should go through the East Side and borrow all the children I could find there, and take them out to it and let them wallow in it.”

“Wouldn’t they do a lot of damage?”

“I shouldn’t mind. I should be too rich to worry about the damage. If they ruined the place beyond repair I’d go and buy another.” She laughed. “It isn’t so impossible as it sounds. I came very near being able to do it.” She paused for a moment, but went on almost at once. After all, if you cannot confide your intimate troubles to a fellow bee-lover to whom can you confide them? “An uncle of mine﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我本来会喜欢那样的日子，”比尔悠悠神往地说，“伦敦也还过得去，可我爱乡下。我的心愿，本是有朝一日置下一座大得没边的农场，一种远离尘寰的牧场地——”

他戛然而止。两个星期以来境遇天翻地覆，自己一不留神就忘了，这已不是头一遭。明明手里握着买下几十座农场的财力，却还在哭穷，说什么买不起农场，岂不可笑。当百万富翁这桩事，真还得花大力气去习惯才成。

“这也是我的心愿，”伊丽莎白急切地说。志趣相投的人，她平生还是头一遭遇上。纳蒂对农事、对田园牧歌生活的一路高见，落到一个热心人耳朵里只有叫人心寒的份。“我要是有钱，就置一座大得惊人的农场，一到夏天，就走遍东区（East Side），把能碰见的孩子们统统借来，带他们到农场去，由着他们在地里打滚撒欢。”

“那还不得把地方作践得不成样子？”

“我才不在乎呢。我那么有钱，才不会为这点作践操心。他们要是把地方糟蹋得修无可修，我就再去买一座。”她笑了。“这事听着玄，其实不见得全然无望。我差一点儿就真能办到了。”她顿了顿，却几乎立刻又往下说。说到底，贴己的烦难若不能对一个同爱蜜蜂的人倾诉，还能对谁倾诉？“我有一位舅舅——”

===Original===

Bill felt himself flushing. He looked away from her. He had a sense of almost unbearable guilt, as if he had just done some particularly low crime and was contemplating another.

“An uncle of mine would have left me enough money to buy all the farms I wanted, only an awful person﻿—an English lord﻿—I wonder if you have heard of him?﻿—Lord Dawlish﻿—got hold of uncle somehow and induced him to make a will leaving all the money to him.”

She looked at Bill for sympathy, and was touched to see that he was crimson with emotion. He must be a perfect dear to take other people’s misfortunes to heart like that.

===Chinese===

比尔觉出自己的脸在发烫。他把目光从她身上挪开。一股几乎承受不住的负疚感攫住了他，仿佛他刚干下一桩格外下作的罪行，正在盘算下一桩。

“我有一位舅舅，本来会留给我一笔钱，我想要多少农场就买得起多少，偏偏有一个万恶的家伙——一个英国勋爵——不知您听说过没有？——达利什勋爵——不知怎么缠上了舅舅，撺掇他立下遗嘱，把钱统统留给了他。”

她望向比尔讨几分同情，却见他激动得满脸通红，不由得心头一热：像这样把别人的不幸痛切于心，这人准是个顶好顶好的人。

===Original===

“I don’t know how he managed it,” she went on. “He must have worked and plotted and schemed, for Uncle Ira wasn’t a weak sort of man whom you could do what you liked with. He was very obstinate. But anyway this Lord Dawlish succeeded in doing it somehow, and then”﻿—her eyes blazed at the recollection﻿—“he had the insolence to write to me through his lawyers, offering me half. I suppose he was hoping to satisfy his conscience. Naturally I refused it.”

“But﻿—but﻿—but why?”

“Why! Why did I refuse it? Surely you don’t think I was going to accept charity from the man who had cheated me?”

===Chinese===

“他怎么得手的，我不知道，”她接着说，“他准是使尽了手脚、机关算尽，因为舅舅艾拉可不是那种任人摆布的软面团。他倔得很。可不管怎么说，这位达利什勋爵居然就办成了；这还不算，”——想起旧事，她两眼冒火——“他还厚着脸皮托律师写信给我，要把一半分给我。我猜他是想安抚他自己的良心。我自然拒了。”

“可——可是——可是为什么呢？”

“为什么！我为什么拒了？你总不见得以为，我会收下一个骗了我的人的施舍吧？”

===Original===

“But﻿—but perhaps he didn’t mean it like that. What I mean to say is﻿—as charity, you know.”

“He did! But don’t let’s talk of it any more. It makes me angry to think of him, and there’s no use spoiling a lovely day like this by getting angry.”

Bill sighed. He had never dreamed before that it could be so difficult to give money away. He was profoundly glad that he had not revealed his identity, as he had been on the very point of doing just when she began her remarks. He understood now why that curt refusal had come in answer to his lawyer’s letter. Well, there was nothing to be done, nothing but wait and hope that time might accomplish something.

===Chinese===

“可——可是，兴许他不是那个意思。我是说——他那份儿不算施舍，你知道。”

“他就是那个意思！不过这档子事别再提了。一想到他我就来气，这么好的天，犯不上拿生气去糟蹋。”

比尔叹了口气。送钱出去竟有这般艰难，他从前做梦也不曾想到。他庆幸自己没有亮明身份——就在她开口讲这番话的当口，他正差一点儿就亮了。他那封律师函换回来如此斩钉截铁的一口回绝，如今谜底也解开了。唉，眼下无计可施，只有等下去，盼着光阴能成全点什么。

===Original===

“What do you want me to do next?” he said. “Why did you open the hive? Did you want to take a look at the queen?”

Elizabeth hesitated. She blushed with pure shame. She had had but one motive in opening the hive, and that had been to annoy him. She scorned to take advantage of the loophole he had provided. Beekeeping is a freemasonry. A beekeeper cannot deceive a brother mason.

She faced him bravely.

“I didn’t want to take a look at anything, Mr. Chalmers. I opened that hive because I wanted you to drop the frame, as my brother did, and get stung, as he was; because I thought that would drive you away, because I thought then that I didn’t want you down here. I’m ashamed of myself, and I don’t know where I’m getting the nerve to tell you this. I hope you will stay on﻿—on and on and on.”

===Chinese===

“接下来您要我做什么？”他说。“您开箱是要干什么？想看看蜂王吗？”

伊丽莎白迟疑了。她臊得满脸通红。她开箱本来就只有一个动机，那就是招他厌烦。他递过来的这个台阶，她不屑去占。养蜂是一门兄弟会的行当。一个养蜂人骗不得同门的弟兄。

她硬起头皮，正面迎了上去。

“我什么都不想看，查默斯先生。我开那个蜂箱，是盼着你把巢框扔了，像我哥哥那样，再叫它们蜇几下，也像我哥哥那样；因为我以为那样就能把你赶走，因为我那时候认定，我不想让你到这儿来。我为自己害臊，也不知道打哪儿来的胆子，把这话对你说出来。我希望你住下去——住下去，住下去，一直住下去。”

===Original===

Bill was aghast.

“Good Lord! If I’m in the way﻿—”

“You aren’t in the way.”

“But you said﻿—”

“But don’t you see that it’s so different now? I didn’t know then that you were fond of bees. You must stay, if my telling you hasn’t made you feel that you want to catch the next train. You will save our lives﻿—mine and Nutty’s too. Oh dear, you’re hesitating! You’re trying to think up some polite way of getting out of the place! You mustn’t go, Mr. Chalmers; you simply must stay. There aren’t any mosquitoes, no jellyfish﻿—nothing! At least there are, but what do they matter? You don’t mind them. Do you play golf?”

===Chinese===

比尔大惊失色。

“老天爷！要是我在这儿碍事——”

“你不碍事。”

“可你刚才说——”

“可你还不明白吗？如今全不一样了。我那时候不知道你喜爱蜜蜂。你一定得留下，要是我这番话还没叫你打定主意去赶下一班火车的话。你要救我们的命——我的命，还有纳蒂的。哎呀，你迟疑了！你在琢磨什么客客气气的脱身之法！你不能走，查默斯先生；说什么你也得留下。这儿没有蚊子，没有水母——什么都没有！至少是有的，可那碍什么事？你不在乎它们。你打高尔夫吗？”

===Original===

“Yes.”

“There are links here. You can’t go until you’ve tried them. What is your handicap?”

“Plus two.”

“So is mine.”

“By Jove! Really?”

===Chinese===

“打。”

“这儿就有球场。不去试上一轮，你不许走。你的差点是多少？”

“正二。”

“我也是正二。”

“好家伙！真的吗？”

===Original===

Elizabeth looked at him, her eyes dancing.

“Why, we’re practically twin souls, Mr. Chalmers! Tell me! I know your game is nearly perfect, but if you have a fault, is it a tendency to putt too hard?”

“Why, by Jove, yes, it is!”

“I knew it. Something told me. It’s the curse of my life too! Well, after that you can’t go away.”

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白望着他，眼睛里光彩流转。

“哎呀，咱们俩简直是一对孪生知音了，查默斯先生！快告诉我！我知道你的球技近乎完美，可你若有一桩毛病，是不是推杆下手太重？”

“哎呀，好家伙，还真是！”

“我就知道。心里早有个声音告诉我了。这也是缠我一生的魔咒！好，听过这一层，你可更走不得了。”

===Original===

“But if I’m in the way﻿—”

“In the way! Mr. Chalmers, will you come in now and help me wash the breakfast things?”

“Rather!” said Lord Dawlish.

===Chinese===

“可要是我在这儿碍事——”

“碍事！查默斯先生，你现在就进屋来，帮我洗早饭的碗碟，好不好？”

“乐意之至！”达利什勋爵说。

## V / 第五章

===Original===
The village of Brookport, Long Island, is a summer place. It lives, like the mosquitoes that infest it, entirely on its summer visitors, that hardy race which, once a year for a period of three months, gives up the comfort and coolness of spacious New York apartments to stew in stuffy cottages along the shores of the Great South Bay. At the time of the death of Mr. Ira Nutcombe, the only all-the-year-round inhabitants were the butcher, the grocer, the drugstore man, the other customary fauna of villages, and Miss Elizabeth Boyd, who rented the ramshackle farm known locally as Flack’s and eked out a precarious livelihood by keeping bees.

If you take down your *Encyclopedia Britannica*﻿—Volume III, **Aus** to **Bis**, you will find that bees are a “large and natural family of the zoological order *Hymenoptera*, characterized by the plumose form of many of their hairs, by the large size of the basal segment of the foot﻿ ﻿… and by the development of a tongue for sucking liquid food,” the last of which peculiarities, it is interesting to note, they shared with Claude Nutcombe Boyd, Elizabeth’s brother, who for quite a long time﻿—till his money ran out﻿—had made liquid food almost his sole means of sustenance. These things, however, are by the way. We are not such snobs as to think better or worse of a bee because it can claim kinship with the *Hymenoptera* family, nor so ill-bred as to chaff it for having large feet. The really interesting passage in the article occurs later, where it says: “The bee industry prospers greatly in America.”

This is one of those broad statements that invite challenge. Elizabeth Boyd would have challenged it. She had not prospered greatly. With considerable trouble she contrived to pay her way, and that was all.

Again referring to the *Encyclopedia*, we find the words: “Before undertaking the management of a modern apiary, the beekeeper should possess a certain amount of aptitude for the pursuit.” This was possibly the trouble with Elizabeth’s venture, considered from a commercial point of view. She loved bees, but she was not an expert on them. She had started her apiary with a small capital, a dollar book of practical hints, and a secondhand queen, principally because she was in need of some occupation that would enable her to live in the country. It was the unfortunate condition of Claude Nutcombe which made life in the country a necessity. At that time he was spending the remains of the money left him by his aunt, and Elizabeth had hardly settled down at Brookport and got her venture under way when she found herself obliged to provide for Nutty a combination of home and sanatorium. It had been the poor lad’s mistaken view that he could drink up all the alcoholic liquor in America.

===Chinese===
长岛（Long Island）的布鲁克波特（Brookport）村，是个消夏的地方。它和滋扰全村的蚊子一样，全靠避暑客养活——那是一族吃苦耐劳的人马，每年一度，为期三个月，甘愿舍弃纽约宽敞公寓的舒适清凉，跑到大南湾（Great South Bay）沿岸一间间闷热的小屋里去受煎熬。艾拉·纳特科姆（Ira Nutcombe）先生去世的当口，村里一年到头住着的，只有屠户、杂货商、药铺伙计和村镇惯见的其余动物群，再有就是伊丽莎白·博伊德（Elizabeth Boyd）小姐——她租下本地人唤作“弗拉克家”（Flack's）的那座东倒西歪的农场，靠养蜂勉强糊口，日子过得朝不保夕。

倘若你取下那部《不列颠百科全书》（Encyclopedia Britannica）——第三卷，Aus 至 Bis——你会查到，蜜蜂是“动物学上膜翅目（Hymenoptera）中一个庞大的自然科，其特征在于许多毛作羽状，足的基节硕大……并且生有一条为吮吸液体食物而发育的舌”。说来有趣，最后这条特征，伊丽莎白的弟弟克劳德·纳特科姆·博伊德（Claude Nutcombe Boyd）也与蜜蜂共有：在相当长的一段时期里——直到他的钱花光为止——液体食物几乎是他唯一的给养。不过这些都属题外话。我们还不至势利到因为一只蜜蜂攀得上膜翅目的亲，便高看它一眼或低看它一眼；也不至失礼到笑话它脚生得大。这篇文章里真正有意思的话在后头，那里说：“养蜂业在美国极为兴旺。”

这属于那种一出口就招人驳难的笼统话。伊丽莎白·博伊德就会驳它。她兴旺得实在有限。她千辛万苦才勉强做到不欠账，如此而已。

再翻回那部百科全书，我们又读到这样一句话：“在着手经营一座现代化蜂场之前，养蜂人应当对这行营生具备一定的天分。”若从商业眼光来看，伊丽莎白那桩事业的病根，恐怕正在这里。她爱蜜蜂，却算不得蜜蜂的行家。她当初创办蜂场，本钱很小，只有一本一块钱的实用入门书和一只二手蜂王，主要的缘故，是她需要一份营生，好让自己能住在乡下。是克劳德·纳特科姆那副光景，使住在乡下成了必需。那时他正花着姑妈留给他的那笔钱的余头；伊丽莎白在布鲁克波特刚刚落脚、把营生张罗上路，便发觉自己不得不给纳蒂（Nutty）置办一个家兼疗养院的所在。这位可怜的少爷先前有个错误看法：他以为全美国的酒，是可以由他一个人喝干的。

===Original===
It is a curious law of Nature that the most undeserving brothers always have the best sisters. Thrifty, plodding young men, who get up early, and do it now, and catch the boss’ eye, and save half their salaries, have sisters who never speak civilly to them except when they want to borrow money. To the Claude Nutcombes of the world are vouchsafed the Elizabeths.

The great aim of Elizabeth’s life was to make a new man of Nutty. It was her hope that the quiet life and soothing air of Brookport, with﻿—unless you counted the dime-in-the-slot musical box at the drugstore﻿—its absence of the fiercer excitements, might in time pull him together and unscramble his disordered nervous system. She liked to listen of a morning to the sound of Nutty busy in the next room with a broom and a dustpan, for in the simple lexicon of Flack’s there was no such word as “help.” The privy purse would not run to maid or hired man. Elizabeth did the cooking and Claude Nutcombe the chores.

Several days after Claire Fenwick and Lord Dawlish, by different routes, had sailed from England, Elizabeth Boyd sat up in bed and shook her mane of hair from her eyes, yawning. Outside her window the birds were singing, and a shaft of sunlight intruded itself beneath the shade. But what definitely convinced her that it was time to get up was the plaintive note of James, the cat, patrolling the roof of the porch. An animal of regular habits, James always called for breakfast at eight-thirty sharp.

===Chinese===
自然间有条古怪的定律：最不成器的弟弟，偏偏总有最好的姐姐。那些节俭勤谨、起得了早、说干就干、会讨老板青眼、薪水能省下一半的年轻人，他们的姐姐却从不同他们好声好气地说话，除非想跟他们借钱。而上天惠赐给世上克劳德·纳特科姆之辈的，是一个个伊丽莎白。

伊丽莎白平生的大志，是把纳蒂改造成一个新人。她指望布鲁克波特清静的日子、安神的空气——若不算药铺里那台投币自奏的音乐盒，此地并无什么剧烈的刺激——假以时日，能把他这个人重新拢拢紧，理顺他那乱作一团的神经系统。她喜欢在早晨听隔壁屋里纳蒂操着扫帚和簸箕忙活的声响，因为在弗拉克家的简明词典里，压根没有“帮工”这个词。私房钱雇不起使女，也雇不起长工。做饭的是伊丽莎白，打杂的是克劳德·纳特科姆。

克莱尔·芬威克（Claire Fenwick）与达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）各走各的水路、先后乘船离开英国几天之后，伊丽莎白·博伊德在床上坐起身来，打着哈欠，把垂在眼前的鬃毛也似的头发甩开。窗外鸟儿在歌唱，一缕阳光从帘影底下挤了进来。然而真正让她确信该起床的，是猫儿詹姆斯（James）在门廊顶上巡逻时那一声声哀怨的叫唤。詹姆斯是只作息极有规律的动物，总在八点半整讨要早饭。

===Original===
Elizabeth got out of bed, wrapped her small body in a pink kimono, thrust her small feet into a pair of blue slippers, yawned again and went downstairs. Having taken last night’s milk from the icebox, she went to the back door and, having filled James’ saucer, stood on the grass beside it, sniffing the morning air.

Elizabeth Boyd was twenty-one, but standing there with her hair tumbling about her shoulders she might have been taken by a not too close observer for a child. It was only when you saw her eyes and the resolute tilt of the chin that you realized that she was a young woman very well able to take care of herself in a difficult world. Her hair was very fair, her eyes brown and very bright, and the contrast was extraordinarily piquant. They were valiant eyes, full of spirit; eyes, also, that saw the humor of things. And her mouth was the mouth of one who laughs easily. Her chin, small like the rest of her, was strong; and in the way she held herself there was a boyish jauntiness. She looked﻿—and was﻿—a capable little person.

She stood beside James like a sentinel, watching over him as he breakfasted. There was a puppy belonging to one of the neighbors, who sometimes lumbered over and stole James’ milk, disposing of it in greedy gulps while its rightful proprietor looked on with piteous helplessness. Elizabeth was fond of the puppy, but her sense of justice was keen and she was there to check this brigandage.

It was a perfect day, cloudless and still. There was peace in the air. James, having finished his milk, began to wash himself. A squirrel climbed cautiously down from a linden tree. From the orchard came the murmur of many bees.

===Chinese===
伊丽莎白下了床，把小小的身子裹进一件粉红和服式晨衣，把小小的脚塞进一双蓝拖鞋，又打了个哈欠，下楼去。她从冰箱里取出昨夜的牛奶，走到后门口，把詹姆斯的碟子倒满，然后站在碟边的草地上，嗅着清晨的空气。

伊丽莎白·博伊德二十一岁，可她这样站在那儿，头发散乱地披在肩头，观者若不凑得很近，多半会当她是个孩子。只有看到她的眼睛、看到她那下巴坚决的一翘，你才明白她是个年轻女子，在这个艰难的世上，完全照管得了自己。她的头发是很淡的金黄色，眼睛是棕色的，亮得不得了，两相映衬，别有一种俏皮撩人的韵味。那是一双勇敢的眼睛，神气十足，又善于从万事万物里看出可笑之处。她的嘴，是一张轻易便漾出笑来的嘴。她的下巴同她整个人一样小巧，却很有力；她的举手投足里，带着一股男孩子似的洒脱劲儿。她看上去——也的确是——一个能干的小人儿。

她像哨兵似的站在詹姆斯身旁，守着它用早饭。邻居家有只小狗，时常摇摇摆摆地跋涉过来，偷詹姆斯的牛奶，几大口贪婪地灌下肚去，可怜的正主只能在一旁眼睁睁看着，无助得叫人心疼。伊丽莎白喜欢那只小狗，可她的正义感极其旺盛，此刻守在此地，正是为了制止这桩强盗行径。

那是个十全十美的日子，万里无云，纹风不动。空气里一片安宁。詹姆斯喝完了牛奶，开始舔洗自己。一只松鼠小心翼翼地从一棵椴树上爬下来。果园那头，传来万千蜜蜂的嗡嗡低语。

===Original===
Aesthetically Elizabeth was fond of still, cloudless days, but experience had taught her to suspect them. As was the custom in that locality, the water supply depended on a rickety wind wheel. It was with a dark foreboding that she returned to the kitchen and turned on one of the taps. For perhaps three seconds a stream of the dimension of a darning needle emerged, then with a sad gurgle the tap relapsed into a stolid inaction. There is no stolidity so utter as that of a waterless tap.

“Damn!” said Elizabeth.

She passed through the dining room to the foot of the stairs.

“Nutty!”

There was no reply.

===Chinese===
论审美，伊丽莎白喜欢这种无风无云的日子；论经验，她早已学会对它们起疑。照那一带地方的老规矩，用水全指望一架摇摇欲坠的抽水风车。她怀着不祥的预感回到厨房，拧开一只水龙头。约莫三秒钟，一股织补针粗细的水流钻了出来，随后那龙头咕的一声哀鸣，重新陷入木然的罢工。天底下没有一种麻木，麻木得过断了水的龙头。

“该死！”伊丽莎白说。

她穿过餐室，走到楼梯口。

“纳蒂！”

没有回音。

===Original===
“Nutty, my precious lamb!”

Upstairs in the room next to her own a long, spare form began to uncurl itself in bed; a face with a receding chin and a small forehead raised itself reluctantly from the pillow, and Claude Nutcombe Boyd signalized the fact that he was awake by scowling at the morning sun and uttering an aggrieved groan.

Alas, poor Nutty! This was he whom but yesterday Broadway had known as the Speed Kid, on whom headwaiters had smiled and lesser waiters fawned; whose snakelike form had nestled in so many a front row orchestra chair. Where were his lobster Newburgs now, his cold quarts that were wont to set the table in a roar?

Nutty Boyd conformed as nearly as a human being may to Euclid’s definition of a straight line. He was length without breadth. From boyhood’s early day he had sprouted like a weed, till now in the middle twenties he gave startled strangers the conviction that it only required a sharp gust of wind to snap him in half. Lying in bed he looked more like a length of hose pipe than anything else. While he was unwinding himself the door opened and Elizabeth came into the room.

===Chinese===
“纳蒂，我的心肝宝贝！”

楼上她隔壁的房间里，一条又长又瘦的身形开始在床上舒展开来；一张下巴后削、额头窄小的脸，不情愿地从枕头上抬起来，克劳德·纳特科姆·博伊德朝着早晨的太阳怒目而视，又发出一声满腹委屈的呻吟，以此昭告自己已经醒了。

唉，可怜的纳蒂！就在昨天，百老汇（Broadway）还认得这位“飞毛腿小子”（Speed Kid）：领班们见了他满脸堆笑，普通侍者们更是曲意奉承；他那蛇一般的身形，曾在多少戏院正厅头排的座椅里安过窝。他那些纽堡龙虾（lobster Newburg）如今安在？那些一摆上桌就叫满座哄堂的冰镇夸脱装香槟，又在何方？

纳蒂·博伊德这个人，几乎像血肉之躯所能做到的那样，完全符合欧几里得（Euclid）给直线下的定义。他有长无宽。打从孩提时代起，他就同野草似的一个劲儿往上蹿，如今到了二十五六岁，乍一见他的陌生人都会惊愕地确信：只消一阵狂风，就能把他拦腰吹断。他躺在床上，看上去最像一段浇园的水管。他还在一边舒展自己的当儿，房门开了，伊丽莎白走了进来。

===Original===
“Good morning, Nutty.”

“What’s the time?” asked her brother hollowly.

“Getting on for nine. It’s a lovely day. The birds are singing, the bees are buzzing, summer’s in the air. It’s one of those beautiful, shiny, heavenly, gorgeous days.”

A look of suspicion came into Nutty’s eyes. Elizabeth was not often as lyrical as this.

“There’s a catch somewhere,” he said.

===Chinese===
“早上好，纳蒂。”

“什么时辰了？”哥哥有气无力地问。

“快九点了。天气好极了。鸟儿在唱，蜜蜂在嗡，满空气里都是夏天。正是那种漂亮、鲜亮、天堂一般、灿烂夺目的好日子。”

纳蒂的眼里浮起一层疑云。伊丽莎白难得有这样好的诗兴。

“这里头准有诈。”他说。

===Original===
“Well, as a matter of fact,” said Elizabeth carelessly, “the water’s off again.”

“Damn!”

“I said that. I’m afraid we aren’t a very original family.”

“What a ghastly joint this is! Why can’t you see old Flack and make him fix that infernal wheel up?”

“I’m going to pounce on him and have another try directly I see him. Meanwhile, darling Nutty, will you get some clothes on and go round to the Smiths and ask them to lend us a pailful?”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，说实在的，”伊丽莎白若无其事地说，“水又停了。”

“该死！”

“这话我说过了。恐怕咱们这一家，独创性不大够。”

“这鬼地方真是活受罪！你怎么不去找老弗拉克，逼他把那座该死的风车修好？”

“我一见着他就要扑上去，再试一回。眼下呢，亲爱的纳蒂，劳驾你把衣裳穿上，到史密斯家（the Smiths）跑一趟，请他们借咱们一桶水，好不好？”

===Original===
“Oh, gosh, it’s over a mile!”

“No, no, not more than three-quarters.”

“Lugging a pail that weighs a ton! The last time I went there their dog bit me.”

“I expect that was because you slunk in all doubled up, and he got suspicious. You should hold your head up and throw your chest out and stride up as if you were a military friend of the family.”

Self-pity lent Nutty eloquence.

“For heaven’s sake! You drag me out of bed at some awful hour of the morning when a rational person would just be turning in; you send me across country to carry pailfuls of water when I’m feeling like a corpse; and on top of that you expect me to behave like a drum major!”

===Chinese===
“哦，天哪，那有一英里多呢！”

“不，不，顶多四分之三英里。”

“拎着一只一吨重的水桶！上回我去，他们家的狗还咬了我一口。”

“我猜那是因为你缩成一团、鬼鬼祟祟地溜进去，它起了疑心。你应该抬起头，挺起胸，大步走上前去，摆出一副你是这家人的军中故旧的气派。”

自怜给了纳蒂雄辩之才。

“看在老天爷分上！你大清早在一个骇人的时辰把我从床上拖起来——那个时辰，明白人正好刚要上床；你又在我感觉自己像一具尸首的时候，打发我横穿乡野去拎一桶桶的水；在这之上，你还指望我走出个鼓乐队领队的气派！”

===Original===
“Dearest, you can behave as you like, so long as you get the fluid. We must have water. I can’t fetch it. I’m a delicately nurtured female.”

“We ought to have a man to do these ghastly jobs.”

“But we can’t afford one. Who do you think I am, Nutty﻿—Hetty Green? Just at present all I ask is to be able to pay expenses. And, as a matter of fact, you ought to be very thankful that you have got﻿—”

“A roof over my head? I know. You needn’t keep rubbing it in.”

Elizabeth flushed.

===Chinese===
“最亲爱的，你爱怎么走怎么走，只要把那种流体弄回来就行。咱们非有水不可。我可提不动。我是娇生惯养的女性。”

“这些要命的活计，本该雇个男人来干。”

“可咱们雇不起。你当我是什么人，纳蒂——海蒂·格林（Hetty Green）吗？眼下我只求收支相抵就心满意足了。而且，实话跟你说，你该大大感激自己还有——”

“头上还有片瓦？我知道。你不必一个劲儿往伤口上撒盐。”

伊丽莎白脸红了。

===Original===
“I wasn’t going to say that at all. What a pig you are sometimes, Nutty! As if I wasn’t only too glad to have you here! What I was going to say was that you ought to be very thankful that you have got to draw water and hew wood.”

A look of absolute alarm came into Nutty’s pallid face.

“You don’t mean to say that you want some wood chopped?”

“I was speaking figuratively. I meant hustle about and work in the open air. The sort of life you are leading now is what millionaires pay hundreds of dollars for at these physical culture places. It has been the making of you.”

“I don’t feel made.”

===Chinese===
“我压根不是要说那个。纳蒂，你有时候真是一头猪！倒好像我不是巴不得你待在这儿似的！我要说的是：你该大大感激自己如今还得挑水劈柴。”

纳蒂苍白的脸上现出十足的惊恐。

“你该不是说你还要人劈木头吧？”

“我是打比方。我是说你该勤快起来，到露天里去干活。你现在过的这种日子，百万富翁们在那些健身院所里得花上几百美元才买得到。正是这日子把你造就成人的。”

“我可没觉出自己成了什么器。”

===Original===
“Your nerves are ever so much better.”

“They aren’t.”

Elizabeth looked at him in alarm.

“Oh, Nutty, you haven’t been﻿—seeing anything again, have you?”

“Not seeing, dreaming. I’ve been dreaming about monkeys. Why should I dream about monkeys if my nerves were all right?”

“I often dream about all sorts of queer things.”

===Chinese===
“你的神经已经好上一大截了。”

“没有好。”

伊丽莎白惊慌地望着他。

“哦，纳蒂，你不会又——看见什么了吧？”

“不是看见，是做梦。我一直在梦见猴子。我的神经要是没毛病，干吗要梦见猴子？”

“我还常常梦见各种古里古怪的东西呢。”

===Original===
“Have you ever dreamed that you were being chased up Broadway by a chimpanzee in evening dress?”

“Never mind, dear, you’ll be quite all right again when you have been living this life down here a little longer.”

Nutty glared balefully at the ceiling.

“What’s that darned thing up there? It looks like a hornet. How on earth do these things get into the house?”

“We ought to have nettings. I am going to pounce on Mr. Flack about that too.”

===Chinese===
“你可曾梦见，自己正被一只穿夜礼服的黑猩猩沿着百老汇追赶？”

“别往心里去，亲爱的，在这种地方再过上一阵这种日子，你就全好了。”

纳蒂恶狠狠地瞪着天花板。

“上头那个讨厌的东西是什么？看着像只马蜂。这些玩意儿到底是怎么钻进屋里来的？”

“咱们本该装纱窗。这一桩，我也要扑到弗拉克先生头上去提。”

===Original===
“Thank goodness this isn’t going to last much longer. It’s nearly two weeks since Uncle Ira died. We ought to be hearing from the lawyers any day now. There might be a letter this morning.”

“Do you think he has left us his money?”

“Do I? Why, what else could he do with it? We are his only surviving relatives, aren’t we? I’ve had to go through life with a ghastly name like Nutcombe as a compliment to him, haven’t I? I wrote to him regularly for Christmas and on his birthday, didn’t I? Well, then! I have a hunch there will be a letter from the lawyers today. I wish you would get dressed and go down to the post office while I’m toting that infernal water. I can’t think why the fools haven’t cabled. You would have supposed they would have thought of that.”

Elizabeth returned to her room to dress. She was conscious of a feeling that nothing was quite perfect in this world. It would be nice to have a great deal of money, for she had a scheme in her mind which called for a large capital; but she was sorry that it could come to her only through the death of her uncle, of whom, despite his somewhat forbidding personality, she had always been fond. She was also sorry that a large sum of money was coming to Nutty at that particular point in his career, just when there seemed a hope that the simple life might pull him together. She knew Nutty too well not to be able to forecast his probable behavior under the influence of a sudden restoration to wealth.

While these thoughts were passing through her mind she happened to glance out of the window. Nutty was shambling through the garden with his pail, a bowed, shuffling pillar of gloom. As Elizabeth watched he dropped the pail and lashed the air violently for a while. From her knowledge of bees﻿—“It is necessary to remember that bees resent outside interference and will resolutely defend themselves,” *Encyc. Brit.*, Vol. III, **Aus** to **Bis**﻿—Elizabeth deduced that one of her little pets was annoying him. This episode concluded, Nutty resumed his pail and the journey, and at this moment there appeared over the hedge the face of Mr. John Prescott, a neighbor. Mr. Prescott, who had dismounted from a bicycle, called to Nutty and waved something in the air. To a stranger the performance would have been obscure, but Elizabeth understood it. Mr. Prescott was intimating that he had been down to the post office for his own mail and, as was his neighborly custom on these occasions, had brought back also letters for Flacks’.

===Chinese===
“谢天谢地，这种日子长不了了。艾拉叔叔去世快两个星期了。律师事务所的信，现在随时都该到了。今儿早上没准就有一封。”

“你当真觉得他会把钱留给咱们？”

“你说我觉不觉得？嘿，不然这笔钱他还能拿去干什么？咱们是他仅存于世的亲人，不是吗？我这辈子顶着纳特科姆这么个可怕的名字过日子，算是对他的敬意，不是吗？圣诞节、生日，我一封不落地给他写信，不是吗？那不就结了！我有预感，今天准有律师事务所的信。趁我扛那要命的水的工夫，劳驾你把衣裳穿好，下邮局去走一趟。我不明白那帮傻瓜为什么不拍电报。照你想，他们总该想到这一层吧。”

伊丽莎白回房更衣去了。她心里萦绕着一种感觉：这世上没有一件事是十全十美的。有大笔的钱自然是美事，她脑子里正盘着一个需要大本钱的计划；可她遗憾的是，这笔钱只能从叔叔的死里来——那位叔叔，尽管为人有点拒人千里，她向来是喜欢的。她还遗憾的是，一大笔钱偏偏挑中纳蒂眼下这个关口降临，正当简朴的生活似乎有希望把他重新收拾拢来的时候。纳蒂的为人她知道得太透，不会料不到他在骤然重新有钱之后，大概会做出什么样的事来。

这些念头在她心里来来去去，她恰巧朝窗外瞥了一眼。纳蒂提着桶，正一步一蹭地穿过园子，是一座佝偻着、蹭着走的阴郁之柱。伊丽莎白正看着，只见他把桶一撂，对着空气猛挥猛打了一阵。凭着对蜜蜂的了解——《不列颠百科全书》第三卷，Aus 至 Bis：“须切记，蜜蜂憎恶外来干扰，必毅然自卫。”——伊丽莎白推断，是她那些小宝贝里头有一只正在招惹他。这段插曲终了，纳蒂重新提起桶赶路；正在此时，篱笆上头探出一张脸来，是邻居约翰·普雷斯科特（John Prescott）先生。普雷斯科特先生已经下了自行车，朝纳蒂喊了一声，一面把什么东西在空中挥动。换了陌生人，这场哑剧准看得莫名其妙，伊丽莎白却一看就懂：普雷斯科特先生是在示意，他方才下到邮局取了自己的邮件，并且照他逢此场合的睦邻惯例，也捎回了弗拉克家的信件。

===Original===
Nutty foregathered with Mr. Prescott and took the letters from him. Mr. Prescott disappeared. Nutty selected one of the letters and opened it. Then, having stood perfectly still for some moments, he suddenly turned and began to run toward the house.

The mere fact that her brother, whose usual mode of progression was a languid saunter, should be actually running, was enough to tell Elizabeth that the letter which Nutty had read was from the London lawyers. No other communication could have galvanized him into such energy. Whether the contents of the letter were good or bad it was impossible at that distance to say. But when she reached the open air, just as Nutty charged up, she saw by his face that it was anguish, not joy, that had spurred him on. He was gasping and he bubbled unintelligible words. His little eyes gleamed wildly.

“Nutty, darling, what is it?” cried Elizabeth, every maternal instinct in her aroused.

He was thrusting a sheet of paper at her, a sheet of paper that bore the superscription of Nichols, Nichols, Nichols and Nichols, with a London address.

“Uncle Ira﻿—” Nutty choked. “A hundred dollars! He’s left me a hundred dollars, and all the rest to a﻿—to a man named Dawlish!”

===Chinese===
纳蒂同普雷斯科特先生会合，从他手里接过信件。普雷斯科特先生不见了。纳蒂拣出一封信，拆开。随后，他一动不动地站了片刻，忽然转过身，朝着房子一路奔跑起来。

她的哥哥平素赶路，做派是懒洋洋地信步闲逛，如今竟然当真跑了起来，单凭这一节，就足够告诉伊丽莎白：纳蒂方才读的那封信，出自伦敦的律师事务所。别的什么信件，也决计不能像通了电流一般，把他激得这样生龙活虎。信里是好消息还是坏消息，隔着那么远，无从判断。可是待她走到屋外，纳蒂正好冲到跟前，她从他脸上看出了：驱使他狂奔的，是痛苦，不是喜悦。他喘着粗气，嘴里咕咕哝哝地冒出一串听不清的字。那双小眼睛疯狂地闪着光。

“纳蒂，亲爱的，出什么事了？”伊丽莎白喊道，浑身的母性本能一齐被唤醒了。

他把一页纸直往她手里塞，那页纸上印着尼科尔斯、尼科尔斯、尼科尔斯与尼科尔斯律师事务所（Nichols, Nichols, Nichols and Nichols）的抬头，附着一个伦敦地址。

“艾拉叔叔——”纳蒂哽住了，“一百美元！他留给我一百美元，其余的全给了一个——一个姓达利什的人！”

===Original===
In silence Elizabeth took the letter. It was even as he had said. A few moments before Elizabeth had been regretting the imminent descent of wealth upon her brother. Now she was inconsistent enough to boil with rage at the shattering blow which had befallen him. That she, too, had lost her inheritance hardly occurred to her. Her thoughts were all for Nutty. It did not need the sight of him, gasping and gurgling before her, to tell her how overwhelming was his disappointment.

It was useless to be angry with the deceased Mr. Nutcombe. He was too shadowy a mark. Besides, he was dead. The whole current of her wrath turned upon the supplanter, this Lord Dawlish. She pictured him as a crafty adventurer, a wretched fortune hunter. For some reason or other she imagined him a sinister person with a black mustache, a face thin and hawklike, and unpleasant eyes. That was the sort of man who would be likely to fasten his talons into poor Uncle Ira.

She had never hated anyone in her life before, but as she stood there at that moment she felt that she loathed and detested William, Lord Dawlish﻿—unhappy, well-meaning Bill, who only a few hours back had set foot on American soil in his desire to nose round and see if something couldn’t be arranged.

Nutty got the water. Life is like that. There is nothing clean cut about it, no sense of form. Instead of being permitted to concentrate his attention on his tragedy, Nutty had to trudge three-quarters of a mile in a hot sun, conciliate a temperamental bull terrier, and trudge back again carrying a heavy pail. It was as if one of the heroes of Greek drama, in the middle of his big scene, had been asked to run round the corner to a delicatessen store.

===Chinese===
伊丽莎白默默接过信。果然同他说的一字不差。片刻之前，伊丽莎白还在惋惜财富即将降到哥哥头上；此刻她全然不顾前后矛盾，为这桩砸到他身上的灭顶大祸，气得满腔怒火直往上冒。她自己同样失掉了遗产，这一层她几乎全没想到。她的心思全在纳蒂身上。不必看他这副在她面前又喘又噎的模样，她也知道他的失望有多沉重。

对已故的纳特科姆先生动怒是无用的。这个靶子太虚了。再说，他已经死了。她怒火的全副洪流，掉过头来，倾泻到那篡夺者的身上——这位达利什勋爵。她把他想象成一个奸诈的冒险家，一个卑劣的猎财者。也不知为了什么缘故，她把他想象成一个阴险的人物：蓄着黑髭，脸孔瘦削如鹰隼，眼神叫人发怵。会把利爪扎进可怜的艾拉叔叔肉里的，正是这路人。

她有生以来从没恨过什么人，可她站在那里的这一刻，只觉得自己对威廉·达利什勋爵（William, Lord Dawlish）厌恶到了极点、痛恨到了极点——而那位达利什，那个不走运的、一片好心的比尔（Bill），不过几个钟头之前，才踏上美国的土地，满心想四处打探打探，看看能不能从中安排点什么。

水，纳蒂终究是取回来了。生活就是这样。它没有一件事是干净利落的，全无章法可言。人家不许他专心致志地沉浸在自家的悲剧里，纳蒂还得顶着烈日跋涉四分之三英里，安抚一条脾气乖张的牛头梗，再提着沉甸甸的桶跋涉回来。这就好比希腊戏剧里的某位英雄，重头戏演到一半，忽然被人差遣去街角的熟食店跑一趟腿。

===Original===
The exercise did not act as a restorative. The blow had been too sudden, too overwhelming. Nutty’s reason﻿—such as it was﻿—tottered on its throne. Who was Lord Dawlish? What had he done, the smooth crook, to ingratiate himself with Uncle Ira? By what insidious means, with what devilish cunning, had he wormed his way into the old man’s favor? These were the questions that vexed Nutty’s mind when he was able to think at all coherently.

Back at the farm Elizabeth cooked breakfast and awaited her brother’s return with a sinking heart. She was a softhearted girl, easily distressed by the sight of suffering; and she was aware that Nutty was scarcely of the type that masks its woe behind a brave and cheerful smile. Her heart bled for Nutty.

There was a weary step outside. Nutty entered, slopping water. One glance at his face was enough to tell Elizabeth that she had formed a too conservative estimate of his probable gloom. Without a word he coiled his long form in a chair. There was silence in the stricken house.

===Chinese===
这番运动并没有起到定神的作用。打击来得太突然，太势不可当。纳蒂的理智——姑且算他有那么一点——在宝座上摇摇晃晃。达利什勋爵是谁？这个圆滑的骗子，究竟做了什么，讨得了艾拉叔叔的欢心？他使的什么阴险手段、耍的什么魔鬼般的狡计，才钻营进老头儿的恩宠里去的？当纳蒂还能大致连贯地思考的时候，折磨着他心头的，便是这些问题。

回到农场，伊丽莎白做好早饭，一颗心直往下沉着，等哥哥回来。她是个心软的姑娘，一见人受苦就难受；她也明白，纳蒂决不是那种把愁苦藏在勇敢欢颜背后的人。她的心为纳蒂淌着血。

外头传来一串疲惫的脚步声。纳蒂走了进来，一路泼洒着桶里的水。只消朝他脸上望一眼，伊丽莎白便知道自己先前对他的阴郁程度所作的估计，还是失之保守了。他一言不发，把自己长长的身躯盘进一把椅子。遭了灾的房子里，一片死寂。

===Original===
“What’s the time?”

Elizabeth glanced at her watch.

“Half-past nine.”

“About now,” said Nutty sepulchrally, “that pill is ringing for his man to prepare his bally bath and lay out his gold-leaf underwear. After that he will drive down to the bank and draw some of our money.”

The day passed wearily for Elizabeth. Nutty having the air of one who is still engaged in picking up the pieces, she had not the heart to ask him to play his customary part in the household duties, so she washed the dishes and made the beds herself. After that she attended to the bees. After that she cooked lunch.

===Chinese===
“几点了？”

伊丽莎白看了看表。

“九点半。”

“大约这会儿，”纳蒂用一种从坟墓里发出来的腔调说，“那个讨厌鬼正在按铃叫他的听差，给他放好那该死的洗澡水，把他的金箔内衣摆出来。完了，他就要坐上汽车下银行去，支我们几个钱了。”

这一天，伊丽莎白过得疲惫不堪。纳蒂那副神气，仿佛还没从事故里把碎片拾掇完，她不忍心再叫他担任家务里他惯常担任的那一份，于是自己洗碗，自己铺床。完了去照看蜜蜂。完了又去做午饭。

===Original===
Nutty was not chatty at lunch, Having observed, “About now the pill is cursing the waiter for bringing the wrong brand of champagne,” he relapsed into a silence which he did not again break.

Elizabeth was busy again in the afternoon. At four o’clock, feeling tired out, she went to her room to lie down until the next of her cycle of domestic duties should come round.

It was late when she came downstairs, for she had fallen asleep. The sun had gone down. Bees were winging their way heavily back to the hives with their honey. She went out into the grounds to try to find Nutty. There had been no signs of him in the house. There were no signs of him in the grounds. It was not like him to have taken a walk, but it seemed the only possibility. She went back to the house to wait. Eight o’clock came, and nine, and it was then that the truth dawned upon her﻿—Nutty had escaped. He had slipped away and gone up to New York.

===Chinese===
吃午饭的时候，纳蒂话语寥寥。撂下一句“大约这会儿，那讨厌鬼正在骂侍者送错了牌子的香槟”，他便重新沉入沉默，此后再没有打破过。

下午，伊丽莎白又忙了一阵。四点钟，她觉得筋疲力尽，回房躺下，等着家务循环的下一项轮到自己头上。

她下楼时，天色已经晚了，因为她睡着了。太阳已经落山。蜜蜂们驮着蜜，沉沉地扇着翅膀往蜂箱飞回去。她走到园子里去寻纳蒂。屋里不见他的人影。园子里也不见。散步可不像他的为人，然而这似乎是唯一的可能。她回到屋里去等。八点来了，九点也来了，直到这时，真相才在她心头亮起来——纳蒂逃了。他悄悄溜走，上纽约去了。

## VI / 第六章

===Original===
Lord Dawlish sat in the apartment on East Twenty-seventh Street. The hour was half-past ten in the evening; the day, the second day after the exodus of Nutty Boyd from the farm. Before him on the table lay a letter. He was smoking pensively.

Lord Dawlish had found New York enjoyable but a trifle fatiguing. There was much to be seen in the city, and he had made the mistake of trying to see it all at once. It had been his intention, when he came home after dinner that night, to try to restore the balance of things by going to bed early. He had sat up longer than he had intended because he had been thinking about this letter.

Immediately upon his arrival in America Bill had sought out a lawyer and instructed him to write to Elizabeth Boyd, offering her one-half of the late Ira Nutcombe’s money. He had had time during the voyage to think the whole matter over, and this seemed to him the only possible course. He could not keep it all. He would feel like a despoiler of the widow and the orphan. Nor would it be fair to Claire to give it all up. If he halved the legacy everybody would be satisfied.

That, at least, had been his view until Elizabeth’s reply had arrived. It was this reply that lay on the table, a brief, formal note setting forth Miss Boyd’s absolute refusal to accept any portion of the money. This was a development which Bill had not foreseen, and he was feeling baffled. What was the next step? He had smoked many pipes in the endeavor to find an answer to this problem, and was lighting another when the doorbell rang.

===Chinese===
达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）坐在东二十七街（East Twenty-seventh Street）的那套公寓里。时间是夜里十点半；日子，则是纳蒂·博伊德（Nutty Boyd）大举撤离农场之后的第二天。他面前的桌上放着一封信。他正若有所思地抽着烟斗。

纽约叫达利什勋爵觉得开心，只是略嫌累人。这座城里有看不完的东西，他犯了个错误：想一口气把它看个遍。那天夜里他吃过晚饭回到寓所，本打算早点上床，好让失衡的事情恢复平衡。他坐到比特意打算的更晚，因为一直在想这封信。

一到美国，比尔（Bill）便访得一位律师，嘱咐他写信给伊丽莎白·博伊德（Elizabeth Boyd），提出把已故艾拉·纳特科姆（Ira Nutcombe）的钱分她一半。航程中有的是工夫把整件事细细想过，在他看来，这似乎是唯一可行的路子。这笔钱他不能全留着不撒手——真那样，他觉得自己简直成了掠夺孤儿寡妇的强盗。可全数放弃，对克莱尔（Claire）也不公平。把遗产对半分开，人人都满意。

至少在伊丽莎白的回信寄到之前，他是这么看的。桌上放着的正是这封回信——一张简短、一本正经的便笺，声明博伊德小姐绝对拒绝接受这笔钱的任何一部分。这是比尔没有料到的进展，他颇觉束手。下一步怎么办？为了给这个问题找出答案，他一烟斗接一烟斗地抽了不少，正要点上新的一斗，门铃响了。

===Original===
He opened the door and found himself confronting an extraordinarily tall and thin young man in evening dress.

Lord Dawlish was a little startled. He had taken it for granted, when the bell rang, that his visitor was Tom, the elevator man from downstairs, a friendly soul who hailed from London and had been dropping in at intervals during the past two days to acquire the latest news from his native land. He stared at this changeling inquiringly. The solution of the mystery came with the stranger’s first words:

“Is Gates in?”

He spoke eagerly, as if Gates were extremely necessary to his well-being. It distressed Lord Dawlish to disappoint him, but there was nothing else to be done.

===Chinese===
他开了门，发现面前立着一个高得出奇、瘦得出奇的年轻人，一身夜礼服。

达利什勋爵吓了一小跳。方才门铃响的时候，他满以为来客是楼下开电梯的汤姆（Tom）——一个和气的家伙，伦敦出身，这两天时不时上来一趟，探听故土的最新消息。他满脸探询地打量着这个仿佛被仙灵掉包换来的家伙。谜底随着生客的第一句话揭开了：

“盖茨在家吗？”

他说得急切，仿佛盖茨（Gates）对他的安身立命有着头等要紧。让他失望，达利什勋爵于心不安，可此外也别无办法。

===Original===
“Gates is in London,” he said.

“What! When did he go there?”

“About four months ago.”

“May I come in a minute?”

“Yes, rather, do.”

===Chinese===
“盖茨在伦敦，”他说。

“什么！他什么时候去的？”

“大约四个月以前。”

“我可以进去待一会儿吗？”

“当然，请进，请进。”

===Original===
He led the way into the sitting-room. The stranger gave abruptly in the middle, as if he were being folded up by some invisible agency, and in this attitude sank into a chair, where he lay back looking at Bill over his knees, like a sorrowful sheep peering over a sharp-pointed fence.

“You’re from England, aren’t you?”

“Yes.”

“Been in New York long?”

“Only a couple of days.”

===Chinese===
他引路走进客厅。那生客的身体当中间霍地一折，仿佛被什么看不见的力量对叠起来，就以这个姿势陷进一把椅子，仰靠在那里，从膝头上方打量比尔，活像一头忧愁的绵羊，从尖桩篱笆顶上探头张望。

“你是英国来的，是不是？”

“是。”

“来纽约久了吗？”

“才来两天。”

===Original===
The stranger folded himself up another foot or so until his knees were higher than his head, and lit a cigarette.

“The curse of New York,” he said mournfully, “is the way everything changes in it. You can’t take your eye off it for a minute. The population’s always shifting. It’s like a damned railway station. You go away for a spell and come back and try to find your old pals, and they’re all gone: Ike’s in Arizona, Mike’s in a sanatorium, Spike’s in jail, and nobody seems to know where the rest of them have got to. I came back from the country two days ago, expecting to find all the old gang along Broadway the same as ever, and I’m darned if I’ve been able to put my hands on one of them! Not a single, solitary one of them! And it’s only six months since I was here last.”

Lord Dawlish made sympathetic noises.

“Of course,” proceeded the other, “the time of year may have something to do with it. Living down in the country you lose count of time, and I forgot that it was July when people go out of the city. I guess that must be what happened. I used to know all sorts of fellows, actors and fellows like that, and they’re all away somewhere. I tell you,” he said with pathos, “I never knew I could be so infernally lonesome as I have been these last two days. If I had known what a rotten time I was going to have I would never have left Brookport.”

===Chinese===
生客把自己又折起一英尺上下，直到膝盖高过了脑袋，然后点上一支香烟。

“纽约的祸根，”他哀伤地说，“就是样样东西都说变就变。你的眼睛一刻也离不得它。人口总是在流动。这地方活像一座该死的火车站。你离开一阵子，回来想找老伙计们，他们全不在了：艾克（Ike）去了亚利桑那，迈克（Mike）进了疗养院，斯派克（Spike）蹲了监狱，剩下的人去了哪里，似乎谁也说不清。我两天前从乡下回来，满以为百老汇（Broadway）沿路还是老班子、老面孔，可我竟连一个也没能逮着手，真是活见鬼！孤零零一个都没有！我上回离开这儿，至今才不过半年。”

达利什勋爵发出几声同情的应和。

“当然，”对方接着说，“节令兴许也有几分干系。住在乡下，人会把日子记丢，我忘了如今是七月，城里人都要出城去。我想准是这么回事。我从前认识各色人等，演员啦，诸如此类的家伙，如今全都到哪儿躲着去了。我跟你说，”他悲切地说，“我从不知道自己竟能孤苦得这般地狱似的，像这两天这样。早知道要过这等倒霉日子，我说什么也不会离开布鲁克波特（Brookport）。”

===Original===
“Brookport?”

“It’s a place down on Long Island.”

Bill was not by nature a plotter, but the mere fact of traveling under an assumed name had developed a streak of wariness in him. He checked himself just as he was about to ask his companion if he happened to know a Miss Elizabeth Boyd who also lived at Brookport.
