It occurred to him that the question would invite a counter question as to his own knowledge of Miss Boyd, and he knew that he would not be able to invent a satisfactory answer to that offhand.

“This evening,” said the thin young man, resuming his dirge, “I was sweating my brain to try to think of somebody I could hunt up in this ghastly, deserted city. It isn’t so easy, you know, to think of fellows’ names and addresses. I can get the names all right, but unless the fellow’s in the telephone book I’m done. Well, I was trying to think of some of my pals who might still be round the place, and I remembered Gates. Remembered his address, too, by a miracle. You’re a pal of his, of course?”

===Chinese===
“布鲁克波特？”

“长岛（Long Island）下面的一个地方。”

比尔天性不是算计之人，可顶着假名旅行这一桩事本身，已在他身上养出一缕机警。话到嘴边——他本要问同伴可巧认不认得一位也住在布鲁克波特的伊丽莎白·博伊德小姐——被他生生收住了。他想到，这一问会招来一句反问：你自己又是怎么认得博伊德小姐的？而他知道，自己没法随口就编出一句像样的答话。

“今天晚上，”瘦削的年轻人重又唱起他的哀歌，“我绞尽脑汁，想在这座鬼气森森的空城里寻出一个可以去寻访的人。你知道，要把人名和住址一齐想起来，可不是那么容易的。名字倒想得出，可那家伙要是不在电话簿上，我就完了。唉，我正想法子记起哪个老伙计也许还在这左近，就想起盖茨来了。连他的住址也记起来了，真是奇迹。你自然是他的朋友？”

===Original===
“Yes, I knew him in London.”

“Oh, I see. And when you came over here he lent you his apartment? By the way, I didn’t get your name?”

“My name’s Chalmers.”

“Well, as I say, I remembered Gates and came down here to look him up. We used to have a lot of good times together a year ago. And now he’s gone too!”

===Chinese===
“是，我在伦敦认识他的。”

“哦，明白了。你过这边来，他就把公寓借给你住了？对了，还没请教贵姓呢？”

“我姓查默斯（Chalmers）。”

“嗯，照方才说的，我想起盖茨，就上这儿来找他。一年前我们常在一块儿乐。如今他也走了！”

===Original===
“Did you want to see him about anything important?”

“Well, it’s important to me. I wanted him to come out to supper. You see it’s this way: I’m giving supper tonight to a girl who’s in that show at the Forty-ninth Street Theater, a Miss Leonard, and she insists on bringing a pal. She says the pal is a good sport, which sounds all right﻿—” Bill admitted that it sounded all right. “But it makes the party three. And of all the infernal things a party of three is the ghastliest.”

Having delivered himself of this undeniable truth, the stranger slid a little farther into his chair and paused.

===Chinese===
“你找他有要紧事吗？”

“嗯，对我自己是要紧的。我想约他出来吃夜宵。事情是这样：今晚我请四十九街剧院（the Forty-ninth Street Theater）那出戏里的一位姑娘吃夜宵，一位伦纳德小姐，她非带一位伴儿来不可。她说那伴儿是个爽快人，听着倒也不坏——”比尔承认听着确实不坏。“可这么一来就成了三个人。而天下种种可恶的事情里头，三人局顶顶煞风景。”

这条无可辩驳的真理发表完毕，生客往椅子里又滑进去一点，停了停。

===Original===
“Look here, what are you doing tonight?” he said.

“I was thinking of going to bed.”

“Going to bed!” The stranger’s voice was shocked, as if he had heard blasphemy. “Going to bed at half-past ten in New York! My dear chap, what you want is a bit of supper. Why don’t you come along?”

Amiability was perhaps the leading quality of Lord Dawlish’s character. He did not want to have to dress and go out to supper, but there was something almost pleading in the eyes that looked at him between the sharply pointed knees.

===Chinese===
“我说，你今晚干什么？”他说。

“我正打算上床睡觉。”

“上床睡觉！”生客的声音里满是震惊，仿佛听见了亵渎神明的话。“在纽约，十点半上床睡觉！我亲爱的老兄，你需要的是一顿夜宵。何不跟我们一道去？”

和蔼可亲，兴许是达利什勋爵性格里居首的品质。他并不想更衣出门去吃夜宵，可那双从刀锋似的膝盖上方望着他的眼睛里，有一种近乎哀恳的神色。

===Original===
“It’s awfully good of you﻿—” he hesitated.

“Not a bit, I wish you would. You would be a lifesaver.”

Bill felt that he was in for it. He got up.

“You will?” said the other. “Good boy! You go and get into some clothes and come along. I’m sorry, what did you say your name was?”

===Chinese===
“你太好了——”他迟疑着。

“哪里的话，你来就好了。你就是我的救命恩人。”

比尔觉得这事是躲不掉了。他站起身来。

“你肯来？”对方说。“好样儿的！去换上出门的衣裳，跟我走。抱歉，你方才说你叫什么来着？”

===Original===
“Chalmers.”

“Mine’s Boyd﻿—Nutcombe Boyd.”

“Boyd!” cried Bill.

Nutty took his astonishment, which was too great to be concealed, as a compliment. He chuckled.

===Chinese===
“查默斯。”

“我姓博伊德（Boyd）——纳特科姆·博伊德。”

“博伊德！”比尔叫出声来。

纳蒂把他那份大得遮掩不住的惊愕当成了恭维。他咯咯笑了。

===Original===
“I thought you would know the name if you were a pal of Gates’. I expect he’s always talking about me. I was pretty well known in this old burg before I had to leave it.”

Bill walked down the long passage to his bedroom with no trace of the sleepiness which had been weighing on him five minutes before. He was galvanized by a superstitious thrill. It was fate, Elizabeth Boyd’s brother turning up like this and making friendly overtures right on top of that letter from her. This astonishing thing could not have been better arranged if he had planned it himself. From what little he had seen of Nutty he gathered that the latter was not hard to make friends with. It would be a simple task to cultivate his acquaintance. And having done so, he could renew negotiations with Elizabeth. The desire to rid himself of half the legacy had become a fixed idea with Bill. He had the impression that he could not really feel clean again until he had made matters square with his conscience in this respect. He felt that he was probably a fool to take that view of the thing, but that was the way he was built and there was no getting away from it.

This irruption of Nutty Boyd into his life was an omen. It meant that all was not yet over. He was conscious of a mild surprise that he had ever intended to go to bed. He felt now as if he never wanted to go to bed again. He felt exhilarated.

===Chinese===
“我就想着，你只要是盖茨的朋友，准知道这个名字。想必他老是念叨我。在被逼离开这座老城之前，我在这地方也算薄有名气的。”

比尔沿着长长的过道走向卧室，五分钟前还压在他身上的那股睡意已经无影无踪。一阵近乎迷信的震悚像电流般把他激醒了。这是天意：伊丽莎白·博伊德的弟弟，恰在她那封信送到之后，这样撞上门来，又这样向他表示亲善。这桩奇事，纵然出自他自己的手笔，也安排不得再好了。虽然同纳蒂只打了片刻交道，他已经看出此人不难结交。培植这份交情将是桩轻省活儿；有了这层交情，他便可以同伊丽莎白重新开谈。把遗产脱手一半，已经成了比尔心里的一桩执念。他觉得，不在这层上同自己的良心把账算清，他就再不能真正觉得洁净。他也知道自己持这种看法多半是犯傻，可他天生就是这副坯子，躲也躲不开。

纳蒂·博伊德这样闯进他的生活，是一个预兆。这意味着事情还没有完。他微微暗自诧异，自己方才居然打算上床睡觉。此刻他觉得，仿佛这辈子再不想睡觉了。他只觉意气飞扬。

===Original===
In these days, when restaurants bask in the absence of a closing-time law, one cannot say that a supper party is actually given in any one place. Supping in New York has become a peripatetic pastime. The supper party arranged by Nutty Boyd was scheduled to start at Riegelheimer’s, on Forty-second Street, and it was there that the revelers assembled. Nutty and Bill had been there a few minutes when Miss Daisy Leonard arrived with her friend. And from that moment Bill was never himself again.

The Good Sport was, so to speak, an outsize in good sports. She loomed up behind the small and demure Miss Leonard like a liner towed by a tug. She was big, blond, skittish and exuberant; she wore a dress like the sunset of a fine summer evening and she effervesced with spacious good will to all men. She was one of those girls who splash into public places like stones into quiet pools. Her form was large, her eyes were large, her teeth were large and her voice was large. She overwhelmed Bill. She hit his astounded consciousness like a shell. She gave him a buzzing in the ears. She was not so much a Good Sport as some kind of an explosion.

He was still reeling from the spiritual impact with this female tidal wave when he became aware, as one who, coming out of a swoon, hears voices faintly, that he was being addressed by Miss Leonard. To turn from Miss Leonard’s friend to Miss Leonard herself was like hearing the falling of gentle rain after a thunderstorm. For a moment he reveled in the sense of being soothed; then, as he realized what she was saying, he started violently. Miss Leonard was looking at him curiously.

===Chinese===
如今这个年头，餐馆正得着没有打烊法令的好处，一场夜宵究竟摆在哪一处，是谁也说不清的。在纽约吃夜宵，已经成了一种游动的营生。纳蒂·博伊德安排的这场夜宵，预定从四十二街（Forty-second Street）的里格尔海默餐厅（Riegelheimer’s）开锣，狂欢的人们便在那里聚齐。纳蒂和比尔到那里才不过几分钟，黛西·伦纳德（Daisy Leonard）小姐便携着她的朋友驾到了。从那一刻起，比尔就再不是原来的比尔了。

那位爽快姑娘（the Good Sport），打个比方说，是爽快人里的特大号。她在娇小娴静的伦纳德小姐身后赫然耸现，好似一艘邮轮跟在一艘拖船后头被牵着走。她人高马大，金发白肤，活泼放达，兴致淋漓；她那身衣裳活像晴夏傍晚的落日，她整个人冒着泡，对普天之下的人类怀着宽宏的善意。她属于那一类姑娘：闯进公共场所，就像石子砸进平静的池塘。她身量大，眼睛大，牙齿大，嗓门也大。她把比尔整个儿压垮了。她像一发炮弹，正中他惊愕的意识，叫他耳朵里嗡嗡直响。与其说她是一位爽快姑娘，不如说她是一场爆炸。

他还在这位女性潮浪的精神冲击下打着晃，便发觉伦纳德小姐在同他讲话——那感觉，恰似昏晕初醒的人隐隐听得人语。从伦纳德小姐的朋友转向伦纳德小姐本人，好比雷暴过后听见细雨洒落。有一瞬，他陶然于这抚慰之感；随即，待他听清她说的什么，他猛地一震。伦纳德小姐正满脸好奇地望着他。

===Original===
“I beg your pardon?” said Bill.

“I’m sure I’ve met you before, Mr. Chalmers.”

“Er﻿—really?”

“But I can’t think where.”

===Chinese===
“你说什么？”比尔说。

“我敢说我从前见过你，查默斯先生。”

“呃——真的吗？”

“可我想不起是在哪儿了。”

===Original===
“I’m sure,” said the Good Sport languishingly, like a sentimental siege gun, “that if I had ever met Mr. Chalmers before I shouldn’t have forgotten him.”

“You’re English, aren’t you?” asked Miss Leonard.

“Yes.”

The Good Sport said she was crazy about Englishmen,

“I thought so from your voice.”

===Chinese===
“我相信，”爽快姑娘媚眼涟涟地说，活像一门多情的攻城炮，“我要是先前见过查默斯先生，决不会把他忘了。”

“你是英国人，对不对？”伦纳德小姐问。

“对。”

爽快姑娘说，她对英国人迷得发疯，

“听你口音我就这么猜来着。”

===Original===
The Good Sport said she was crazy about the English accent.

“It must have been in London that I met you. I was in the revue at the Alhambra last year.”

“By George, I wish I had seen you,” interjected the infatuated Nutty.

The Good Sport said she was crazy about London.

===Chinese===
爽快姑娘说，她对英国口音迷得发疯。

“我准是在伦敦见过你的。去年我在阿罕布拉剧院（the Alhambra）演时事讽刺剧来着。”

“天哪，我要是看过就好了，”神魂颠倒的纳蒂插嘴道。

爽快姑娘说，她对伦敦迷得发疯。

===Original===
“I seem to remember,” went on Miss Leonard, “meeting you out at supper. Do you know a man named Delaney in the Coldstream Guards?”

Bill would have liked to deny all knowledge of Delaney, though the latter was one of his best friends, but his natural honesty prevented him.

“I’m sure I met you at a supper he gave at Oddy’s one Friday night. We all went on to Covent Garden. Don’t you remember?”

“Talking of supper,” broke in Nutty, earning Bill’s hearty gratitude thereby, “where’s the headwaiter? I want to find my table.”

===Chinese===
“我好像记得，”伦纳德小姐接着说，“是在外头吃夜宵的时候见过你的。你认不认得冷溪近卫团（Coldstream Guards）里一个名叫德莱尼（Delaney）的人？”

比尔本想对德莱尼其人矢口表示一无所知，尽管后者是他顶要好的朋友之一，可他天生的诚实不容他这么办。

“我敢肯定，是有一个星期五夜里，在他做东、在奥迪餐厅（Oddy’s）的那场夜宵上见过你的。后来我们还一块儿去了科文特花园（Covent Garden）。你不记得了？”

“说起夜宵，”纳蒂插进话来，为此博得了比尔由衷的感激，“领班上哪儿去了？我要找我们的桌子。”

===Original===
He surveyed the restaurant with a melancholy eye.

“Everything changed!” He spoke sadly, as Ulysses might have done when his boat put in at Ithaca. “Every darned thing different since I was here last. New waiters, headwaiter I never saw before in my life, different-colored carpet.”

“Cheer up, Nutty, old thing,” said Miss Leonard. “Cut the Rip van Winkle stuff and find our table. You’ll feel better when you’ve had something to eat. I hope you had the sense to slip the headwaiter something solid, or there won’t be any table. Funny how these joints go up and down in New York. A year ago the whole management would turn out and kiss you if you looked like spending a couple of dollars here. Now it costs the earth to get in at all.”

“Why’s that?” asked Nutty.

===Chinese===
他用一只忧郁的眼睛扫视这家餐馆。

“全变了！”他悲声说道，仿佛尤利西斯（Ulysses）的船靠上伊萨卡（Ithaca）时也会这样开口。“打我上回离开，件件东西都变了样，见鬼。侍者换了生面孔，领班我这辈子压根没见过，地毯的颜色也两样了。”

“打起精神来，纳蒂，老伙计，”伦纳德小姐说，“别玩你那套瑞普·凡·温克尔（Rip van Winkle）了，快找我们的桌子。吃上点东西你就舒坦了。但愿你还懂规矩，给领班塞了点扎实的东西，不然可就没有桌子了。真怪，纽约这些馆子的行情起起落落。一年前，只要你看上去像要在这儿花上两块钱，全店上下都会涌出来亲你。如今想进门就得大放血。”

“这是为什么？”纳蒂问。

===Original===
“Lady Pauline Wetherby, of course. Didn’t you know this was where she danced?”

“Never heard of her,” said Nutty, in a sort of ecstasy of wistful gloom. “That will show you how long I’ve been away. Who is she?”

Miss Leonard invoked the name of Mike.

“Don’t you ever get the papers in your village, Nutty?”

===Chinese===
“自然是贵女波琳·韦瑟比（Lady Pauline Wetherby）呗。你不知道她就在这儿跳舞？”

“从来没听说过，”纳蒂说，一副怅惘阴郁到了出神地步的神气。“这你就知道我走了多久了。她是谁？”

伦纳德小姐惊呼了一声“迈克”（Mike）。

“你们村里从不送报纸吗，纳蒂？”

===Original===
“I never read the papers. I don’t suppose I’ve read a paper for years. I can’t stand ’em. Who is Lady Pauline Wetherby?”

“She does Greek dances﻿—at least I suppose it’s Greek. All these undress stunts are nowadays, unless they’re Russian. She’s an English peeress.”

Miss Leonard’s friend said she was crazy about these picturesque old English families, and they went in to supper.

===Chinese===
“我从来不看报。我猜我有好几年没沾过报纸了。我见不得那玩意儿。贵女波琳·韦瑟比是谁？”

“她跳希腊舞——至少我猜是希腊舞。如今这类半裸的绝活儿都是希腊的，除非是俄国的。她是位英国女贵族。”

伦纳德小姐的这位朋友说，她对这许多古趣盎然的英国世家迷得发疯。随后众人入席吃夜宵去了。

===Original===
Looking back on the evening later and reviewing its leading features, Lord Dawlish came to the conclusion that he never completely recovered from the first shock of the Good Sport. He was conscious all the time of a dreamlike feeling, as if he were watching himself from somewhere outside himself. From some conning tower in this fourth dimension he perceived himself eating broiled lobster and drinking champagne and heard himself bearing an adequate part in the conversation; but his movements were largely automatic. Everything in the place conspired to stupefy his faculties. Accustomed to the quieter atmosphere of London restaurants, he was stunned by the din. It was before nightclubs spread over London like an epidemic, and he had not learned the lesson which the Londoner today knows so well, that there is practically no limit to the noise which half a dozen earnest Senegambians can produce if left alone with a few banjos and a drum or two. He was aware dimly of conversation.

“… It’s the absolute truth. I hunted up and down Broadway for two days and didn’t find a soul I knew. And then I thought of a pal of mine named Gates. And he was gone too. But luckily Chalmers﻿ ﻿…”

“… I got him in a corner and I said to him: ‘If you’re a gentleman, Mr. Ritchall, you’ll see that justice is done. You know I was promised I could be in this number, and﻿—’ He’s as deaf as a post, you know, but fortunately I’ve a good, strong voice﻿ ﻿…”

“… Who’s that girl over there? I’ve met her somewhere.”

“… I feel a hundred. I feel as if I had been away a million years﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
事后回想这一晚，检点它的诸般要目，达利什勋爵得出结论：爽快姑娘给他的头一记冲击，他始终没有完全缓过劲来。他自始至终有一种梦游似的感觉，仿佛从自身之外的什么地方注视着自己。从这第四维空间里的某座指挥塔上，他看见自己在吃烤龙虾、喝香槟，听见自己在谈话中承担了应有的份额；可他的动作大半是机械的。这地方样样都在合谋麻痹他的官能。他过惯了伦敦餐馆里较为安静的气氛，被这片喧嚣震得发懵。那还是夜总会尚未像瘟疫一般蔓延到伦敦的年代，他还没有学到今日伦敦人烂熟于心的那条道理：只要放任不管，六七个一本正经的塞内冈比亚（Senegambian）人守着几把班卓琴和一两面鼓，能弄出的响动实际上是没有底的。他隐约听得见一些片断的谈话。

“……这是千真万确的。我在百老汇上上下下打听了两天，一个熟人也没碰上。后来我想起一个名叫盖茨的朋友。他也走了。好在还有查默斯……”

“……我把他逼到一个角落，对他说：‘里查尔（Ritchall）先生，您要是位上等人，您就会主持公道。您明知道人家答应过我让我进这个节目的，可是——’您知道，他聋得跟门柱似的，幸亏我这条嗓子又亮又足……”

“……那边那个姑娘是谁？我好像在哪儿见过她。”

“……我觉得自己有一百岁了。我觉得仿佛已经离开了一百万年……”

---

===Original===
“… So the end of it was that next night, when the number came on, I walked straight up and﻿ ﻿…”

“… Only her hair was a different color then.”

“Waiter!”

“… He had the nerve to stand there and pull that old-time stuff on me!”

“By Jove! Really?”

“Waiter!”

===Chinese===
“……结果，第二天晚上那个节目一上场，我就径直走上前去，就……”

“……只不过那时候她的头发还是另一种颜色。”

“侍者！”

“……他居然有脸站在那儿，跟我耍那套老掉牙的把戏！”

“哎呀！真的？”

“侍者！”

===Original===
“… She used to be married to a man named Fothergill or Groves or something, and she got a divorce because﻿ ﻿…”

“Yes, sir?”

“Bring another﻿ ﻿…”

“… I simply said to him quite quietly: ‘Mr. Zizzbaum, as heaven is my witness, they were at least three sizes too small, so how could I be expected﻿ ﻿…’ ”

Pop!

===Chinese===
“……她从前嫁过一个叫福瑟吉尔（Fothergill）要么叫格罗夫斯（Groves）什么的男人，后来离了婚，因为……”

“来了，先生？”

“再来一瓶……”

“……我只是心平气和地对他说：‘兹兹鲍姆（Zizzbaum）先生，老天在上，那东西起码小了三号，叫我怎么能……’”

砰！

===Original===
Time passed. It seemed to Lord Dawlish, watching from without, that things were livening up. He seemed to perceive a quickening of the tempo of the revels, an added abandon. Nutty was getting quite bright. He had the air of one who recalls the good old days, of one who in familiar scenes reenacts the joys of his vanished youth. The chastened melancholy induced by many months of carrying pails of water, of scrubbing floors with a mop and of jumping like a firecracker to avoid excited bees, had been purged from him by the lights and the music and the wine. He was telling a long anecdote, laughing at it, throwing a crust of bread at an adjacent waiter, and refilling his glass at the same time. It is not easy to do all these things simultaneously, and the fact that Nutty did them with notable success was proof that he was picking up.

Miss Daisy Leonard was still demure, but as she had just slipped a piece of ice down the back of Nutty’s neck, one may assume that she was feeling at her ease, and had overcome any diffidence or shyness which might have interfered with her complete enjoyment of the festivities. As for the Good Sport, she was larger, blonder and more exuberant than ever, and she was addressing someone as “Bill.”

Perhaps the most remarkable phenomenon of the evening, as it advanced, was the change it wrought in Lord Dawlish’s attitude toward this same Good Sport. He was not conscious of the beginning of the change; he awoke to the realization of it suddenly. At the beginning of supper his views on her had been definite and clear. When they had first been introduced to each other he had had a stunned feeling that this sort of thing ought not to be allowed at large, and his battered brain had instinctively recalled that line of Tennyson: “The curse is come upon me.” But now, warmed with food and drink and smoking an excellent cigar, he found that a gentler, more charitable mood had descended upon him.

===Chinese===
时间过去了。从外面旁观的达利什勋爵觉得，场面正热闹起来。他似乎觉出狂欢的节奏在加快，添了几分放纵。纳蒂渐渐神采焕发。他一派缅怀旧日好时光的神气，像个在熟识的场景里重演逝去青春之乐的人。那股因连月提桶挑水、拿拖把擦地、为躲兴奋的蜜蜂而蹦得像只爆竹所生出的受足了惩戒的忧郁，已经被灯光、音乐和美酒涤荡干净了。他正讲一桩长长的轶事，边讲边笑，顺手朝旁边的侍者丢一块面包皮，同时还在给自己的杯子续酒。这些事要一齐做到并不容易；纳蒂样样做得像模像样，足证他正在缓过劲来。

黛西·伦纳德小姐依然娴静，不过她刚把一块冰塞进了纳蒂的后脖颈，可以想见她已经放开手脚，把任何可能妨碍她尽兴的羞怯或腼腆都克服掉了。至于那位爽快姑娘，她比先前更庞大、更金光、更奔放，而且正在管什么人叫“比尔”。

随着夜宵渐深，当晚顶顶可观的现象，兴许是它对达利什勋爵就这位爽快姑娘所持态度的改变。转变并非自起头就有觉察；他是猛然惊觉的。夜宵开头，他对她的看法明确而清晰。两人初见介绍之时，他只觉天旋地转，认定这等事物不该放任在外乱跑，他挨了重击的脑子本能地浮起丁尼生（Tennyson）的诗句：“诅咒已降临我身。”可如今，饭暖了身，酒热了肠，嘴上又叼着一支上好雪茄，他发觉一种较温柔、较宽厚的情绪已经降临到自己身上。

---

===Original===
He argued with himself in extenuation of the girl’s peculiar idiosyncrasies. Was it, he asked himself, altogether her fault that she was so massive and spoke as if she were addressing an open-air convention in a strong gale? Perhaps it was hereditary. Perhaps her father had been a circus giant and her mother the strong woman of the troupe. And for the unrestraint of her manner defective training in early girlhood would account. He began to regard her with a quiet, kindly commiseration, which in its turn changed into a sort of brotherly affection. He discovered that he liked her. He liked her very much. She was so big and jolly and robust, and spoke in such a clear, full voice. He was glad that she was patting his hand. He was glad that he had asked her to call him Bill. He was glad﻿—for it showed that he had won her confidence﻿—that she had twice told him the rather long story of how badly the stage director had treated her by leaving her out of the Bully, Bully Summer Time number.

People were dancing now. It has been claimed by patriots that American dyspeptics lead the world. This supremacy, though partly due no doubt to vast supplies of pie absorbed in youth, may be attributed to a certain extent also to the national habit of dancing during meals. Lord Dawlish had that sturdy reverence for his interior organism which is the birthright of every Briton, and at the beginning of supper he had resolved that nothing should induce him to court disaster in this fashion. But as the time went on he began to waver.

The situation was awkward. Nutty and Miss Leonard were repeatedly leaving the table to tread the measure, and on these occasions the Good Sport’s wistfulness was a haunting reproach. Nor was the spectacle of Nutty in action without its effect on Bill’s resolution. Nutty dancing was a sight to stir the most stolid. Six months’ abstinence had keyed him up, and he was throwing himself into the thing in a way that recalled the gentleman in the poem who had fed on honeydew and drunk the milk of Paradise:

===Chinese===
他跟自己辩论，为这姑娘的种种特异怪癖开脱。他问自己：她生得这样庞大，说话像在八级大风里对露天大会演说，这能全怪她吗？说不定是遗传。说不定她父亲是马戏班的巨人，母亲是班子里的女力士。至于她举止的无拘无束，少女时代教养的欠缺足以解释。他开始以一种静静的、善意的怜悯看待她，这怜悯接着又变作一种兄弟般的情谊。他发现自己喜欢她。非常喜欢她。她那么高大、快活、壮实，嗓音又那么清亮饱满。她轻拍他的手背，他高兴；他请她叫他比尔，他高兴；她把舞台导演怎样亏待她、不让她上《顶好顶好夏日时光》（Bully, Bully Summer Time）那个节目的那段不短的故事对他讲了两遍，他也高兴——这说明他赢得了她的信任。

如今人们跳起舞来了。爱国者们声称，美国的消化不良患者独步全球。这份霸业，固然一部分无疑要归功于幼年吞下的海量馅饼，某种程度上却也须归因于全国上下用餐时跳舞的习惯。达利什勋爵对自己的五脏庙怀着一种坚固的敬意，这是每个英国人生而俱有的家产；夜宵开头他就拿定主意，任凭什么也休想诱使他这样招灾惹祸。可时间一久，他开始动摇了。

局面尴尬。纳蒂和伦纳德小姐一趟趟离席去踏舞步，每逢这种时候，爽快姑娘那怅然若失的神情便是一道萦绕不去的责备。纳蒂起舞的光景，对比尔的决心也并非全无作用。纳蒂跳舞，是一副能撼动最木讷之人的景象。六个月的禁舞把他上足了弦，他一头扎进这支舞里，那架势令人想起诗中那位吃过蜜露、饮过天堂之乳的先生：

===Original===
> Beware, beware!
 

 His flashing eyes, his floating hair.
 

 Weave a circle round him thrice.

A stimulating spectacle!

===Chinese===
> 当心，当心！
 

 他目光灼灼，长发飘飘。
 

 绕他三匝，画一个圆圈。

好一幅提神的景象！

===Original===
Bill wavered. The music had started again now, one of those twentieth-century eruptions of sound that begin like a train going through a tunnel and continue like audible electric shocks, that set the feet tapping beneath the table and the spine thrilling with an unaccustomed exhilaration. Every drop of blood in his body cried to him “Dance!” He could resist no longer.

“Shall we?” he said.

His companion rose as if impelled by powerful machinery. She said she was crazy about dancing. Bill should not have danced. He was an estimable young man, honest, amiable, with high ideals. He had played an excellent game of football at the university; his golf handicap was plus two; and he was no mean performer with the gloves. But we all of us have our limitations, and Bill had his. He was not a good dancer. He was energetic, but he required more elbow room than the ordinary dancing floor provides. As a dancer, in fact, he closely resembled a Newfoundland puppy trying to run across a field.

===Chinese===
比尔动摇了。这时音乐又奏响了，正是那路二十世纪的声音爆发：起头像一列火车钻进隧道，接着像一阵阵听得见的电击，激得人两只脚在桌子底下打起拍子，脊梁骨给一种陌生的狂喜酥麻穿透。他浑身的每一滴血都朝他喊：“跳！”他再也抵抗不住了。

“我们跳一支？”他说。

他的舞伴霍然起身，仿佛受什么强力机械的驱动。她说她迷死了跳舞。比尔本不该跳舞的。他是个可敬的年轻人，诚实，和气，理想崇高。在大学里他的橄榄球打得很出色；高尔夫差点到了“加二”；戴上拳套也非等闲之辈。可我们人人都有短处，比尔也有他的：他不是个好的舞者。他劲头十足，需要的回旋余地却超出寻常舞池所能供给。事实上，作为舞者，他活像一只企图跑过一片田野的纽芬兰幼犬。

===Original===
It takes a good deal to daunt the New York dancing man, but the invasion of the floor by Bill and the Good Sport undoubtedly caused a profound and even painful sensation. Linked together they formed a living projectile which might well have intimidated the bravest. Nutty was their first victim. They caught him in midstep﻿—one of those fancy steps which he was just beginning to exhume from the cobwebbed recesses of his memory﻿—and swept him away. After which they descended irresistibly upon a stout gentleman of middle age, chiefly conspicuous for the glittering diamonds which he wore and the stoical manner in which he danced to and fro on one spot of not more than a few inches in size in the exact center of the room. He had apparently staked out a claim to this small spot, a claim which the other dancers had decided to respect; but Bill and the Good Sport, coming up from behind, had him two yards away from it at the first impact. Then, scattering apologies broadcast like a medieval monarch distributing largesse, Bill whirled his partner round by sheer muscular force and began what he intended to be a movement toward the farther corner, skirting the edge of the floor. It was his simple belief that there was more safety there than in the middle.

He had not reckoned with Heinrich Joerg. Indeed he was not aware of Heinrich Joerg’s existence. Yet fate was shortly to bring them together with far-reaching results. Heinrich Joerg had left the Fatherland some three years before with the prudent purpose of escaping military service. After various vicissitudes in the land of his adoption﻿—which it would be extremely interesting to relate, but which must wait for a more favorable opportunity﻿—he had secured a useful and not ill-recompensed situation as one of the staff of Riegelheimer’s Restaurant. He was, in point of fact, a waiter, and he comes into the story at this point bearing a tray full of glasses, knives, forks, and pats of butter on little plates. He was setting a table for some new arrivals, and in order to obtain more scope for that task he had left the crowded aisle beyond the table and come round to the edge of the dancing floor.

He should not have come out on the dancing floor. In another moment he was admitting that himself. For just as he was lowering his tray and bending over the table in the pursuance of his professional duties, along came Bill at his customary high rate of speed, propelling his partner before him, and for the first time since he left home Heinrich was conscious of a regret that he had done so. There are worse things than military service.

===Chinese===
要叫纽约的跳舞行家却步，本来得费老大劲，可比尔和爽快姑娘进场这一遭，无疑造成了深刻乃至惨痛的轰动。两人挽作一体，构成一枚活体炮弹，胆气再壮的人也要心惊。纳蒂是头一个牺牲者。他们在他换步的中途撞上他——那是他刚开始从记忆结满蛛网的角落里刨出来的一种花式步法——把他一裹而走。随后他们无可抗拒地扑向一位体格壮实的中年绅士，此人最惹眼的，一是浑身上下钻石生光，二是他跳舞的架势：在正屋子正中央一块不过几英寸见方的地方原地来回踏动，一副四大皆空的气度。他显然早已插标圈占了这一小方地盘，其余舞客也决议尊重这一圈占；可比尔和爽快姑娘从背后上来，头一下冲撞就把他拱出了两码开外。随后，比尔像中世纪君主散施赏赐一般，把道歉四下抛撒，凭着一身蛮力抡着舞伴转了个圈，开始向远处的墙角移动——照他的算盘，要贴着舞池边缘挪过去。他朴素的信念是，那边总比中央安全些。

他没有把海因里希·约尔格（Heinrich Joerg）算在内。说实在的，他根本不知道海因里希·约尔格的存在。可命运不久就要把他们捏到一处，后果深远。海因里希·约尔格大约三年前离开祖国（the Fatherland），存心审慎，为的是逃避兵役。在收留他的国度里经历了几番浮沉——说来会极其有趣，但只能留待更相宜的机会——他谋得了一个有用而报酬不恶的差事，在里格尔海默餐厅当差。说白了，他是个侍者。此刻他走进故事，端着一只托盘，满满盛着杯子、刀、叉，还有小碟上的一小块一小块黄油。他正给一批新到的客人摆台，为了腾出施展的空间，他离开桌子那边拥挤的过道，绕到了舞池边上。

他原不该上那舞池的。顷刻之后，这一点连他自己也承认了。他正放下托盘、俯身桌上履行职业职责，比尔便以他惯常的高速度呼啸而至，把舞伴推在前面开路；离家以来头一遭，海因里希对自己当初的举动生出一丝悔意。世上比服兵役更糟的事情是有的。

===Original===
It was the table that saved Bill. He clutched at it and it supported him. He was thus enabled to keep the Good Sport from falling, and to assist Heinrich to rise from the morass of glasses, knives, and pats of butter in which he was wallowing. Then, the dance having been abandoned by mutual consent, he helped his now somewhat hysterical partner back to their table.

Remorse came upon Bill, He was sorry that he had danced; sorry that he had upset Heinrich; sorry that he had subjected the Good Sport’s nervous system to such a strain; sorry that so much glass had been broken and so many pats of butter bruised beyond repair. But of one thing, even in that moment of bleak regrets, he was distinctly glad, and that was that all these things had taken place three thousand miles away from Claire Fenwick. He had not been appearing at his best, and he was glad that Claire had not seen him.

As he sat and smoked the remains of his cigar, while renewing his apologies and explanations to his partner and soothing the ruffled Nutty with well-chosen condolences, he wondered idly what Claire was doing at that moment. Claire at that moment, having been an astonished eyewitness of the whole performance, was resuming her seat at a table at the other end of the room.

===Chinese===
救了比尔的是那张桌子。他一把抓住，桌子撑住了他。多亏这样，他既没让爽快姑娘跌倒，又协助海因里希从那片杯子、刀叉和黄油块的泥沼里爬起身来。随后，舞是双方合意作罢的，他搀着那位此刻已有些歇斯底里的舞伴，回到自己的桌上。

悔恨向比尔袭来。他悔自己跳了舞；悔自己撞翻了海因里希；悔自己叫爽快姑娘的神经系统受了这样一场大罪；悔那么些杯子碎掉，那么多黄油块伤损得再不能修复。可有一件事，纵然在这样一片凄凉的悔意里，他仍旧分明觉得庆幸：这一切都发生在离克莱尔·芬威克（Claire Fenwick）三千英里之外。他这一晚实在算不得体面，他庆幸克莱尔没有看见。

他坐着抽完雪茄的残段，一面继续向舞伴赔情赔理，一面拿措辞得体的吊慰安抚那位毛了毛的纳蒂，心下漫然地想：这会儿克莱尔在做什么呢。而这会儿的克莱尔，恰恰是这整场表演目瞪口呆的见证人，正在屋子另一头的一张桌旁重新落座。

## VII / 第七章

===Original===

There were two reasons why Lord Dawlish was unaware of Claire Fenwick’s presence at Riegelheimer’s Restaurant. Riegelheimer’s is situated in a basement below a ten-story building, and in order to prevent this edifice from falling into his patrons’ soup the proprietor had been obliged to shore up his ceiling with massive pillars. One of these obtruded itself between the table which Nutty had secured for his supper party and the table at which Claire was sitting with her friend, Lady Wetherby, and her steamer acquaintance, Mr. Dudley Pickering. That was why Bill had not seen Claire from where he sat; and the reason that he had not seen her when he left his seat and began to dance was that he was not one of your dancers who glance airily about them. When Bill danced he danced.
He would have been stunned with amazement if he had known that Claire was at Riegelheimer’s that night. And yet it would have been remarkable, seeing that she was the guest of Lady Wetherby, if she had not been there. When you have traveled three thousand miles to enjoy the hospitality of a friend who does near-Greek dances at a popular restaurant, the least you can do is to go to the restaurant and watch her step. Claire had arrived with Polly Wetherby and Mr. Dudley Pickering at about the time when Nutty, his gloom melting rapidly, was instructing the waiter to open the second bottle.
Of Claire’s movements between the time when she secured her ticket at the White Star offices at Southampton and the moment when she entered Riegelheimer’s Restaurant, it is not necessary to give a detailed record. She had had the usual experiences of the ocean voyager. She had fed, read and gone to bed. The only notable event in her trip had been her intimacy with Mr. Dudley Pickering.

===Chinese===

达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）没有察觉克莱尔·芬威克（Claire Fenwick）也在里格尔海默餐厅（Riegelheimer's），缘由有二。这家餐厅开在一幢十层大楼的地窖里，店东为了不让整座建筑塌进顾客的汤里，不得不立起几根粗大的柱子撑住天花板。其中一根，恰好横在纳蒂（Nutty）为他的晚餐聚会定下的那张桌子，同克莱尔与她的朋友韦瑟比伯爵夫人（Lady Wetherby）、以及她同船结识的达德利·皮克林（Dudley Pickering）先生所坐的那张桌子之间。这便是比尔（Bill）从自己座位上望不见克莱尔的缘故；至于他离席起舞之后仍旧没望见她，则是因为他绝非那种一边跳一边四下轻飘飘张望的舞客。比尔跳舞，便是全心全意地跳舞。
倘若他知道克莱尔那晚就在里格尔海默，他准会惊得目瞪口呆。可话说回来，她既身为韦瑟比伯爵夫人的客人，若不在那里，反倒叫人称奇。你漂洋过海三千英里，来叨光一位朋友的款待，而这位朋友在一家走红餐馆里跳近乎希腊风的舞蹈，那么你起码也该走进这家餐馆，观赏观赏她的步法。克莱尔是同波莉·韦瑟比（Polly Wetherby）和达德利·皮克林先生一道到的，那当口，纳蒂的愁云惨雾正迅速消融，他刚吩咐侍者去开第二瓶酒。
至于克莱尔在南安普顿（Southampton）白星航运公司（White Star）办事处订下船票之后，直到踏进里格尔海默餐厅大门之前这一段行止，无须细录。她经历的无非是远洋旅客的老一套：吃饭，读书，就寝。此行唯一值得一提的大事，是她同达德利·皮克林先生的过从甚密。

===Original===

Dudley Pickering was a middle-aged Middle Westerner, who by thrift and industry had amassed a considerable fortune out of automobiles. He could accommodate you with an automobile suited to every stage of your growing prosperity. When you were young and struggling you bought his Little Pick at four hundred dollars. Becoming older and more opulent you put down eleven hundred for his Pickering Gem. And it might be that in time, having passed through the intermediate stages and being in a position to blow the expense, you found yourself the possessor of a Pickering Giant, the best car on the market. Everybody spoke well of Dudley Pickering. The papers spoke well of him, Bradstreet spoke well of him, and he spoke well of himself. On board the liner he had poured the saga of his life into Claire’s attentive ears, and though by the end of the voyage she had forgotten that he had started life with half a dollar, she still remembered that he was ending it with twenty or thirty millions, and there was a gentle sweetness in her manner which encouraged Mr. Pickering mightily, for he had fallen in love with Claire on sight.
It would seem that a schoolgirl in these advanced days would know what to do when she found that a man with thirty million dollars was in love with her; yet there were factors in the situation which gave Claire pause. Lord Dawlish, of course, was one of them. She had not mentioned Lord Dawlish to Mr. Pickering, and﻿—doubtless lest the sight of it might pain him﻿—she had abstained from wearing her engagement ring during the voyage. But she had not completely lost sight of the fact that she was engaged to Bill. Another thing that caused her to hesitate was the fact that Dudley Pickering, however wealthy, was a most colossal bore. As far as Claire could ascertain on their short acquaintance, he had but one subject of conversation﻿—automobiles.

===Chinese===

达德利·皮克林是个中西部人，人到中年，靠勤俭与苦干从汽车上积攒起一笔可观的财富。你的家业发达到哪一步，他就有一款汽车配得上你。你年轻奋斗的时节，花四百美元买他的“小皮克”（Little Pick）；年纪渐长、手头渐宽，再掏一千一百块买他的“皮克林珍宝”（Pickering Gem）；兴许再过些年，中间的阶梯步步登完，你也阔绰得起靡费之时，便发觉自己已坐拥一辆“皮克林巨人”（Pickering Giant）——市面上顶好的汽车。人人都说达德利·皮克林的好话。报纸说他好，布拉德斯特里特征信所（Bradstreet）说他好，他自己也说自己好。邮轮上，他把一生的英雄史诗尽数倾倒进克莱尔恭听的耳朵里；到航程终了，她虽已忘了他创业时兜里只有半个美元，却还记得他收场时坐拥两三千万；而她言谈举止间那点温存的甜意，又给了皮克林先生极大的鼓舞——因为他见到克莱尔的第一眼便坠入了情网。
照如今这开明的世道，便是一个女学生，也该知道发现一个坐拥三千万美元的男人爱上自己时该当如何；可眼前的局面里另有几重因素，叫克莱尔踌躇。达利什勋爵自然是其中之一。她不曾向皮克林先生提过达利什勋爵，而且——想必是怕那物件刺他的眼——整个航程都没戴她的订婚戒指。可她还没有全然忘了自己是同比尔订了婚的人。另一重叫她迟疑的，是达德利·皮克林纵然家财万贯，却是个顶顶乏味的人物。就克莱尔短短相识所能查明，他谈话的题目只有一个——汽车。

===Original===

To Claire an automobile was a shiny thing with padded seats, in which you rode if you were lucky enough to know somebody who owned one. She had no wish to go more deeply into the matter. Dudley Pickering’s attitude toward automobiles, on the other hand, more nearly resembled that of a surgeon toward the human body. To him a car was something to dissect, something with an interior that was interesting to explore and fascinating to talk about. He revealed the internal mechanism of his Pickering Giant in a way that was almost indecent. He laid bare its vital organs and lectured on them. He spoke freely of things that a modest automobile hides from view. Claire listened with a radiant display of interest, but she had her doubts as to whether any amount of money would make it worthwhile to undergo this sort of thing for life. She was still in this hesitant frame of mind when she entered Riegelheimer’s Restaurant, and it perturbed her that she could not come to some definite decision on Mr. Pickering, for those subtle signs which every woman can recognize and interpret told her that the latter, having paved the way by talking machinery for a week, was about to boil over and speak of higher things. At the very next opportunity she was certain he intended to propose.
The presence of Lady Wetherby acted as a temporary check on the development of the situation, but after they had been seated at their table a short time the lights of the restaurant were suddenly lowered, a colored spotlight became manifest near the roof, and classical music made itself heard from the fiddles in the orchestra. You could tell it was classical, because the banjo players were leaning back and chewing gum; and in New York restaurants only death or a classical specialty can stop banjoists. There was a spatter of applause and Lady Wetherby removed her sandals.

===Chinese===

在克莱尔眼里，汽车不过是一种亮闪闪、带软垫座位的物件，谁若运气好，认识一位车主，便可坐上去兜一圈。她无意深究这门学问。达德利·皮克林对汽车的态度却截然两样，更近乎外科医生对待人体。在他看来，汽车是用来解剖的东西：内里值得探察，谈起来令人着迷。他展示“皮克林巨人”内部机件的那副样子，几乎近于有伤风化。他把它的要害器官一一袒露，还就着它们开讲。一辆本分守礼的汽车素来藏而不露的种种部件，他也说得口无遮拦。克莱尔听得满面春色，兴趣装得十足，心里却直犯嘀咕：不论多少钱，可值得终身受用这一套？她踏进里格尔海默餐厅时，仍旧存着这份踌躇；皮克林先生的事定不下来，颇叫她心绪不宁——因为女人个个识得出、读得懂的那些微妙征兆告诉她：这位先生拿一个星期的机械谈资铺好了路，眼看就要满溢出来，要谈更高一层的事情了。她拿得稳，一得机会，他便要求婚。
韦瑟比伯爵夫人在场，暂时拖住了局势的进展。可他们落座没多久，餐馆的灯忽然暗了下来，屋顶近处亮起一道彩色追光，乐队的小提琴里流出古典音乐。你一听便知是古典的，因为弹班卓琴的乐师们都靠回椅背、嚼起了口香糖；在纽约的餐馆里，能叫班卓琴手住手的，唯有死亡，或是古典节目。稀稀落落一阵掌声，韦瑟比伯爵夫人除下了凉鞋。

===Original===

“This,” she explained to Claire, “is where I do my stunt. Watch it. I invented the steps myself. Classical stuff. It’s called the Dream of Psyche.”
It was difficult for one who knew her as Claire did to associate Polly Wetherby with anything classical. On the road, in England, when they had been fellow members of the number two company of The Heavenly Waltz, Polly had been remarkable chiefly for a fund of humorous anecdote and a gift, amounting almost to genius, for doing battle with militant landladies. And renewing their intimacy after a hiatus of a little less than a year, Claire had found her unchanged. The moment before the music started Lady Wetherby, ever a warm patron of sport, had been arguing forcefully in favor of the view﻿—opposed, it seemed, by a bunch of boneheaded boobs on certain of the daily papers﻿—that the Tennessee Bearcat, though eclipsed by showier rivals over the ten-round route, would be lightweight champion of the world tomorrow if he could only succeed in luring his most prominent rival into the ring for a forty-five-round contest. Claire found herself wondering how her friend could possibly shake off this mood and prepare herself at a moment’s notice to give an artistic rendition of the Dream of Psyche.
As a matter of fact, Lady Wetherby did not. Perhaps it was the association of ideas, but it seemed to Claire that the Dream of Psyche, as interpreted in terms of the dance by her friend, was far less like a Dream of Psyche than a troubled nightmare of the Tennessee Bearcat, fallen asleep while brooding on how he should induce the lightweight champion to fight him to a finish. As the performance proceeded she could well believe that it was Polly Wetherby who had invented the steps.

===Chinese===

“这个嘛，”她向克莱尔解释，“就到了我露绝活的时候了。瞧着。舞步是我自己编的。古典范儿。名叫《普绪克之梦》（Dream of Psyche）。”
像克莱尔这样深知她为人的，很难把波莉·韦瑟比同任何古典的东西联系到一处。当年在英国巡回演出，两人同在《天堂圆舞曲》（The Heavenly Waltz）二号班子里搭伙，波莉的出名，主要靠一肚皮逗笑的段子，还靠一副差不多可称天才的斗嘴功夫——专跟好斗的女房东们过招。此番重逢，相隔不到一年，克莱尔发觉她一点没变。就在音乐响起前一刻，这位素来热心体育赛事的韦瑟比伯爵夫人，还在慷慨激昂地为一种主张辩护——照她看来，唱反调的尽是几家日报上一撮蠢头蠢脑的笨蛋——即：田纳西“山猫”（the Tennessee Bearcat）虽在十个回合的赛程里常被花哨的对手抢去风头，可只要能把那位最扎眼的宿敌诱进拳台打满四十五回合，明日的轻量级世界拳王便非他莫属。克莱尔不禁纳闷：她这位朋友究竟要怎样甩脱这副心绪，好说变就变，当场献演一场艺术性的《普绪克之梦》。
实际上，韦瑟比伯爵夫人并没有甩脱。也许是联想在作怪，可克莱尔觉得，她这位朋友以舞蹈诠释的《普绪克之梦》，与其说像普绪克之梦，远不如说像田纳西“山猫”的一场愁梦——他盘算着怎样说动轻量级拳王同他见个输赢，想着想着睡着了，便做下这一场噩梦。表演愈往下走，克莱尔愈加相信：这些舞步，确乎出自波莉·韦瑟比的手笔。

===Original===

It was a truculent affair, this Dream of Psyche. It was not so much dancing as shadowboxing. It began mildly enough to the accompaniment of pizzicato strains from the orchestra﻿—Psyche in her training quarters. Rallentando﻿—Psyche punching the bag. Diminuendo﻿—Psyche using the medicine ball. Presto﻿—Psyche doing road work. Forte﻿—The night of the fight. And then things began to move to a climax. With the fiddles working themselves to the bone and the piano bounding under its persecutor’s blows, Lady Wetherby ducked, sidestepped, rushed and sprang, moving her arms in a manner that may have been classical Greek, but to the untrained eye looked much more like the last round of an open-air bout at Ebbet’s Field.
It was halfway through the exhibition, when you could smell the sawdust and hear the seconds shouting advice under the ropes, that Claire, who never having seen anything in her life like this extraordinary performance had been staring spellbound, awoke to the realization that Dudley Pickering was proposing to her. It required a woman’s intuition to divine this fact, for Mr. Pickering was not coherent. He did not go straight to the point. He rambled. But Claire understood, and it came to her that this thing had taken her before she was ready. In a brief while she would have to give an answer of some sort, and she had not clearly decided what answer she meant to give.

===Chinese===

这场《普绪克之梦》，性质凶悍。与其说是跳舞，不如说是练空拳。开场倒还温和，配着乐队的拨奏——普绪克在训练营里。渐慢——普绪克在击打沙袋。渐弱——普绪克在推药球。急板——普绪克在练跑步。强音——决战之夜。随后，场面步步推向高潮。小提琴演奏得卖命到骨头缝里，钢琴在施暴者的捶击下乱蹦，韦瑟比伯爵夫人闪躲、侧步、突进、腾跃，双臂挥动的姿势或许当真合乎古典希腊风，可在外行眼里，倒更像埃贝茨球场（Ebbet's Field）露天拳赛打到最后一回合。
演出进行到一半——那当口你仿佛已闻得见场里的木屑气味，听得见助手们在围绳底下高声支招——生平从未见识过这等奇观的克莱尔，正看得目眩神迷，忽然醒悟到：达德利·皮克林是在向她求婚。这份洞察非靠女人的直觉不可，因为皮克林先生语无伦次。他不直奔主题。他东拉西扯。可克莱尔明白了，并且察觉这件事来得猝然，她还没准备好。要不了多久，她总得给个或这样或那样的答复；而她到底打算给什么答复，心里还没拿定主意。

===Original===

Then while he was still skirting his subject, before he had wandered to what he really wished to say, the music stopped, the applause broke out again, and Lady Wetherby returned to the table like a pugilist seeking his corner at the end of a round. Her face was flushed and she was breathing hard.
“They pay me money for that!” she observed genially. “Can you beat it!”
The spell was broken. Mr. Pickering sank back in his chair in a punctured manner. And Claire, making monosyllabic replies to her friend’s remarks, was able to bend her mind to the task of finding out how she stood on this important Pickering issue. That he would return to the attack as soon as possible she knew; and next time she must have her attitude clearly defined one way or the other.

===Chinese===

就在他仍在题目边上绕圈子、还没游荡到他真正想说的话头时，音乐停了，掌声再度爆发，韦瑟比伯爵夫人回到桌边，好似一个回合终了、寻回自己场角的拳手。她满面红光，气喘吁吁。
“这个他们还付我钱呢！”她乐呵呵地说，“你说可笑不可笑！”
咒语破了。皮克林先生像一只被戳破的气球，蔫蔫地缩回椅子里。克莱尔对朋友的谈兴只报以单音节，得以分出心思，去盘算这桩要紧的皮克林公案上，自己究竟处在什么位置。她知道，他必得一有机会便卷土重来；下一回，她非把态度明明白白定在一头不可。

===Original===

Lady Wetherby, having got the Dance of Psyche out of her system and replaced it with a glass of iced coffee, was inclined for conversation.
“Algie called me up on the phone this evening, Claire.”
“Yes?”

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵夫人跳完《普绪克之梦》，发散了个干净，换上一杯冰咖啡，便来了谈兴。
“今晚阿尔吉来过电话，克莱尔。”
“是吗？”

===Original===

Claire was examining Mr. Pickering with furtive side glances. He was not handsome, nor, on the other hand, was he repulsive. Undistinguished was the adjective that would have described him. He was inclined to stoutness, but not unpardonably so; his hair was thin, but he was not aggressively bald; his face was dull, but certainly not stupid. There was nothing in his outer man which thirty million dollars would not offset. As regarded his other qualities, his conversation was certainly not exhilarating. But that also was not, under certain conditions, an unforgivable thing. No, looking at the matter all round and weighing it with care, the real obstacle, Claire decided, was not any quality or lack of qualities in Dudley Pickering﻿—it was Lord Dawlish and the simple fact that it would be extremely difficult, if she discarded him in favor of a richer man without any ostensible cause, to retain her self-respect.
“I think he’s weakening.”
“Yes.”
Yes, that was the crux of the matter. She wanted to retain her good opinion of herself. And in order to achieve that end it was essential that she find some excuse, however trivial, for breaking off the engagement.

===Chinese===

克莱尔正偷偷用眼角打量皮克林先生。他不英俊，可另一方面也不讨人嫌。形容他的形容词，该是“平庸无奇”。他体形偏胖，但还没胖得不可饶恕；头发稀疏，却还没秃得咄咄逼人；面孔呆板，可断乎不蠢。论外表，他身上没有哪一样是三千万美元抵偿不了的。至于别的品性：他的谈吐确实提不起人的兴致。可这放在某些条件之下，也算不得不可饶恕。不，把事情通盘看过、细细掂量之后，克莱尔断定，真正的障碍并不在达德利·皮克林有什么品性或缺什么品性——障碍是达利什勋爵，是这么一个再简单不过的事实：倘若她平白无故甩掉他、另就一个更有钱的人，她要保住自尊心，只怕难上加难。
“我看他是撑不住了。”
“嗯。”
是啊，症结正在于此。她要保全的，是对自己的好评。而要达到这个目的，关键在于找一个借口，无论多么微不足道，来解除这桩婚约。

===Original===

“His voice was quite hollow, poor dear! You know, Claire, I’m wild about Algie, but it would never do to let him think he could boss me. He’s the kind that if you give him a thingummy takes a what-d’you-call-it.”
“Yes?”
A waiter approached the table.
“Mr. Pickering?”
The thwarted lover came to life with a start.
“Eh?”

===Chinese===

“他嗓音听着空落落的，可怜的人儿！克莱尔，你知道，我爱阿尔吉爱得要命，可万万不能叫他觉得能拿捏我。他就是那种人——你给他一根‘那个啥’，他就敢拿走一个‘叫什么来着’。”
“是吗？”
一个侍者走到了桌边。
“皮克林先生？”
那位求爱受挫的情人猛地一惊，回过神来。
“啊？”

===Original===

“A gentleman wishes to speak to you on the telephone.”
“Oh, yes. I was expecting a long-distance call, Lady Wetherby, and left word I should be here. Will you excuse me?”
Lady Wetherby watched him as he bustled across the room.
“What do you think of him, Claire?”
“Mr. Pickering? I think he’s very nice.”

===Chinese===

“有位先生想请您听电话。”
“哦，对。韦瑟比伯爵夫人，我正等一个长途电话，留了话说我在这儿。失陪一下？”
韦瑟比伯爵夫人望着他匆匆忙忙穿过店堂。
“你觉得他这个人怎么样，克莱尔？”
“皮克林先生吗？我觉得他很好。”

===Original===

“He admires you frantically. I hoped he would. That’s why I wanted you to come over on the same ship with him.”
“Polly! I had no notion that you were such a schemer.”
“I would just love to see you two fix it up,” continued Lady Wetherby earnestly. “He may not be what you might call a cut-up, but he’s a darned good sort, and thirty millions helps, doesn’t it? You don’t want to overlook that thirty millions, Claire!”

===Chinese===

“他仰慕你仰慕得疯了。我就盼着这样。所以当初我才要你跟他同一条船过来。”
“波莉！我可没想到你竟是这样工于心计的人。”
“我真心乐意看你俩成一桩好事，”韦瑟比伯爵夫人恳切地接着说，“他也许算不上你说的那种活宝，可他是个顶好的人，再说三千万帮得上忙，是不是？那三千万你可别不当回事，克莱尔！”

===Original===

“I do like Mr. Pickering.”
“Claire, he asked me if you were engaged.”
“What!”
“When I told him you weren’t, he beamed. Honestly, you’ve only got to lift your little finger and﻿—Oh, good Lord, there’s Algie!”

===Chinese===

“皮克林先生我是真喜欢的。”
“克莱尔，他问过我，你订婚了没有。”
“什么！”
“我告诉他没订，他那张脸立马笑开了花。说真的，你只消动一动小指头就成——哎呀，老天，阿尔吉来了！”

===Original===

Claire looked up. A dapper, trim little man of about forty was threading his way among the tables in their direction. It was a year since Claire had seen Lord Wetherby, but she recognized him at once. He had a red, weather-beaten face with a suspicion of side-whiskers, small, pink-rimmed eyes with sandy eyebrows, the smoothest of sandy hair, and a chin so cleanly shaven that it was difficult to believe that hair had ever grown there. Although his evening dress was perfect in every detail he conveyed a subtle suggestion of horsiness. He was one of those English aristocrats who seem just to have missed being grooms, and who escape the groom type only by their shiny cleanliness and the extreme excellence of the fit of their clothes. He reached the table and sat down without invitation in the vacant chair.
“Pauline!” he said sorrowfully.
“Algie,” said Lady Wetherby tensely, “I don’t know what you’ve come here for, and I don’t remember asking you to sit down and put your elbows on the table, but I want to begin by saying that I will not be called Pauline. My name’s Polly. You’ve got a way of saying Pauline, as if it were a gentlemanly cuss-word, that makes me want to scream. And while you’re about it, why don’t you say how-d’you-do to Claire? You ought to remember her, she was my bridesmaid.”

===Chinese===

克莱尔抬起头。一个衣着利落、整洁干练的小个子男人，年纪四十上下，正穿行在桌子中间朝他们走来。克莱尔有一年没见韦瑟比伯爵（Lord Wetherby）了，可她一眼便认出了他。他一张风吹日晒的红脸膛，鬓边依稀有连鬓胡子的痕迹，一双小眼睛眼圈泛粉，配着沙色的眉毛，沙色的头发梳理得溜光水滑；下巴剃得干干净净，叫人难以相信那里头也曾长过毛发。他的晚礼服每个细节都无可挑剔，周身却隐隐透出一股马厩气。他是那种英国贵族：眼看着就要沦为马倌了，全凭一身锃亮的洁净和衣裳的极端合体，才算逃出了马倌的行当。他走到桌边，不等相请，便在空椅子上坐了下来。
“波琳！”他哀伤地说。
“阿尔吉，”韦瑟比伯爵夫人绷紧了声音说，“我不知道你上这儿来做什么，也记不得请过你坐下、把胳膊肘支到桌上；可我要先说一句：别管我叫波琳。我叫波莉。你一说‘波琳’，那个腔调，就像在念一句上等人骂人的粗话，听得我只想尖叫。趁你在这儿，怎么不同克莱尔问声好？你该记得她，她是我的伴娘。”

===Original===

“How do you do, Miss Fenwick? Of course I remember you perfectly. I’m glad to see you again.”
“And now, Algie, what is it? Why have you come here?” Lord Wetherby looked doubtfully at Claire. “Oh, that’s all right,” said Lady Wetherby. “Claire knows all about it﻿—I told her.”
“Ah! Then if Miss Fenwick has heard of our little tiff﻿—”
“Don’t call it a little tiff. It was a scrap!”
“My dear! Really!”
“A scrap!” repeated Lady Wetherby firmly. “A regular all-in, what-Sherman-said scrap, which you began. And if you think you’re going to wriggle out of it by calling it a little tiff, take one additional guess!”

===Chinese===

“你好，芬威克小姐？我当然记得你，记得清清楚楚。很高兴又见到你。”
“好了，阿尔吉，什么事？你上这儿来做什么？”韦瑟比伯爵迟疑地看了看克莱尔。“哦，不要紧，”韦瑟比伯爵夫人说，“这件事克莱尔全知道——我告诉她了。”
“啊！那么，既然芬威克小姐已经听说过我们那点小口角——”
“别叫它小口角。那是一场大干仗！”
“亲爱的！不至于吧！”
“大干仗！”韦瑟比伯爵夫人斩钉截铁地重复道，“一场货真价实的大打出手，谢尔曼（Sherman）将军说的那种仗，还是你先挑起来的。你要是觉得管它叫小口角就能蒙混过去，那你不妨再猜一回！”

===Original===

“My dear, I am not trying to wriggle out of it. I think I was justified in taking the attitude I did toward your snake Clarence. I appeal to Miss Fenwick, if, as you say, she knows all the facts of the case, to say whether it is reasonable to expect a man of my temperament, a nervous, highly strung artist, to welcome the presence of snakes at the breakfast table. I trust that I am not an unreasonable man, but I decline to admit that a long green snake is a proper thing to keep about the house.”
“You had no right to strike the poor thing.”
“In that one respect I was perhaps a little hasty. I happened to be stirring my tea at the moment his head rose above the edge of the table. I was not entirely myself that morning. My nerves were somewhat disordered. I had lain awake much of the night planning a canvas.”
“Planning a what?”
“A canvas, my dear﻿—a picture.”

===Chinese===

“亲爱的，我并没有想蒙混过去。我认为我对你那条蛇克拉伦斯（Clarence）所持的态度是有道理的。我请芬威克小姐评评理——既然照你的说法，本案的种种事实她都已知晓——看看指望一个秉性如我的人，一个神经脆弱、一绷就断的艺术家，去欢迎蛇光临早餐桌，是否合理。我自信不是个不讲理的人，可我断乎不能承认：一条长长的绿蛇，是适宜养在家里的东西。”
“你动手打那可怜的东西，就没有道理。”
“唯独那一点，我或许急躁了些。它的脑袋从桌沿冒出来的时候，我恰好在搅茶。那天早上我心神不属。我的神经有些紊乱。我大半夜没合眼，在筹划一幅画布。”
“筹划一幅什么？”
“一幅画布，亲爱的——也就是一幅画。”

===Original===

Lady Wetherby turned to Claire.
“I want you to listen to Algie, Claire. You hear the way he pulls the art-yard stuff? A year ago he didn’t know one end of a paint brush from the other. He didn’t know he had any nerves. If you had brought him the artistic temperament on a plate with a bit of watercress round it, he wouldn’t have recognized it. And now, just because he’s got a studio in Washington Square, he thinks he has a right to be a sort of dopeless dope fiend, going up in the air if you speak to him suddenly and running about the place hitting snakes with teaspoons as if he were Michelangelo!”
“My dear, you do me an injustice. It is true that as an artist I developed late﻿—But why should we quarrel? If it will help to pave the way to a renewed understanding between us, I am prepared to apologize for striking Clarence. That is conciliatory, I think, Miss Fenwick?”
“Very.”
“Miss Fenwick considers my attitude conciliatory, my dear.”
“It’s something,” admitted Lady Wetherby grudgingly.

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵夫人转向克莱尔。
“克莱尔，我要你听听阿尔吉这套话。你听见他这一嘴艺术场子的行话了吧？一年以前，画笔哪头是哪头他都分不清。他都不知道自己还有神经。那时候你就是把艺术家脾气盛在盘子里端给他，边上还配一撮西洋菜，他也认不出来。可如今，只因为在华盛顿广场（Washington Square）有了一间画室，他就觉得自己有资格当一种不沾毒品的瘾君子了：你冷不丁同他讲话，他就暴跳如雷，满屋子乱窜，拿茶匙打蛇，俨然自己就是米开朗琪罗（Michelangelo）！”
“亲爱的，你这可冤枉我了。不错，作为艺术家，我是大器晚成——可我们何必争吵？倘若这有助于为彼此重归于好铺平道路，我愿意为打了克拉伦斯一事道歉。这总算是息事宁人的态度吧，芬威克小姐？”
“很是。”
“芬威克小姐认为我的态度是息事宁人的，亲爱的。”
“总算是一点表示。”韦瑟比伯爵夫人勉强承认。

===Original===

Lord Wetherby drained the highball which Dudley Pickering had left behind him and seemed to draw strength from it, for he now struck a firmer note.
“But, though expressing regret for my momentary loss of self-control, I cannot recede from the position I have taken up as regards the essential unfitness of Clarence’s presence in the home.”
Lady Wetherby looked despairingly at Claire.
“The very first words I heard Algie speak, Claire, were at Newmarket during the three-o’clock race one May afternoon. He was hanging over the rail, yelling like an Indian, and what he was yelling was: ‘Come on, you blighter, come on. By the living jingo, Brickbat wins in a walk!’ And now he’s pulling stuff about receding from essential positions! Oh, well, he wasn’t an artist then!”

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵把达德利·皮克林撂下没喝的那杯高杯酒一饮而尽，仿佛从中汲取了力气，因为他的调子如今硬了起来。
“不过，虽说我对自己一时的失态深表遗憾，但在克拉伦斯根本不宜待在家中这一立场上，我绝无后退的余地。”
韦瑟比伯爵夫人绝望地看了看克莱尔。
“我头一回听见阿尔吉讲话，克莱尔，是在纽马基特（Newmarket），五月里一个下午三点钟那场赛马。他扒在栏杆上，喊得跟印第安人似的，喊的是：‘加油呀，你这个家伙，加油。老天爷在上，布里克巴特（Brickbat）闲庭信步，赢定了！’如今他倒拽起‘根本立场、绝无后退’这一套了！唉，谁叫他那时还不是艺术家呢！”

===Original===

“My dear Pau﻿—Polly. I am purposely picking my words on the present occasion in order to prevent the possibility of further misunderstandings. I consider myself an ambassador.”
“You would be shocked if you knew what I consider you!”
“I am endeavoring to the best of my ability﻿—”
“Algie, listen to me! I am quite calm at present, but there’s no knowing how soon I may hit you with a chair if you don’t come to earth quick and talk like an ordinary human being. What is it that you are driving at?”

===Chinese===

“亲爱的波——波莉。眼下这个场合，我是字斟句酌，为免再生误会。我把自己看作一位大使。”
“要是你知道我把你看作什么，你非吓坏了不可！”
“我正在竭尽所能地——”
“阿尔吉，听我说！我这会儿还算心平气和，可你若不赶紧落到地面上、像普通人一样讲话，难保哪一刻我就抄起椅子砸你。你到底想说什么？”

===Original===

“Very well, it’s this: I’ll come home if you get rid of that snake.”
“Never!”
“It’s surely not much to ask of you, Polly.”
“I won’t!”
Lord Wetherby sighed.
“When I led you to the altar,” he said reproachfully, “you promised to love, honor and obey me. I thought at the time it was a bit of swank!”

===Chinese===

“很好，是这样的：你打发走那条蛇，我就回家。”
“休想！”
“这总不算过分的要求吧，波莉。”
“我不干！”
韦瑟比伯爵叹了口气。
“当初我把你领到圣坛前，”他埋怨道，“你许诺过爱我、敬我、顺从我。我当时就琢磨，那不过是句摆排场的空话！”

===Original===

Lady Wetherby’s manner thawed. She became more friendly.
“When you talk like that, Algie, I feel there’s hope for you after all. That’s how you used to talk in the dear old days when you’d come to me to borrow half a crown to put on a horse! You﻿—”
This excursion into reminiscence appeared to embarrass Lord Wetherby. He indicated Claire with a gesture.
“My dear!” he said deprecatingly. “Miss Fenwick!”
“Oh, Claire’s an old pal of mine. You can’t shock her. She knows all about us.”
“Nevertheless﻿—”

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵夫人的态度解了冻。她和软下来。
“你这么讲话，阿尔吉，我倒觉得你终究还有救。想当年那些好日子，你来找我借半个克朗押一匹马的时候，就是这么讲话的！你——”
这一趟怀旧之旅似乎窘住了韦瑟比伯爵。他做了个手势，指了指克莱尔。
“亲爱的！”他不以为然地说，“芬威克小姐！”
“嗐，克莱尔是我的老朋友。吓不着她的。咱们的事她全知道。”
“尽管如此——”

===Original===

“Oh, very well. Listen, Algie, now that you seem to be getting more reasonable, I wish I could make you understand that I don’t keep Clarence for sheer love of him. He’s a commercial asset. He’s an advertisement. You must know that I have got to have something to﻿—”
“I admit that may be so as regards the monkey Eustace. Monkeys as aids to publicity have, I believe, been tested and found valuable by other artists. I am prepared to accept Eustace, but the snake is worthless.”
“Oh, you don’t object to Eustace then?”
“I do strongly, but I concede his uses.”
“You would live in the same house as Eustace?”
“I would endeavor to do so. But not in the same house with Eustace and Clarence.”

===Chinese===

“哦，好吧。听着，阿尔吉，如今你看来通情理些了，我倒盼着你能明白：我养克拉伦斯，并非单纯疼他。他是一项商业资产。他是一幅活广告。你总该明白，我总得有点什么来——”
“我承认，就猴子尤斯塔斯（Eustace）而论，或许如此。拿猴子作宣传的帮手，我相信别的艺术家已经试过，证明有价值。尤斯塔斯我可以接受，可那条蛇一文不值。”
“哦，这么说尤斯塔斯你不反对？”
“反对得厉害，可我承认他有他的用处。”
“你肯同尤斯塔斯住在一幢房子里？”
“我愿勉力一试。但尤斯塔斯和克拉伦斯同时在，那可不行。”

===Original===

There was a pause.
“I don’t know that I’m so struck on Clarence myself,” said Lady Wetherby weakly.
“My darling!”
“Wait a minute. I’ve not said I would get rid of him.”

===Chinese===

一时无话。
“要说克拉伦斯，我自己也不见得有多迷他。”韦瑟比伯爵夫人有气无力地说。
“我的心肝！”
“等等。我可没说我要把他打发走。”

===Original===

“But you will?”
Lady Wetherby’s hesitation lasted but a moment.
“All right, Algie. I’ll send him to the Bronx Zoo tomorrow.”
“My precious pet!”

===Chinese===

“可你会的，是不是？”
韦瑟比伯爵夫人的犹豫只持续了一瞬。
“好吧，阿尔吉。明天我就把他送到布朗克斯动物园（Bronx Zoo）去。”
“我的宝贝心肝！”

===Original===

A hand, reaching under the table, enveloped Claire’s in a loving clasp. From the look on Lord Wetherby’s face she supposed that he was under the delusion that he was bestowing this attention on his wife.
“You know, Algie, darling,” said Lady Wetherby, melting completely, “when you get that yearning note in your voice I just flop and take the full count.”
“My sweetheart, when I saw you doing that Dream of What’s-the-girl’s-bally-name dance just now, it was all I could do to keep from rushing out on the floor and hugging you.”
“Algie!”
“Polly!”

===Chinese===

一只手从桌子底下伸过来，把克莱尔的手一把握住，情意绵绵。从韦瑟比伯爵脸上的神情看，她估摸他是在错觉之下，只道这番温存是给了自己的妻子。
“你知道，阿尔吉，亲爱的，”韦瑟比伯爵夫人彻底心软了，“你的嗓音里一带上那种渴念的调调，我就瘫了，被数满十秒，乖乖认输。”
“我的甜心，方才看你跳那支《叫什么倒霉名字来着之梦》，我差点儿按捺不住，冲进场子里搂住你。”
“阿尔吉！”
“波莉！”

===Original===

“Do you mind letting go of my hand, please, Lord Wetherby?” said Claire, on whom these saccharine exchanges were beginning to have a cloying effect.
For a moment Lord Wetherby seemed somewhat confused, but, pulling himself together, he covered his embarrassment with a pomposity that blended poorly with his horsey appearance.
“Married life, Miss Fenwick,” he said, “as you will no doubt discover some day for yourself, must always be a series of mutual compromises, of cheerful give and take. The lamp of love﻿—”
His remarks were cut short by a crash at the other end of the room. There was sharp cry and the splintering of glass. The place was full of a sudden, sharp confusion. They jumped up with one accord. Lady Wetherby spilled her iced coffee; Lord Wetherby dropped the lamp of love. Claire, who was nearest the pillar that separated them from the part of the restaurant where the accident had happened, was the first to see what had taken place.

===Chinese===

“劳驾，韦瑟比伯爵，能不能请你放开我的手？”克莱尔说。这套甜腻腻的你来我往，已开始叫她腻味得发慌。
韦瑟比伯爵一时颇为慌乱，但他定了定神，摆出一副堂而皇之的架子来遮羞——这副架子同他那身马厩气的外表，实在难以调和。
“婚姻生活，芬威克小姐，”他说，“你日后自会有亲身领教的一天，它永远必须是一连串彼此的妥协、愉快的互谅互让。爱情之灯——”
他的宏论被店堂另一头一声巨响打断了。一声尖叫，玻璃迸碎。满店霎时乱作一团，闹声尖利。众人不约而同跳起身来。韦瑟比伯爵夫人泼翻了她的冰咖啡；韦瑟比伯爵失手摔了那盏爱情之灯。出事的场所与他们这边隔着一根柱子；克莱尔离那根柱子最近，头一个看清了发生了什么事。

===Original===

A large man, dancing with a large girl, appeared to have charged into a small waiter, upsetting him and his tray and the contents of his tray. The various actors in the drama were now engaged in sorting themselves out from the ruins. The man had his back toward her, and it seemed to Claire that there was something familiar about that back. Then he turned and she recognized Lord Dawlish.
She stood transfixed. For a moment surprise was her only emotion. How came Bill to be in America? Then other feelings blended with her surprise. It is a fact that Lord Dawlish was looking singularly disreputable. The unwonted exercise of the dance had flushed his face, rumpled his hair and imparted a damp untidiness to his collar. He had not yet become aware that there was a pat of butter clinging to his left shoulder, and that did not tend to lessen the dissolute nature of his appearance.
From Bill Claire’s eyes traveled to his partner and took in with one swift feminine glance her large, exuberant blondness. There is no denying that, seen with a somewhat biased eye, the Good Sport resembled rather closely a poster advertising a burlesque show. Claire returned to her seat. Lord and Lady Wetherby continued to talk, but she allowed them to conduct the conversation without her assistance.

===Chinese===

一个大块头男人搂着一个大块头姑娘跳舞，看来是一头撞上了一个小个子侍者，把侍者、他的托盘、连同托盘里的东西统统撞翻。这出戏的各位登场人物，此刻正纷纷从废墟里把自己分拣出来。那男人背朝着她；克莱尔觉得那个背影有点眼熟。随即他转过身来，她认出了达利什勋爵。
她怔在原地。头一刻，惊讶是她唯一的情绪。比尔怎么会到了美国？随即，别的感触同惊讶掺到了一处。事实是，达利什勋爵的模样出奇地落魄。跳舞这场不惯的劳作叫他满脸通红、头发蓬乱，领子上也浸出一圈潮乎乎的凌乱。他还没有察觉左肩上粘着一小块黄油；这一细节自然无助于冲淡他那副浪荡子的仪表。
克莱尔的目光从比尔移到他的舞伴身上，以女人家敏捷的一瞥，把她那一身丰盈繁茂的金黄尽收眼底。无可否认，带着几分成见看去，这位“爽快姑娘”（the Good Sport）同滑稽杂耍秀的海报实在相去不远。克莱尔回到自己的座位。韦瑟比伯爵夫妇继续交谈；她听凭他们自己谈去，不加援手。

===Original===

“You’re very quiet, Claire,” said Polly.
“I’m thinking.”
“A very good thing, too, so they tell me. I’ve never tried it myself. Algie, darling, he was a bad boy to leave his nice home, wasn’t he? He didn’t deserve to have his hand held.”

===Chinese===

“你真安静，克莱尔，”波莉说。
“我在想事情。”
“听人家说，想事情是桩大好事。我自己可从来没试过。阿尔吉，亲爱的，他丢下好好的家跑出来，是个坏孩子，是不是？他不配有人握着他的手。”

## VIII / 第八章

===Original===

It had been a great night for Nutty Boyd. If the vision of his sister Elizabeth, back at the farm, speculating sadly on the whereabouts of her wandering boy ever came before his mental eye, he certainly did not allow it to interfere with his appreciation of the festivities. At Frolics in the Air, whither they moved after draining Riegelheimer’s of what joys it had to offer, and at Peale’s, where they went after wearying of Frolics in the Air, he was in the highest spirits. It was only occasionally that the recollection came to vex him that this could not last, that﻿—since his Uncle Ira had played him false﻿—he must return anon to the place whence he had come. When this happened a moody silence fell upon him; but he quickly recovered himself, and played the host again with that merry absence of parsimony that had endeared him in the past to so many of Broadway’s horse leeches.

Why, in a city of all-night restaurants, these parties ever break up, one cannot say, but a merciful Providence sees to it that they do, and just as Lord Dawlish was contemplating an eternity of the company of Nutty and his two companions, the end came. Miss Leonard said that she was tired. Her friend said that it was a shame to go home at dusk like this, but if the party was going to be broken up, she supposed there was nothing else for it. Bill was too sleepy to say anything.

The Good Sport lived round the corner, and only required Lord Dawlish’s escort for a couple of blocks. But Miss Leonard’s hotel was in the neighborhood of Washington Square, and it was Nutty’s pleasing task to drive her thither. Engaged thus, he received a shock that electrified him.

===Chinese===

对纳蒂·博伊德（Nutty Boyd）来说，这一晚过得着实痛快。姐姐伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）人在农庄，正为游荡在外的弟弟的下落黯然悬想——这幅景象即便偶尔掠过他的心头，他也断然不许它搅了自己取乐的兴致。把里格尔海默餐厅（Riegelheimer’s）所能供应的乐子榨取一空之后，一行人移师“空中嬉戏”（Frolics in the Air）；腻味了“空中嬉戏”，又转赴皮尔餐厅（Peale’s）；在这一处处场面上，他始终兴高采烈。只是偶尔，一个念头浮上来搅得他心烦：这样的日子长不了——既然舅舅艾拉（Ira）骗了他，他早晚得打回他来的那个地方去。每逢这种时候，他便郁郁地沉默下来；不过很快就能缓过神，重又做起东道，拿出那份乐呵呵绝无吝啬气的做派——往日里，百老汇的马蟥们正是为此个个把他引为知己。

在这么一座通宵餐馆遍地皆是的城市里，这类聚会居然还会散场，个中缘故谁也说不上来；不过慈悲的老天爷自会操心，关照它们散掉。正当达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）盘算着要同纳蒂和他的两位女伴共度永恒之际，散场的时刻到了。伦纳德小姐（Miss Leonard）说她累了。她的朋友说，天刚擦黑就打道回府，实在说不过去；不过既然散场已是大势所趋，想必也没有别的法子。比尔困得一句话也说不出来。

爽快姑娘（the Good Sport）就住在拐角那边，只需达利什勋爵陪送两个街区。可伦纳德小姐下榻的旅馆在华盛顿广场（Washington Square）一带，送她回去这桩美差便落在了纳蒂身上。就在尽职途中，一个震动击中了他，把他电了个正着。

===Original===

“That pal of yours,” said Miss Leonard drowsily. She was half asleep. “What did you say his name was?”

“Chalmers, he told me. I only met him tonight.”

“Well, it isn’t, it’s something else. It”﻿—Miss Leonard yawned﻿—“it’s Lord something.”

===Chinese===

“你那位朋友，”伦纳德小姐睡意沉沉地说。她已经半睡半醒。“你方才说他叫什么名字来着？”

“查默斯，他自己报的名。我今晚才头一回见他。”

“嗯，不叫这个，是别的名儿。他呀”——伦纳德小姐打了个哈欠——“是个什么勋爵来着。”

===Original===

“How do you mean, Lord something?”

“He’s a lord﻿—at least he was when I met him in London.”

“Are you sure you met him in London?”

“Of course I’m sure. He was at that supper Captain Delaney gave at Oddy’s. There can’t be two men in﻿—yeow!﻿—in England who dance like that.”

===Chinese===

“什么叫‘什么勋爵’？”

“他是位勋爵——至少我在伦敦见着他那会儿是。”

“你拿得准是在伦敦见的他？”

“当然拿得准。德莱尼上尉（Captain Delaney）在奥迪餐厅（Oddy’s）做东的那顿宵夜，他就在座。像他那样跳舞的男人，全英——哎唷！——全英国不会有第二个。”

===Original===

The recollection of Bill’s performance stimulated Miss Leonard into a temporary wakefulness, and she giggled.

“He danced like one of those college boys bucking the center! He danced just the same way that night in London. I wish I could remember his name. I almost had it a dozen times tonight. It’s something with a window in it.”

“A window?” Nutty’s brain was a little fatigued, and he felt himself unequal to grasping this. “How do you mean, a window?”

===Chinese===

比尔的舞技重现心头，伦纳德小姐一时精神起来，咯咯直笑。

“他跳起舞来，活脱是大学球队里硬冲中路的愣小子！那天夜里在伦敦，他跳的就是这个路数。可惜他的名字想不起来了。今晚有十来回，眼看就到嘴边了。好像那名字里带着一扇窗户似的。”

“窗户？”纳蒂的脑子已经疲惫不堪，自觉领会不了这个。“什么叫‘带着一扇窗户’？”

===Original===

“No, not a window, a door! I knew it was something about a house. I know now, his name’s Lord Dawlish.”

Nutty’s fatigue fell from him like a garment.

“It can’t be!”

“It is.”

===Chinese===

“不对，不是窗户，是门！我就说嘛，这名字准跟房子沾边儿。想起来了，他叫达利什勋爵。”

纳蒂的倦意像一件外衣，整个儿从他身上滑落。

“不会吧！”

“就是。”

===Original===

Miss Leonard’s eyes had closed and she spoke in a muffled voice.

“Are you sure?”

“Mm-mm.”

“By gad!”

===Chinese===

伦纳德小姐早已合上双眼，答话的声音闷闷的。

“你拿准了？”

“嗯——嗯。”

“我的老天！”

===Original===

Nutty was wide awake now and full of inquiries; but his companion unfortunately was asleep, and he could not put them to her. A gentleman cannot prod a lady﻿—and his guest, at that﻿—in the ribs in order to wake her up and ask her questions. Nutty sat back and gave himself up to feverish thought.

He could think of no reason why Lord Dawlish should have come to America calling himself William Chalmers, but that was no reason why he should not have done so. And Daisy Leonard, who all along had remembered meeting him in London, had identified him.

Nutty was convinced. Arriving finally at Miss Leonard’s hotel, he woke her up and saw her in at the door; then, telling the man to drive to Forty-three East Twenty-seventh Street, he urged his mind to rapid thought. He had decided as a first step in the following up of this matter to invite Bill down to Elizabeth’s farm, and the thought occurred to him that this had better be done tonight, for he knew by experience that on the morning after these little jaunts he was seldom in the mood to seek people out and invite them to go anywhere.

All the way to the apartment he continued to think and it was wonderful what possibilities there seemed to be in this little scheme of courting the society of the man who had robbed him of his inheritance. He had worked on Bill’s feelings so successfully as to elicit a loan of a million dollars, and was just proceeding to marry him to Elizabeth when the cab stopped with the sudden sharpness peculiar to New York cabs and he woke up to find himself at his destination.

===Chinese===

纳蒂到这会儿睡意全消，满肚子都是问题；偏生他的同伴不幸已经睡熟，这些问题没处打发。一位绅士总不能为了叫醒一位小姐——况且还是自家的女客——去戳人家的肋骨，好问长问短。纳蒂往椅背上一靠，任凭思绪高烧似的翻腾。

达利什勋爵凭什么顶着一个威廉·查默斯（William Chalmers）的名字跑到美国来，他想不出半点道理；可这并不等于他就不该这么做。而黛西·伦纳德（Daisy Leonard）从头至尾记得在伦敦见过他，如今把他认了出来。

纳蒂信服了。终于到了伦纳德小姐的旅馆，他把她唤醒，看着她进了门；随后吩咐车夫开往东二十七街43号，一面督促自己的脑子飞速盘算。追查这桩事情，第一步他已拿定主意：邀比尔下乡，去伊丽莎白的农庄住上几天；转念又想，此事最好今晚就办妥，因为经验早已教会他：每逢这等小小出游过后的早晨，他难得有兴致去找什么人，约人家去什么地方。

一路回公寓，他盘算不止；这桩跟夺走他遗产的家伙攀交情的小小算盘，内里竟似藏着无限前程，委实妙不可言。他在比尔的感情上下功夫，成效如此卓著，居然借出了一百万美元，正要接着把他许配给伊丽莎白，这时出租车带着纽约出租车特有的那股突兀急促劲儿猛地刹住，他醒过神来，发觉自己已到了目的地。

===Original===

Bill was in bed when the bell rang and received his late host in his pajamas, wondering, as he did so, whether this was the New York custom, to foregather again after a party had been broken up and chat till breakfast. But Nutty, it seemed, had come with a motive, not from a desire for more conversation.

“Sorry to disturb you, old man,” said Nutty. “I looked in to tell you that I was going down to the country tomorrow. I wondered whether you would care to come and spend a day or two.”

Bill was delighted. This was better than he had hoped for.

===Chinese===

门铃响时比尔已经上床，他穿着睡衣出来接待方才的东道主，一面暗自纳闷：散了场再聚拢来，一直聊到吃早饭，莫非这就是纽约的规矩？可看样子，纳蒂此来别有图谋，并非还嫌话没聊够。

“抱歉打搅你，老兄，”纳蒂说，“我顺路过来说一声：我明天要下乡。不知你愿不愿意一起去住上一两天？”

比尔大喜过望。这比他指望的还要称心。

===Original===

“Rather!” he said. “Thanks awfully!”

“There are plenty of trains in the afternoon,” said Nutty. “I don’t suppose either of us will feel like getting up early. I’ll call for you here at half-past six, and we’ll have an early dinner and make the seven-fifteen, shall we? We live very simply, you know. You won’t mind that?”

“My dear chap!”

“That’s all right, then,” said Nutty, closing the door. “Good night.”

===Chinese===

“那还用说！”他说，“多谢多谢！”

“下午的火车多的是，”纳蒂说，“我看咱们俩谁也不会乐意早起。我六点半到这儿来接你，咱们早点儿吃晚饭，赶七点一刻那班车，好不好？你知道，我们过日子很简朴。你不介意吧？”

“我的好伙计！”

“那就说定了，”纳蒂说着掩上了门。“晚安。”

## X / 第十章

===Original===
In the days that followed their interrupted love scene at Riegelheimer’s Restaurant that night of Lord Dawlish’s unfortunate encounter with the tray-bearing waiter, Dudley Pickering’s behavior had perplexed Claire Fenwick. She had taken it for granted that next day at the latest he would resume the offer of his hand, heart and automobiles. But time passed and he made no move in that direction. Of limousine bodies, carburetors, spark plugs and inner tubes he spoke with freedom and eloquence, but the subject of love and marriage he avoided absolutely. His behavior was inexplicable.

Claire was piqued. She was in the position of a hostess who has swept and garnished her house against the coming of a guest and waits in vain for that guest’s arrival. She had made up her mind what to do when Dudley Pickering proposed to her next time, and thereby, it seemed to her, had removed all difficulties in the way of that proposal. She little knew her Pickering.

===Chinese===
里格尔海默餐厅（Riegelheimer’s）那一夜，达利什勋爵不幸同端托盘的侍者撞了个正着，一场情话就此中断；打那以后连日里，达德利·皮克林（Dudley Pickering）的行止一直叫克莱尔·芬威克（Claire Fenwick）摸不着头脑。她想当然地认定，最迟第二天，他便会重整旗鼓，再度献上手、心和汽车。可是时间流逝，他在那个方向寸步未动。说起豪华轿车车身、汽化器、火花塞和内胎，他挥洒自如、口若悬河；至于爱情与婚姻这个话题，他避得干干净净。他的行径实在费解。

克莱尔着恼了。她的处境，好比一位主妇把房子里里外外洒扫装点停当，专候一位客人驾临，那客人却左等不来、右等也不来。达德利·皮克林下次向她求婚时她该怎么应对，她早已打定了主意；在她想来，这一来，那桩求婚路上的难处已一概扫清。她实在没识透她那位皮克林。

===Original===
Dudley Pickering was not a self-starter in the motordrome of love. He needed cranking. He was that most unpromising of matrimonial material, a shy man with a cautious disposition. If he overcame his shyness caution applied the foot brake. If he succeeded in forgetting caution shyness shut off the gas. At Riegelheimer’s some miracle had made him not only reckless but unselfconscious. Possibly the Dream of Psyche had gone to his head. At any rate he had been on the very verge of proposing to Claire when the interruption had occurred, and in bed that night, reviewing the affair, he had been appalled at the narrowness of his escape from taking a definite step. Except in the way of business he was a man who hated definite steps. He never accepted even a dinner invitation without subsequent doubts and remorse. The consequence was that in the days that followed the Riegelheimer episode, what Lord Wetherby would have called the lamp of love burned rather low in Mr. Pickering, as if the acetylene were running out. He still admired Claire intensely and experienced disturbing emotions when he beheld her perfect tonneau and wonderful headlights; but he regarded her with a cautious fear. Although he sometimes dreamed sentimentally of marriage in the abstract, of actual marriage, of marriage with a flesh and blood individual, of marriage that involved clergymen and Voices that Breathe O’er Eden and giggling bridesmaids and cake, Dudley Pickering was afraid with a terror that woke him sweating in the night. His shyness shrank from the ceremony, his caution jibbed at the mysteries of married life. So his attitude toward Claire, the only girl who had succeeded in bewitching him into the opening words of an actual proposal, was a little less cordial and affectionate than if she had been a rival automobile manufacturer.

===Chinese===
在爱情的赛车场上，达德利·皮克林可不是自带起动器的车型：他得靠人摇柄来发动。他是婚配原料里最没指望的一等货色：一个性情谨慎的腼腆人。腼腆刚被克服，谨慎便踩下脚刹；谨慎刚被忘掉，腼腆又关死油门。在里格尔海默餐厅那一夜，不知哪路神迹显灵，竟弄得他不但鲁莽放胆，而且浑忘了自我。兴许是《普绪克之梦》（Dream of Psyche）冲昏了他的头脑。反正，打断发生的那一刻，他已站到了向克莱尔开口求婚的悬崖边上；当夜躺在床上把这事细细一回味，他惊出一身冷汗——自己方才离迈出那“明确一步”，只差了一根头发丝。除了生意场上，他是个恨透“明确一步”的人。哪怕应承一顿晚饭的邀请，事后也少不得疑虑丛生、追悔莫及。结果是，里格尔海默风波之后的那些天里，用韦瑟比伯爵（Lord Wetherby）的话说，皮克林先生心里那盏爱神的灯烧得相当低迷，仿佛电石气快耗尽了。他依旧热烈地爱慕着克莱尔，一端详她那无可挑剔的车厢后座和美妙出奇的车前灯，心头便涌起撩乱的情思；可他望她的目光里，总带着一层小心翼翼的畏怯。虽然他有时也伤感情地憧憬婚姻——抽象的婚姻、实在的婚姻、同一位有血有肉之人的婚姻、牵扯到牧师、《伊甸园上飘过的声音》（The Voice that Breathed O’er Eden）、咯咯发笑的伴娘和大蛋糕的婚姻——达德利·皮克林却怕得厉害，那种恐怖能在半夜把他吓醒，一身冷汗。那套仪式叫他的腼腆望而却步，婚后生活的重重玄机叫他的谨慎像马儿一样突然停蹄。于是，对待克莱尔——这位唯一成功迷得他吐出实打实求婚开头话的姑娘——他的态度竟不及对待一位竞争的汽车制造商来得亲切热络。

===Original===
Matters were in this state when Lady Wetherby, who having danced classical dances for three months without a break required a rest, shifted her camp to the house which she had rented for the summer at Brookport, Long Island, taking with her Algie, her husband, the monkey Eustace, and Claire and Mr. Pickering, her guests. The house was a large one, capable of receiving a big party, but she did not wish to entertain on an ambitious scale. The only other guest she proposed to put up was Roscoe Sherriff, her press agent, who was to come down as soon as he could get away from his metropolitan duties.

It was a pleasant and romantic place, the estate which Lady Wetherby had rented. Standing on a hill the house looked down through green trees on the gleaming waters of the bay. Smooth lawns and shady walks it had, and rustic seats beneath spreading cedars. Yet for all its effect on Dudley Pickering it might have been a gas works. He roamed the smooth lawns with Claire, and sat with her on the rustic benches and talked guardedly of lubricating oil. There were moments when Claire was almost impelled to forfeit whatever chance she might have had of becoming mistress of thirty million dollars and a nourishing business for the satisfaction of administering just one wholehearted slap on his round and thinly covered head.

===Chinese===
事情正僵在这个地步，韦瑟比夫人（Lady Wetherby）——她一连三个月不间断地跳古典舞，需要歇一口气了——把营盘挪到了她在长岛（Long Island）布鲁克波特（Brookport）租下避暑的那幢房子，随行带着她的丈夫阿尔吉（Algie）、猴子尤斯塔斯（Eustace），以及两位客人克莱尔和皮克林先生。房子很大，容得下满满一群宾客，可她并不想大张旗鼓地待客。她打算留宿的另一位客人只有罗斯科·谢里夫（Roscoe Sherriff），她的宣传经纪人——只等他在城里的差事一脱开身，便动身下来。

韦瑟比夫人租下的这处庄园，是个宜人而浪漫的所在。房子坐落山冈，透过葱茏的绿树，俯瞰着湾中粼粼的水光。这里有望不平整的草坪、成荫的小径，还有枝柯铺展的雪松底下粗木搭就的座椅。然而就它在达德利·皮克林身上起的作用而论，这儿简直可以是一座煤气厂。他同克莱尔在平滑的草坪上溜达，同她并肩坐在粗木长椅上，小心翼翼地谈着润滑油。有好些时候，克莱尔几乎按捺不住，宁肯舍弃做三千万美元的女主人、经营一份兴旺产业的一切机会，也想换取痛痛快快朝他那颗滚圆而头发稀薄的脑袋上，结结实实扇上一记耳光的快意。

===Original===
And then Roscoe Sherriff came down, and Dudley Pickering, who for days had been using all his resolution to struggle against the siren, suddenly found that there was no siren to struggle against. No sooner had the press agent appeared than Claire deserted him shamelessly and absolutely. She walked with Roscoe Sherriff. Mr. Pickering experienced the discomfiting emotions of the man who pushes violently against an abruptly yielding door, or treads heavily on the top stair where there is no top stair. He was shaken, and the clamlike stolidity which he had assumed as protection gave way.

He hated Roscoe Sherriff. It was unreasonable of him, seeing that the other had rescued him from the company of Claire; but it was one of the incongruities which make human nature the diverting thing it is, that a stout, middle-aged man, who does not wish to marry a beautiful girl himself, may seethe with jealous fury at the spectacle of this same beautiful girl reveling in the society of a young, slim man with hypnotic eyes and a cooing voice. Roscoe Sherriff had these advantages. A press agent has to have them in order to get free advertising past suspicious editors. Circumstances had molded Roscoe Sherriff into the livest press agent in New York, but Nature had intended him for the baritone hero of a musical comedy, one of those debonair young fellows who curvet down to the footlights in beautifully fitting trousers when the guests cry: “Why, here comes Jack himself! Hurrah!” At Lady Wetherby’s country house he was what is technically known as the life and the soul of the party, and Dudley Pickering hated him bitterly.

===Chinese===
跟着，罗斯科·谢里夫下乡来了；连日来强拴着心猿、竭尽全力抵抗那位海妖的达德利·皮克林，忽然发现压根儿就没有什么海妖可抵抗了。那位宣传经纪人前脚一露面，克莱尔后脚便无耻而彻底地把他抛下了。她同罗斯科·谢里夫散步去了。皮克林先生尝到了那种人的败兴滋味——那人猛力去推一扇门，门却蓦地开了个空；要不就是一脚重重踩在楼梯顶层那一蹬上，那里偏偏没有那一蹬。他震动了；他那副充作护身甲壳的、蛤蛎一般的木然，也随之土崩瓦解。

他恨罗斯科·谢里夫。这恨得没有道理，因为对方分明把他从克莱尔的陪伴里解救了出来；然而，人性之所以是件妙趣横生的东西，正在这类不合情理之处：一个自己并不想娶那位美丽姑娘的粗壮中年人，眼见这位美丽的姑娘同一个眼睛能勾魂、嗓音甜得咕咕叫的清瘦年轻人厮混得其乐陶陶，竟也会妒火中烧、翻江倒海。罗斯科·谢里夫正有这副好本钱。宣传经纪人不能没有这副本钱，不然休想让免费广告从疑心重重的编辑手里过关。环境把罗斯科·谢里夫锻造成了纽约最活络的宣传经纪人，可老天爷造他的时候，原本是打算让他做音乐喜剧里那位男中音主角的——就是那路风流倜傥的年轻人，客人一声喊“哎呀，杰克本人来啦！好哇！”，便穿着熨帖合身的漂亮裤子，腾跃着跑到脚灯跟前。在韦瑟比夫人的乡间宅子里，用行话说，他是那种所谓全场的灵魂与开心果，而达德利·皮克林恨他恨得切齿。

===Original===
Night had descended upon Brookport. Eustace, the monkey, was in his little bed; Lord Wetherby in the smoking-room. It was Sunday, the day of rest. Dinner was over, and the remainder of the party were gathered in the drawing room, with the exception of Mr. Pickering, who was smoking a cigar on the porch. A full moon turned Long Island into a fairyland.

Gloom had settled upon Dudley Pickering and he smoked sadly. All rather stout automobile manufacturers are sad when there is a full moon. It makes them feel lonely. It stirs their hearts to thoughts of love. Marriage loses its terrors for them, and they think wistfully of hooking some fair woman up the back and buying her hats. Such was the mood of Mr. Pickering, when through the dimness of the porch there appeared a white shape, moving softly toward them.

“Is that you, Mr. Pickering?”

Claire dropped into the seat beside him. From the drawing room came the soft tinkle of a piano. The sound blended harmoniously with the quiet peace of the night. Mr. Pickering let his cigar go out and clutched the sides of his chair.

===Chinese===
夜幕降下布鲁克波特。猴子尤斯塔斯睡进了他的小床；韦瑟比伯爵在吸烟室里。那天是星期日，安息的日子。晚饭已经吃过，聚会众人其余都聚在客厅里，唯有皮克林先生例外，他正在游廊上抽雪茄。一轮满月，把长岛变作了仙境。

阴郁罩定了达德利·皮克林，他闷闷地抽着雪茄。月圆时分，凡是体格稍稍发福的汽车制造商都要伤感。月亮叫他们觉得孤独。月亮搅得他们心头荡起爱的念头。婚姻于他们失了可怖，他们怅然神往，想着从背后钩住哪位美人儿，给她买帽子戴。皮克林先生正沉在这种心境里，忽见游廊的幽暗深处，一个白色的身影轻轻朝这边移了过来。

“是你吗，皮克林先生？”

克莱尔在他身旁的椅子上落了座。客厅里传来钢琴轻柔的叮咚。那琴声同夜的静谧安详交融无间。皮克林先生听任雪茄自生自灭地熄了，两只手死死攥住椅子两边。

===Original===
> “Oi’ll﻿—er﻿—sing thee saw-ongs ov Arrabee,
 

 Und﻿—ah ta-ales of farrr Cash-mee-eere,
 

 Wi-ild tales to che-eat thee ovasigh
 

 Und charrrrm thee to-oo a tear-er.”

===Chinese===
> “俺要——呃——唱那阿拉比——的歌谣哟，
 

 还——啊，那远方卡什米——米尔的故——事，
 

 野——性的故事，骗走你一声叹——息，
 

 再迷得你流——下一滴泪珠儿。”

===Original===
Claire gave a little sigh.

“What a beautiful voice Mr. Sherriff has!”

Dudley Pickering made no reply. He thought Roscoe Sherriff had a beastly voice. He resented Roscoe Sherriff’s voice. He objected to Roscoe Sherriff’s polluting this fair night with his cacophony.

===Chinese===
克莱尔轻轻叹了口气。

“谢里夫先生这嗓子多美啊！”

达德利·皮克林没有答话。他认定罗斯科·谢里夫的嗓子可憎透顶。他对罗斯科·谢里夫那副嗓子满腔怨愤。他不能容许罗斯科·谢里夫用那一路刺耳的噪聒，来糟蹋这样一个良夜。

===Original===
“Don’t you think so, Mr. Pickering?”

“Uh-huh.”

“That doesn’t sound very enthusiastic. Mr. Pickering, I want you to tell me something. Have I done anything to offend you?”

Mr. Pickering started violently.

“Eh?”

“I have seen so little of you these last few days. A little while ago we were always together, having such interesting talks. But lately it has seemed to me that you have been avoiding me.”

===Chinese===
“你不这么想吗，皮克林先生？”

“嗯哼。”

“听着可不大热乎呢。皮克林先生，我要你对我说句实话。我是不是做了什么，把你得罪了？”

皮克林先生猛地一惊。

“啊？”

“这几天我难得见着你几面。不久以前，我们还总在一处，谈天谈得那么有趣。可近来我总觉得，你一直在躲着我。”

===Original===
A feeling of helplessness swept over Mr. Pickering. He was vaguely conscious of a sense of being treated unjustly, of there being a flaw in Claire’s words somewhere if he could only find it, but the sudden attack had deprived him of the free and unfettered use of his powers of reasoning. He gurgled wordlessly, and Claire went on, her low, sad voice mingling with the moonlight in a manner that caused thrills to run up and down his spine. He felt paralyzed. Caution urged him to make some excuse and follow it with a bolt to the drawing room, but he was physically incapable of taking the excellent advice. Sometimes when you are out in your Pickering Gem or your Pickering Giant the car hesitates, falters and stops dead, and your chauffeur, having examined the carburetor, turns to you and explains the phenomenon in these words: “The mixture is too rich.” So was it with Mr. Pickering now. The moonlight alone might not have held him; Claire’s voice alone might not have held him; but against the two combined he was powerless. The mixture was too rich. He sat and breathed a little stertorously, and there came to him that conviction that comes to all of us now and then, that we are at a crisis of our careers and that the moment through which we are living is a moment big with fate.

===Chinese===
一阵手足无措的感觉漫过了皮克林先生全身。他隐隐觉得自己受了委屈，隐隐觉得克莱尔的话里总有哪儿不对，只消他能找出来就好；可这猝然的进攻，已叫他那套推理的本事失了自由、使唤不灵了。他喉咙里咯咯作响，一个字也吐不出。克莱尔继续说下去，她那低回忧伤的嗓音同月光交融在一处，激得他脊梁骨上上下下窜过一阵阵战栗。他只觉浑身瘫软。谨慎催他寻个托词，随即一溜烟奔回客厅；可他体力上已无力采纳这条绝妙好计。有时候，你驾着“皮克林珍宝”（Pickering Gem）或“皮克林巨人”（Pickering Giant）出门，车子迟疑、踉跄，猛地瘫住不动；你的司机检查过汽化器，回身向你解释这一现象，用的正是这句话：“混合气太浓了。”皮克林先生此刻正是如此。单是月光，未必拴得住他；单是克莱尔的嗓音，未必拴得住他；可两样合在一处，他便全无还手之力了。混合气太浓了。他坐在那儿，呼吸略略粗重；一种信念浮上心头——那种我们人人偶尔都会浮起的信念：自己正立在生涯的关头，眼下活着的这一刻，是满怀命运的一刻。

===Original===
The voice in the drawing room stopped. Having sung songs of Araby and tales of far Cashmere, Mr. Roscoe Sherriff was refreshing himself with the colored comic supplement of the Sunday paper. But Lady Wetherby, seated at the piano, still touched the keys softly, and the sound increased the richness of the mixture which choked Dudley Pickering’s spiritual carburetor. It is not fair that a rather stout manufacturer of automobiles should be called upon to sit in the moonlight while a beautiful girl, to the accompaniment of soft music, reproaches him with having avoided her.

===Chinese===
客厅里的歌声停了。唱罢了阿拉比之歌和远方卡什米尔的故事，罗斯科·谢里夫先生正拿星期日报纸的彩色连环画副刊解闷。可是坐在钢琴前的韦瑟比夫人，仍旧轻轻地按着琴键；那琴声愈发加浓了那种混合气，把达德利·皮克林精神上的汽化器呛住了。一位体格略胖的汽车制造商，竟被按在月光底下，听一位美丽的姑娘在轻柔乐声的伴奏里数落他躲着她——这实在有欠公道。

===Original===
“I should be so sorry, Mr. Pickering, if I had done anything to make a difference between us﻿—”

“Guk!” said Mr. Pickering.

“I have so few real friends over here.”

“Guk!”

Claire’s voice trembled.

“I﻿—I get a little lonely, a little homesick sometimes﻿—”

===Chinese===
“皮克林先生，我若是做了什么，叫你我之间生出了嫌隙，那我会难过死了——”

“咕克！”皮克林先生说。

“我在这边真正的朋友太少了。”

“咕克！”

克莱尔的嗓音颤抖了。

“我——我有时候觉得有点儿孤单，有点儿想家——”

===Original===
She paused, musing, and a spasm of pity rent the bosom beneath Dudley Pickering’s ample shirt. Claire suddenly became to him a figure of pathos to be compared with Ruth:

===Chinese===
她若有所思地顿住了；一股怜惜的痉挛，绞过达德利·皮克林宽大衬衫底下的心口。克莱尔霎时在他眼里成了一个哀婉动人的形象，堪与路得（Ruth）相比：

===Original===
> When sick for home
 

 She stood in tears amid the alien corn.

===Chinese===
> 当思乡的病痛袭来，
 

 她含着泪，伫立在异乡的谷田间。

===Original===
There was a buzzing in his ears and a lump choked his throat.

“Of course I am loving the life here. I think America’s wonderful, and nobody could be kinder than Lady Wetherby. But﻿—I miss my home. It’s the first time I have been away for so long. I feel very far away sometimes. There are only three of us at home, my mother, myself, and my little brother﻿—little Percy.”

Her voice trembled again as she spoke the last two words, and it was possibly this that caused Mr. Pickering to visualize Percy as a sort of Little Lord Fauntleroy, his favorite character in English literature. He had a vision of a small, delicate, wistful child pining away for his absent sister. Consumptive probably. Or curvature of the spine.

===Chinese===
他耳朵里嗡嗡作响，喉咙口哽着一团东西。

“当然，这儿的日子我是喜欢的。我觉得美国好极了，韦瑟比夫人的厚道更没得比。可是——我想家。这是我头一回离家这么久。有时候我只觉得离家远得很。家里一共就我们三个人：我母亲，我，还有我弟弟——小珀西。”

说到末了两个字，她的嗓音又颤了一颤；多半正是这一颤，叫皮克林先生把珀西想成了小勋爵方特罗伊（Little Lord Fauntleroy）一流的人物——他心爱的英国文学角色。他眼前浮出一个娇小孱弱、惹人怜思的孩子，正为姐姐不在身边而形容憔悴。多半还害着痨病。要不然就是脊柱弯曲。

===Original===
