He found Claire’s hand in his. He supposed dully he must have reached out for it. Soft and warm it lay there, while the universe paused breathlessly. And then from the semidarkness beside him there came the sound of a stifled sob, and his fingers closed as if someone had touched a button.

“Guk!” he said softly.

“We have always been such chums. He is only ten﻿—such a dear boy. He must be missing me﻿—”

She stopped, and simultaneously Dudley Pickering began to speak.

===Chinese===
他发觉克莱尔的手已在自己的手里。他迟钝地寻思，想必是自己伸手握住的。那只手温软地躺在那里，天宇万物都屏息凝定了。接着，他身旁的半明半暗里传来一声强忍的啜泣，他的手指应声扣拢，仿佛有人按了一下电钮。

“咕克！”他轻轻说。

“我们向来是要好的伙伴。他才十岁——那么个可爱的孩子。他一定在惦记我——”

她住了口，与此同时，达德利·皮克林开口说话了。

===Original===
There is this to be said for your shy, cautious man, that on the rare occasions when he does tap the vein of eloquence that vein becomes a geyser. For several minutes Dudley Pickering spouted verbiage like an Old Faithful. It was as if after years of silence and monosyllables he was endeavoring to restore the average.

He began by touching on his alleged neglect and avoidance of Claire. He called himself names and more names. He plumbed the depths of repentance and remorse. Proceeding from this, he eulogized her courage, the pluck with which she presented a smiling face to the world while tortured inwardly by separation from her little brother Percy. He then turned to his own feelings.

But there are some things which the historian should hold sacred, some things which he should look on as proscribed material for his pen, and the actual words of a stout manufacturer of automobiles, proposing marriage in the moonlight, fall into this class. It is enough to say that Dudley Pickering was definite. He left no room for doubt as to his meaning.

===Chinese===
你那号腼腆而谨慎的人，有一桩好处可说：难得几回他真凿开了雄辩的矿脉，那矿脉立刻便成一眼间歇喷泉。一连好几分钟，达德利·皮克林活像一座“老忠实泉”（Old Faithful），喷吐出滔滔不绝的言语。仿佛在多年的沉默与单音节度日之后，他正努力把平均值补算回来。

他起头先检讨自己那桩所谓的冷落克莱尔、躲着克莱尔之罪。他给自己扣了一顶又一顶骂名。他把悔恨与自责的深井探到了底。由此进而，他颂扬起她的勇气——她内心受着同小弟弟珀西分离的煎熬，人前却始终拿笑脸迎人，好一股胆气。随后，他转到了自己的感情。

然而有些事情，史家应当奉为神圣；有些材料，他应当看作笔下禁地——一位体格发福的汽车制造商在月光里求婚时所说的原话，便属于这一类。只消说一句就够了：达德利·皮克林这回说得斩钉截铁。他的意思不容人有半点疑义。

===Original===
“Dudley!”

She was in his arms. He was embracing her. She was his﻿—the latest model, self-starting, with limousine body and all the newest. No, no, his mind was wandering. She was his, this divine girl, this queen among women, this﻿—

From the drawing room Roscoe Sherriff’s voice floated out in unconscious comment:

===Chinese===
“达德利！”

她已在他怀里。他正拥着她。她是他的了——最新型号，自起动，豪华轿车车身，样样配齐。不，不对，他的心思跑野了。她是他的，这位天仙般的姑娘，这位女中魁首，这位——

客厅里飘出罗斯科·谢里夫的歌声，无意之间作了一句评注：

===Original===
> “Goodbye, boys!
 

 I’m going to be married tomorrow.
 

 Goodbye, boys!
 

 I’m going from sunshine to sorrow.
 

 No more sitting up till broad daylight.”

===Chinese===
> “再见吧，伙伴们！
 

 明儿我就要结婚了。
 

 再见吧，伙伴们！
 

 我要从阳光走进忧愁。
 

 再不能熬夜坐到天大亮。”

===Original===
Did a momentary chill cool the intensity of Dudley Pickering’s ardor? If so he overcame it instantly. He despised Roscoe Sherriff. He flattered himself that he had shown Roscoe Sherriff pretty well who was who and what was what.

They would have a wonderful wedding﻿—dozens of clergymen, scores of organs playing “The Voice That Breathed O’er Eden,” platoons of bridesmaids, wagonloads of cake. And then they would go back to Detroit and live happy ever after. And it might be that in time to come there would be given to them little runabouts.

===Chinese===
一瞬间的凉意，可曾冷却了达德利·皮克林炽热的痴情？即便冷却了，他顷刻便克服过去。他鄙薄罗斯科·谢里夫。他暗自得意，认为自己已经让罗斯科·谢里夫好好见识了谁是谁、什么是什么。

他们要办一场盛大的婚礼——几十位牧师，几十架风琴齐奏《伊甸园上飘过的声音》，一排排的伴娘，一车又一车的蛋糕。然后他们回底特律（Detroit）去，从此幸福度日。往后天长日久，说不定上天还会赐给他们几辆小小的单排座敞车呢。

===Original===
> “I’m going to a life
 

 Of misery and strife,
 

 So goodbye, boys!”

===Chinese===
> “我要去过一种日子
 

 满是苦难和纷争，
 

 所以再见吧，伙伴们！”

===Original===
Hang Roscoe Sherriff! What did he know about it, confound him! Dudley Pickering turned a deaf ear to the song and wallowed in his happiness.

Claire walked slowly down the moonlit drive. She had removed herself from her Dudley’s embraces, for she wished to be alone, to think. The engagement had been announced. All that part of it was over﻿—Dudley’s stammering speech, the unrestrained delight of Polly Wetherby, the facetious rendering of “The Wedding Glide” on the piano by Roscoe Sherriff, and it now remained for her to try to discover a way of conveying the news to Bill. It had just struck her that, though she knew that Bill was in America, she had not his address.

What was she to do? She must tell him. Otherwise it might quite easily happen that they might meet in New York when she returned there. She pictured the scene. She saw herself walking with Dudley Pickering. Along came Bill. Claire, darling!﻿ ﻿… Heavens, what would Dudley think? It would be too awful! She couldn’t explain. No, somehow or other, even if she put detectives on his trail, she must find him, and be off with the old love now that she was on with the new.

She reached the gate and leaned over it. And as she did so someone in the shadow of a tall tree spoke her name. A man came into the light and she saw that it was Lord Dawlish.

===Chinese===
该死的罗斯科·谢里夫！他懂得什么，叫他见鬼去！达德利·皮克林对那歌声充耳不闻，一头扎进自己的幸福里打滚。

克莱尔缓缓走下月色下的林荫车道。她已从她那位达德利的怀抱里抽出身来，因为她想独自待一会儿，理一理思绪。婚约已经宣布。那一整段都过去了——达德利结结巴巴的演说，波莉·韦瑟比（Polly Wetherby）毫无节制的欢喜，罗斯科·谢里夫在钢琴上嬉皮笑脸弹出的《婚礼滑步曲》（The Wedding Glide）；如今剩下的，是她得设法想出一条把消息传给比尔（Bill）的路子。她这才恍然记起：她虽知道比尔人在美国，手里却没有他的地址。

她该怎么办呢？她必须告诉他。不然的话，等她回纽约，两人轻而易举就会撞上。她在心里排演那一幕。她看见自己同达德利·皮克林散着步，比尔迎面走来。“克莱尔，亲爱的！……”天哪，达德利会怎么想？那可糟透了！她没法解释。不行，无论如何，哪怕雇侦探去钉他的梢，她也得把他找出来——既然新欢已经上了手，旧爱就得赶忙脱手。

她走到大门口，探身伏在门上。就在这时，一棵高树的暗影里，有个人唤了一声她的名字。一个男人走进月光里，她看出，那是达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）。

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===

Lord Dawlish had gone for a moonlight walk that night because, like Claire, he wished to be alone to think. He had fallen with a pleasant ease and smoothness into the rather curious life lived at Elizabeth Boyd’s bee farm. A liking for picnics had lingered in him from boyhood, and existence at Flack’s was one prolonged picnic. He found that he had a natural aptitude for the more muscular domestic duties, and his energy in this direction enchanted Nutty, who before his advent had had a monopoly of these tasks.

Nor was this the only aspect of the situation that pleased Nutty. When he had invited Bill to the farm he had had a vague hope that good might come of it, but he had never dreamed that things would turn out as well as they promised to do, or that such a warm and immediate friendship would spring up between his sister and the man who had diverted the family fortune into his own pocket. Bill and Elizabeth were getting on splendidly. They were together all the time﻿—walking, golfing, attending to the numerous needs of the bees or sitting on the porch. Nutty’s imagination began to run away with him. He seemed to smell the scent of orange blossoms, to hear the joyous pealing of church bells﻿—in fact, with the difference that it was not his own wedding that he was anticipating, he had begun to take very much the same view of the future that was about to come to Dudley Pickering.

Elizabeth would have been startled and embarrassed if she could have read his thoughts, for they might have suggested to her that she was becoming a great deal fonder of Bill than the shortness of their acquaintance warranted. But though she did not fail to observe the strangeness of her brother’s manner, she traced it to another source than the real one. She looked on it as a manifestation of disordered nerves. Nutty had a habit of starting back and removing himself when, entering the porch, he perceived that Bill and his sister were already seated there. His own impression on such occasions was that he was behaving with consummate tact. Elizabeth supposed that he had had some sort of a spasm.

===Chinese===

那天夜里，达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）出门去月下漫步，因为他同克莱尔（Claire）一样，也想独自清静清静，理一理心思。伊丽莎白·博伊德（Elizabeth Boyd）养蜂场里那颇为奇特的生活，他已经安适而顺畅地融入其中。对野餐的爱好，他打童年起便存留未泯，而弗拉克家（Flack’s）的日子，正是一场绵延不绝的野餐。他发现自己天生擅长操持家里那些顶费筋力的家务活，他这方面的干劲叫纳蒂（Nutty）喜不自胜——在这位驾临之前，这些活计向来是纳蒂独揽的。

叫纳蒂称心的还不止局面这一层。当初他请比尔（Bill）来农场，心里就朦朦胧胧地盼着能开出点好结果来，只是做梦也不曾想到，事情会像眼下的苗头预示的这样圆满，更不曾想到，妹妹同那个把一家人的家财拐进了自己腰包的人之间，会这么快地生出一段火热的交情。比尔和伊丽莎白处得好极了。两人时时刻刻待在一处——散步，打高尔夫，照料蜜蜂的诸般需求，或是坐在门廊里。纳蒂的想象力开始脱缰狂奔。他仿佛嗅到了橙花的芬芳，听见教堂钟声欢快地齐鸣——实话说，除去他预期的婚礼并非自己的这一节差别，他对那即将到来的前程所抱的展望，已经同达德利·皮克林（Dudley Pickering）一般无二。

伊丽莎白倘若能读到纳蒂的心思，准会又惊又窘，因为那些心思分明在向她提示：她对比尔的好感之深，已经超出了相识未久所应有的分寸。不过，哥哥举止的古怪她虽看在眼里，追索的缘由却不是真正的那一个。她把这看作神经失和的症候。纳蒂有个毛病：一走进门廊，发觉比尔和他妹妹已经坐在那里，他便往后一缩，抽身退走。每逢这种场合，他自己觉得自己的做派圆熟得体到了极点。伊丽莎白则猜想，他准是哪里的筋抽了一回。

===Original===

Lord Dawlish, if he had been able to diagnose correctly the almost paternal attitude which had become his host’s normal manner these days, would have been equally embarrassed but less startled, for conscience had already suggested to him from time to time that he had been guilty of a feeling toward Elizabeth warmer than any feeling that should come to an engaged man. Lying in bed at the end of his first week at the farm he reviewed the progress of his friendship with her, and was amazed at the rapidity with which it had grown.

He could not conceal it from himself﻿—Elizabeth appealed to him. Being built on a large scale himself, he had always been attracted by small women. There was a smallness, a daintiness, a liveliness about Elizabeth that was almost irresistible. She was so capable, so cheerful in spite of the fact that she was having a hard time. And then their minds seemed to blend so remarkably. There were no odd corners to be smoothed away. Never in his life had he felt so supremely at his ease with one of the opposite sex. He loved Claire﻿—he drove that fact home almost angrily to himself﻿—but he was forced to admit that he had always been aware of something in the nature of a barrier between them. Claire was querulous at times, and always a little too apt to take offense. He had never been able to talk to her with that easy freedom that Elizabeth invited. Talking to Elizabeth was like talking to an attractive version of oneself. It was a thing to be done with perfect confidence, without any of that apprehension which Claire inspired lest the next remark might prove the spark to cause an explosion. But Claire was the girl he loved, there must be no mistake about that.

He came to the conclusion that the key to the situation was the fact that Elizabeth was American. He had read so much of the American girl, her unaffectedness, her genius for easy comradeship. Well, this must be what the writer fellows meant. He had happened upon one of those delightful friendships without any suspicion of sex in them, of which the American girl had the monopoly. Yes, that must be it. It was a comforting explanation. It accounted for his feeling at a loose end whenever he was away from Elizabeth for as much as half an hour. It accounted for the fact that they understood each other so well. It accounted for everything so satisfactorily that he was able to get to sleep that night after all.

===Chinese===

至于达利什勋爵，倘若他能准确诊断出东家这些时日已成本色的那副近乎老父一般的姿态，他也会同样窘迫，只是不至于那么吃惊，因为良心早已不时向他递话：他对伊丽莎白怀有的那种情愫，已经热得逾越了一个订了婚的人所应有的分寸。在农场住满头一个星期的那晚，他躺在床上，把这段友谊的进展细细盘点了一遍，只惊诧它长势之猛。

他瞒不过自己——伊丽莎白对他有吸引力。他本人身躯高大，一向为娇小的女子所倾心。伊丽莎白身上有一种娇小、精致、活泼的味道，几乎令人无从抵御。她那么能干，日子过得那么艰难，人却还是那么兴致勃勃。再者，两人的心灵又那么出奇地投合，没有一处需要磨平的怪角死角。他平生还不曾同哪位异性相处得这般自在到十分。他爱克莱尔——他几乎带着恼怒，把这个事实往自己心上钉——可他不得不承认，他与克莱尔之间向来隔着一层近似屏障的东西。克莱尔时不时爱发牢骚，又总是略嫌太容易动气。他同她讲话，从来达不到伊丽莎白招引人那样的自在随便。同伊丽莎白谈话，就像同一个更讨人喜欢的自己谈话，尽可放胆而言，绝无克莱尔带来的那份提防——生怕下一句话就是引爆的火星。可是克莱尔才是他爱的姑娘，这一点决计含糊不得。

他得出的结论是：解开这局面的钥匙，在于伊丽莎白是美国人。关于美国姑娘，他从前读过的文字着实不少：她们的不加矫饰，她们那种亲若同袍、易于相与的天才。好，写书的那些家伙指的准是这个。他是撞上了那种令人愉快、全无一丝男女之嫌的友谊——这类友谊，向来是美国姑娘的专利品。对了，准是这个。这个解释叫人宽心。它说明了他为什么一离开伊丽莎白半个钟头，便觉得空落落的没了着落；说明了两个人为什么这样相知。它把一切都解释得那么圆满，那一晚他终究还是睡着了。

===Original===

But next morning﻿—for his conscience was one of those persistent consciences﻿—he began to have doubts again. Nothing clings like a suspicion in the mind of a conscientious young man that he has been allowing his heart to stray from its proper anchorage. Could it be that he was behaving badly toward Claire? The thought was unpleasant, but he could not get rid of it. He extracted Claire’s photograph from his suitcase and gazed solemnly upon it.

At first he was shocked to find that it only succeeded in convincing him that Elizabeth was quite the most attractive girl he ever had met. The photographer had given Claire rather a severe look. He had told her to moisten the lips with the tip of the tongue and assume a pleasant smile, with the result that she seemed to glare. She had a rather markedly agressive look, queenly perhaps, but not very comfortable.

But there is no species of self-hypnotism equal to that of a man who gazes persistently at a photograph with the preconceived idea that he is in love with the original of it. Little by little Bill found that the old feeling began to return. He persevered. By the end of a quarter of an hour he had almost succeeded in capturing anew that first, fine, careless rapture which, six months ago, had caused him to propose to Claire and walk on air when she accepted him.

===Chinese===

可是第二天早上——他的良心属于那种缠磨不休的良心——疑心又抬头了。什么事也不及这样一种疑心缠人：一个良知谨严的年轻人，疑心自己听任那颗心偏离了正当的锚地。莫非他待克莱尔的行为不厚道？这念头叫人不好受，却怎么也甩不脱。他从手提箱里取出克莱尔的照片，神情肃穆地对着它凝视起来。

起初他大为震动，因为凝视的结果只是叫他愈发确信：伊丽莎白才真是他平生所见的姑娘里顶有吸引力的一个。照相师把克莱尔照得颇为严厉。他嘱咐她用舌尖润润嘴唇，摆出一副愉快的笑容，结果她看上去倒像在瞪眼怒视。她那副神气颇带几分咄咄逼人，或许不乏女王气派，却叫人瞧着不大舒坦。

然而，世间种种自我催眠之术，没有一样比得上这一种：一个男子先存定了爱着照片原主的心思，再一双眼睛死死盯住照片不放。一点一点地，比尔发觉旧情开始回潮。他锲而不舍。到一刻钟将尽，他差不多已经重新捉住了那最初的、洒脱的、无忧无虑的狂喜——六个月前，正是这股狂喜促使他向克莱尔求婚，又在她应允之后叫他脚下踩着云朵走路。

===Original===

He continued the treatment throughout the day, and by dinner time had arranged everything with his conscience in the most satisfactory manner possible. He loved Claire with a passionate fervor; he liked Elizabeth very much indeed. He submitted this diagnosis to conscience, and conscience graciously approved and accepted it.

It was Sunday that day. That helped. There is nothing like Sunday in a foreign country for helping a man to sentimental thoughts of the girl he has left behind him elsewhere. And the fact that there was a full moon clinched it. Bill was enabled to go for an after-dinner stroll in a condition of almost painful loyalty to Claire.

From time to time, as he walked along the road, he took out the photograph and did some more gazing. The last occasion on which he did this was just as he emerged from the shadow of a large tree that stood by the roadside, and a gush of rich emotion rewarded him.

===Chinese===

这套疗法他一整天没有间断，到开晚饭的时分，已经同良心把一切安排停当，圆满得无可复加。他爱克莱尔，爱得热烈如火；他喜欢伊丽莎白，喜欢得真心实意。他把这份诊断呈交良心，良心惠然核准，照单全收。

那天恰是星期天。这一层也帮了忙。要引动一个男子去柔情怀想他留在别处的姑娘，异国的星期天天下第一。何况当晚月轮正满，事情就此一锤定音。比尔于是能够带着一种近乎苦楚的忠诚，饭后出门散步去了。

他沿着大路走，不时掏出照片来，再加一番凝视。最后一回这么办的当口，他刚从路旁一棵大树的浓荫里走出来，一股丰沛的深情滚滚而来，聊充犒赏。

===Original===

“Claire!” he murmured.

An exclamation at his elbow caused him to look up. There, leaning over a gate, the light of the moon falling on her beautiful face, stood Claire herself.

===Chinese===

“克莱尔！”他喃喃唤道。

胳膊肘边一声惊呼，叫他抬起了眼睛。只见克莱尔本人就立在那里，倚着一扇栅门，月光正洒在她美丽的脸上。

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===

In trying interviews, as in sprint races, the start is everything. It was the fact that she recovered more quickly from her astonishment that enabled Claire to dominate her scene with Bill. She had the advantage of having a less complicated astonishment to recover from, for, though it was a shock to see him there when she had imagined that he was in New York, it was not nearly such a shock as it was to him to see her there when he had imagined that she was in England. She had adjusted her brain to the situation while he was still gaping.

“Well, Bill?”

This speech in itself should have been enough to warn Lord Dawlish of impending doom. As far as love, affection and tenderness are concerned, a girl might just as well hit a man with an ax as say “Well, Bill?” to him when they have met unexpectedly in the moonlight after long separation. But Lord Dawlish was too shattered by surprise to be capable of observing nuances. If his love had ever waned or faltered, as conscience had suggested earlier in the day, it was at full blast now.

“Claire!” he cried.

He was moving to take her in his arms, but she drew back.

===Chinese===

棘手的谈话同短跑竞赛一样，起跑便是一切。克莱尔之所以能在同比尔这场交锋中稳占上风，靠的就是她从惊愕中恢复得更快。她占的便宜是，她要从中恢复的那份惊愕本就不那么复杂：虽说在这里撞见他——她原以为他人在纽约——也是一惊，可这一惊远不及他的那一惊厉害；他原以为她远在英国呢。他还在张口结舌，她的大脑已经把局面调适停当了。

“怎么，比尔？”

单是这句话，本身就该足以提醒达利什勋爵大祸临头了。论爱情、柔情与温存，久别之后在月光下不期重逢，姑娘对男人说一声“怎么，比尔？”，同抡起斧子劈他一下，功效不相上下。可达利什勋爵被惊愕震得晕头转向，顾不上体察这些微妙之处。他的爱情若有过半点消减或动摇——正如当天早些时候他的良心暗示过的那样——此刻也已火力全开。

“克莱尔！”他喊道。

他正要上前把她搂进怀里，她却退了开去。

===Original===

“No, really, Bill!” she said; and this time it did filter through into his disordered mind that all was not well. A man who is a good deal dazed at the moment may fail to appreciate a remark like “Well, Bill?” but for a girl to draw back and say, “No, really, Bill!” in a tone not exactly of loathing, but certainly of pained aversion, is a deliberately unfriendly act. The three short words, taken in conjunction with the movement, brought him up with as sharp a turn as if she had punched him in the eye.

“Claire! What’s the matter?”

She looked at him steadily. She looked a at him with a sort of queenly woodenness, as if he were behind a camera with a velvet bag over his head and had just told her to moisten the lips with the tip of the tongue. Her aspect staggered Lord Dawlish. A cursory inspection of his conscience showed nothing but purity and whiteness, but he must have done something or she would not be staring at him like this.

===Chinese===

“不，说真的，比尔！”她说；这一回，话总算渗进了他乱作一团的心里：事情不妙。一个头晕眼花的人，兴许听不出“怎么，比尔？”这样的话里的名堂；可一个姑娘倒退一步，说一声“不，说真的，比尔！”——语气说厌恶还不至于，却分明带着痛苦的嫌弃——这便是有意示敌的举动了。这三个短促的字眼，连同那个动作，猛地把他刹住，仿佛一拳捣在了他眼窝上。

“克莱尔！出什么事了？”

她目不转睛地望着他。她望着他的那副神情，带着一种女王般的木然，仿佛他是照相馆里把脑袋蒙在黑丝绒袋里、藏在照相机后头的摄影师，刚吩咐她用舌尖润一润嘴唇。她这副模样把达利什勋爵镇住了。他的良心粗粗验看一遍，只见纯白洁净，别无他物；可他必定做了什么亏心事，不然她不会这样瞪着他。

===Original===

“I don’t understand!” was the only remark that occurred to him.

“Are you sure?”

“What do you mean?”

“I was at Riegelheimer’s Restaurant﻿—Ah!”

===Chinese===

“我不明白！”——他脑子里想得出的只有这一句。

“你敢肯定？”

“你什么意思？”

“我当时在里格尔海默餐厅——啊！”

===Original===

The sudden start which Lord Dawlish had given at the opening words of her sentence justified the concluding word. Innocent as his behavior had been that night at Riegelheimer’s, he had been glad at the time that he had not been observed. It now appeared that he had been observed, and it seemed to him that Long Island suddenly flung itself into a whirling dance. He heard Claire speaking a long way off.

“I was there with Lady Wetherby. It was she who invited me to come to America. I went to the restaurant to see her dance﻿—and I saw you!”

With a supreme effort Bill succeeded in calming down the excited landscape. He willed the trees to stop dancing, and they came reluctantly to a standstill. The world ceased to swim and flicker.

===Chinese===

她话音才起，达利什勋爵浑身便猛地一跳；句尾那个“啊”字，有这一跳作注脚足矣。那天夜里他在里格尔海默餐厅行止清白，当时却暗自庆幸没人瞧见。如今看来，终究叫人瞧见了；刹那间，他觉得整座长岛猛地卷进了一场天旋地转的舞蹈。克莱尔的声音从很远的地方传来。

“我是陪韦瑟比夫人去的。是她请我来美国的。我上那家餐厅是去看她跳舞——结果看见了你！”

比尔拼出超人的气力，总算把激动不安的景物安抚了下来。他以意念喝令树木停止跳舞，树木老大不情愿地站定了。世界不再游移明灭。

===Original===

“Let me explain,” he said.

The moment he had said the words he wished he could recall them. Their substance was right enough, it was the sound of them that was wrong. They sounded like a line from a farce, where the erring husband has been caught by the masterful wife. They were ridiculous. Worse than being merely ridiculous, they created an atmosphere of guilt and evasion.

“Explain! How can you explain? It is impossible to explain. I saw you with my own eyes making an exhibition of yourself with a horrible creature in salmon-pink. I’m not asking you who she is. I’m not questioning you about your relations with her at all. I don’t care who she was. The mere fact that you were at a public restaurant with a person of that kind is enough. No doubt you think I am making a great deal of fuss about a very ordinary thing. You consider that it is a man’s privilege to do these things, if he can do them without being found out. But it ended everything as far as I am concerned. Am I unreasonable? I don’t think so. You steal off to America, thinking I am in England, and behave like this. How could you do that if you really loved me? It’s the deceit of it that hurts me.”

===Chinese===

“让我解释，”他说。

话一出口，他就恨不得收回。话的实质原没有什么不妥，不妥的是这话的腔调。它听上去活像闹剧里的一句台词——出轨的丈夫叫厉害的太太当场拿住。它可笑。比可笑更糟的是，它造出了一股心虚躲闪的气氛。

“解释！你怎么解释？根本没法解释。我亲眼看见你，同一个穿鲑鱼粉色衣裳的可怕家伙当众丢人现眼。我不问你她是谁。你们的关系如何，我也压根不问。我才不在乎她是谁。你同那样一种货色的人出现在公共餐厅，单凭这一条就够了。不用猜，你准觉得我在小题大做，把一桩再平常不过的事闹得沸反盈天。在你看来，只要干得不被发觉，这类事就是男人的特权。可对我而言，一切都完了。我蛮不讲理吗？我不觉得。你偷偷溜到美国来，以为我在英国，就干出这种事。你若是真心爱我，怎么做得出来？伤我的心的是这份欺骗。”

===Original===

Lord Dawlish drew in a few breaths of pure Long Island air, but he did not speak. He felt helpless. If he were to be allowed to withdraw into the privacy of the study and wrap a cold, wet towel about his forehead and buckle down to it, he knew that he could draft an excellent and satisfactory explanation of his presence at Riegelheimer’s with the Good Sport. But to do it on the spur of the moment like this was beyond him.

Claire was speaking again. She had paused for a while after her recent speech, in order to think of something else to say; and during this pause had come to her mind certain excerpts from one of those admirable articles on love, by Luella Delia Philpotts, which do so much to boost the reading public of these United States into the higher planes. She had read it that afternoon in the Sunday paper, and it came back to her now.

“I may be hypersensitive,” she said, dropping her voice from the accusatory register to the lower tones of pathos, “but I have such high ideals of love. There can be no true love where there is not perfect trust. Trust is to love what﻿—”

===Chinese===

达利什勋爵吸了几口长岛纯净的空气，但没有作声。他一筹莫展。他心里明白，若容他退进书房的清静里，拿一条冷湿毛巾裹住额头，专心致志地干起来，他准能起草一份出色而周全的解释，说明他何以会同爽快姑娘出现在里格尔海默餐厅。可要他这样临阵即席交卷，就万万办不到了。

克莱尔又开口了。方才那通话之后，她停了一会儿，好想些新的说辞；就在这段停顿里，几段文字浮上了她的心头——那是露埃拉·迪莉娅·菲尔波茨（Luella Delia Philpotts）所著的那些妙不可言的爱情文章里的段落，正是这类文章，把合众国的读书公众一程一程托举到更高的境界上去。文章是那天下午她在星期天报纸上读到的，此刻历历回到了眼前。

“我也许过于敏感，”她说，嗓音从控诉的音区降下来，换成了凄婉的低音，“可是我对爱情抱有崇高的理想。没有全然的信任，就谈不上真正的爱情。信任之于爱情，恰如——”

===Original===

She paused again. She could not remember just what Luella Delia Philpotts had said trust was to love. It was something extremely neat and true, but it had slipped her memory.

“A woman has the right to expect the man she is about to marry to regard their troth as a sacred obligation that shall keep him as pure as a young knight who has dedicated himself to the quest of the Holy Grail. And I find you in a public restaurant, dancing with a creature with yellow hair, upsetting waiters, and staggering about with pats of butter all over you.”

Here a sense of injustice stung Lord Dawlish. It was true that after his regrettable collision with Heinrich, the waiter, he had discovered butter upon his person, but it was only one pat. Claire had spoken as if he had been festooned with butter.

===Chinese===

她又停住了。露埃拉·迪莉娅·菲尔波茨究竟把信任比作爱情的什么，她想不起来了。那原是个极精当、极透辟的比方，只是打她的记忆里滑脱了。

“女人有权指望她即将下嫁的男人，把两人的婚誓奉为神圣的义务，使他保持纯洁，纯得像一位献身寻访圣杯（Holy Grail）的少年骑士。可我看到的，却是你出现在公共餐厅里，同一个黄头发的家伙跳舞，撞翻侍者，浑身糊满一块块黄油，东倒西歪。”

听到这里，一股冤屈之感刺痛了达利什勋爵。不错，同那位侍者海因里希一场令人遗憾的碰撞之后，他确曾发现自己身上沾着黄油，可只有一块。听克莱尔的口气，倒好像他周身缠满了黄油扎成的花彩。

===Original===

“I am not angry with you, only disappointed. What has happened has shown me that you do not really love me, not as I think of love. Oh, I know that when we are together you think you do, but absence is the test. Absence is the acid test of love that separates the base metal from the true. After what has happened we can’t go on with our engagement. It would be farcical. I could never feel that way toward you again. We shall always be friends, I hope. But as for love﻿—love is not a machine. It cannot be shattered and put together again.”

She turned and began to walk up the drive. Hanging over the top of the gate like a wet sock Lord Dawlish watched her go. The interview was over, and he could not think of one single thing to say. Her white dress made a patch of light in the shadows. She moved slowly, as if weighed down by sad thoughts, like one who, as Luella Delia Philpotts beautifully puts it, paces with measured step behind the coffin of a murdered heart. The bend of the drive hid her from his sight.

===Chinese===

“我并不是生你的气，只是失望。经过的事让我看清，你并不是真心爱我，不是我心目中的那种爱。哦，我知道，咱们在一起的时候，你自以为爱；可是分离才是考验。分离是爱情的酸性试金，能把贱金属从真金里析出来。经过这些事，咱们的婚约没法再维持下去了。那会成为一场闹剧。我对你的那份感情，永远不会再回来了。我希望我们永远都是朋友。至于爱情——爱情不是机器。它碎了，就再也拼不拢了。”

她转过身，顺着车道走上去。达利什勋爵挂在门顶上，活像一只湿淋淋的袜子，望着她远去。谈话结束了，他却想不出一件可以说的话。她那身白裙在暗影里铺出一片光亮。她走得缓慢，仿佛被哀愁的思绪坠着，像露埃拉·迪莉娅·菲尔波茨笔下写得美不胜收的那号人，迈着有分寸的步子，跟在一张被谋害的心灵的棺木后面。车道一拐弯，她便从他的视野里消失了。

===Original===

About twenty minutes later Dudley Pickering, smoking sentimentally in the darkness hard by the porch, received a shock. He was musing tenderly on his Claire, who was assisting him in the process by singing in the drawing room, when he was aware of a figure, the sinister figure of a man who, pressed against the netting of the porch, stared into the lighted room beyond.

Dudley Pickering’s first impulse was to stride briskly up to the intruder, tap him on the shoulder and ask him what the devil he wanted; but a second look showed him that the other was built on too ample a scale to make this advisable. He was a large, fit-looking intruder.

Mr. Pickering was alarmed. There had been the usual epidemic of burglaries at Brookport that season. Houses had been broken into, valuable possessions removed. In one case a negro butler had been struck over the head with a gas pipe, and given a headache. In these circumstances it was unpleasant to find burly strangers looking in at windows.

===Chinese===

约莫二十分钟后，达德利·皮克林正倚在游廊近旁的黑暗里，满腹柔情地抽着烟，猛然受了惊吓。他正柔肠百转地想念着他的克莱尔——克莱尔也在客厅里唱着歌，为这一进程尽力相助——忽觉眼前出现了一条人影：一个男人的不祥身影，紧紧贴着游廊的纱网，直勾勾地盯着纱网那头灯火通明的房间。

达德利·皮克林的第一个冲动，是大步流星直奔闯入者，拍拍他的肩膀，问问他究竟见鬼想要什么；再一看，他发现对方的体格实在过于宏伟，此举未免不智。这是一位高大而健硕的闯入者。

皮克林先生警觉起来。那年季节，布鲁克波特照例闹起了盗窃的时疫。人家遭撬，财物遭窃。有一回，一个黑人管家叫人拿煤气管在头上敲了一下，敲出了个头疼。在此情形下，发现彪形生人趴在窗上朝里张望，委实叫人不快。

===Original===

“Hi!” cried Mr. Pickering.

The intruder leaped a foot. It had not occurred to Lord Dawlish, when in an excess of wistful yearning he had decided to sneak up to the house in order to increase his anguish by one last glimpse of Claire, that other members of the household might be out in the grounds. He was just thinking sorrowfully, as he listened to the music, how like his own position was to that of the hero of Tennyson’s “Maud”﻿—a poem to which he was greatly addicted﻿—when Mr. Pickering’s “Hi!” came out of nowhere and hit him like a torpedo.

He turned in agitation. Mr. Pickering having prudently elected to stay in the shadows, there was no one to be seen. It was as if the voice of conscience had shouted “Hi!” at him. He was just wondering if he had imagined the whole thing, when he perceived the red glow of a cigar and beyond it a shadowy form.

===Chinese===

“喂！”皮克林先生喊道。

闯入者蹦起一英尺高。达利什勋爵怀着满腔怅惘的渴念，决意潜近这幢房子，好偷看克莱尔最后一眼，给痛苦再添上一分；他可没想到，宅子里的旁人兴许会到园子里来。他正听着音乐，凄凄地寻思：自己眼下的处境，同丁尼生（Tennyson）《莫德》（Maud）里那位主人公的处境何其相似——那首诗他是嗜读成癖的——皮克林先生那声“喂！”没头没脑地钻了出来，一枚鱼雷似的正中目标。

他惊惶地转过身。皮克林先生老成持重，决意稳坐暗处，四下里一个人影也无。倒像是良心的声音冲他喝了一声“喂！”。他正疑心这一切是不是自己的幻觉，忽见一支雪茄的红光，红光再往外，一个朦胧的身影。

===Original===

It was not the fact that he was in an equivocal position, staring into a house which did not belong to him, with his feet on somebody’s else private soil, that caused Bill to act as he did. It was the fact that at that moment he was not feeling equal to conversation with anybody on any subject whatsoever. It did not occur to him that his behavior might strike a nervous stranger as suspicious. All he aimed at was the swift removal of himself from a spot infested by others of his species. He ran, and Mr. Pickering, having followed him with the eye of fear, went rather shakily into the house, his brain whirling with professional cracksmen and gas pipes and assaulted butlers, to relate his adventure.

“A great, hulking, ruffianly sort of fellow glaring in at the window,” said Mr. Pickering. “I shouted at him and he ran like a rabbit.”

“Gee! Must have been one of the gang that’s been working down here,” said Roscoe Sheriff, “giving the place the double-o before breaking in. There might be a quarter of a column in that, properly worked, but I guess I’d better wait until he actually does bust the place.”

===Chinese===

比尔之所以有这般举动，并不是因为自己处境可疑——瞪眼张望一幢不属于他的房子，双脚踏在别人家的私地上——而是因为那一刻，他实在没有心情同任何人就任何话题交谈。他压根没想到，在一位神经紧张的生人眼里，这番行径可能显得形迹可疑。他一心所求的，只是火速撤离这处同类出没的地带。他跑了。皮克林先生拿一双惊惧的眼睛目送他逃走，随后身子微微发颤地进了屋，脑袋里盘旋着职业惯窃、煤气管和挨打的管家，去讲述他的奇遇。

“一个身躯高大、五大三粗、凶神恶煞的家伙，趴在窗户上朝里瞪眼，”皮克林先生说，“我朝他一喝，他跑得像只兔子。”

“乖乖！准是近来在这一带活动的那伙人里的，”罗斯科·谢里夫说，“动手之前，先把这地方上上下下打量了一个来回。这事要好好经营，兴许能榨出四分之一栏来，不过我想，最好还是等他真把这地方砸开了再说。”

===Original===

“We must notify the police!”

“Notify the police, and have them butt in and stop the thing and kill a good story?” There was honest amazement in the press agent’s voice “Let me tell you, it isn’t so easy to get publicity these days that you want to go out of your way to stop it!”

Mr. Pickering was appalled. A dislike of this man, which had grown less vivid since his scene with Claire, returned to him with redoubled force.

===Chinese===

“必须报告警察！”

“报告警察，叫他们横插一杠，把事情搅了，弄死一个好故事？”宣传经纪人的话音里透着由衷的惊愕：“听我说，如今弄点宣传可不是件容易事，你倒还要存心去拦它！”

皮克林先生骇然。对这人的厌恶——自他同克莱尔那一幕之后已淡薄了些——此刻加倍凶猛地卷土重来。

===Original===

“Why, we may all be murdered in our beds!” he cried.

“Front-page stuff!” said Roscoe Sherriff with gleaming eyes. “And three columns at least. Fine!”

It might have consoled Lord Dawlish somewhat, as he lay awake that night, to have known that the man who had taken Claire from him﻿—though at present he was not aware of such a man’s existence﻿—also slept ill.

===Chinese===

“哎呀，咱们兴许都要在睡梦里给人谋害了！”他喊道。

“头版素材！”罗斯科·谢里夫两眼放光地说。“至少三大栏。好极了！”

那天夜里，达利什勋爵辗转难眠；倘若他知道那个从他手里夺走克莱尔的男人——虽然此刻他还不知道世上有这么个人——也睡得同样不安稳，他心里兴许还能得着几分安慰。

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===

Lady Wetherby sat in her room, writing letters. The rest of the household were variously employed. Roscoe Sheriff was prowling about the house, brooding on campaigns of publicity. Dudley Pickering was walking in the grounds with Claire. In a little shack in the woods that adjoined the high road, which he had converted into a temporary studio, Lord Wetherby was working on a picture which he proposed to call “Innocence,” a study of a small Italian child he had discovered in Washington Square. Lady Wetherby, who had been taken to see the picture, had suggested “The Black Hand’s Newest Recruit” as a better title than the one selected by the artist.

It is a fact to be noted that of the entire household only Lady Wetherby could fairly be described as happy. It took very little to make Lady Wetherby happy. Fine weather, good food, and a complete abstention from classical dancing﻿—give her these and she asked no more. She was, moreover, delighted at Claire’s engagement. It seemed to her, for she had no knowledge of the existence of Lord Dawlish, a genuine manifestation of love’s young dream. She liked Dudley Pickering and she was devoted to Claire. It made her happy to think that it was she who had brought them together.

But of the other members of the party, Dudley Pickering was unhappy because he feared that burglars were about to raid the house; Roscoe Sherriff because he feared they were not; Claire because, now that the news of the engagement was out, it seemed to be everybody’s aim to leave her alone with Mr. Pickering, whose undiluted society tended to pall. And Lord Wetherby was unhappy because he found Eustace, the monkey, a perpetual strain upon his artistic nerves. It was Eustace who had driven him to his shack in the woods. He could have painted far more comfortably in the house, but Eustace had developed a habit of stealing up to him and plucking the leg of his trousers; and an artist simply cannot give of his best with that sort of thing going on.

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵夫人坐在自己房里写信。宅子里其余各人各干各的营生。罗斯科·谢里夫满宅子逡巡，肚里盘算着一出出的宣传战役。达德利·皮克林同克莱尔在园中散步。紧挨大路的林子里有间小棚屋，被韦瑟比伯爵改成了临时画室，他正在里面画一幅画，打算题名《天真》（Innocence），画的是他在华盛顿广场偶然发现的一个意大利小孩。人们领韦瑟比伯爵夫人去看过那幅画，她说，比起画家自己选定的题目，《黑手会最新入会者》（The Black Hand’s Newest Recruit）这个名称更像样。

有一件事值得记下：满宅上下，唯一当得起快活二字来形容的，只有韦瑟比伯爵夫人。叫韦瑟比伯爵夫人快活，本不怎么费事。好天气、好饭菜，再加上同古典舞一刀两断——有这三样，她便别无所求。再说，克莱尔订了婚，她也欢喜得很。她压根不知道世上还有个达利什勋爵，因此在眼里，这是青春爱恋的美梦一场真情流露。她喜欢达德利·皮克林，又一向疼爱克莱尔。一想到牵线搭桥的是自己，她心里便美滋滋的。

不过同行诸人里，达德利·皮克林闷闷不乐，是怕窃贼眼看要来洗劫这所房子；罗斯科·谢里夫闷闷不乐，是怕他们不来；克莱尔闷闷不乐，是因为订婚的消息一传开，人人似乎都存心把她单独留给皮克林先生，而这位先生毫不掺水的陪伴，天长日久是要叫人生腻的。韦瑟比伯爵也闷闷不乐，因为他发觉那只猴尤斯塔斯叫他艺术家的神经永无宁日。把他逼到林中那间棚屋去的，正是尤斯塔斯。在宅子里作画，他本可以舒坦得多，可尤斯塔斯养成了个习惯，老爱蹑手蹑脚蹭到他跟前，扯他的裤腿；耳根底下闹着这种事，艺术家是断然拿不出看家本领的。

===Original===

Lady Wetherby wrote on. She was not fond of letter-writing and she had allowed her correspondence to accumulate; but she was disposing of it in an energetic and conscientious way when the entrance of Wrench, the butler, interrupted her.

Wrench had been imported from England at the request of Lord Wetherby, who had said that it soothed him and kept him from feeling homesick to see a butler about the place. Since then he had been hanging to the establishment, as it were, by a hair. He gave the impression of being always on the point of giving notice. There were so many things connected with his position of which he disapproved. He had made no official pronouncement of the matter, but Lady Wetherby knew that he disapproved of her classical dancing. His last position had been with the Dowager Duchess of Waveney, the well-known political hostess, who﻿—even had the somewhat generous lines on which she was built not prevented the possibility of such a thing﻿—would have perished rather than dance barefooted in a public restaurant. Wrench also disapproved of America. That fact had been made plain immediately upon his arrival in the country. He had given America one look, and then his mind was made up﻿—he disapproved of it.

“If you please, m’lady!”

Lady Wetherby turned. The butler was looking even more than usually disapproving, and his disapproval had, so to speak, crystallized, as if it had found some more concrete and definite objective than either barefoot dancing or the United States.

“If you please, m’lady﻿—the hape!”

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵夫人埋头写她的信。她不爱好写信，信债一任其积攒；此刻正干劲十足、尽心竭力地清偿，不防男管家伦奇走了进来，把她打断了。

伦奇是应韦瑟比伯爵之请专程从英国进口来的；伯爵说过，宅子里有个男管家走来走去，可以安他的神，免他害思乡病。打那以后，伦奇便好比一根头发吊在这户人家门下，一副随时都要递辞呈的样子。他这份差事里头，看不入眼的事情实在太多。他未曾就此发布过正式文告，可韦瑟比伯爵夫人心里明白，他看不惯她跳古典舞。他上一份差事伺候的是韦弗尼老公爵夫人（Dowager Duchess of Waveney），那位以政治沙龙闻名的女主人；就算撇开她那副丰腴体态天生与此事无缘不谈，她也宁可一死，断不肯在一爿公开的餐馆里赤脚起舞。伦奇还不惯美国。这一层，他一踏上这片国土便亮了相。他瞅了美国一眼，主意就此打定——看不惯。

“恕我禀告，太太！”

韦瑟比伯爵夫人转过身来。男管家脸上那副不以为然比平日更重几分，而且那份不以为然不妨说是已经结晶成形，仿佛寻到了一个比赤脚舞或美利坚合众国都更具体、更确切的靶子。

“恕我禀告，太太——那位哈猴（hape，伦奇对 ape 一词的滑稽讹音）！”

===Original===

It was Wrench’s custom to speak of Eustace in a tone of restrained disgust. He disapproved of Eustace. The Dowager Duchess of Waveney, though she kept open house for members of parliament, would have drawn the line at monkeys.

“The hape is behaving very strangely, m’lady,” said Wrench frostily.

It has been well said that in this world there is always something. A moment before Lady Wetherby had been feeling completely contented, without a care on her horizon. It was foolish of her to have expected such a state of things to last, for what is life but a series of sharp corners round each of which fate lies in wait for us with a stuffed eelskin? Something in the butler’s manner, a sort of gloating gloom which he radiated, told her that she had arrived at one of these corners now.

“The hape is seated on the kitchen sink, m’lady, throwing new-laid eggs at the scullery maid, and cook desired me to step up and ask for instructions.”

“What!” Lady Wetherby rose in agitation. “What’s he doing that for?” she asked weakly.

===Chinese===

伦奇说起尤斯塔斯，向来用一种克制着的厌恶腔调。他看不惯尤斯塔斯。韦弗尼老公爵夫人家里虽说对议员们门户大开，猴类却断乎过不了她的门槛。

“那位哈猴行事蹊跷得很，太太，”伦奇冷冰冰地说。

有句老话说得好：这世上总有事儿等着你。方才前一分钟，韦瑟比伯爵夫人还心满意足，眼前天地间连一丝愁影都没有。她竟指望这种光景长驻，未免天真；须知人生是什么？无非一连串的急转弯，命运攥着一条填塞起来的鳗鱼皮，守在每一个弯口后头等着我们。男管家那副做派里有点什么——一种幸灾乐祸的阴郁，正打他周身往外辐射——告诉夫人，眼下她已经撞上其中一个弯口了。

“那位哈猴蹲在厨房水槽上，太太，拿刚下的鸡蛋掷帮厨女仆，厨娘要小的上来请示示下。”

“什么！”韦瑟比伯爵夫人慌得站起身。“他干吗要这么着？”她有气无力地问。

===Original===

A slight, dignified gesture was Wrench’s only reply. It was not his place to analyze the motives of monkeys.

“Throwing eggs!”

The sight of Lady Wetherby’s distress partially melted the butler’s stern reserve. He unbent so far as to supply a clue.

“As I understand from cook, m’lady, the animal appears to have taken umbrage at a lack of cordiality on the part of the cat. It seems that the hape attempted to fondle the cat, but the latter scratched him; being suspicious,” said Wrench, “of his bona fides.” He scrutinized the ceiling with a dull eye. “Whereupon,” he continued, “he seized her tail and threw her with considerable force. He then removed himself to the sink and began to hurl eggs at the scullery maid.”

Lady Wetherby’s mental eye attempted to produce a picture of the scene, but failed.

===Chinese===

伦奇唯一的回应，是一个矜持而尊严的姿势。分析猴类的动机，非他分内之事。

“掷鸡蛋！”

一见韦瑟比伯爵夫人愁苦的模样，男管家那副森严的矜持松动了几分。他屈尊纡贵，提供了一条线索。

“照厨娘告诉我的话，太太，这畜生似乎嫌那只猫不够热络，动了肝火。那位哈猴有心爱抚那只猫，猫却挠了它一下，疑心它的 bona fides（诚意）来着，”伦奇说。他目光呆滞地细察着天花板。“于是乎，”他接着道，“它揪住她的尾巴，使了好大的劲儿把她掼了出去，随后挪窝上了水槽，开始朝帮厨女仆扔鸡蛋。”

韦瑟比伯爵夫人调动心灵的眼睛，试图把这一幕想出来，没有想成。

===Original===

“I suppose I had better go down and see about it,” she said.

Wrench withdrew his gaze from the ceiling.

“I think it would be advisable, m’lady. The scullery maid is already in hysterics.”

Lady Wetherby led the way to the kitchen. She was wroth with Eustace. This was just the sort of thing out of which Algie would be able to make unlimited capital. It weakened her position with Algie. There was only one thing to do﻿—she must hush it up.

Her first glance, however, at the actual theater of war, gave her the impression that matters had advanced beyond the hushing-up stage. A yellow desolation brooded over the kitchen. It was not so much a kitchen as an omelette. There were eggs everywhere, from floor to ceiling. She crunched her way in on a carpet of oozing shells.

===Chinese===

“我看我最好下去瞧瞧，”她说。

伦奇把目光从天花板上收了回来。

“我看这是应当的，太太。帮厨女仆已经歇斯底里大发作了。”

韦瑟比伯爵夫人领头往厨房去。她对尤斯塔斯满腔怒火。这等事情，正是阿尔吉最能拿去大做无穷文章的题目。这叫她在阿尔吉跟前矮了一头。眼下只有一条路——必须把这事捂住。

可头一眼望见这片真正的战区，她便觉得，事情早已越过了捂得住的阶段。一片黄色的荒芜笼罩着厨房。与其说这是一间厨房，不如说这是一张摊鸡蛋。鸡蛋满屋都是，从地板直到天花板。她踩着满地渗汁的碎蛋壳铺成的地毯，咯吱咯吱走了进去。

===Original===

Her entry was a signal for a renewal on a more impressive scale of the uproar that she had heard while opening the door. The air was full of voices. The cook was expressing herself in Norwegian, the parlor maid in what appeared to be Erse. On a chair in a corner the scullery maid sobbed and whooped. The odd-job man, who was a baseball enthusiast, was speaking in terms of high praise of Eustace’s combined speed and control.

The only calm occupant of the room was Eustace himself who, either through a shortage of ammunition or through weariness of the pitching arm, had suspended active hostilities and was now looking down on the scene from a high shelf. There was a brooding expression in his deep-set eyes. He massaged his right ear with the sole of his left foot in a somewhat distrait manner.

“And the first thing that happens,” said the odd-job man fervently, “me brave monk starts in to warm up. He went to it, ma’am, like he was pitching the first game of the World’s Series. Gee, you’d orter of seen his fast one! Walter Johnson’s got nothing on him!”

The sincerity of his enthusiasm did not touch Lady Wetherby. She had but a moderate affection for the national game.

===Chinese===

她这一进门，方才开门时听见的那片喧闹，顿时以更壮观的规模卷土重来。满耳朵都是人声。厨娘在用挪威话抒发情感，客厅女仆用的像是爱尔兰盖尔语（Erse）。角落里的椅子上一名帮厨女仆又哭又嚎。打杂伙计是个棒球迷，正满口赞誉尤斯塔斯球速与控球兼备。

屋里唯一安之若素的住客是尤斯塔斯自己。它或是弹药告罄，或是投球的那条手臂疲了，已经暂停积极敌对行动，此刻正从一处高搁板上俯瞰战局。深陷的眼窝里一派沉思之色。它有点心不在焉，用左脚掌按摩着自己的右耳。

“可倒好，头一出戏，”打杂伙计满腔热忱地说，“俺们那位勇士猴就开始热身了。太太，他那个打法，就跟在世界职业棒球大赛（World’s Series）头一场里投球似的。老天，你真该瞧瞧他那记快球！沃尔特·约翰逊（Walter Johnson）也没他这两下子！”

他这一片热忱虽出自真心，却打动不了韦瑟比伯爵夫人。对这项国技，她素来只有几分淡泊的情分。

===Original===

“Eustace!” she cried severely.

Eustace lowered his foot and gazed at her meditatively, then at the odd-job man, who was comparing him favorably with Grover Alexander, then at the scullery maid, whose voice rose high above the din.

“I rather fancy, m’lady,” said Wrench dispassionately, “that the animal is about to hurl a plate.”

It had escaped the notice of those present that the shelf on which the rioter had taken refuge was within comfortable reach of the dresser, but Eustace himself had not overlooked this important strategic point. As the butler spoke, Eustace picked up a plate and threw it at the scullery maid, whom he seemed definitely to have picked out as the most hostile of the allies. It was a fast inshoot, and hit the wall just above her head.

“Attaboy!” said the odd-job man reverently.

===Chinese===

“尤斯塔斯！”她厉声喝道。

尤斯塔斯放下脚，若有所思地望望她，又望望打杂伙计——后者正把他同格罗弗·亚历山大（Grover Alexander）相比，还觉得他更胜一筹——再望望帮厨女仆，那姑娘的哭嚎高高地压过了满屋的喧腾。

“依我愚见，太太，”伦奇不动声色地说，“这畜生眼看要掷盘子了。”

在场众人都没留意，闹事者避难的这块搁板，离餐具柜一伸手便够得着；这个紧要的战略要点，尤斯塔斯自己却没有放过。男管家话音未落，尤斯塔斯已抄起一只盘子，朝帮厨女仆掷去——他显然认定，联军里头数她敌意最重。那是一记又急又刁的内角球，正中她头顶上方的墙壁。

“好小子！”打杂伙计满怀敬意地说。

===Original===

Lady Wetherby turned on him with some violence. His detached attitude was the most irritating of the many irritating aspects of the situation. She paid this man a weekly wage to do odd jobs. The capture of Eustace was essentially an odd job. Yet, instead of doing it, he hung about with the air of one who has paid his half-dollar and bought his bag of peanuts, and has now nothing to do but look on and enjoy himself.

“Why don’t you catch him?” she cried. “Why don’t you do something?”

The odd-job man came out of his trance. A sudden realization came upon him that life was stern and life was earnest, and that if he did not wish to jeopardize a good situation he must curb his devotion to the great American sport. Everybody was looking at him expectantly. It seemed to be definitely up to him. It was imperative that, whatever he did, he should do it quickly. There was an apron hanging over the back of a chair. He changed abruptly from fan to matador. More with the idea of doing something than because he thought he would achieve anything definite thereby, he picked up the apron and flung it at Eustace. Luck was with him. The apron enveloped Eustace just as he was winding up for another inshoot and was off his balance. He tripped and fell, clutched at the apron to save himself, and came to the ground swathed in it, giving the effect of an apron mysteriously endowed with life. The triumphant odd-job man, pressing his advantage like a good general, gathered up the ends, converted it into a rude bag, and one more was added to the long list of the victories of the human over the brute intelligence.

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵夫人猛地转向他。眼前这局面恼人处千头万绪，最恼人的就得数他这副置身事外的姿态。她每周出工钱雇这个人，为的就是让他打零活。捉拿尤斯塔斯，本质上正是一桩零活。可他偏不动手，只在旁边荡来荡去，那副神情，活像一个付了半块钱门票、又买了一袋花生的看客，如今再没什么可干，只消袖手旁观、自得其乐便是。

“你怎么不去逮他？”她嚷道。“你就不能干点什么？”

打杂伙计从出神状态里惊醒过来。他蓦地省悟：人生是严酷的，人生是正经的；他若不想砸了一份好差事，就得收敛收敛，别太痴恋这项伟大的美国国术。众人都眼巴巴地望着他。看样子这事儿千真万确要指望他了。当务之急是，不管干什么，都得快。一把椅背上头挂着条围裙。他霎时从看客摇身一变，做了斗牛士。他抄起围裙朝尤斯塔斯掷去，与其说指望借此收到什么确切的成效，不如说只为手上干点什么。运气在他这一边。恰逢尤斯塔斯引臂待发、又要掷一记内角球而重心不稳的当口，围裙将它整个罩住。它绊了一下，跌倒，慌忙去抓围裙稳住身子，落地时已被裹了个严严实实，看上去活像一条凭空得了性命的围裙。得胜的打杂伙计如良将一般穷追猛打，把围裙各个角收拢一扎，做成一只粗陋的口袋——人类智力战胜兽类智力的那份漫长战绩簿上，又添了一笔。

===Original===

Everybody had a suggestion now. The cook advocated drowning. The parlor maid favored the idea of hitting the prisoner with a broom handle. Wrench, eyeing the struggling apron disapprovingly, mentioned that Mr. Pickering had bought a revolver that morning.

“Put him in the coal cellar,” said Lady Wetherby.

Wrench was more farseeing.

“If I might offer the warning, m’lady,” said Wrench, “not the cellar. It is full of coal. It would be placing temptation in the animal’s way.”

===Chinese===

这下人人都有了主意。厨娘主张淹死。客厅女仆赞成拿扫帚把儿敲打囚犯。伦奇不以为然地瞅着那团挣扎的围裙，提起皮克林先生当天上午买了一支左轮手枪。

“把他关进煤窖，”韦瑟比伯爵夫人说。

伦奇想得更为长远。

“恕我进言，太太，”伦奇说，“煤窖使不得。窖里满满是煤。那等于把这诱惑径直摆到这畜生眼前。”

===Original===

The odd-job man endorsed this.

“He’d pitch a great game with coal, ma’am,” he said, almost wistfully.

“Put him in the garage then,” said Lady Wetherby.

The odd-job man departed, bearing his heaving bag at arm’s length. The cook and the parlor maid addressed themselves to comforting and healing the scullery maid. Wrench went off to polish silver, Lady Wetherby to resume her letters. The cat was the last of the party to return to the normal. She came down from the chimney an hour later, covered with soot, demanding restoratives.

===Chinese===

打杂伙计附议。

“拿煤球他能投出一场好球呢，太太，”他说，语气近乎怅惘。

“那就把他关进汽车房，”韦瑟比伯爵夫人说。

打杂伙计拎着那只一起一伏挣动的口袋，伸直了胳膊走了。厨娘和客厅女仆转而去抚慰帮厨女仆、替她治伤。伦奇擦银器去了，韦瑟比伯爵夫人回去接着写信。这一伙人里头，最后一个恢复正常的是那只猫。一个钟头之后，她才从烟囱里下来，满身烟灰，索要恢复元气的补品。

===Original===

Lady Wetherby finished her letters. She cut them short, for Eustace’s insurgence had interfered with her flow of ideas. She went into the drawing room, where she found Roscoe Sheriff strumming on the piano.

“Eustace has been raising Cain,” she said.

The press agent looked up hopefully. He had been wearing a rather preoccupied air.

“How’s that?” he asked.

“Throwing eggs and plates in the kitchen.”

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵夫人把信写完了。几封信都写得短，因为尤斯塔斯这场叛乱搅乱了她的文思。她走进客厅，见罗斯科·谢里夫正在钢琴上随手乱弹。

“尤斯塔斯大闹了一场，”她说。

宣传经纪人满怀希望地抬起头。方才他脸上一直笼着一层心事重重的神气。

“怎么讲？”他问。

“在厨房里掷鸡蛋又掷盘子。”

===Original===

The gleam of interest which had come into Roscoe Sherriff’s face died out.

“You couldn’t get more than a fill-in at the bottom of a column on that,” he said regretfully. “I’m a little disappointed in that monk. I hoped he would pan out bigger. Well, I guess we’ve just got to give him time. I have a hunch that he’ll set the house on fire or do something with a punch like that one of these days. You mustn’t get discouraged. Why, that puma I made Valerie Devenish keep looked like a perfect flivver for four whole months. A child could have played with it. Miss Devenish called me up on the phone, I remember, and handed me the worst kind of beef. Said she was darned if she was going to spend the rest of her life maintaining an animal that might as well be stuffed for all the pep it showed, and that she was going right out to buy a white mouse instead. Fortunately I talked her round.

“A few weeks later she came round and thanked me with tears in her eyes. The puma had suddenly struck real midseason form. It clawed the elevator boy, bit a postman, chased the coon on the switchboard half a dozen blocks along Central Park West, held up the traffic for miles and was finally shot by a policeman. Why, for the next few days there was nothing in the papers at all but Miss Devenish and her puma. There was a war on at the time, in Mexico or somewhere, and we had it backed off the front page so far that it was over before it could get back. So, you see, there’s always hope. I’ve been nursing the papers with bits about Eustace, so as to be ready for the grandstand play when it comes﻿—and all we can do is to wait. It’s something if he’s been throwing eggs. It shows he’s waking up.”

===Chinese===

罗斯科·谢里夫脸上方才亮起的那点兴味，熄灭了。

“这桩事情，顶多值一条报尾补白，”他惋惜地说，“那只猴儿叫我有点失望。我原指望他能成更大的气候。也罢，依我看，只能再给他些时间。我有种预感：这几天里他不是放火烧宅，就是要干一桩够劲儿的。你千万不能泄气。嘿，我撺掇瓦莱丽·德夫尼什（Valerie Devenish）养的那头美洲狮，足足有四个月瞧着就是个十足的废物，三岁小孩都能拿它当玩意儿耍。德夫尼什小姐记得给我挂过电话，把我好一顿数落，说她死也不肯后半辈子养这么一头畜生——瞧它那点精神头儿，做成标本都嫌多——还说这就出门买只小白鼠去替代。幸亏我把她劝转了。

“几个礼拜之后，她自己也转过来了，含着眼泪谢我。那头美洲狮忽然打出了赛季中段的巅峰状态。它抓伤了电梯工，咬了一个邮差，追着接线房的那个黑鬼，沿中央公园西路一口气跑过六条街区，把几英里长的交通堵得死死的，末了让一名警察开了枪。嗬，往后那几天，报上除了德夫尼什小姐和她的美洲狮，简直登不进别的。那阵子正打着一场仗，在墨西哥还是哪儿来着；我们把它从头版一路往后挤，挤得它还没爬回头版就打完了。所以你瞧，希望总是在的。我一直在各家报纸那儿悉心喂着尤斯塔斯的几条小消息，好等那出轰动大戏开场时派上用场——眼下我们能做的，就是等。它肯掷鸡蛋，就算是个苗头。说明它醒了。”

===Original===

The door opened and Lord Wetherby entered. He looked fatigued. He sank into a chair and sighed.

“I cannot get it,” he said. “It eludes me.”

He lapsed into a somber silence.

“I’ll be the goat,” said Lady Wetherby cautiously. “What can’t you get?”

“The expression﻿—the expression I want to get into, the child’s eyes in my picture, ‘Innocence.’ ”

===Chinese===

门开了，韦瑟比伯爵走了进来。他满面倦色，往一把椅子里一沉，叹了口气。

“我抓不住它，”他说，“它躲着我。”

随即陷入一片阴沉的静默。

“那我来当这个冤大头，”韦瑟比伯爵夫人小心翼翼地说，“你抓不住的是什么？”

“那种神情——我想画进去的那种神情，我那幅《天真》里那孩子的眼神。”

===Original===

“But you have got it.”

Lord Wetherby shook his head.

“Well, you had when I saw the picture,” persisted Lady Wetherby. “This child you’re painting has just joined the Black Hand. He has been rushed in young over the heads of the waiting list because his father had a pull. Naturally the kid wants to do something to justify his election, and he wants to do it quick. You have caught him at the moment when he sees an old gentleman coming down the street and realizes that he has only got to sneak up and stick his little knife﻿—”

“My dear Polly. I welcome criticism, but this is mere﻿—”

===Chinese===

“可你已经画出来了呀。”

韦瑟比伯爵摇了摇头。

“反正我看画的时候是有的，”韦瑟比伯爵夫人不肯松口，“你画的这孩子刚加入黑手会。他爸有门路，把候补名单上排着的老资格一概压下去，他小小年纪便被火速拉进了会。这小家伙自然要干出一件事来，好不辜负大家对他的推选，而且要快。你恰恰逮住了这么个当口：他望见街上过来一位老先生，意识到自己只消悄悄摸过去，把小刀子往上一捅——”

“亲爱的波莉。批评我欢迎，可这也简直是——”

===Original===

Lady Wetherby stroked his coat sleeve fondly.

“Never mind. Algie. I was only joshing you, precious. I thought the picture was coming along fine when you showed it to me. I’ll come and take another look at it.”

Lord Wetherby shook his head.

“I should have a model. An artist cannot mirror Nature properly without a model. I wish you would invite that child down here.”

“No, Algie, there are limits, I wouldn’t have him within a mile of the place.”

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵夫人爱怜地抚了抚他的西装袖子。

“别往心里去，阿尔吉。我不过逗你玩呢，宝贝。你拿给我看的时候，我觉得那幅画进行得挺好。我这就再去瞧它一眼。”

韦瑟比伯爵摇了摇头。

“我该有个模特。没有模特，艺术家没法如实地映照自然。你要肯把那孩子请到这儿来就好了。”

“不行，阿尔吉，凡事总得有个限度。一英里之内我都容不下这孩子。”

===Original===

“Yet you keep Eustace.”

“Well, you made me engage Wrench. It’s fifty-fifty. I wish you wouldn’t keep picking on Eustace, Algie dear. He does no harm. Mr. Sherriff and I were just saying how peaceable he is. He wouldn’t hurt﻿—”

Claire came in.

“Polly,” she said, “did you put that monkey of yours in the garage? He’s just bitten Dudley in the leg.”

===Chinese===

“可你倒养着尤斯塔斯。”

“可你逼着我雇了伦奇。这笔账扯平了。我说亲爱的阿尔吉，你就别老跟尤斯塔斯过不去了。他又不伤人。方才谢里夫先生还同我说，他多么安分守己。他才不会伤——”

克莱尔走了进来。

“波莉，”她说，“你把你那只猴子关进汽车房了没有？它刚咬了达德利的腿。”

===Original===

Lord Wetherby uttered an exclamation.

“Now perhaps﻿—”

“We went in just now to have a look at the car,” continued Claire. “Dudley wanted to show me the commutator on the exhaust box or the wind screen or something, and he was just bending over when Eustace jumped out from nowhere and pinned him. I’m afraid he has taken it to heart rather.”

Roscoe Sherriff pondered.

“Is this worth half a column?” He shook his head. “No, I’m afraid not. The public doesn’t know Pickering. If it had been Charlie Chaplin or William J. Bryan or someone on those lines, we could have had the papers bringing out extras. You can visualize William J. Bryan being bitten in the leg by a monkey. It hits you. You’ve seen his legs at Chautauqua meetings. But Pickering! Eustace might just as well have bitten the leg of the table,” he concluded bitterly.

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵惊叫了一声。

“这下子总该——”

“我们方才进去看汽车来着，”克莱尔接着说，“达德利要指给我看排气箱上的整流器，还是挡风玻璃来着，反正是那么个部件。他刚一弯腰，尤斯塔斯不知打哪儿蹿了出来，一下扑住了他。我怕他把这事看得挺重。”

罗斯科·谢里夫沉吟起来。

“这事值不值半栏？”他摇了摇头。“不值，恐怕不值。公众不认得皮克林。换上查理·卓别林（Charlie Chaplin），或是威廉·詹宁斯·布莱恩（William J. Bryan），或是那一路的人物，报馆都得出号外。威廉·詹宁斯·布莱恩叫猴子咬了腿——你能想见那画面，那一下打得中人心。肖托夸集会（Chautauqua）上，他那两条腿谁没见过。可皮克林！尤斯塔斯还不如去咬桌子腿哪，”他酸溜溜地收了尾。

===Original===

Lord Wetherby reasserted himself.

“Now that the animal has become a public menace﻿—”

“He’s nothing of the kind,” said Lady Wetherby. “He’s only a little upset today.”

“Do you mean, Pauline, that even after this you will not get rid of him?”

“Certainly not, poor dear.”

===Chinese===

韦瑟比伯爵重又振作起来。

“既然这畜生已经成了公害——”

“它才不是什么公害，”韦瑟比伯爵夫人说，“他不过是今天有点心烦意乱。”

“波琳，你的意思是，出了这档子事，你还不肯打发他走？”

“当然不打发，可怜的宝贝儿。”

===Original===

“Very well,” said Lord Wetherby with frozen calm. “I give you warning that if he attacks me I shall defend myself.”

He brooded. Lady Wetherby turned to Claire.

“What happened then? Did you shut the door of the garage?”

“Yes, but not until Eustace had got away. He slipped out like a streak and disappeared. It was too dark to see which way he went.”

===Chinese===

“很好，”韦瑟比伯爵说，平静得结了冰，“我把话放在这儿：它若敢袭击我，我必自卫。”

他闷闷地寻思。韦瑟比伯爵夫人转向克莱尔。

“后来呢？汽车房的门你关上了没有？”

“关了，可尤斯塔斯先溜出去了。它一眨眼蹿出去，没了影儿。天太黑，瞧不见它往哪边跑了。”

===Original===

Dudley Pickering limped heavily into the room.

“I was just telling them about you and Eustace, Dudley.”

Mr. Pickering nodded moodily. He was too full for words.

“I think Eustace must be mad,” said Claire.

Roscoe Sherriff uttered a cry of rapture.

===Chinese===

达德利·皮克林拖着伤腿，一步一沉地挪进屋来。

“我正跟他们讲你和尤斯塔斯的事呢，达德利。”

皮克林先生郁郁地点了点头。他满心得连话都盛不下了。

“我看尤斯塔斯准是疯了，”克莱尔说。

罗斯科·谢里夫发出一声狂喜的欢呼。

===Original===

“You’ve said it!” he exclaimed. “I knew we should get action sooner or later. It’s the puma over again. Now we are all right. Now I have something to work on. ‘Monkey Menaces Countryside.’ ‘Long Island Summer Colony in Panic.’ ‘Mad Monkey Bites One﻿—’ ”

A convulsive shudder galvanized Mr. Pickering’s portly frame.

“ ‘Mad Monkey Terrorizes Long Island. One Dead!’ ” murmured Roscoe Sherriff wistfully. “Do you feel a sort of shooting, Pickering﻿—a kind of burning sensation under the skin? Lady Wetherby, I guess I’ll be getting some of the papers on the phone. We’ve got a big story.”

He hurried to the telephone, but it was some little time before he could use it. Dudley Pickering was in possession, talking earnestly to the local doctor.

===Chinese===

“这话说到魂儿上了！”他嚷道，“我就知道迟早要出动静。跟美洲狮那回一模一样。这下行了。这回我有得可做了。‘猴子威胁乡里。’‘长岛避暑村镇一片恐慌。’‘疯猴咬伤一人——’”

一阵痉挛的战栗，激得皮克林先生那肥胖的身躯猛地一挺。

“‘疯猴肆虐长岛。一人毙命！’”罗斯科·谢里夫神往地喃喃念道。“皮克林，你可觉得有点刺痛——皮底下火烧火燎的滋味？韦瑟比伯爵夫人，我看我得给几家报馆挂电话了。咱们手上出了大新闻。”

他急匆匆奔向电话机，可一时半会儿还轮不上他用。达德利·皮克林正占着线，郑重其事地同本地大夫通话。

## XIV / 第十四章

===Original===

It was Nutty Boyd’s habit to retire immediately after dinner to his bedroom. What he did there Elizabeth did not know. Sometimes she pictured him reading, sometimes thinking. Neither supposition was correct. Nutty never read. Newspapers bored him and books made his head ache. And, as for thinking, he had the wrong shape of forehead. The nearest he ever got to meditation was a sort of trance-like state, a kind of suspended animation in which his mind drifted sluggishly like a log in a backwater. Nutty, it is regrettable to say, went to his room after dinner for the purpose of imbibing two or three surreptitious rye highballs.

He behaved in this way, he told himself, purely in order to spare Elizabeth anxiety. There had been in the past a fool of a doctor who had prescribed total abstinence for Nutty, and Elizabeth knew this. Therefore, Nutty held, to take the mildest of snorts with her knowledge would have been to fill her with fears for his safety. So he went to considerable inconvenience to keep the matter from her notice, and thought rather highly of himself for doing so.

It certainly was inconvenient, there was no doubt of that. It made him feel like a cross between a hunted fawn and a burglar. But he had to some extent diminished the possibility of surprise by leaving his door open; and tonight he approached the closet where he kept the materials for refreshment with a certain confidence. He had left Elizabeth on the porch in a hammock, apparently anchored for some time. Lord Dawlish was out in the grounds somewhere. Presently he would come in and join Elizabeth on the porch. The risk of interruption was negligible.

===Chinese===

纳蒂·博伊德（Nutty Boyd）的习惯，是晚饭一撤便立刻退回卧室。他在里面做些什么，伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）并不知道。有时她想象他在读书，有时想象他在沉思。两种猜想都不对。纳蒂从来不读书。报纸叫他烦闷，书本叫他头疼。至于沉思，他额头的形状生得不对。他最接近冥想的地步，是一种恍惚出神，一种近于假死的悬停，其间他的心思像一截木头漂在死水湾里，有气无力地打转。说来可惜，纳蒂晚饭后回房去，图的是偷偷灌下两三杯黑麦威士忌高杯酒。

他这样做，他对自己说，纯粹是为了免得伊丽莎白担惊。从前有过一个蠢头蠢脑的医生，给纳蒂开过滴酒不入的方子，这件事伊丽莎白是知道的。所以在纳蒂看来，若是当着她的面沾一滴最温和的酒，便是叫她为自己的安危提心吊胆。于是他不辞相当的麻烦，把这件事瞒过她的眼目，并且为此颇为看得起自己。

这的确麻烦，这一点无可怀疑。这叫他觉得自己仿佛是被猎逐的小鹿同夜行贼的混种。不过他把房门敞着，倒也多少减小了猝不及防的可能；今晚他朝那只存放提神材料的壁橱走去，心里带着几分自信。他把伊丽莎白留在门廊的吊床上，看上去那吊床已经抛锚，要泊上好一阵。达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）在外头的园子里什么地方。过一会儿他会进来，到门廊上陪伊丽莎白坐。被打断的风险微不足道。

===Original===

Nutty mixed himself a highball, and settled down to brood bitterly, as he often did, on the doctor who had made that disastrous statement. Doctors were always saying things like that﻿—sweeping things which nervous people took too literally. It was true that he had been in pretty bad shape at the moment when the words had been spoken. It was just at the end of his Broadway career, when, as he handsomely admitted, there was a certain amount of truth in the opinion that his interior needed a vacation. But since then he had been living in the country, breathing good air, taking things easy. In these altered conditions and after this lapse of time it was absurd to imagine that a moderate amount of alcohol could do him any harm.

It hadn’t done him any harm, that was the point. He had tested the doctor’s statement and found it incorrect. He had spent three hectic days and nights in New York, and﻿—after a reasonable interval﻿—had felt much the same as usual. And since then he had imbibed each night, and nothing had happened. What it came to was that the doctor was a chump, a pessimist and a blighter. Simply that and nothing more.

Having come to this decision Nutty mixed another highball. He went to the head of the stairs and listened. He heard nothing. He returned to his room.

===Chinese===

纳蒂给自己调好一杯高杯酒，坐下来，同往常一样悲愤地反刍那位医生——那句要命的话就是打他嘴里说出来的。医生们总爱说这一类话——一竿子扫尽一片的大话，神经质的人偏偏句句当真。不错，那句话出口的时节，他的光景确实相当不妙。那正是他百老汇（Broadway）生涯收梢的当口；当时有一种意见，说他的内脏需要休一个假——连他自己也慷慨承认，这话有几分道理。可打那以后，他一直住在乡下，呼吸着好空气，日子过得从容。条件既已改观，时日又已过去这么久，还去想象区区适量的酒能伤他分毫，未免荒唐。

而事实是，它压根没伤着他，这正是要害所在。他检验过医生的说法，发现此言不确。他在纽约轰轰烈烈闹了三天三夜，过后——隔了一段合理的间歇——他自觉同往常并无二致。而且打那以后他夜夜沾唇，什么事也没有。归结起来就是：那个医生是个蠢货，一个悲观主义者，一个讨厌鬼。就这么多，再无别的。

打定这个主意，纳蒂又调了一杯高杯酒。他走到楼梯口侧耳细听。什么也没听到。他回房去了。

===Original===

Yes, that was it, the doctor was a chump. He had hinted at all sorts of terrible things as the penalty for the slightest deviation from his instructions, and his bluff had been called. So far from doing him any harm, these nightly potations brightened Nutty up, gave him heart and enabled him to endure life in this hole of a place. He felt a certain scornful amusement. Doctors, he supposed, had to get off that sort of talk to earn their money.

He reached out for the bottle, and as he grasped it his eye was caught by something on the floor. A brown monkey with a long gray tail was sitting there staring at him.

There was one of those painful pauses. Nutty looked at the monkey, rather like an elongated Macbeth inspecting the ghost of Banquo. The monkey looked at Nutty. The pause continued. Nutty shut his eyes, counted ten slowly and opened them. The monkey was still there. “Boo!” said Nutty in an apprehensive undertone.

===Chinese===

对，就是这么回事，那个医生是个蠢货。他曾暗示，哪怕对他的叮嘱有丝毫偏离，种种可怕的报应便会接踵而至，如今他的唬人把戏被叫穿了。这些夜夜的杯中物非但没伤他分毫，反倒叫纳蒂精神焕发，勇气倍增，使他得以忍受这个鬼地方的生活。他生出几分轻蔑的快意。医生嘛，他寻思，总得说说这一类话才挣得来饭钱。

他伸手去够酒瓶，刚一抓住，地板上的什么东西便攫住了他的目光。一只棕色的猴子，拖着一条长长的灰尾巴，端端正正坐在那儿盯着他。

接着便是一阵痛苦难堪的冷场。纳蒂望着猴子，活像一根拉长了的麦克白（Macbeth），正端详班柯（Banquo）的鬼魂。猴子望着纳蒂。冷场继续着。纳蒂闭上眼睛，慢慢数到十，再睁开来。猴子还在那儿。“哇！”纳蒂心惊胆战地低声喊了一嗓子。

===Original===

The monkey looked at him.

Nutty shut his eyes again. He would count sixty this time. A cold fear had laid its clammy fingers on his heart. This was what that doctor﻿—not such a chump after all﻿—must have meant!

Nutty began to count. There seemed to be a heavy lump inside him and his mouth was dry; but otherwise he felt all right. That was the gruesome part of it﻿—this dreadful thing had come upon him at a moment when he could have sworn that he was as sound as a bell. If this had happened in the days when he ranged the Great White Way, sucking up deleterious moisture like a cloud, it would have been intelligible. But it had sneaked upon him like a thief in the night; it had stolen unheralded into his life when he had practically reformed. What was the good of practically reforming if this sort of thing was going to happen to one?

===Chinese===

猴子望着他。

纳蒂重新闭上眼睛。这回他要数到六十。一股冰冷的恐惧把它黏湿的手指按上了他的心口。那个医生——倒也不全然是蠢货——指的想必就是这个！

纳蒂数了起来。他肚子里仿佛坠着一块重东西，嘴里发干；除此之外倒也一切如常。可怕就可怕在这里——这场大祸临头的当口，他本可以指天发誓，说自己硬朗得像口好钟。倘使这事出在他纵横白色大道（Great White Way）的年月，出在他像一朵云似的把伤身浆液吸个没完的日子，倒也情有可原。可它偏偏像个贼，趁着夜色溜了进来；偏偏在他几乎已经改邪归正的时候，不打一声招呼就潜入了他的生活。既然这种事照样会落到一个人头上，那“几乎改邪归正”又顶什么用？

===Original===

“… Fifty-nine﻿ ﻿… sixty.”

He opened his eyes. The monkey was still there, in precisely the same attitude, as if it was sitting for its portrait. Panic surged upon Nutty. He lost his head completely. He uttered a wild yell and threw the bottle at the apparition.

Life had not been treating Eustace well that evening. He seemed to have happened upon one of those days when everything goes wrong. The cat had scratched him, the odd-job man had swathed him in an apron, and now this stranger, in whom he had found at first a pleasant restfulness, soothing after the recent scenes of violence in which he had participated, did this to him. He dodged the missile and clambered on the top of the wardrobe. It was his instinct in times of stress to seek the high spots. And then Elizabeth hurried into the room.

===Chinese===

“……五十九……六十。”

他睁开眼睛。猴子还在那儿，姿势分毫不差，仿佛正坐着让人给它画像。恐慌朝纳蒂席卷而来。他完全乱了方寸。他发出一声狂叫，抄起瓶子朝那鬼影掷了过去。

那天晚上，尤斯塔斯（Eustace）的境遇也不顺遂。他似乎赶上了诸事不顺的日子。猫抓了他，打杂的拿围裙把他裹了个严实，如今又轮到这位生客——起初他从这位生客身上觉出一种可意的安稳，正可抚慰他近来亲身卷入的那些刀兵场面——竟对他下此毒手。他一闪身躲过来物，攀上了衣柜顶。危难时刻往高处去，这是他的本能。就在这时，伊丽莎白匆匆赶进了房间。

===Original===

Elizabeth had been lying in the hammock on the porch when her brother’s yell had broken forth. It was a lovely, calm, moonlight night, and she had been reveling in the peace of it, when suddenly this outcry from above had shot her out of her hammock like an explosion. She ran upstairs, fearing she knew not what. She found Nutty sitting on the bed, looking like an overwrought giraffe.

“Whatever is the﻿—” she began; and then things began to impress themselves on her senses.

The bottle which Nutty had thrown at Eustace had missed the latter, but it had hit the wall and was now lying in many pieces on the floor, and the air was heavy with the scent of it. The remains seemed to leer at her with a kind of furtive swagger, after the manner of broken bottles. A quick thrill of anger ran through Elizabeth. She had always felt more like a mother to Nutty than a sister, and now she would have liked to exercise the maternal privilege of slapping him.

===Chinese===

哥哥那一声狂叫迸发的时候，伊丽莎白正躺在门廊的吊床上。那是个可爱而宁静的月夜，她正沉醉于一片安宁之中，楼上这声呼号却猝然炸响，像爆裂一样把她从吊床上弹了起来。她奔上楼去，惶惶然也不知怕的是什么。她见到纳蒂坐在床上，一副神经过敏的长颈鹿模样。

“这究竟是怎么——”她刚开口，屋里的种种物象便已强印上她的感官。

纳蒂掷向尤斯塔斯的那只瓶子没有命中，却撞上了墙，此刻正碎成许多片躺在地板上，空气里浓得化不开的，全是它的气味。那堆残骸用碎瓶子惯有的做派，贼头贼脑而又自命不凡地朝她斜睨着。一股怒火倏地传遍伊丽莎白全身。她待纳蒂，从来与其说是姐姐，不如说是母亲，此刻她真想行使一回母亲打孩子的特权。

===Original===

“Nutty!”

“I saw a monkey!” said her brother hollowly. “I was standing over there and I saw a monkey! Of course it wasn’t there really. I flung the bottle at it, and it seemed to climb into that wardrobe.”

“This wardrobe?”

“Yes.”

Elizabeth struck it a resounding blow with the palm of her hand, and Eustace’s face popped over the edge, peering down anxiously. “I can see it now,” said Nutty. A sudden faint hope came to him. “Can you see it?” he asked.

===Chinese===

“纳蒂！”

“我看见一只猴子！”她弟弟声音空落落地说。“我就站在那边，我看见一只猴子！当然，它其实并不在那儿。我把瓶子朝它掷过去，它好像爬进那只衣柜里去了。”

“这只衣柜？”

“对。”

伊丽莎白抡圆手掌，砰地给了衣柜一记山响的拍击，尤斯塔斯的脸从柜顶边上噗地探出来，忐忑不安地朝下张望。“现在我看见了，”纳蒂说。一线微弱的希望蓦地升上他的心头。“你看得见它吗？”他问。

===Original===

Elizabeth did not speak for a moment. This was an unusual situation, and she was wondering how to treat it. She was sorry for Nutty, but Providence had sent this thing and it would be foolish to reject it. She must look on herself in the light of a doctor. It would be kinder to Nutty in the end. She had the feminine aversion to the lie deliberate. Her ethics on the suggestio falsi were weak. She looked at Nutty questioningly.

“See it?” she said.

“Don’t you see a monkey on the top of the wardrobe?” said Nutty, becoming more definite.

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白一时没有作声。这局面非同小可，她正在琢磨该怎么处置。她替纳蒂难过，可是上天把这件东西送上了门，拒之不受未免愚蠢。她必须把自己摆在医生的位置上。到头来，这才是对纳蒂更仁慈。女性对蓄意谎言的憎恶，在她身上一样不缺；可要论以隐瞒引人误信（suggestio falsi）的过错，她的道德约束向来薄弱。她朝纳蒂投去探询的一瞥。

“看见它？”她说。

“你没看见衣柜顶上有一只猴子吗？”纳蒂说，口吻越发明确起来。

===Original===

“There’s a sort of bit of wood sticking out﻿—”

Nutty sighed.

“No, not that. You don’t see it. I didn’t think you would.”

He spoke so dejectedly that for a moment Elizabeth weakened, but only for an instant.

“Tell me all about this, Nutty,” she said.

===Chinese===

“是有个木头似的东西翘在外面——”

纳蒂叹了口气。

“不，不是那个。你是看不见它。我原就料到你看不见。”

他说得那样灰心丧气，伊丽莎白一度软了下来，但也只软了一霎。

“把这件事原原本本讲给我听，纳蒂，”她说。

===Original===

Nutty was beyond the desire for evasion and concealment. His one wish was to tell. He told all.

“But, Nutty, how silly of you!”

“Yes.”

“After what the doctor said.”

“I know.”

===Chinese===

纳蒂早已没了躲闪遮掩的心思。他唯一的愿望就是倾吐。他和盘托出。

“可是，纳蒂，你多么糊涂呀！”

“是啊。”

“医生说过那样的话，你还……”

“我知道。”

===Original===

“You remember his telling you﻿—”

“I know. Never again!”

“What do you mean?”

“I quit. I’m going on the wagon.”

Elizabeth embraced him maternally.

===Chinese===

“你记得他跟你讲过——”

“我知道。再也不喝了！”

“你这是什么意思？”

“我戒了。我要加入戒酒大军了。”

伊丽莎白以慈母的姿势拥抱了他。

===Original===

“That’s a good child,” she said. “You really promise?”

“I don’t have to promise, I’m just going to do it.”

Elizabeth compromised with her conscience by becoming soothing.

“You know, this isn’t so very serious, Nutty, darling. I mean it’s just a warning.”

“It’s warned me all right.”

===Chinese===

“这才是个乖孩子，”她说。“你真答应？”

“我用不着答应，我只管做去。”

伊丽莎白同自己的良心达成妥协，改用安抚的调子。

“你知道，这事儿没那么严重，纳蒂，亲爱的。我是说，这不过是个警告罢了。”

“警告得够透彻了。”

===Original===

“You will be perfectly all right if﻿—”

Nutty interrupted her.

“You’re sure you can’t see anything?”

“See what?”

Nutty’s voice became almost apologetic.

“I know it’s just imagination, but the monkey seems to me to be climbing down from the wardrobe.”

===Chinese===

“你保管会好端端的，只要——”

纳蒂打断了她。

“你确定看不见什么吗？”

“看见什么？”

纳蒂的声音近乎抱歉起来。

“我知道这只是幻觉，可那猴子在我眼里，似乎正打衣柜上往下爬。”

===Original===

“I can’t see anything climbing down the wardrobe,” said Elizabeth, as Eustace touched the floor.

“It’s come down now. It’s crossing the carpet.”

“Where?”

“It’s gone now. It went out of the door.”

“Oh!”

===Chinese===

“我可看不见什么东西在打衣柜上往下爬，”伊丽莎白说。这时尤斯塔斯四掌恰好着地。

“眼下它下来了。它正穿过地毯。”

“哪儿？”

“这会儿它没了。打门里出去了。”

“哦！”

===Original===

“I say, Elizabeth, what do you think I ought to do?”

“I should go to bed and have a nice long sleep, and you’ll feel﻿—”

“Somehow I don’t feel much like going to bed. This sort of thing upsets a chap, you know!”

“Poor dear!”

“I think I’ll go for a long walk.”

===Chinese===

“我说，伊丽莎白，你看我该怎么办？”

“要我说，你该上床去，美美睡上一大觉，你就会觉得——”

“不知怎么，我不大想上床。这种事叫人乱了方寸，你知道的！”

“可怜的人儿！”

“我看我出去走个长路好了。”

===Original===

“That’s a splendid idea.”

“I think I’d better do a good lot of walking from now on. Didn’t Chalmers bring down some Indian clubs with him? I think I’ll borrow them. I ought to keep out in the open a lot, I think. I wonder if there’s any special diet I ought to have. Well, anyway, I’ll be going for that walk.”

At the foot of the stairs Nutty stopped. He looked quickly into the porch, then looked away again.

“What’s the matter?” asked Elizabeth.

===Chinese===

“这主意好极了。”

“我看打今往后，我顶好多多走路。查默斯（Chalmers）不是带了几根印度棒（Indian clubs）下来吗？我想去借来用用。我想我应当多多待在露天里。不知是不是有什么特别的饮食我该遵行。好，横竖我先去走那条路了。”

纳蒂走到楼梯脚下，停住了。他朝门廊飞快地瞥了一眼，又急忙把目光挪开。

“怎么了？”伊丽莎白问。

===Original===

“I thought for a moment I saw the monkey sitting on the hammock.”

He went out of the house and disappeared from view down the drive, walking with long, rapid strides.

Elizabeth’s first act, when he had gone, was to fetch a banana from the icebox. Her knowledge of monkeys was slight, but she fancied they looked with favor on bananas. It was her intention to conciliate Eustace.

She had placed Eustace by now. Unlike Nutty, she read the papers, and she knew all about Lady Wetherby and her pets. The fact that Lady Wetherby, as she had been informed by the grocer in friendly talk across the counter, had rented a summer home in the neighborhood made Eustace’s identity positive.

===Chinese===

“有一会儿，我恍惚看见那猴子正坐在吊床上。”

他出了房子，迈着又长又急的大步顺着车道走去，从视野里消失了。

他一走，伊丽莎白的头一件事，便是打冰匣里取了一根香蕉。她对猴子的学问有限，但她相信这类动物对香蕉素有好感。她的打算是安抚尤斯塔斯。

到这时，她已经给尤斯塔斯验明正身了。同纳蒂不一样，她读报纸，韦瑟比夫人（Lady Wetherby）和她的那些宝贝动物的底细她全知道。据杂货店老板隔着柜台闲谈时告诉她，韦瑟比夫人在这一带租下了一幢避暑宅子——这么一来，尤斯塔斯的身份便确凿无疑了。

===Original===

She had no very clear plans as to what she intended to do with Eustace, beyond being quite resolved that she was going to board and lodge him for a few days. Nutty had had the jolt he needed, but it might be that the first freshness of it would wear away, in which event it would be convenient to have Eustace on the premises. She regarded Eustace as a sort of medicine. A second dose might not be necessary, but it was as well to have the mixture handy. She took another banana, in case the first might not be sufficient to soothe her visitor’s wounded spirit. She then returned to the porch.

Eustace was sitting on the hammock, brooding. The complexities of life were weighing him down a good deal. He was not aware of Elizabeth’s presence until he found her standing by him. He had just braced himself for flight when he perceived that she bore rich gifts.

Eustace was always ready for a light snack﻿—readier now than usual, for air and exercise had sharpened his appetite. He took the banana in a detached manner, as if to convey the idea that it did not commit him to any particular course of conduct. It was a good banana, and he stretched out a hand for the other. Elizabeth sat down beside him, but he did not move. He was convinced now of her good intentions. It was thus that Lord Dawlish found them when he came in from the garden.

===Chinese===

至于拿尤斯塔斯怎么办，她并没有十分清晰的计划，只有一点是拿定了主意的：管他几天的膳宿。纳蒂所需要的当头一棒已经挨过了，可这一棒的药劲头一轮兴许会消退，果真如此，宅子里养着这么一个尤斯塔斯，就方便多了。她把尤斯塔斯看作一味药。第二服未必用得着，可这剂合药搁在手边总是稳妥的。她又拿了一根香蕉，以防头一根不足以抚平客人受了伤的心灵。随后她回到门廊去。

尤斯塔斯坐在吊床上，闷闷沉思。生活的种种错综复杂压得他好沉。他起初没有觉察伊丽莎白在场，直到发觉她就站在身旁。他刚鼓足劲儿预备逃走，却看清楚她带着丰厚的礼物。

尤斯塔斯向来随时准备进一点小点心——如今比平日更是踊跃，野外的空气和运动叫他的胃口见长。他超然物外似的接过香蕉，仿佛要表明：收下这一根，并不构成对他此后任何行动方针的承诺。那是一根好香蕉，他又伸出手去要另一根。伊丽莎白在他身边坐下，他没有动。如今他确信她怀有善意了。达利什勋爵从园子里进来时，见到的便是这么一幅情景。

===Original===

“Where has your brother gone to?” he asked. “He passed me just now at eight miles an hour. Great Scott! What’s that?”

“It’s a monkey. Don’t frighten him, he’s rather nervous.”

She tickled Eustace under the ear, for their relations were now friendly.

“Nutty went for a walk because he thought he saw it.”

===Chinese===

“你弟弟上哪儿去了？”他问。“刚才他以每小时八英里的速度打我身边冲了过去。我的老天！那是什么？”

“是只猴子。别吓着他，他挺容易受惊的。”

她挠了挠尤斯塔斯耳根的痒痒，如今他们已经是朋友了。

“纳蒂散步去了，因为他恍惚看见了它。”

===Original===

“Thought he saw it?”

“Thought he saw it,” repeated Elizabeth firmly. “Will you remember, Mr. Chalmers, that, as far as he is concerned, this monkey has no existence.”

“I don’t understand.”

Elizabeth explained.

“You see now?”

===Chinese===

“恍惚看见了？”

“恍惚看见了，”伊丽莎白斩钉截铁地重复道。“查默斯先生，你给我记住：至少就纳蒂而言，这只猴子是不存在的。”

“我不明白。”

伊丽莎白解释了一遍。

“这下明白了吧？”

===Original===

“I see. But how long are you going to keep the animal?”

“Just a day or two﻿—in case.”

“Where are you going to keep it?”

“In the outhouse. Nutty never goes there, it’s too near the beehives.”

“I suppose you don’t know who the owner is?”

===Chinese===

“明白了。可你打算把这畜生留多久？”

“不过一两天——以防万一。”

“你打算把它养在哪儿？”

“养在外屋里。纳蒂从来不去那儿，离蜂箱太近了。”

“想必你还不知道它的主人是谁吧？”

===Original===

“Yes, I do; it must be Lady Wetherby.”

“Lady Wetherby!”

“She’s a woman who dances at one of the restaurants. I read in a Sunday paper about her monkey. She has just taken a house near here. I don’t see who else the animal could belong to. Monkeys are rarities on Long Island.”

Bill was silent. “Sudden a thought came like a fullblown rose, flushing his brow.” For days he had been trying to find an excuse for calling on Lady Wetherby as a first step toward meeting Claire again. Here it was. There would be no need to interfere with Elizabeth’s plans. He would be vague. He would say he had just seen the runaway, but would not add where. He would create an atmosphere of helpful sympathy. Perhaps, later on, Elizabeth would let him take the monkey back.

===Chinese===

“不，我知道；必定是韦瑟比夫人的。”

“韦瑟比夫人！”

“一位在一家餐馆里跳舞的女士。我在一份星期日报纸上读到过她的猴子。她刚在这附近租下了一幢房子。这畜生除了她还能是谁家的呢。长岛（Long Island）地面上，猴子可算稀罕物。”

比尔（Bill）沉默了。“蓦然间一个念头涌来，恰似一朵盛放的玫瑰，染红了他的眉宇。”多少天来，他一直在寻一个登门拜访韦瑟比夫人的借口，好迈出同克莱尔（Claire）重逢的第一步。眼下借口送上门来了。无须惊动伊丽莎白的安排。他要含糊其辞。他要说自己刚见到了那个逃亡的家伙，但不补充在哪儿见的。他要营造一种乐于帮忙、满怀同情的气氛。兴许过些时候，伊丽莎白会让他把猴子送回去。

===Original===

“What are you thinking about?” asked Elizabeth.

“Oh, nothing,” said Bill.

“Perhaps we had better stow away our visitor for the night.”

“Yes.”

Elizabeth got up.

===Chinese===

“你在想什么？”伊丽莎白问。

“哦，没什么，”比尔说。

“咱们顶好把客人安顿着过夜了。”

“好。”

伊丽莎白站起身。

===Original===

“Poor, dear Nutty may be coming back at any moment now,” she said.

But poor, dear Nutty did not return for a full two hours. When he did he was dusty and tired, but almost cheerful.

“I didn’t see the brute once all the time I was out,” he told Elizabeth. “Not once!”

Elizabeth kissed him fondly and offered to heat water for a bath; but Nutty said he would take it cold. From now on, he vowed, nothing but cold baths. He conveyed the impression of being a blend of repentant sinner and hardy Norseman. Before he went to bed he approached Bill on the subject of Indian clubs.

===Chinese===

“可怜的、亲爱的纳蒂这会儿随时都可能回来，”她说。

可是可怜的、亲爱的纳蒂足足过了两个钟头才回来。回来的时候，他灰头土脸，一身疲惫，人却近乎欢畅。

“我出去这一整段时间，那畜生一回也没见着，”他对伊丽莎白说。“一回也没有！”

伊丽莎白疼爱地亲了亲他，提出替他烧热水洗澡；纳蒂却说他要洗冷水。他起誓，打今往后，除了冷水澡什么也不沾。他给人的印象，是一个忏悔的罪人同一个吃苦耐劳的北欧壮汉的合成体。上床之前，他找比尔谈起了印度棒的事。

===Original===

“I want to get myself into shape, old top,” he said.

“Yes?”

“I’ve got to cut it out﻿—tonight I thought I saw a monkey.”

“Really?”

“As plain as I see you now.” Nutty gave the clubs a tentative swing. “What do you do with these darned things? Swinging them about and all that? All right, I see the idea. Good night.”

But Bill did not pass a good night. He lay awake long, thinking over his plans for the morrow.

===Chinese===

“我想把自己练出个样子来，老兄，”他说。

“唔？”

“我非戒绝不可——今天夜里，我恍惚看见了一只猴子。”

“是吗？”

“跟这会儿看见你一样一清二楚。”纳蒂拿起棒来试着抡了一抡。“这些该死的玩意儿怎么个使法？抡来抡去，诸如此类？行了，我明白其中道理了。晚安。”

可是比尔这一夜过得并不安生。他睁着眼睛躺了半宿，把明天的计划翻来覆去地掂量。

## XIX / 第十九章

===Original===
Bill leaned his back against the gate that separated the grounds of the bee farm from the high road, and mused pleasantly. He was alone. Elizabeth was walking up the drive on her way to the house to tell the news to Nutty. James, the cat, who had come down from the roof of the outhouse, was sharpening his claws on a neighboring tree. After the whirl of excitement that had been his portion for the past few hours, the peace of it all appealed strongly to Bill. It suited the mood of quiet happiness which was upon him.

Quietly happy, that was how he felt now that it was all over. The white heat of emotion had subsided to a gentle glow of contentment conducive to thought. He thought tenderly of Elizabeth. She had turned to wave her hand before going into the house, and he was still smiling fatuously. Wonderful girl! Lucky chap he was! Rum, the way they had come together! Talk about Fate, what?

He stooped to tickle James, who had finished stropping his claws and was now enjoying a friction massage against his leg, and began to brood on the inscrutable ways of Fate.

===Chinese===
比尔背靠着那扇隔开养蜂场园地与公路的栅栏门，美滋滋地想开了心事。这里只剩他一个人。伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）正沿着车道往宅子走，要把这个消息告诉纳蒂（Nutty）。猫儿詹姆斯（James）从棚屋顶上下来了，正在旁边一棵树上磨爪子。过去几个钟头，落在他名下的尽是旋风般的惊险热闹；两相对照，这一片安宁便格外对比尔的胃口，正合他此刻心头那一泓静悄悄的幸福。

静静的幸福——一切都了结之后，他此刻正是这般心境。情感的白热已经退潮，化作一汪温润的餍足微光，正宜沉思。他一往情深地想起伊丽莎白。进宅之前，她回身挥了挥手，他到这会儿还在痴痴地笑。好一个了不得的姑娘！他这小子多走运！两人凑到一块儿的经过，多么邪门！这就叫命运，是不是？

他俯下身去逗詹姆斯——它磨够了爪子，这会儿正贴着他的腿享受摩擦按摩——一边冥想起命运那不可索解的路数来。

===Original===
Rum thing, Fate! Most extraordinary!

Suppose he had never gone down to Marvis Bay that time. He had wavered between half a dozen places; it was pure chance that he had chosen Marvis Bay. If he hadn’t he would never have met old Nutcombe. Probably old Nutcombe had wavered between half a dozen places too. If they hadn’t both happened to choose Marvis Bay they would never have met. And if they hadn’t been the only visitors there they might never have got to know each other. And if old Nutcombe hadn’t happened to slice his approach shots he would never have put him under an obligation. Queer old buster, old Nutcombe, leaving a fellow he hardly knew from Adam a cool million quid just because he cured him of slicing.

It was at this point in his meditations that it suddenly occurred to Bill that he had not yet given a thought to what was immeasurably the most important of any of the things that ought to be occupying his mind just now. What was he to do about this Lord Dawlish business?

===Chinese===
命运这东西真怪！怪到了家！

假如那一回他没下马维斯湾（Marvis Bay）去。他曾在五六处地方之间拿不定主意，选中马维斯湾纯属偶然。不去，他就绝遇不见老纳特科姆（Nutcombe）。多半老纳特科姆当初也在五六处地方之间摇摆过。两个人不是恰好都挑了马维斯湾，就永远照不上面。而那处海滨若不是只他们两位游人，兴许也熟络不成。再倘若老纳特科姆的近洞球碰巧不打右曲，他也决计承不上这份大恩。老纳特科姆这个怪老家伙，就因为治好了他右曲球的毛病，竟把整整一百万英镑留给了一个他几乎素不相识的家伙。

冥想行至此处，比尔猛然省悟：眼下有一件事，论要紧，比这一切加起来还不知要紧多少，他竟还没顾上想它半分。达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）这档子事，他究竟打算怎么办？

===Original===
Life at Brookport had so accustomed him to being plain Bill Chalmers that it had absolutely slipped his mind that he was really Lord Dawlish, the one man in the world whom Elizabeth looked on as an enemy. What on earth was he to do about that? Tell her?

But if he told her, wouldn’t she chuck him on the spot?

This was awful. The dreamy sense of well-being left him. He straightened himself to face this problem, ignoring the hints of James, who was weaving circles about his legs expectant of more tickling. A man cannot spend his time tickling cats when he has to concentrate on a dilemma of this kind.

Suppose he didn’t tell her? How would that work out? Was a marriage legal if the cove who was being married went through it under a false name? He seemed to remember seeing a melodrama in his boyhood, the plot of which turned on that very point. Yes, it began to come back to him. An unpleasant bargee with a black mustache had said, “This woman is not your wife!” and caused the dickens of a lot of unpleasantness; but there in its usual slipshod way memory failed. Had subsequent events proved the bargee right or wrong? It was a question for a lawyer to decide. Jerry Nichols would know. Well, there was plenty of time, thank goodness, to send Jerry Nichols a prepaid cable, asking for his professional opinion, and to get the straight tip long before the wedding day arrived.

===Chinese===
在布鲁克波特（Brookport）过日子，他做惯了普普通通的比尔·查默斯（Bill Chalmers），以至于压根忘了自己其实是达利什勋爵——而普天之下，伊丽莎白眼里当作仇家的，单单就是这一位。这可怎么办才好？对她实话实说？

可他若说了，她会不会当场把他甩了？

这可糟透了。梦一般的康乐之感舍他而去。他直起身子来面对这个难题，顾不上詹姆斯的暗示——它正绕着他的两条腿兜圈子，巴望再多挠上几把。人碰上这样的两难，非得凝神专志不可，总不能把光阴都耗在逗猫上。

假如他不说呢？往后又如何？成亲的那位老兄若是顶着假名行完婚礼，这桩婚事还作数不作数？他仿佛记得少年时看过一出情节剧，剧情恰恰就吊在这个关节上。对，渐渐想起来了。一个蓄着黑髭、讨人嫌的驳船船夫说过：“这个女人不是你的妻子！”惹出了天翻地覆的一场不痛快；可记忆照例马虎潦草，偏偏在这里断了线。后来的剧情到底证明船夫是错是对？这得请律师来断。杰里·尼科尔斯（Jerry Nichols）准知道。好在，谢天谢地，时间有的是：拍一封电费先付的越洋电报向杰里·尼科尔斯讨一句行家意见，离大喜的日子还早得很，准信儿稳稳赶在前头。

===Original===
Laying this part of it aside for the moment and assuming that the thing could be worked, what about the money? Like a chump, he had told Elizabeth on the first day of his visit that he hadn’t any money except what he made out of his job of secretary of the club. He couldn’t suddenly spring five million dollars on her and pretend that he had forgotten all about it till then.

Of course he could invent an imaginary uncle or something and massacre him during the honeymoon. Something in that. He pictured the thing in his mind. Breakfast. Elizabeth doing out the scrambled eggs. “What’s the matter, Bill? Why did you exclaim like that? Is there some bad news in the letter you are reading?” “Oh, it’s nothing﻿—only my Uncle John’s died and left me five million dollars.”

The scene worked out so well that his mind became a little above itself. It suggested developments of serpentine craftiness. Why not get Jerry Nichols to write him a letter about his Uncle John and the five millions? Jerry liked doing that sort of thing. He would do it like a shot, and chuck in a lot of legal words to make it sound right. It began to be clear to Bill that any move he took﻿—except full confession, at which he jibbed﻿—was going to involve Jerry Nichols as an ally; and this discovery had a soothing effect on him. It made him feel that the responsibility had been shifted. He couldn’t do anything till he had consulted Jerry, so there was no use in worrying.
