And, being one of those rare persons who can cease worrying instantly when they have convinced themselves that it is useless, he dismissed the entire problem from his mind and returned to the more congenial occupation of thinking of Elizabeth.

===Chinese===
这一层暂且按下，姑且假定这事办得成——那么钱呢？他傻得可以，来做客的头一天就对伊丽莎白交了底：他除了在俱乐部当秘书挣的那几个钱，别无进项。总不能冷不丁朝她捧出五百万美元，再装作此前忘得精光。

当然，他可以凭空捏一位舅舅之类，好在蜜月里把他“克”死。这倒有几分门道。他在心里把这幕戏排演了一遍。早餐桌。伊丽莎白正分盛炒蛋。“怎么啦，比尔？你干吗那样惊叫？你看的信里有什么坏消息吗？”“哦，没什么——不过是我约翰舅舅过世了，给我留下五百万美元。”

这一幕排演得如此圆满，他的脑子不由得有几分得意忘形，又生出蛇一样七弯八绕的狡狯花样来。何不叫杰里·尼科尔斯写封信来，讲讲他约翰舅舅和那五百万的事？杰里就好办这路差事，保管一口应承，还要塞进一大把法律字眼，叫它听着头头是道。比尔渐渐看明白了：他无论走哪步棋——全盘招供这一条除外，这他打退堂鼓——都得拉上杰里·尼科尔斯做同盟；这一发现叫他心头一松，只觉担子已经卸到了别人肩上。不先请教杰里，他寸步难行，那便犯不着发愁。而他恰是那种稀罕人：一旦说服了自己愁也无用，愁便立时收场。他把整个难题从脑子里撵了出去，回头操起那桩更对胃口的营生——想伊丽莎白。

===Original===
It was a peculiar feature of his position that he found himself unable to think of Elizabeth without also thinking of Claire. He tried to, but failed. Every virtue in Elizabeth seemed to call up the recollection of a corresponding defect in Claire. It became almost mathematical. Elizabeth was so straight﻿—on the level, they called it over here. Claire was a corkscrew among women. Elizabeth was sunny and cheerful. Querulousness was Claire’s besetting sin. Elizabeth was such a pal. Claire had never been that. The effect that Claire had always had on him was to deepen the conviction, which never really left him, that he was a bit of an ass. Elizabeth, on the other hand, bucked him up and made him feel as if he really amounted to something.

How different they were! Their very voices﻿—Elizabeth had a sort of quiet, soothing, pleasant voice, the kind of voice that somehow suggested that she thought a lot of a chap without her having to say it in so many words. Whereas Claire’s voice﻿—he had noticed it right from the beginning﻿—Claire’s voice﻿—

While he was trying to make clear to himself just what it was about Claire’s voice that he had not liked, he was granted the opportunity of analyzing by means of direct observation its failure to meet his vocal ideals, for at this moment it spoke behind him.

===Chinese===
他的处境有一桩怪处：想伊丽莎白，竟断然撇不开克莱尔（Claire）。他想撇，撇不成。伊丽莎白每一样长处，都勾起克莱尔身上相应一处短处的记忆，几乎成了一道一一对应的算术。伊丽莎白那样光明磊落——用这边的行话，叫“来真的”。克莱尔是女人堆里的一把螺丝锥。伊丽莎白开朗爽利。动辄抱怨是克莱尔戒不掉的老毛病。伊丽莎白是个真朋友。克莱尔从来够不上。克莱尔素日给他的影响，是把他心底那点从未散尽的自觉越垫越深：他这人有点呆。伊丽莎白却相反，给他打气，让他觉得自己到底还算个人物。

两个人多么不同！连嗓音都两样——伊丽莎白的嗓音沉静、熨帖、悦耳，那一种嗓音不知怎么就叫人觉出：她看重你这个人，虽则一个字也不用挑明。至于克莱尔的嗓音——他打一开始就留了心——克莱尔的嗓音——

他正竭力向自己说清楚，克莱尔的嗓音究竟哪一点叫他不喜欢，机会就送上了门——此刻他可以凭实地考察来剖析这副嗓音何以不副他的听觉理想了，因为它正在他背后开口。

===Original===
“Bill!”

She was standing in the road, her head still covered with that white, filmy something which had commended itself to Mr. Pickering’s eye. She was looking at him in a way that seemed somehow to strike a note of appeal. She conveyed an atmosphere of softness and repentance, a general suggestion of prodigal daughters revisiting old homesteads.

“We seem always to be meeting at gates, don’t we!” she said with a faint smile.

It was a deprecating smile, wistful.

===Chinese===
“比尔！”

她立在路上，头上依然蒙着那件入过皮克林先生（Mr. Pickering）法眼的白色轻纱物事。她望着他，那眼神不知怎么总敲着一点恳求的调门。她周身透着一种柔顺与悔悟的气息，一言以蔽之，活脱是浪荡女儿重返故园的架势。

“咱们好像总在门边相逢，可不是么！”她说着，露出浅浅一笑。

那是歉抑的一笑，怅怅然的。

===Original===
“Bill!” she said again, and stopped. She laid her left hand lightly on the gate. Bill had a sort of impression that there was some meaning behind this action, that if he were less of a chump than Nature had made him, he would at this point receive some sort of a revelation. But being as Nature had made him, he did not get it. He was one of those men to whom a girl’s left hand is simply a girl’s left hand, irrespective of whether it wears rings on its third finger or not.

This having become evident to Claire after a moment of silence, she withdrew her hand in rather a disappointed way and prepared to attack the situation from another angle.

“Bill, I’ve come to say something to you.”

Bill was looking at her curiously. He could not have believed that, even after what had happened, he could face her with such complete detachment, that she could so extraordinarily not matter. He felt no resentment toward her. It was simply that she had gone out of his life.

===Chinese===
“比尔！”她又说了一声，便停住了。她把左手轻轻搭在门上。比尔隐隐觉得这动作背后藏着点意思：倘若他生得少几分呆气，此刻就该得着某种启示了。可他天生就是这副坯子，启示没有降临。他属于那样一种男人：姑娘的左手在他眼里就只是姑娘的左手，无名指上戴没戴戒指，全不相干。

静了一霎，克莱尔看明白了这一层，相当失望地收回手，准备换个路数进攻。

“比尔，我是来同你说句话的。”

比尔好奇地打量她。他先前怎么也不会相信：纵然出了那些事，他面对她竟能如此全然超然，她在他心里竟能这样出奇地无足轻重。他对她并无怨怼。只不过是，她已经走出了他的人生。

===Original===
“Bill, I’ve been a fool.”

He made no reply to this, for he could think of no reply that was sufficiently polite. “Yes?” sounded as if he meant to say that that was just what he had expected. “Really?” had a sarcastic ring. He fell back on facial expression, to imply that he was interested and that she might tell all.

Claire looked away down the road and began to speak in a low, quick voice.

“I’ve been a fool all along. I lost you through being a fool. When I saw you dancing with that girl in the restaurant I didn’t stop to think. I was angry. I was jealous. I ought to have trusted you, but﻿—Oh, well, I was a fool.”

===Chinese===
“比尔，我干了傻事。”

这句话他没有答理，因为他想不出一句足够客气的回话。“是吗？”听着像在说：这原在我意料之中。“真的吗？”又带了讥诮。他只得退而借助面部表情，示意自己颇有兴致，请她尽管照实讲来。

克莱尔把目光投向大路深处，开了口，声音又低又急。

“我从头到尾都在干傻事。就因为犯傻，我把你丢了。在餐馆里看见你同那姑娘跳舞，我脑子一热，什么也没想。我火了。我妒了。我本该信你的，可是——唉，反正我干了傻事。”

===Original===
“My dear girl, you had a perfect right﻿—”

“I hadn’t. I was an idiot. Bill, I’ve come back to ask you if you can’t forgive me.”

“I wish you wouldn’t talk like that﻿—there’s nothing to forgive.”

The look which Claire gave him in answer to this was meek and affectionate, but inwardly she was wishing that she could bang his head against the gate. His slowness was maddening. Long before this he should have leaped into the road in order to fold her in his arms. Her voice shook with the effort she had to make to keep it from sharpness.

“I mean, is it too late? I mean, can you really forgive me? Oh, Bill”﻿—she stopped herself by the fraction of a second from adding “you idiot!”﻿—“can’t we be the same again to each other? Can’t we﻿—pretend all this has never happened?”

===Chinese===
“我亲爱的姑娘，你本来完全有权利——”

“我没有。我是个白痴。比尔，我回来是要问问你，看你还能不能原谅我。”

“求你别这么讲话——没有什么可原谅的。”

克莱尔听了这话，望他的眼神温顺而深情，心里却在盼着能把他的脑袋往门上撞。他这迟钝真叫人发疯。早在这之前许久，他就该一个箭步跳下路去，把她搂进怀里。她竭力不让嗓音变尖，声气都发了颤。

“我是说，是不是太迟了？我是说，你当真能原谅我吗？哦，比尔”——只差一丝丝工夫，她把“你这蠢货！”咽了回去——“咱们彼此还能不能同从前一样？咱们能不能——就当这一切从来没发生过？”

===Original===
Exasperating as Bill’s wooden failure to play the scene in the spirit in which her imagination had conceived it was to Claire, several excuses may be offered for him. He had opened the evening with a shattering blow at his faith in woman. He had walked twenty miles at a rapid pace. He had heard shots and found corpses and carried the latter by the tail across country. Finally he had had the stunning shock of discovering that Elizabeth Boyd loved him. He was not himself. He found a difficulty in concentrating. With the result that, in answer to this appeal from a beautiful girl whom he had once imagined that he loved, all he could find to say was: “How do you mean?”

Claire, never an adept at patience, just succeeded in swallowing the remark that sprang into her mind. It was incredible to her that a man could exist who had so little intuition. She had not anticipated the necessity of being compelled to put the substance of her meaning in so many blunt words, but it seemed that only so could she make him understand.

“I mean, can’t we be engaged again, Bill?”

Bill’s overtaxed brain turned one convulsive handspring, and came to rest with a sense of having dislocated itself. This was too much. This was not right. No fellow at the end of a hard evening ought to have to grapple with this sort of thing. What on earth did she mean, springing questions like that on him? How could they be engaged? She was going to marry someone else, and so was he. Something of these thoughts he managed to put into words:

===Chinese===
比尔木头木脑，接不上她想象中排定的这场戏，克莱尔叫他气得要死；不过替他设身着想，倒也有几条可恕之情。这一晚开锣，他对女人的信心先就吃了一记粉碎性的重击。他迈开大步急走，走了二十英里。他听过枪响，撞见过尸首，还拎着死者的尾巴越野跑了一程。末了，他又挨了那记天旋地转的震骇：发现伊丽莎白·博伊德（Elizabeth Boyd）爱他。他神不守舍，心思聚不拢。结果便是，面对这位他曾自以为爱过的美丽姑娘的一片恳求，他所能搜罗出来的答话只有一句：“你这是什么意思？”

克莱尔向来与耐心无缘，好歹把涌上舌尖的那句话咽了回去。世上竟存在直觉这样稀薄的男人，她觉得匪夷所思。她原没料到，竟落到这步田地，非得把自己那点意思拆成这样直统统的大白话；可看来非如此，他便听不懂。

“我是说，咱们能不能重新订婚，比尔？”

比尔那颗超载的脑子痉挛着翻了一个筋斗，落定下来，只觉得关节脱了臼。这太过分了。这不对头。苦熬了一个晚上的人，不该再叫他招架这路难题。她冷不丁朝他抛这样的问话，究竟安的什么心？他们还订什么婚？她就要嫁别人了，他也就要娶别人了。这些念头，他好歹把几分塞进了话里：

===Original===
“But you’re engaged to﻿—”

“I’ve broken my engagement with Mr. Pickering.”

“Great Scott! When?”

“Tonight. I found out his true character. He is cruel and treacherous. Something happened﻿—it may sound nothing to you, but it gave me an insight into what he really was. Polly Wetherby had a little monkey, and just because it bit Mr. Pickering he shot it.”

“Pickering!”

“Yes. He wasn’t the sort of man I should have expected to do a mean, cruel thing like that. It sickened me. I gave him back his ring then and there. Oh, what a relief it was! What a fool I was ever to have got engaged to such a man!”

===Chinese===
“可你不是跟——订了婚吗——”

“我同皮克林先生把婚约退了。”

“老天爷！什么时候？”

“今天夜里。我看清了他的真面目。他又残忍又奸诈。出了一件事——在你听来也许算不得什么，可它叫我看了个透，他到底是个什么货色。波莉·韦瑟比（Polly Wetherby）养着一只小猴，就因为它咬了皮克林先生一口，他一枪把它打死了。”

“皮克林！”

“是。干出这样下作残忍的事来，他本不该是我料想中的那路人。这叫我恶心透了。我当场就把戒指退了给他。哦，真是如释重负！我竟跟这样一个人订过婚，傻透了！”

===Original===
Bill was puzzled. He was one of those simple men who take their fellows on trust, but who, if once that trust is shattered, can never recover it. Like most simple men, he was tenacious of ideas when he got them, and the belief that Claire was crooked was not lightly to be removed from his mind. He had found her out during his self-communion that night, and he could never believe her again. He had the feeling that there was something behind what she was saying. He could not put his finger on the clue, but that there was a clue he was certain.

“I only got engaged to him out of pique. I was angry with you, and﻿—well, that’s how it happened.”

Still Bill could not believe. It was plausible. It sounded true. And yet some instinct told him that it was not true. And while he waited, perplexed, Claire made a false step.

The thing had been so close to the top of her mind ever since she had come to the knowledge of it that it had been hard for her to keep it down. Now she could keep it down no longer.

“How wonderful about old Mr. Nutcombe, Bill!” she said.

===Chinese===
比尔满腹狐疑。他属于那种老实人：惯于把旁人一概往好里信，可信一旦碎了，便再也焊不拢。同大多数老实人一样，认下的理他就攥住不放；克莱尔心术不正这个念头，不是轻易请得出他的脑子的。那天夜里他独自省察的当儿就识破了她，往后再也信她不过。他总觉得她这番话背后藏着名堂。名堂的线索他指不出来，可有线索，这个他拿得稳。

“我跟他订婚，不过是一时负气。我在生你的气，后来——唉，事情就那样成了。”

比尔仍旧不能信。这话讲得通。听着也像真的。可有一种本能告诉他，这不是真的。他正满腹疑团地等着，克莱尔走了一步错棋。

那件事打她知道的那一刻起，就一直顶在她心念的最上层，按下去好不容易。这会儿，她再也按不住了。

“比尔，老纳特科姆先生的事，多叫人称奇呀！”她说。

===Original===
A vast relief rolled over Bill. Despite his instinct, he had been wavering. But now he understood. He had found the clue.

“You got my letter, then?”

“Yes, it was forwarded on from the theater. I got it tonight.”

Too late she realized what she had said, and the construction that an intelligent man would put on it. Then she reflected that Bill was not an intelligent man. She shot a swift glance at him. To all appearances he suspected nothing.

“It went all over the place,” she hurried on. “The people at the Portsmouth theater sent it to the London office, who sent it home, and mother mailed it on to me.”

“I see.”

===Chinese===
一阵大大的如释重负漫过了比尔。尽管本能在替他掌舵，他其实一直摇摆不定。这会儿他明白了。线索找着了。

“这么说，我的信你收到了？”

“收到了，从剧院转过来的。今晚才到我手里。”

等她醒悟自己说了什么，已经迟了——一个机灵的男人会从中听出话外之音。转而一想，比尔不是机灵的男人。她飞快地瞟了他一眼。看样子他丝毫没有起疑。

“信一路转了好几道手，”她赶忙接下去。“朴次茅斯（Portsmouth）剧院的人把它寄给伦敦的办事处，伦敦又寄回家去，我妈妈再转寄给我。”

“原来如此。”

===Original===
There was a silence. Claire drew a step nearer.

“Bill!” she said softly.

Bill shut his eyes. The moment had come which he had dreaded. Not even the thought that she was crooked, that she had been playing with him, could make it any better. She was a woman and he was a man. That was all that mattered, and nothing could alter it.

“I’m sorry,” he said. “It’s impossible.”

Claire stared at him in amazement. She had not been prepared for this. He met her eyes, but every nerve in his body was protesting.

===Chinese===
一阵沉默。克莱尔又凑近一步。

“比尔！”她柔声唤道。

比尔闭上了眼睛。他一直惧怕的这一刻到了。她是心术不正，她是耍了他——连这样的念头也无济于事。她是女人，他是男人。要紧的尽在于此，任什么也更改不得。

“对不起，”他说。“这不可能。”

克莱尔惊愕地瞪住他。这一手她没有防到。他迎着她的目光，可他浑身的每一根神经都在抗议。

===Original===
“Bill!”

“I’m sorry.”

“But, Bill!”

He set his teeth. It was just as bad as he had thought it would be.

===Chinese===
“比尔！”

“对不起。”

“可是，比尔！”

他咬紧牙关。情形同他预想的一样糟。

===Original===
“But, Bill, I’ve explained. I’ve told you how﻿—”

“I know.”

Claire’s eyes opened wide.

“I thought you loved me.” She came closer. She pulled at his sleeve. Her voice took on a note of soft raillery. “Don’t be absurd, Bill! You mustn’t behave like a sulky schoolboy. It isn’t like you, this. You surely don’t want me to humble myself more than I have done.” She gave a little laugh. “Why, Bill, I’m proposing to you! I know I’ve treated you badly, but I’ve explained why. You must be just enough to see that it wasn’t altogether my fault. I’m only human. And if I made a mistake I’ve done all I can to undo it. I﻿—”

===Chinese===
“可是，比尔，我都解释过了。我都跟你说了是怎么——”

“我知道。”

克莱尔的眼睛睁得老大。

“我还当你爱我。”她凑得更近，扯着他的衣袖。嗓音里添了一层软软的打趣。“别犯傻了，比尔！你可不许闹得像个赌气的小学生。这样子可不像你。你总不至于还要我再自贬身分吧，我已经贬得够低了。”她轻轻一笑。“瞧你，比尔，这可是我在向你求婚呢！我知道我待你不好，可缘故我也解释过了。你得公道些，看清这不全怪我。我也不过是个人。错既已铸成，能补救的我都尽力补了。我——”

===Original===
“Claire, listen. I’m engaged!”

She fell back. For the first time the sense of defeat came to her. She had anticipated many things. She had looked for difficulties. But she had not expected this. A feeling of cold fury surged over her at the way Fate had tricked her. She had gambled recklessly on her power of fascination, and she had lost. Mr. Pickering, at that moment brooding in solitude in the smoking-room of Lady Wetherby’s house, would have been relieved could he have known how wistfully she was thinking of him.

“You’re engaged?”

“Yes.”

“Well!” She forced another laugh. “How very﻿—rapid of you! To whom?”

===Chinese===
“克莱尔，你听我说。我订婚了！”

她退了一步。败局之感头一回临到她身上。她料到过许多种可能。她防备过重重难关。独独没有防到这一手。命运这般戏弄她，一股冰冷的怒火在她胸中涌起。她拿自己的魅力孤注一掷，输了个精光。此刻皮克林先生正独自一个，闷坐在韦瑟比夫人（Lady Wetherby）宅子的吸烟室里出神；他若能知道她这会儿正怎样怅然念着自己，心里倒能宽解几分。

“你订婚了？”

“订了。”

“好啊！”她又挤出一声笑。“你这手脚可真——快得可以！跟谁？”

===Original===
“To Elizabeth Boyd.”

“I’m afraid I’m very ignorant, but who is Elizabeth Boyd? The ornate lady you were dancing with at the restaurant?”

“No!”

“Who, then?”

“She is old Ira Nutcombe’s niece. The money ought to have been left to her. That was why I came over to America, to see if I could do anything for her.”

“And you’re going to marry her? How very romantic﻿—and convenient! What an excellent arrangement for her. Which of you suggested it?”

===Chinese===
“跟伊丽莎白·博伊德。”

“恐怕我孤陋寡闻，可伊丽莎白·博伊德是谁？就是那天在餐馆里同你跳舞的那位花枝招展的女士？”

“不是！”

“那是谁？”

“她是老艾拉·纳特科姆（Ira Nutcombe）的侄女。那笔钱本该是她的。我当年漂洋过海来美国，就是想看看能不能替她做点什么。”

“然后你就要娶她了？多浪漫——又多便当！替她打算，这安排真是再好没有。这个主意是谁起的头？”

===Original===
Bill drew in a deep breath. All this was, he supposed, unavoidable, but it was not pleasant.

Claire suddenly abandoned her pose of cool amusement. The fire behind it blazed through.

“You fool!” she cried passionately. “Are you blind? Can’t you see that this girl is simply after your money? A child could see it.”

Bill looked at her steadily.

===Chinese===
比尔深深吸了一口气。这些盘问，他寻思原是躲不过的，可实在不是滋味。

克莱尔霍地抛开那副冷眼含笑的假面，底下的火蹿了上来。

“你这傻瓜！”她激愤地叫道。“你眼睛瞎了吗？看不出这姑娘图的就是你的钱？小孩子都看得出来。”

比尔定定地望着她。

===Original===
“You’re quite wrong. She doesn’t know who I am.”

“Doesn’t know who you are? What do you mean? She must know by this time that her uncle left his money to you.”

“But she doesn’t know that I am Lord Dawlish. I came to America under another name. She knows me as Chalmers.”

===Chinese===
“你大错特错了。她不知道我是谁。”

“不知道你是谁？什么意思？她舅舅把钱留给了你，到这会儿她总该如何道了。”

“可她不知道我就是达利什勋爵。我到美国来用的是另一个名字。她认得的我，姓查默斯。”

===Original===
Claire was silent for a moment.

“How did you get to know her?” she asked more quietly.

“I met her brother by chance in New York.”

“By chance!”

“Quite by chance. A man I knew in England lent me his rooms in New York. He happened to be a friend of Boyd’s. Boyd came to call on him one night, and found me.”

===Chinese===
克莱尔沉默了片刻。

“你是怎么认识她的？”她问得平静了些。

“我在纽约碰巧遇上她哥哥。”

“碰巧！”

“千真万确是碰巧。我在英格兰认识的一个熟人，把他在纽约的寓所借给我住。他恰好是博伊德（Boyd）的朋友。一天夜里博伊德来拜访他，撞见了我。”

===Original===
“Odd! Had your mutual friend been away from New York long?”

“Some months.”

“And in all that time Mr. Boyd had not discovered that he had left. They must have been great friends! What happened then?”

“Boyd invited me down here.”

===Chinese===
“怪了！你们这位共同的朋友，离开纽约很久了吗？”

“好几个月了。”

“那么些日子里，博伊德先生竟没发觉他早已离开。两位的交情一定深厚得很！后来呢？”

“博伊德请我到这儿来的。”

===Original===
“Down here?”

“They live in this house.”

“Is Miss Boyd the girl who keeps the bee farm?”

“She is.”

===Chinese===
“到这儿？”

“他们就住这幢房子。”

“博伊德小姐就是办养蜂场的那位姑娘？”

“正是。”

===Original===
Claire’s eyes suddenly lit up. She began to speak in a louder voice.

“Bill, you’re an infant, a perfect infant! Of course she’s after your money. Do you really imagine for one instant that this Elizabeth Boyd of yours and her brother don’t know as well as I do that you are really Lord Dawlish? I always thought you had a trustful nature! You tell me the brother met you by chance. Chance! And invited you down here. I bet he did! He knew his business! And now you’re going to marry the girl so that they will get the money, after all! Splendid! Oh, Bill, you’re a wonderful, wonderful creature. Your innocence is touching.”

She swung round.

“Good night,” she called over her shoulder.

He could hear her laughing as she went down the road.

===Chinese===
克莱尔的两眼霍地亮了。她开口的声音高了上去。

“比尔，你是个孩子，一个十足道的孩子！她图的当然是你的钱。你真有那么一瞬当真以为，你这位宝贝伊丽莎白·博伊德和她哥哥，会不像我一样明明白白知道你其实就是达利什勋爵？我一向就说，你这人天生一副轻信的脾气！你还对我说，她哥哥是碰巧遇上你的。碰巧！还请你到这儿来。那还用问，准是他干的！他懂行得很！如今你又要娶这姑娘，好教他们到头来把那笔钱弄到手！妙极了！哦，比尔，你真是个了不起、了不起的角色。你这份天真，真叫人心疼。”

她猛地转身。

“晚安。”她回头抛下一声。

她沿大路去了，一路的笑声传进他的耳朵。

## XV / 第十五章

===Original===

Lady Wetherby was feeling battered. She had not realized how seriously Roscoe Sherriff took the art of publicity, nor what would be the result of the half-hour he had spent at the telephone on the night of the departure of Eustace.

Roscoe Sherriff’s eloquence had fired the imagination of editors. There had been a notable lack of interesting happenings this summer. Nobody seemed to be striking or murdering or having violent accidents. The universe was torpid. In these circumstances the escape of Eustace seemed to present possibilities. Reporters had been sent down. There were three of them living in the house now, and Wrench’s air of disapproval was deepening every hour.

It was their strenuousness which had given Lady Wetherby that battered feeling. There was strenuousness in the air, and she resented it on her vacation. She had come to Long Island to vegetate, and with all this going on round her vegetation was impossible. She was not long alone. Wrench entered. “A gentleman to see you, m’lady.”

===Chinese===

韦瑟比夫人只觉得浑身像挨了一顿棒打。她先前没有意识到，罗斯科·谢里夫把宣传这门艺术当真到了何种地步，也没有料到，尤斯塔斯出逃那晚他在电话机前耗掉的半个钟头，会结出怎样的果实。

罗斯科·谢里夫的雄辩点燃了诸位编辑的想象力。今年夏天，引人入胜的新闻实在寥落。似乎没人罢工，没人行凶，也没人遭遇横祸，整个宇宙昏昏欲睡。在这种情况下，尤斯塔斯的出逃便显出了可供开发的苗头。记者们被打发下乡，如今已有三位住进宅里，伦奇脸上不以为然的神色正一个钟头深过一个钟头。

叫韦瑟比夫人生出这副挨打之感的，正是这班人的干劲。空气里都透着干劲，而她正在休假，对此满心抵触。她到长岛来，原打算做一株与世无争的植物；可身边闹出这许多动静，这植物是做不成了。她并没独自待上多久。伦奇进来了。“夫人，有位先生要见您。”

===Original===

In the good old days, when she had been plain Polly Davis, of the personnel of the chorus of various musical comedies, Lady Wetherby would have suggested a short way of disposing of this untimely visitor; but she had a position to keep up now.

“From some darned paper?” she asked wearily.

“No, m’lady. I fancy he is not connected with the press.”

There was something in Wrench’s manner that perplexed Lady Wetherby, something almost human, as if Wrench were on the point of coming alive. She did not guess it, but the explanation was that Bill, quite unwittingly, had impressed Wrench. There was that about Bill that reminded the butler of London and dignified receptions at the house of the Dowager Duchess of Waveney. It was deep calling to deep.

===Chinese===

若在从前的美好岁月，她还只是平平无奇的波莉·戴维斯，在各出音乐喜剧的伴舞队里混饭吃，韦瑟比夫人早就给这位不识相的访客支一个速战速决的打发法子了；可如今她得撑住身份。

“又是哪家讨厌的报纸？”她有气无力地问。

“不是，夫人。我瞧他不像报界的人。”

伦奇的态度里有点什么东西叫韦瑟比夫人纳闷，那东西近乎人情，仿佛伦奇眼看就要活转过来了。她没有猜到缘由；其实说来简单：比尔不知不觉之间镇住了伦奇。比尔身上有股气息，叫这位管家想起伦敦，想起韦弗尼老公爵夫人府上那些庄重体面的招待会。此正是深渊与深渊相应。

===Original===

“Where is he?”

“I have shown him into the drawing room, m’lady.”

Lady Wetherby went downstairs and found a large young man awaiting her, looking nervous.

Bill was feeling nervous. A sense of the ridiculousness of his mission had come upon him. After all, he asked himself, what on earth had he got to say? A presentiment had come upon him that he was about to look a perfect ass. At the sight of Lady Wetherby his nervousness began to diminish. Lady Wetherby was not a formidable person. In spite of her momentary peevishness, she brought with her an atmosphere of geniality and camaraderie.

===Chinese===

“人在哪儿？”

“我请他在客厅候着，夫人。”

韦瑟比夫人下楼来，见一个大个子青年正等着她，神色紧张。

比尔心里正发虚。他忽然觉出自己这趟差事何等可笑。他自问：说到底，他究竟有什么话好说？一种预感袭上心头：他就要出大丑了。可一见韦瑟比夫人，他的紧张便开始消退。韦瑟比夫人并不可怖。尽管她此刻一脸愠色，她身上却带着一股和气与义气。

===Original===

“It’s about your monkey,” he said, coming to the point at once.

Lady Wetherby brightened.

“Oh! Have you seen it?”

He was glad that she put it like that.

===Chinese===

“是为您那只猴子的事，”他开门见山地说。

韦瑟比夫人眼睛一亮。

“哦！您见着它了？”

她这样问法，他正求之不得。

===Original===

“Yes. It came round our way last night.”

“Where is that?”

“I am staying at a farm near here, a place they call Flack’s. The monkey got into one of the rooms.”

“Yes?”

“And then﻿—er﻿—then it got out again, don’t you know.”

===Chinese===

“是的。昨天夜里它跑到我们那边去了。”

“你们那边在哪儿？”

“我就住在附近一座农场上，那地方大家管它叫弗拉克家。猴子钻进了其中一个房间。”

“后来呢？”

“然后呢——呃——然后它又跑出去了，您明白吧。”

===Original===

Lady Wetherby looked disappointed.

“So it may be anywhere now?” she said.

In the interests of truth, Bill thought it best to leave this question unanswered.

“Well, it’s very good of you to have bothered to come out and tell me,” said Lady Wetherby. “It gives us a clue, at any rate. Thank you. At least we know now in which direction it went.”

===Chinese===

韦瑟比夫人面露失望。

“这么说，它如今可能在任何地方了？”她说。

为着维护真理起见，比尔认为这个问题还是不答为妙。

“嗯，劳您特意跑来告诉我，真是太承情了，”韦瑟比夫人说，“好歹给了我们一条线索。谢谢您。至少我们如今知道它是往哪个方向去的。”

===Original===

There was a pause. Bill gathered that the other was looking on the interview as terminated, and that she was expecting him to go, and he had not begun to say what he wanted to say. He tried to think of a way of introducing the subject of Claire that should not seem too abrupt.

“Er﻿—” he said.

“Well,” said Lady Wetherby simultaneously.

“I beg your pardon.”

“You have the floor,” said Lady Wetherby. “Shoot!”

===Chinese===

一阵冷场。比尔听出对方已经把这次会面当作了结，正等着他告辞，而他想说的话却还没起头。他竭力想找出一个由头，把克莱尔的话头引出来，又不显得太突兀。

“呃——”他说。

“怎么？”韦瑟比夫人同时说。

“您先请。”

“发言权归你了，”韦瑟比夫人说，“开火吧！”

===Original===

It was not what she had intended to say. For months she had been trying to get out of the habit of saying that sort of thing, but she still suffered relapses. Only the other day she had told Wrench to check some domestic problem or other with his hat, and he had nearly given notice. But if she had been intending to put Bill at his ease she could not have said anything better.

“You have a Miss Fenwick staying with you, haven’t you?” he said.

Lady, Weatherby beamed.

===Chinese===

这不是她存心要说的话。几个月来她一直想戒掉这一类口头禅，却仍旧时时旧病复发。就在前几天，她还吩咐伦奇把家里一桩什么麻烦事“随帽子一并寄存了”，险些逼得他递辞呈。不过她若是存心要让比尔松快下来，再也说不出更见效的话了。

“府上住着一位芬威克小姐，是吧？”他说。

韦瑟比夫人眉开眼笑。

===Original===

“Do you know Claire?”

“Yes, rather!”

“She’s my best friend. We used to be in the same company when I was in England.”

“So she has told me.”

“She was my bridesmaid when I married Lord Wetherby.”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===

“你认识克莱尔？”

“认识，太认识啦！”

“她是我顶要好的朋友。我在英国那阵子，我们同在一个剧团里。”

“她同我说起过。”

“我嫁韦瑟比勋爵的时候，她还是我的伴娘。”

“是啊。”

===Original===

Lady Wetherby was feeling perfectly happy now, and when Lady Wetherby felt happy she always became garrulous. She was one of those people who are incapable of looking on anybody as a stranger after five minutes’ acquaintance. Already she had begun to regard Bill as an old friend.

“Those were great days,” she said cheerfully. “None of us had a beau, and Algie was the hardest-up of the whole bunch. After we were married we went to the Savoy for the wedding breakfast, and when it was over and the waiter came with the check Algie said he was sorry, but he had had a bad week at Lincoln and hadn’t the price on him. He tried to touch me, but I passed. Then he had a go at the best man, but the best man had nothing in the world but one suit of clothes and a spare collar. Claire was broke, too, so the end of it was that the best man had to sneak out and pawn my watch and the wedding-ring.”

The room rang with her reminiscent laughter, Bill supplying a bass accompaniment. Bill was delighted. He had never hoped that it would be granted to him to become so rapidly intimate with Claire’s hostess. Why, he had only to keep the conversation in this chummy vein for a little while longer and she would give him the run of the house.

“Miss Fenwick isn’t in now, I suppose?” he asked.

===Chinese===

韦瑟比夫人此刻心满意足，而韦瑟比夫人一心满意足，话匣子便关不上了。她是那路人：相处不过五分钟，就再没法把谁当外人看。这会儿她已经开始把比尔当老朋友了。

“那些日子真够味儿，”她兴高采烈地说，“我们谁都没有对象，阿尔吉又是一伙人里手头最紧的。完婚之后，我们上萨伏伊大饭店（the Savoy）吃婚宴，吃到末了，堂倌拿着账单过来，阿尔吉说抱歉得很，林肯赛马场（Lincoln）上那一周他手气背透了，身上没带那笔钱。他想跟我借，我没理他。又去找男傧相，可那位男傧相全世界统共只有一套衣裳，外加一个备用的假领。克莱尔也是囊空如洗，闹到最后，只好委屈男傧相溜出去，把我的表和结婚戒指当掉了。”

满屋子都响着她追怀往事的笑声，比尔在底下配着低音。比尔乐坏了。他何曾指望过，自己竟能同克莱尔的女主人亲热得这般神速。嘿，只消再把谈话在这条亲热的轨道上多保持一会儿，她就要把他当自家人，任他在宅子里自由出入了。

“芬威克小姐这会儿不在家吧？”他问。

===Original===

“No, Claire’s out with Dudley Pickering. You don’t know him, do you?”

“No.”

“She’s engaged to him.”

It is an ironical fact that Lady Wetherby was by nature one of the firmest believers in existence in the policy of breaking things gently to people. She had a big, soft heart and she hated hurting her fellows. As a rule, when she had bad news to impart to anyone, she administered the blow so gradually and with such mystery as to the actual facts that the victim, having passed through the various stages of imagined horrors, was genuinely relieved when she actually came to the point to find that all that had happened was that he had lost all his money. But now in perfect innocence, thinking only to pass along an interesting bit of information, she had crushed Bill as effectively as if she had used a club for that purpose.

===Chinese===

“不在，克莱尔同达德利·皮克林出门了。你不认识他吧？”

“不认识。”

“她同他订婚了。”

说来讽刺，韦瑟比夫人天生就是天下最笃信“坏话要慢慢说”这门方针的人之一。她心肠又大又软，最见不得同伴受伤害。照她的惯例，凡要向谁报告噩耗，她总把这一击施放得无比和缓，把实情包裹得无比神秘，害得那受难者历遍想象中诸般恐怖的阶梯，待到她终于言归正传，发现原来不过是自己破了产，反倒如释重负，千恩万谢。可这一回，她全然出于无心，只想着传递一件有趣的新闻，却把比尔砸了个结结实实，简直同抡起一根木棒专门行凶没有两样。

===Original===

“I’m tickled to death about it,” she went on, as it were, over her hearer’s prostrate body. “It was I who brought them together, you know. I wrote telling Claire to come out here on the Atlantic, knowing that Dudley was sailing on that boat. I had a hunch they would hit it off together. Dudley fell for her right away, and she must have fallen for him, for they had only known each other for about a couple of weeks when they came and told me they were engaged. It happened last Sunday.”

“Last Sunday!”

It had seemed to Bill a moment before that he would never again be capable of speech, but this statement dragged the words out of him. Last Sunday! Why, it was last Sunday that Claire had broken off her engagement with him.

“Last Sunday at nine o’clock in the evening, with a full moon shining and soft music going on offstage. Real third-act stuff.”

===Chinese===

“这事我乐得心花怒放，”她接着说，俨然是从听者横陈的尸身上跨了过去，“是他们俩牵线的就是我，你知道。我写信叫克莱尔搭‘大西洋号’过来，因为我知道达德利正坐那条船。我有预感，他们俩准投缘。达德利一眼就看中了她，她呢，想必也看中了他，因为前后不过认识两三个礼拜，他们就来告诉我，说订婚了。就是上个星期天的事。”

“上个星期天！”

一分钟之前，比尔还觉得自己此生再也发不出人声了，可这句话硬把字眼从他喉咙里拖了出来。上个星期天！嗨，上个星期天，正是克莱尔同他解除婚约的日子。

“上个星期天晚上九点钟，一轮满月当头照着，幕后还奏着轻柔的音乐。十足第三幕收场的派头。”

===Original===

Bill felt positively dizzy. He groped back in his memory for facts. He had gone out for his walk after dinner. They had dined at eight. He had been walking some time. Why, in Heaven’s name, this was the quickest thing in the amatory annals of civilization. His brain was too numbed to work out a perfectly accurate schedule, but it looked as if she must have got engaged to this Pickering person before she met him, Bill, in the road that night.

“It’s a wonderful match for dear old Claire,” resumed Lady Wetherby, twisting the knife in the wound with a happy unconsciousness. “Dudley’s not only a corking good fellow, but he has thirty million dollars stuffed away in the stocking and a business that brings him in a perfectly awful mess of money every year. He’s the Pickering of the Pickering automobiles, you know.”

Bill got up. He stood for a moment holding to the back of his chair before speaking. It was almost exactly thus that he had felt in the days when he had gone in for boxing and had stopped forceful swings with the more sensitive portions of his person.

“That﻿—that’s splendid,” he said. “I﻿—I think I’ll be going.”

“I heard the car outside just now,” said Lady Wetherby. “I think it’s probably Claire and Dudley come back. Won’t you wait and see her?”

===Chinese===

比尔只觉得天旋地转。他在记忆里摸索事实。晚饭后他出门散步。他们是八点开的饭。他已经走了一程了。老天爷在上，这真是文明世界风流账上最快的一笔。他的脑子麻木得排不出一张分毫不差的时间表，可算起来，她同这位皮克林先生订婚，想必赶在那天晚上路上遇见他比尔之前。

“这门亲事对我们亲爱的老克莱尔真是天大的好事，”韦瑟比夫人接着说，一面浑然不觉、兴高采烈地往伤口里拧刀子，“达德利不但是个顶呱呱的好人，还有三千万美元囤在袜筒里，外加一桩生意，每年给他源源送进一笔叫人咋舌的大钱。皮克林汽车的皮克林就是他本人，你知道。”

比尔站起身。他扶着椅背立了片刻，才开口说话。当年他玩拳击，用身上比较娇嫩的部位硬接过几记凶狠的大摆拳，那时的滋味同此刻几乎一模一样。

“那——那真是好极了，”他说，“我——我想我该走了。”

“方才我听见外头有汽车响，”韦瑟比夫人说，“多半是克莱尔和达德利回来了。你不等一等，见见她？”

===Original===

Bill shook his head.

“Well, goodbye for the present then. You must come round again. Any friend of Claire’s﻿—and it was bully of you to bother about looking in to tell me about Eustace.”

Bill had reached the door. He was about to turn the handle when someone turned it on the other side.

“Why, here is Dudley,” said Lady Wetherby. “Dudley, this is a friend of Claire’s.”

===Chinese===

比尔摇了摇头。

“那么，暂且再会。你一定得再来串门。凡是克莱尔的朋友——再说你肯劳神跑一趟，告诉我尤斯塔斯的消息，真是再好不过。”

比尔已经走到门边。正要拧门把，那边先有人把它拧动了。

“哟，达德利来了，”韦瑟比夫人说，“达德利，这是克莱尔的一位朋友。”

===Original===

Dudley Pickering was one of those men who take the ceremony of introduction with a measured solemnity. It was his practice to grasp the party of the second part firmly by the hand, hold it, look into his eyes in a reverent manner, and get off some little speech of appreciation, short, but full of feeling. The opening part of this ceremony he performed now. He grasped Bill’s hand firmly, held it and looked into his eyes. And then having performed his business, he fell down on his lines. Not a word proceeded from him. He dropped the hand and stared at Bill amazedly and﻿—more than that﻿—with fear.

Bill, too, uttered no word. It was not one of whose chatty meetings.

But if they were short on words, both Bill and Mr. Pickering were long on looks. Bill stared at Mr. Pickering. Mr. Pickering stared at Bill.

Bill was drinking in Mr. Pickering. The stoutness of Mr. Pickering﻿—the elderliness of Mr. Pickering﻿—the dullness of Mr. Pickering﻿—all these things he perceived. And illumination broke upon him.

Mr. Pickering was drinking in Bill. The largeness of Bill﻿—the embarrassment of Bill﻿—the obvious villainy of Bill﻿—none of these things escaped his notice. And illumination broke upon him also.

For Dudley Pickering, in the first moment of their meeting, had recognized Bill as the man who had been lurking in the grounds and peering in at the window, the man at whom on the night when he had become engaged to Claire he had shouted “Hi!”

===Chinese===

达德利·皮克林属于那一路人：行介绍之礼必有一套从容不迫的庄严。他的惯例是，把缔约的乙方的手牢牢攥住，握定，虔敬地注视对方的双眼，再致上几句嘉许之辞，短促，却感情充沛。这一套仪程的开场部分，他此刻照章履行了。他把比尔的手牢牢攥住，握定，注视他的双眼。随后，戏做到这一步，他忘了词。他半个字也没吐出来。他撒开手，惊愕地瞪着比尔，而且——不止是惊愕——还带着惧怕。

比尔也一言不发。这可算不上世上那种健谈的会面。

不过这两位，话虽短缺，眼色却充足得很。比尔瞪着皮克林先生，皮克林先生瞪着比尔。

比尔把皮克林先生看了个通透。皮克林先生的肥胖——皮克林先生的老成——皮克林先生的无趣——凡此种种，他一一收入眼底。霎时间豁然开朗。

皮克林先生也把比尔看了个通透。比尔的高大——比尔的局促——比尔那显而易见的奸恶——样样都没能逃过他的眼睛。霎时间他同样豁然开朗。

原来达德利·皮克林在两人照面的头一瞬，就已经认出比尔正是那个潜行园中、扒着窗户往里张望的人，正是他同克莱尔订婚那晚冲着喝过一声“嗨！”的那个人。

===Original===

“Where’s Claire, Dudley?” asked Lady Wetherby.

Mr. Pickering withdrew his gaze reluctantly from Bill.

“Gone upstairs.”

“I’ll go and tell her that you’re here, Mr.﻿–﻿You never told me your name?”

===Chinese===

“克莱尔呢，达德利？”韦瑟比夫人问。

皮克林先生恋恋不舍地把目光从比尔身上收回来。

“上楼去了。”

“我这就去告诉她你来了，先生——你还没告诉我你贵姓呢？”

===Original===

Bill came to life with an almost acrobatic abruptness. There were many things of which at that moment he felt absolutely incapable, and meeting Claire was one of them.

“No, I must be going,” he said hurriedly. “Goodbye.”

He came very near running out of the room. Lady Wetherby regarded the practically slammed door with wide eyes.

“Quick exit of Nut Comedian?” she said. “Whatever was the matter with the man? He’s scorched a trail in the carpet!”

===Chinese===

比尔一下子活了过来，动作利索得近乎杂耍。此时此刻，世上有许多事是他绝对无力胜任的，其中一件便是同克莱尔见面。

“不，我非走不可了，”他慌忙说，“再见。”

他险些是跑着冲出那间屋子的。韦瑟比夫人圆睁双眼，望着那扇形同摔上的房门。

“‘怪人谐星’，急速退场？”她说，“这人到底犯了什么毛病？地毯都快叫他蹿出火星来了！”

===Original===

Mr. Pickering was trembling violently.

“Do you know who that was? He was the man!” said Mr. Pickering.

“What man?”

“The man I caught looking in at the window that night!”

“What nonsense! You must be mistaken. He said he knew Claire quite well.”

===Chinese===

皮克林先生抖得厉害。

“你知道刚才是谁？就是他！就是那个人！”皮克林先生说。

“哪个人？”

“那天晚上叫我撞见扒窗张望的那个人！”

“胡说！你准是弄错了。他说他同克莱尔熟得很。”

===Original===

“But when you suggested that he should meet her he ran.”

This aspect of the matter had not occurred to Lady Wetherby.

“So he did!”

“What did he tell you that showed he knew Claire?”

“Well, now that I come to think of it, he didn’t tell me anything. I did the talking. He just sat there.” Mr. Pickering quivered with combined fear and excitement and inductive reasoning.

===Chinese===

“可你一提议让他见她，他就溜了。”

这一层道理，韦瑟比夫人倒还没想到过。

“他还真是溜了！”

“他说过什么，能证明他认识克莱尔？”

“呃，如今细想起来，他什么也没告诉我。话都归我一个人说。他就坐在那儿。”皮克林先生兼着惧怕、兴奋与归纳推理，浑身乱颤。

===Original===

“It was a trick!” he cried. “Remember what Sherriff said that night when I told you about finding the man looking in at the window. He said that the fellow was spying round as a preliminary move. Today he trumps up an obviously false excuse for getting into the house. Was he left alone in the room at all?”

“Yes. Wrench loosed him in here and then came up to tell me.”

“For several minutes, then, he was alone in the house. Why, he had time to do all he wanted to do!”

“Calm down!”

“I am perfectly calm. But﻿—”

===Chinese===

“这是诡计！”他嚷道，“还记得那天晚上，我说撞见有人扒窗张望，谢里夫是怎么讲的？他说这家伙四下窥探，是先手棋。今天他又编出一个明摆着是假的由头混进宅子。他是不是独自一人留在屋里来着？”

“是。伦奇把他放进这间屋，就上楼告诉我去了。”

“这么说，他有几分钟工夫独自一人在宅子里。嘿，他要把想干的事都干了，时间绰绰有余！”

“你镇定点！”

“我镇定得很。可是——”

===Original===

“You’ve been seeing too many crook plays, Dudley. A man isn’t necessarily a burglar because he wears a decent suit of clothes.”

“Why was he lurking in the grounds that night?”

“You’re just imagining that it was the same man.”

“I am absolutely positive it was the same man.”

“Well, we can easily settle one thing about him, at any rate. Here comes Claire. Claire, old girl,” she said, as the door opened, “do you know a man named﻿—Darn it, I never got his name, but he’s﻿—”

===Chinese===

“盗贼戏你看得太多啦，达德利。穿一身体面衣裳的人，未必就是梁上君子。”

“那他那天晚上潜行在园子里，又是为什么？”

“同一个人，那是你凭空想出来的。”

“我千真万确，就是同一个人。”

“好吧，无论如何，有一样事咱们轻易就能弄明白。克莱尔来了。克莱尔，老姑娘，”门开时她说道，“你认不认识一个叫——该死，我连他的名字都没问着，可他——”

===Original===

Claire stood in the doorway, looking from one to the other.

“What’s the matter, Dudley?” she said.

“Dudley’s gone clean up in the air,” explained Lady Wetherby tolerantly. “A friend of yours called to tell me he had seen Eustace﻿—”

“So that was his excuse, was it?” said Dudley Pickering. “Did he say where Eustace was?”

“No; he said he had seen him, that was all.”

===Chinese===

克莱尔站在门口，目光从这张脸扫到那张脸。

“出什么事了，达德利？”她说。

“达德利气得整个人上了天，”韦瑟比夫人宽宏地解释说，“你的一个朋友来告诉我，说看见尤斯塔斯了——”

“原来这就是他的由头，是不是？”达德利·皮克林说，“他说没说尤斯塔斯在哪儿？”

“没有。他说他见过它，就这么一句。”

===Original===

“An obviously trumped-up story. He had heard of Eustace’s escape, and he knew that any story connected with him would be a passport into the house!”

Lady Wetherby turned to Claire.

“You haven’t told us yet if you know the man. He was a big, tall, broad gazook,” said Lady Wetherby. “Very English.”

“He faked the English,” said Dudley Pickering. “That man was no more an Englishman than I am. He acted well, but I could see the Tenderloin sticking out of him.”

“Be patient with him, Claire,” urged Lady Wetherby. “He’s been going to the movies too much, and thinks every man who has had his trousers pressed is a social gangster. This man was the most English thing I’ve ever seen﻿—talked like this.”

===Chinese===

“明摆着是编的故事。他听说了尤斯塔斯出逃的风声，又知道凡是同它沾边的话头，就是进这座宅子的通行证！”

韦瑟比夫人转向克莱尔。

“你还没说呢，认不认识那个人。是个又高又大、膀阔腰圆的大块头，”韦瑟比夫人说，“英国派头十足。”

“他那英国派头是装的，”达德利·皮克林说，“那人跟我一样，半点英国种也没有。戏演得不坏，可我看得见他浑身上下透着嫩腰区（Tenderloin）的气味。”

“你多担待他些，克莱尔，”韦瑟比夫人劝道，“他电影看得太多，如今谁要是裤线笔挺，他就认定是上流社会混进来的匪徒。这位先生是我平生所见最英国不过的人物——他说话是这样的。”

===Original===

She gave a passable reproduction of Bill’s speech. Claire started.

“I don’t know him!” she cried.

Her mind was in a whirl of agitation. Why had Bill come to the house? What had he said? Had he told Dudley anything?

“I don’t recognize the description,” she said quickly. “I don’t know anything about him.”

“There!” said Dudley Pickering triumphantly.

===Chinese===

她学着比尔的腔调，学了个八九不离十。克莱尔猛地一震。

“我不认识他！”她喊道。

她的心在纷乱的激荡里打转。比尔为什么要到这座宅子里来？他说了什么？他可曾向达德利吐露了什么？

“你描述的这人我不认得，”她赶紧说，“他这个人我一点儿也不知道。”

“听见没有！”达德利·皮克林得意洋洋地说。

===Original===

“It’s queer,” said Lady Wetherby. “You’re sure you don’t know him, Claire?”

“Absolutely sure.”

“He said he was living at a place near here called Flack’s.”

“I know the place,” said Dudley Pickering. “A sinister, tumbledown sort of place. Just where he would be living.”

“I thought it was a bee farm,” said Lady Wetherby. “One of the tradesmen told me about it. I saw a most corkingly pretty girl bicycling down to the village one morning, and they told me she was named Boyd and kept a bee farm at Flack’s.”

===Chinese===

“这就怪了，”韦瑟比夫人说，“你敢肯定你不认识他，克莱尔？”

“千真万确。”

“他说他就住在附近一个叫弗拉克家的地方。”

“我知道那地方，”达德利·皮克林说，“阴气森森、东倒西塌的一个去处。正是他这种人住的地方。”

“我还当那是座养蜂场呢，”韦瑟比夫人说，“有个买卖人同我讲起过。一天早上，我瞧见一个俊得叫人惊叹的姑娘骑车下村去，他们告诉我，她姓博伊德，在弗拉克家经营一座养蜂场。”

===Original===

“A blind!” said Mr. Pickering stoutly. “The girl’s the man’s accomplice. It’s quite easy to see the way they work. The girl comes and settles in the place so that everybody knows her. That’s to lull suspicion. Then the man comes down for a visit and goes about cleaning up the neighboring houses. You can’t get away from the fact that this summer there have been half a dozen burglaries down here; and nobody has found out who did them.”

Lady Wetherby looked at him indulgently.

“And now,” she said, “having got us scared stiff, what are you going to do about it?”

“I am going,” he said with determination, “to take steps.”

He went out quickly, the keen, tense man of affairs.

===Chinese===

“幌子！”皮克林先生斩钉截铁地说，“那姑娘就是那人的同伙。他们那一套怎么运转，一望便知。先由姑娘来这地方落脚，叫人人都认得她。这是为了麻痹人的疑心。然后那男人下来做客，把左近的宅子挨家扫荡。这个夏天此地接连出了六起盗窃案，至今没人查出作案的人是谁——这个事实，你绕不过去。”

韦瑟比夫人纵容地望着他。

“那么如今呢，”她说，“把我们吓得魂飞魄散之后，你打算怎么办呢？”

“我打算，”他斩钉截铁地说，“采取步骤。”

他快步走了出去，一副干练紧绷的事务家气派。

===Original===

“Bless him!” said Lady Wetherby. “I’d no idea your Dudley had so much imagination, Claire. He’s a perfect bombshell.”

Claire laughed shakily.

“It is odd, though,” said Lady Wetherby meditatively, “that this man should have said that he knew you, when you don’t﻿—”

Claire turned impulsively.

“Polly, I want to tell you something. Promise you won’t tell Dudley. I wasn’t telling the truth just now. I do know this man. I was engaged to him once!”

===Chinese===

“老天保佑他！”韦瑟比夫人说，“我还真不知道，你这达德利有这么大的想象力，克莱尔。他简直是一颗活炸弹。”

克莱尔颤着声笑了笑。

“不过也真蹊跷，”韦瑟比夫人沉思着说，“你不认识他，这人却说他认识你——”

克莱尔一阵冲动，转过身来。

“波莉，我有件事要告诉你。答应我别告诉达德利。方才我没说实话。这个人我是认识的。我跟他订过婚！”

===Original===

“What!”

“For goodness’ sake don’t tell Dudley.”

“But﻿—”

“It’s all over now; but I used to be engaged to him.”

“Not when I was in England?”

“No, after that.”

===Chinese===

“什么！”

“看在老天爷分上，千万别告诉达德利。”

“可是——”

“如今都过去了；我从前同他订过婚。”

“不是我在英国那阵子吧？”

“不是，是那以后。”

===Original===

“Then he didn’t know you are engaged to Dudley now?”

“N-no. I﻿—I haven’t seen him for a long time.”

Lady Wetherby looked remorseful.

“Poor man! I must have given him a jolt! But why didn’t you tell me about him before?”

“Oh, I don’t know.”

“Oh, well, I’m not inquisitive. There’s no rubber in my composition. It’s your affair.”

===Chinese===

“那么他不知道你如今同达德利订了婚？”

“不——不知道。我——我好久没见着他了。”

韦瑟比夫人一脸懊悔。

“可怜的人！我这一下准把他震得够呛！可你先前怎么从来没跟我讲过他？”

“唉，我也说不上。”

“哦，好了，我不是爱打听的人。我这副构造里没有‘橡皮脖子’（rubber，谓好伸长脖子张望者）的成分。那是你自己的事。”

===Original===

“You won’t tell Dudley?”

“Of course not. But why not? You’ve nothing to be ashamed of.”

“No, but﻿—”

“Well, I won’t tell him anyway. But I’m glad you told me about him. Dudley was so eloquent about burglars that he almost had me going. I wonder where he rushed off to?”

Dudley Pickering had rushed off to his bedroom and was examining a revolver there. He examined it carefully, keenly. Preparedness was Dudley Pickering’s slogan. He looked rather like a stout sheriff in a film drama.

===Chinese===

“你不会告诉达德利吧？”

“当然不会。可有什么不能说的？你又没什么可丢人的。”

“是没有，可是——”

“好了好了，反正我不会告诉他。不过你肯同我讲他，我很高兴。达德利那套窃贼论讲得天花乱坠，险些把我也说信了。他慌慌张张又跑哪儿去了呢？”

达德利·皮克林一溜烟跑回卧室，正在那里查验一把左轮手枪。他查验得仔细，查验得锐利。常备不懈是达德利·皮克林的座右铭。他那模样，活脱脱是电影剧里一位胖墩墩的警长。

## XVI / 第十六章

===Original===

In the interesting land of India, where snakes abound and scorpions are common objects of the wayside, a native who has had the misfortune to be bitten by one of the latter pursues an admirably commonsense plan. He does not stop to lament, nor does he hang about analyzing his emotions. He runs and runs and runs, and keeps on running until he has worked the poison out of his system. Not until then does he attempt introspection.

Lord Dawlish, though ignorant of this fact, pursued almost identically the same policy. He did not run on leaving Lady Wetherby’s house, but he took a very long and very rapid walk, than which in times of stress there are few things of greater medicinal value to the human mind. To increase the similarity, he was conscious of a curious sense of being poisoned. He felt stifled﻿—in want of air.

Bill was a simple young man and he had a simple code of ethics. Above all things he prized and admired and demanded from his friends the quality of straightness. It was his one demand. He had never actually had a criminal friend, but he was quite capable of intimacy with even a criminal, provided only that there was something spacious about his brand of crime and that it did not involve anything mean or underhand.

===Chinese===

在那片妙趣横生的印度大地上，蛇类孳生，蝎子是路旁随处可见的寻常物件；不幸教蝎子蜇了的当地人，奉行一套令人叹赏的极合常识的方针。他不停下来哀叹，也不磨蹭着剖析自己的情绪，只是跑啊，跑啊，跑，一直跑到毒汁从身体里排净为止。到那时，他才肯作内省的功夫。

达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）虽然不知此中道理，做法却几乎同这一方针一般无二。他离开韦瑟比夫人（Lady Wetherby）的宅子时没有跑，却是来了一场又长又快的步行——人到了紧张关头，论对心神的药效，很少有东西比得上这个。相似之处还有一层：他觉出一种古怪的中了毒似的感觉。他憋闷——透不过气来。

比尔是个单纯的年轻人，道德准则也单纯。在朋友身上，他最珍视、最敬慕也最苛求的，是方正这一品质。这是他唯一的要求。他事实上从没有结交过罪犯，可即便同罪犯，他也尽可以亲厚相与，只消此人那路罪案做得有几分大开大合的气派，不掺半点卑鄙鬼祟的意味。

===Original===

It was the fact that Mr. Breitstein, whom Claire had wished him to insinuate into his club, though acquitted of actual crime, had been proved guilty of meanness and treachery, that had so prejudiced Bill against him. The worst accusation that he could bring against a man was that he was not square, that he had not played the game.

Claire had not been square. It was that, more than the shock of surprise at Lady Wetherby’s news, that had sent him striding along the State Road at the rate of five miles an hour, staring before him with unseeing eyes. She had fooled him. She had lied to him. A sudden recollection of their last interview brought a dull flush to Bill’s face and accelerated his speed. He felt physically ill.

It was not immediately that he had arrived at even this sketchy outline of his feelings. For perhaps a mile he walked as the scorpion stung natives ran﻿—blindly, wildly, with nothing in his mind but a desire to walk faster and faster, to walk as no man had ever walked before. And then﻿—one does not wish to be unduly realistic, but the fact is too important to be ignored﻿—he began to perspire. And hard upon that unrefined but wonder-working flow came a certain healing of spirit. Dimly at first, but every moment more clearly, he found it possible to think.

===Chinese===

克莱尔曾想叫他施展手腕，把布赖特斯坦（Mr. Breitstein）安插进自己的俱乐部；此人虽免了真正犯罪的实据，卑鄙与背信的罪名却是坐实了的——比尔因此对他恶感极深。他能加给一个人的最重的指控，便是此人不够方正，没有半点比赛风度。

克莱尔就不方正。正是这一点，而不光是听韦瑟比夫人说出那桩消息时的震惊，教他以一小时五英里的步子沿州道大步流星，两眼直瞪瞪望着前方，什么也没看见。她耍了他。她骗了他。蓦地记起两人最后一次会面的光景，比尔脸上泛起一阵暗红，脚下愈发加快。他浑身不适，像生了病。

就连这副粗具轮廓的心绪，也不是他一下子就理出来的。头一英里上下，他走得同被蝎子蜇了的土人跑的一个样——盲目，狂乱，脑子里除了一味要走得更快、再快些、要走得开天辟地头一份，什么都没有。随后——这话未免失之粗鄙，可事实重大，不容回避——他出汗了。紧随着这股不登大雅之堂却有回天之效的涌流，他的心灵开始某种程度的愈合。起先还影影绰绰，可是每一分钟都比前一分钟更清晰：他发现自己能够思考了。

===Original===

In a man of Bill’s temperament there are so many qualities wounded by a blow such as he had received that it is hardly surprising that his emotions, when he began to analyze them, were mixed. Now one, now another of his wounds presented itself to his notice. And then individual wounds would become difficult to distinguish in the mass of injuries. Spiritually he was in the position of a man who has been hit simultaneously in a number of sensitive spots by a variety of hard and hurtful things. He was as little able, during the early stages of his meditations, to say where he was hurt most, as a man who had been stabbed in the back, bitten in the ankle, hit in the eye, smitten with a blackjack and kicked on the shin in the same moment of time. All that such a man would be able to say with certainty would be that unpleasant things had happened to him; and that was all that Bill was able to say.

Little by little, walking swiftly the while, he began to make a rough inventory. He sorted out his injuries, catalogued them. It was perhaps his self-esteem that had suffered least of all, for he was by nature modest. He had a saving humility, valuable in a crisis of this sort.

But he had looked up to Claire. He had thought her straight. And all the time that she had been saying those things to him that night of their last meeting she had been engaged to another man, a fat, bald, doddering, senile fool, whose only merit was his money. Scarcely a fair description of Mr. Pickering, but in a man in Bill’s position a little bias is excusable.

===Chinese===

像比尔这样的性子，吃了这样一记重击，伤到的痛处太多，因而待到他着手剖析，只见百感杂陈，几乎不足为奇。一时这一处伤惹他注目，一时又是那一处；接着，各处伤便混作一团，彼此难辨。在精神上，他的处境好比一个人同时被种种坚硬伤人的家伙打中了多处要害。冥想初期，他说不出自己哪儿伤得最重，正如一个人在同一刻后背着了一刀、脚踝教人咬了一口、眼睛吃了拳、脑袋挨了一记黑杰克短棍、迎面骨又着了一脚，同样说不出哪儿最疼。这样的人唯一说得准的一句话，就是倒霉的事全叫他摊上了；比尔能说的，也不过这一句。

他一面疾走，一面渐渐开始作一份粗略的清点，把伤一处一处理出来，编目入册。伤得最轻的或许要数自尊心，因为他天性谦抑，有一种能救急的谦卑，正宜于这类危局。

可是他一向敬重克莱尔（Claire），当她为人方正。殊不知在最后一次相会的那夜，她一边对他说着那些话，一边早已许给了另一个男人——一个臃肿秃顶、老态龙钟、昏聩糊涂的蠢货，唯一的长处是有钱。这样形容皮克林先生（Mr. Pickering）未免太不公道，但落在比尔如今的境地，稍稍存点偏见，情有可原。

===Original===

Bill walked on. He felt as if he could walk forever. Automobiles whirred past, honking peevishly, but he heeded them not. Dogs trotted out to exchange civilities, but he ignored them. The poison in his blood drove him on.

And then quite suddenly and unexpectedly the fever passed. Almost in mid-stride he became another man, a healed, sane man, keenly aware of a very vivid thirst and a desire to sit down and rest before attempting the ten miles of cement road that lay between him and home. Half an hour at a roadhouse completed the cure. It was a weary but clearheaded Bill who trudged back through the gathering dusk.

He found himself thinking of Claire as of someone he had known long ago, someone who had never touched his life. She seemed so far away that he wondered how she could ever have affected him for pain or pleasure. He looked at her across a chasm. This is the real difference between love and infatuation, that infatuation can be slain cleanly with a single blow. In the hour of clear vision which had come to him Bill saw that he had never loved Claire. It was her beauty that had held him, that and the appeal which her circumstances had made to his pity. Their minds had not run smoothly together. Always there had been something that jarred, a subtle antagonism. And she was crooked.

===Chinese===

比尔走个不停。他觉得自己能一直走到地老天荒。汽车嗖嗖掠过，赌气似的揿着喇叭，他不理会。狗儿颠颠地跑上来要互道寒暄，他不理睬。血里的毒汁驱策他向前。

后来，那股热潮忽地退了，来得全无征兆。几乎在一跨步之间，他换了一个人——一个痊愈的、神志清明的人，只敏锐地觉得渴得厉害，想坐下来歇口气，再去对付横在他与家之间的那十英里水泥路。在一家路边酒馆里坐上半点钟，治疗便告完成。穿过渐浓的暮色往回跋涉的比尔，已是倦怠而头脑清明。

他发觉自己想起克莱尔来，如同想起多年前的故人，一个从不曾真正触及他生命的人。她显得那样遥远，他简直纳闷：这样的人当初怎能叫他痛苦，叫他欢喜。他是隔着深渊望她。爱情与痴迷的真正分别就在这里：痴迷可以一刀下去，杀得干干净净。在这神清目明的一刻，比尔看清了：他从未爱过克莱尔。拴住他的是她的美貌，此外便是她的境遇勾动他的怜惜。两颗心从来没有合过拍，总有什么东西硌着，一种微妙的抵牾。何况她为人不正。

===Original===

Almost unconsciously his mind began to build up an image of the ideal girl, the girl he would have liked Claire to be, the girl who would conform to all that he demanded of woman. She would be brave. He realized now that, even though it had moved his pity, Claire’s querulousness had offended something in him. He had made allowances for her, but the ideal girl would have had no need of allowances. The ideal girl would be plucky, cheerfully valiant, a fighter. She would not admit the existence of hard luck.

She would be honest. Here, too, she would have no need of allowances. No temptation would be strong enough to make her do a mean act or think a mean thought, for her courage would give her strength, and her strength would make her proof against temptation. She would be kind. That was because she would also be extremely intelligent, and, being extremely intelligent, would have need of kindness to enable her to bear with a not very intelligent man like himself. For the rest, she would be small and alert and pretty and﻿—fair-haired﻿—and brown-eyed﻿—and she would keep a bee farm and her name would be Elizabeth Boyd.

Having arrived with a sense of mild astonishment at this conclusion, Bill found, also to his surprise, that he had walked ten miles without knowing it and that he was turning in at the farm gate. Somebody came down the drive, and he saw that it was Elizabeth.

===Chinese===

不知不觉间，他心里开始搭起一个理想姑娘的形象——他但愿克莱尔是的那样的姑娘，凡他对女子有所期待处无不合辙的姑娘。她要勇敢。他如今才明白，克莱尔那副怨天尤人的脾气，虽说曾惹动他的怜惜，却也触犯了他心里的某样东西。他一向容让她，可理想的姑娘无须谁容让。理想的姑娘要有胆气，欢欢喜喜地奋勇争先，是个斗士。她绝不承认世上有“倒霉”二字。

她要诚实。这一层同样无须谁容让。天底下没有一种诱惑够分量，能叫她做一件卑鄙的事、起一个卑鄙的念头，因为她的胆气会给她力量，她的力量会教她对诱惑百毒不侵。她要善良。那是因为她还得出奇地聪明；而正因出奇地聪明，才须得善良作本钱，好忍受一个像他自己这样不太聪明的人。至于其余，她要娇小、机灵、标致，还是——金头发——棕眼睛——还经营着一座养蜂场，而她的名字，就叫伊丽莎白·博伊德（Elizabeth Boyd）。

比尔得出了这个结论，心头微微一惊；接着又吃一惊地发现，自己不知不觉竟已走了十英里，正拐进农场的院门。有个人从车道上迎面下来，他看清了，是伊丽莎白。

===Original===

She hurried to meet him, small and shadowy in the uncertain light. James, the cat, walked rheumatically at her side. She came up to Bill, and he saw that her face wore an anxious look. He gazed at her with a curious feeling that it was a very long time since he had seen her last.

“Where have you been?” she said, her voice troubled. “I couldn’t think what had become of you.”

“I went for a walk.”

===Chinese===

她匆匆迎上来，明灭不定的光里，小小的一个影影绰绰的身影。猫儿詹姆斯（James）迈着一副风湿腿，一步一挨地走在她身边。她到了比尔跟前，他看出她面带焦色。他怔怔地望着她，心里有一种奇怪的感觉：仿佛打上次见她，已经隔了很久很久。

“你上哪儿去了？”她说，声音里满是不安。“我都想不出你出了什么事。”

“我散步去了。”

===Original===

“But you’ve been gone hours and hours.”

“I went to a place called Morrisville.”

“Morrisville!” Elizabeth’s eyes opened wide. “Have you walked twenty miles?”

“Why, I﻿—I believe I have.”

===Chinese===

“可你一去去了好几个钟头。”

“我走到了一个叫莫里斯维尔（Morrisville）的地方。”

“莫里斯维尔！”伊丽莎白眼睛睁得滚圆。“你走了二十英里？”

“呃，我——我相信是走了。”

===Original===

It was the first time he had been really conscious of it. Elizabeth looked at him in consternation. Perhaps it was the association in her mind of unexpected walks with the newly born activities of the repentant Nutty that gave her the feeling that there must be some mental upheaval on a large scale back of this sudden ebulliation of long-distance pedestrianism. She remembered that the thought had come to her once or twice during the past week that all was not well with her visitor, and that he had seemed downcast and out of spirits.

She hesitated.

“Is anything the matter, Mr. Chalmers?”

“No,” said Bill decidedly. He would have found a difficulty in making that answer with any ring of conviction earlier in the day, but now it was different. There was nothing whatever the matter with him now. He had never felt happier.

===Chinese===

这一点他是头一回真切地意识到。伊丽莎白惊惶地望着他。或许是在她心里，这场来路不明的远足同悔改中的纳蒂（Nutty）新萌发的种种活动连在了一起，她便觉得，这突如其来的长途徒步狂热背后，必有一场声势浩大的精神剧变。她记起过去一个星期里，这念头曾一两回掠过心头：她这位房客有些不对劲，看上去垂头丧气、意兴阑珊。

她迟疑了一下。

“出了什么事吗，查默斯先生（Mr. Chalmers）？”

“没有。”比尔斩钉截铁地说。要搁在今天早些时候，这个回答他很难答出几分确凿的底气；此刻却不同了。如今他百事顺遂，什么毛病也没有。他这一辈子还没这样快活过。

===Original===

“You’re sure?”

“Absolutely. I feel fine.”

“I thought﻿—I’ve been thinking for some days﻿—that you might be in trouble of some sort.”

Bill swiftly added another to that list of qualities which he had been framing on his homeward journey. That girl of his would, of course, be angelically sympathetic.

===Chinese===

“真的没事？”

“千真万确。我好得很。”

“我还当——这几天我一直在想——你兴许遇上了什么麻烦。”

比尔立刻往归途上拟就的那张品质清单里又添了一条：他那姑娘，自然还得有天使一般的体贴心肠。

===Original===

“It’s awfully good of you,” he said, “but honestly I feel like﻿—I feel great.”

The little troubled look passed from Elizabeth’s face. Her eyes twinkled.

“You’re really feeling happy?”

“Tremendously.”

“Then let me damp you. We’re in an awful fix!”

===Chinese===

“你真是太好了，”他说，“不过说真的，我觉得自己——我觉得好极了。”

那一点愁容从伊丽莎白脸上褪去了。她眼里闪出狡黠的光。

“你当真觉得快活？”

“快活透了。”

“那我来给你泼点冷水。咱们眼下正陷在大麻烦里！”

===Original===

“What! In what way?”

“About the monkey.”

“Has he escaped?”

“That’s the trouble﻿—he hasn’t.”

“I don’t understand.”

“Come and sit down and I’ll tell you. It’s a shame to keep you standing after your walk.”

===Chinese===

“什么！怎么个麻烦法？”

“为了那只猴子。”

“它逃了？”

“麻烦就在这儿——它没逃。”

“我不明白。”

“来，坐下，我讲给你听。走了这么些路还让你干站着，太不像话了。”

===Original===

They made their way to the massive stone seat which Mr. Flack, the landlord, had bought at a sale and dumped in a moment of exuberance in the farm grounds.

“This is the most hideous thing on earth,” said Elizabeth casually, “but it will do to sit on. Now tell me, why did you go to Lady Wetherby’s this afternoon?”

It was all so remote, it seemed so long ago that he had wanted to find an excuse for meeting Claire again, that for a moment Bill hesitated in actual perplexity, and before he could speak Elizabeth had answered the question for him.

“I suppose you went out of kindness of heart to relieve the poor lady’s mind,” she said. “But you certainly did the wrong thing. You started something!”

===Chinese===

两人朝那张敦实的石凳走去——那是房主弗拉克先生（Mr. Flack）在一场拍卖上买来的家伙，兴头一来，随手撂在了农场场院里。

“这是天下顶丑的物件，”伊丽莎白随口说，“不过坐一坐还凑合。好了，说吧，今天下午你上韦瑟比夫人家做什么去了？”

一切都那样隔世，他想找个借口再见克莱尔一面的往事，仿佛已是上辈子的事，比尔一时真给问怔住了；还没等他开口，伊丽莎白已替他作答。

“我猜你是发了善心，去给那位可怜的太太宽宽心，”她说。“可你这事准是办砸了。你捅了马蜂窝了！”

===Original===

“But I don’t understand. Of course I didn’t tell her the animal was here.”

“What did you tell her?”

“I said I had seen it, don’t you know.”

“That was enough.”

“I’m awfully sorry.”

“Oh, we shall pull through all right, but we must act at once. We must be swift and resolute. We must saddle our chargers and up and away, and all that sort of thing. Show a flash of speed,” she explained kindly, at the sight of Bill’s bewildered face.

===Chinese===

“可我不明白。我当然没告诉她那畜生在这儿。”

“那你告诉她什么了？”

“我说我见过它了，你知道的。”

“这就够了。”

“实在抱歉。”

“哦，咱们总能安然渡过去的，只是必须立刻行动。要快，要果断。要给战马备好鞍，翻身上马，扬长而去，诸如此类。要拿出风驰电掣的劲头，”她见比尔一脸茫然，好心地解释道。

===Original===

“But what has happened?”

“The press is on our trail. I’ve been interviewing reporters all the afternoon.”

“Reporters!”

===Chinese===

“可到底出了什么事？”

“报界盯上咱们的踪迹了。一下午我都在应付记者的采访。”

“记者！”

===Original===

“Millions of them. The place is alive with them. Keen, hatchet-faced young men, and every one of them was the man who really unraveled some murder mystery or other, though the police got the credit for it. They told me so.”

“But, I say, how on earth﻿—”

“﻿—did they get here? I suppose Lady Wetherby invited them.”

“But why?”

===Chinese===

“有好几百万。这地方都叫他们爬满了。一个个目光锐利、脸似斧削的年轻先生，而且个个都正是当年真正侦破过某一桩凶杀疑案的那一位，只是功劳叫警察领了去。这是他们亲口告诉我的。”

“可是，我说，他们究竟怎么会——”

“——找到这儿来的？我猜是韦瑟比夫人请来的。”

“可为什么呢？”

===Original===

“She wants the advertisement, of course. I know it doesn’t sound sensational﻿—a lost monkey; but when it’s a celebrity’s lost monkey it makes a difference. Suppose King George had lost a monkey, wouldn’t your London newspapers give it a good deal of space? Especially if it had thrown eggs at one of the ladies in waiting and bitten the Duke of Norfolk in the leg? That’s what our visitor has been doing apparently. At least he threw eggs at the scullery maid and bit a millionaire. It’s practically the same thing. At any rate, there it is. The newspaper men are here, and they seem to regard this farm as their center of operations. I had the greatest difficulty in inducing them to go home to their well-earned dinners. They wanted to camp out on the place. As it is, there may still be some of them round, hiding in the grass with notebooks, and telling each other in whispers that they were the men who really solved the murder mystery. What are we going to do about it?”

Bill had no suggestions.

“You realize our position? I wonder if we could be arrested for kidnapping? The monkey is far more human than most of the millionaire children who get kidnapped. It’s an awful fix. Did you know that Lady Wetherby is going to offer a reward for the animal?”

“No, really?”

===Chinese===

“她要的是宣传，那还用说。我知道这事儿听上去不够耸动——一只走失的猴子；可要是名流失的猴子，那就大不一样了。假如乔治国王（King George）丢了一只猴子，你们伦敦的报纸难道不会拿出大块版面？尤其这猴子要是还朝一位侍女官扔过鸡蛋、在诺福克公爵（Duke of Norfolk）腿上咬过一口？我们那位客人干的，看来正是这一路。至少，它朝厨下的女仆扔了鸡蛋，还咬了一位百万富翁。实际上是一码事。总之，情形就摆在这儿了。报馆的人全来了，而且看样子打算把这农场当作他们的作战大本营。我好说歹说，才劝动他们回家去吃他们理应享用的晚饭。他们原打算就在场子里扎营。就眼下这光景，四周保不齐还有人转悠，揣着笔记本藏在草窠里，交头接耳，互相念叨自己正是当年真正破获那桩凶杀疑案的人。咱们拿这事怎么办呢？”

比尔毫无主意。

“你可明白咱们的处境？我怀疑咱们会不会让人家当绑票犯抓起来？这猴子比大多数遭绑架的百万富翁家的孩子通人性得多。局面糟透了。你可知道，韦瑟比夫人打算为这畜生悬赏？”

“不知道，真的？”

===Original===

“Five hundred dollars!”

“Surely not!”

“She is. I suppose she feels she can enter it to necessary expenses for publicity and still be ahead of the game, taking into account the advertising she’s going to get.”

“She said nothing about that when I saw her.”

“No, because it won’t be offered until tomorrow or the day after. One of the newspaper men told me that. The idea is, of course, to make the thing exciting just when it would otherwise be dying as a news item. Cumulative interest. It’s a good scheme, too, but it makes it very awkward for me. I don’t want to be in the position of keeping a monkey locked up with the idea of waiting until somebody starts a bull market in monkeys. I consider that that sort of thing would stain the spotless escutcheon of the Boyds. It would be a low trick for that old established family to play. Not but what poor dear Nutty would do it like a shot,” she concluded meditatively.

===Chinese===

“五百美元！”

“不会吧！”

“她就是要。我猜她盘算的是，这笔钱可以记进宣传的必要开销，再把她将到手的广告效应算上，只赚不赔。”

“我见她的时候，她对这一层只字未提。”

“是没提，因为悬赏要到明天或者后天才登出来。这是一个报馆的人告诉我的。用意自然是趁这桩新闻眼看要凉下去的时候，把它重新炒热。让兴趣利上滚利。这法子确实高明，可叫我难堪透顶。我不愿意落到这步田地：把一只猴子锁在屋里，专门等着哪一位兴出猴子行情、掀起一场牛市。依我看，那种行径要玷污博伊德家那面一尘不染的盾徽。对那样一个源远流长的世家来说，这是下三滥的伎俩。话虽这样说，可怜亲爱的纳蒂多半会马上照办不误。”她若有所思地收了尾。

===Original===

Bill was impressed.

“It does make it awkward, what?”

“It makes it more than awkward, what! Take another aspect of the situation. The night before last my precious Nutty, while ruining his constitution with the demon rum, thought he saw a monkey that wasn’t there and instantly resolved to lead a new and better life. He hates walking, but he has now begun to do his five miles a day. He loathes cold baths, but he now wallows in them. I don’t know his views on Indian clubs, but I should think that he has a strong prejudice against them, too, but now you can’t go near him without being brained. Are all these good things to stop as quickly as they began? If I know Nutty, he would drop them exactly one minute after he heard that it was a real monkey he saw that night. And how are we to prevent his hearing? By a merciful miracle he was out taking his walk when the newspaper men began to infest the place today, but that might not happen another time. What conclusion does all this suggest to you, Mr. Chalmers?”

“We ought to get rid of the animal.”

“We certainly ought. And not take it back and leave it at the front door either. We must take it as near Lady Wetherby’s house as we can manage with safety, and then trust to its homing instincts.”

===Chinese===

比尔听得肃然。

“这确实叫人为难，是不是？”

“岂止是为难，可不是！再看看局面的另一层。前天夜里，我宝贝的纳蒂，一边拿魔鬼朗姆酒糟蹋自己的身子骨，一边恍恍惚惚看见了一只其实并不存在的猴子，当即立誓要改过自新、重新做人。他恨透了走路，如今每天五英里雷打不动。他嫌恶冷水浴，如今天天泡在里面不出来。印度棒他持何见解我不清楚，想必也是深恶痛绝，可如今你休想走近他而不被开了瓢。这些桩桩件件的好事，难道也要同它们来时一样，转瞬便散？我了解纳蒂：只消听说那晚看见的猴子是真猴子，不出一分钟他就会撂个精光。咱们又怎么能瞒住他、不叫他听说？托一个慈悲的奇迹，今天报馆的人马开始盘踞这地方的时候，他恰好出门走路去了；可是下回未必有这样的造化。凡此种种，查默斯先生，依你看，该得出什么结论？”

“咱们应当把这畜生打发走。”

“咱们确实该打发走。而且不能拎回去搁在前门口就算完事。得在稳妥的前提下，把它带到离韦瑟比夫人的宅子越近越好，其余的，就信它那恋家的本能。”

===Original===

“We’d better do it tonight.”

“This very minute. But don’t you bother to come. You must be tired out, poor thing.”

“I never felt less tired,” said Bill stoutly.

Elizabeth looked at him in silence for a moment.

“You’re rather splendid, you know, Mr. Chalmers. You make a great partner for an adventure of this kind. You’re nice and solid.”

===Chinese===

“最好今晚就去。”

“这会儿就去。不过你就不必劳神跟去了。你准累坏了，可怜人儿。”

“我从来没觉得这么不累过。”比尔倔强地说。

伊丽莎白默默地看了他一会儿。

“说真的，查默斯先生，你这人相当了不起。这种冒险，有你这样的搭档再好不过。你扎实，靠得住。”

===Original===

The outhouse lay in the neighborhood of the hives, a gaunt, wooden structure surrounded by bushes. Elizabeth glanced over her shoulder as she drew the key from her pocket.

“You can’t think how nervous I was this afternoon,” she said. “I thought every moment one of those newspaper men would look in here. I﻿—James! James! I thought I heard James in those bushes﻿—I kept heading them away. Once I thought it was all up.” She unlocked the door. “One of them was about a yard from the window, just going to look in. Thank goodness, a bee stung him at the psychological moment, and﻿—Oh!”

“What’s the matter?”

“Come and get a banana.”

===Chinese===

棚屋坐落在蜂箱近旁，是一座瘦骨嶙峋的木头房子，四下围着灌木。伊丽莎白从衣袋里掏钥匙时，回头望了一眼。

“你想不到今天下午我神经绷得多紧，”她说。“我时时刻刻担心哪个报馆的人往这儿张望。我——詹姆斯！詹姆斯！我以为听见詹姆斯在那丛灌木里——我一直把他们往别处支。有一回，我以为全完了。”她拧开门锁。“有一个离窗户不到一码远，正要往里看。谢天谢地，一只蜜蜂在最要命的节骨眼上蜇了他一口，跟着——哎呀！”

“怎么啦？”

“走，去拿根香蕉。”

===Original===

They walked to the house. On the way Elizabeth stopped.

“Why, you haven’t had any dinner either!” she said.

“Never mind me,” said Bill, “I can wait. Let’s get this thing finished first.”

“You really are a sport, Mr. Chalmers,” said Elizabeth gratefully. “It would kill me to wait a minute. I shan’t feel happy until I’ve got it over. Will you stay here while I go up and see that Nutty’s safe in his room,” she added, as they entered the house. “We don’t want him strolling out in the middle of it.”

===Chinese===

两人朝房子走去。半道上，伊丽莎白停住了脚。

“咦，你还没吃晚饭呢！”她说。

“别管我，”比尔说，“我等得起。先把这桩事办完要紧。”

“你可真是个爽快人，查默斯先生，”伊丽莎白感激地说。“多等一分钟我也熬不住。事情没了结，我这心里总不踏实。我上楼去看看纳蒂是不是安安稳稳在他房里，你在这儿等等，好吗？”进屋的时候她补了一句。“咱们可不想叫他半道上溜达出去。”

===Original===

She stopped abruptly. A feline howl had broken the stillness of the night, followed instantly by a sharp report.

“What was that?”

“It sounded like a car backfiring.”

“No, it was a shot. One of the neighbors, I expect. You can hear miles away on a night like this. I suppose a cat was after his chickens. Thank goodness, James isn’t a pirate cat. Wait while I go up and see Nutty.”

===Chinese===

她猛地顿住。一声猫儿似的号叫划破了夜的沉寂，紧跟着是一声清脆的爆响。

“那是什么响？”

“听着像汽车放炮。”

“不，是枪声。想是哪位邻居。这样的夜里，几英里外都听得见。多半是猫偷他的鸡去了。谢天谢地，詹姆斯可不是当海盗的猫。你等一等，我上楼看看纳蒂。”

===Original===

She was gone only a moment.

“It’s all right,” she said. “I peeped in. He’s doing deep-breathing exercises at his window, which looks out the other way. Come along.”

When they reached the outhouse they found the door open.

“Did you do that?” said Elizabeth.

“No.”

===Chinese===

她去了没有多大一会儿。

“没事，”她说。“我张了一眼。他正在窗口做深呼吸呢，他那扇窗朝另一头开。走吧。”

两人到了棚屋，只见门敞着。

“是你弄开的？”伊丽莎白说。

“不是。”

===Original===

“I don’t remember doing it myself. It must have swung open. Well, this saves us a walk. He’ll have gone.”

“Better take a look round, what?”

“Yes, I suppose so, but he’s sure not to be there. Have you a match?”

Bill struck one and held it up.

===Chinese===

“我也没记得开过。准是门自己荡开的。也好，省得咱们再跑一趟。它多半已经走了。”

“还是四下里看一眼的好，是不是？”

“也好，就依你，不过它肯定不在里头。你带火柴了吗？”

比尔擦亮一根，举了起来。

===Original===

“Good Lord!”

The match went out.

“What is it? What has happened?”

Bill was fumbling for another match.

===Chinese===

“我的老天爷！”

火柴灭了。

“怎么啦？出了什么事？”

比尔正摸索着再找一根火柴。

===Original===

“There’s something on the floor. It looks like﻿—I thought for a minute﻿—”

The small flame shot out of the gloom, flickered, then burned with a steady glow. Bill stooped, bending over something on the ground. The match burned down.

Bill’s voice came out of the darkness.

“I say, you were right about that noise. It was a shot. The poor little chap’s down there on the floor with a hole in him the size of my fist.”

===Chinese===

“地上有个东西。看着像——有一会儿我还当是——”

小小的火苗从幽暗里蹿出，颤了颤，随即燃成一团安稳的光。比尔俯下身，凑近地上那样东西。火柴烧到了根。

黑暗里传来比尔的声音。

“我说，那声响你猜对了。真是枪声。可怜的小家伙躺在底下地上，身上一个窟窿，有我的拳头那么大。”

## XVII / 第十七章

===Original===

Boyhood, like measles, is one of those complaints which a man should catch young and have done with, for when it comes in middle life it is apt to be serious. Dudley Pickering had escaped boyhood at the time when his contemporaries were contracting it. It is true that for a few years after leaving the cradle he had exhibited a certain immatureness, but as soon as he put on knickerbockers and began to go about a little he outgrew all that. He avoided altogether the chaotic period which usually lies between the years of ten and fourteen. At ten he was a thoughtful and sober-minded young man, at fourteen almost an old fogy. A love of machinery, developing early, helped the steadying process. While other boys were breaking windows or laying the foundations for home runs and flying tackles that would afterward enable them to pass with credit through college, Dudley Pickering was scrutinizing engines, studying textbooks, talking on terms of grave equality with mechanicians.

And now﻿—thirty-odd years overdue﻿—boyhood had come upon him. As he examined the revolver in his bedroom wild and unfamiliar emotions seethed within him. He did not realize it, but they were the emotions which should have come to him thirty years before and driven him out to hunt Indians in the garden. An imagination which might well have become atrophied through disuse had him as thoroughly in its control as ever he had had his Pickering Giant.

He believed almost with devoutness in the plot which he had detected for the spoliation of Lord Wetherby’s summer home, that plot of which he held Lord Dawlish to be the mainspring. And it must be admitted that circumstances had combined to help his belief. If the atmosphere in which he was moving was not sinister then there was no meaning in the word.

===Chinese===

童年好比麻疹，属于那类毛病：人应当趁年少染上、及早痊愈，因为若到中年才发作，往往就要凶险了。达德利·皮克林（Dudley Pickering）正当同龄孩子一个个染上童年的年纪，却把它躲了过去。诚然，刚离开摇篮后的头几年，他也显出过某种稚气未脱的迹象；可一等穿上灯笼裤，能四处走动了，他便把这些统统长了个干净。通常横在十岁到十四岁之间的那段混沌时期，他压根不曾经历。十岁上，他已经是个思虑周详、心智持重的年轻人；到十四岁，几乎成了个老古板。对机械的酷爱萌发得早，更助长了这番沉稳的进程。别的男孩子正忙着砸窗户，或者为日后的全垒打和飞身擒抱奠定根基——将来全凭这两样，他们才好在大学里体体面面地混过去——达德利·皮克林却在细看发动机、研读教科书，同机械师们煞有介事地平等论交。

而如今——迟到了三十来年——童年到底找上了他。他在卧室里端详那把左轮手枪时，一阵阵狂野而陌生的情绪在胸中翻涌。他自己并未察觉：这些正是本该在三十年前来到他身上、撺掇他冲进花园里去猎印第安人的那路情绪。一种因久废不用几乎要萎缩掉的想象力，如今把他牢牢攥在手心，正如当年他把“皮克林巨人”牢牢攥在手心。

他几乎怀着虔敬之心，笃信自己侦破的那桩图谋洗劫韦瑟比伯爵避暑宅邸的阴谋，并认定这桩阴谋的主发条，非达利什勋爵莫属。必须承认，种种情形也凑到一处，着实替他这份信念添了柴火。他身临其境的这股气氛若还不算凶险，那“凶险”二字便再无意义可言。

===Original===

Summer homes had been burgled, there was no getting away from that﻿—half a dozen at least in the past two months. He was a stranger in the locality, so had no means of knowing that summer homes were always burgled on Long Island every year, as regularly as the coming of the mosquito and the advent of the jellyfish. It was one of the local industries. People left summer homes lying about loose in lonely spots, and you just naturally got in through the cellar window. Such was the Long Islander’s simple creed.

This created in Mr. Pickering’s mind an atmosphere of burglary, a receptiveness, as it were, toward burglars as phenomena, and the extremely peculiar behavior of the person whom in his thoughts he always referred to as The Man crystallized it. He had seen The Man hanging about, peering in at windows. He had shouted “Hi!” and The Man had run. The Man had got into the house under the pretense of being a friend of Claire’s. At the suggestion that he should meet Claire he had dashed away in a panic. And Claire, both then and later, had denied absolutely any knowledge of him.

As for the apparently blameless beekeeping that was going on at the place where he lived, that was easily discounted. Mr. Pickering had heard somewhere or read somewhere﻿—he rather thought that it was in those interesting but disturbing chronicles of Raffles﻿—that the first thing an intelligent burglar did was to assume some open and innocent occupation to avert possible inquiry into his real mode of life. Mr. Pickering did not put it so to himself, for he was rarely slangy even in thought, but what he felt was that he had caught The Man and his confederate with the goods. He had it on them.

===Chinese===

避暑宅邸接连遭窃，这是抵赖不掉的——单过去两个月，至少就有五六处。他在此地人地两生，无从知道长岛（Long Island）的避暑宅邸年年都要遭窃，其规律一如蚊子之如期而至、水母之应时驾到。这是本地产业之一。人们把避暑宅邸随随便便撂在僻静地方，你只消从地窖窗户钻进去就是，自然而然，顺理成章。这便是长岛人的朴素信条。

这就叫皮克林先生心里生出了一种窃案的氛围，一种可以说对窃贼这一现象随时准备接纳的心态；而那个他心里一向称作“那个人”（The Man）的人物，行止乖张至极，恰好把这份心态凝成了形。他见过那个人在左近逡巡，朝窗户里张望。他大喝一声“喂！”，那个人拔腿就跑。那个人借口是克莱尔的朋友，混进了宅子；一提出让他同克莱尔见个面，他便慌不迭地一溜烟跑了。而克莱尔，无论当时还是事后，都断然否认认识此人。

至于他住处那边进行的那桩看上去清白无瑕的养蜂营生，要勾销也容易。皮克林先生不知从哪儿听说过、也不知在哪儿读到过——他比较倾向于认为，是在拉弗尔斯（Raffles）那些读来有趣却扰得人心神不宁的盗贼纪事里——说一个精明的窃贼要办的头一件事，便是给自己安上一门公开而无辜的行当，好叫旁人无从查究他真实的生活门道。皮克林先生自然不曾在心里这样措辞，他即便思想也很少沾俚语；他的感受是：那个人和他的同伙，叫他当场拿获，人赃俱在。他把柄已经攥在手里了。

===Original===

If Mr. Pickering had had his boyhood at the proper time and finished with it, he would no doubt have acted otherwise than he did. He would have contented himself with conducting a war of defense. He would have notified the police, and considered that all that remained for him personally to do was to stay in his room at night with his revolver. But boys will be boys. The only course that seemed to him in any way satisfactory in this his hour of rejuvenation was to visit the bee farm, the hotbed of crime, and keep an eye on it. He wanted to go there and prowl.

He did not anticipate any definite outcome of his visit. In his boyish, elemental way he just wanted to take a revolver, and a pocketful of cartridges, and prowl.

It was a great night for prowling. A moon, so little less than full that the eye could barely detect its slight tendency to become concave, shone serenely, creating a desirable combination of black shadows where the prowler might hide, and great stretches of light in which the prowler might reveal his wickedness without disguise. Mr. Pickering walked briskly along the road, then less briskly as he drew nearer the farm. An opportune belt of shrubs that ran from the gate adjoining the road to a point not far from the house gave him just the cover he needed. He slipped into his belt of shrubs and began to work his way through them.

===Chinese===

倘若皮克林先生在恰当的年纪得过一场童年、并且痊愈收场，他无疑不会像如今这样行事。他满可以满足于只打一场防御战：知会警方，然后认定自己分内该做的，不过是夜里握着左轮手枪守在房中。可是男孩子毕竟是男孩子。在他这返老还童的时辰，在他看来多少还算差强人意的唯一路数，便是亲临那座养蜂场——罪恶的温床——盯住它。他要去那儿潜行。

他并没指望这趟出行会有什么明确的结果。以一种孩子气的、全凭本能的方式，他就是想揣上一把左轮手枪、满满一衣袋子弹，去潜行一番。

这是个潜行的绝妙夜晚。一轮月亮，只比满月亏了一丝一毫，肉眼几乎辨不出它那一点点转亏趋凹的动向，恬然朗照，造就了一种可心的组合：一面是黑影幢幢，可供潜行者藏身；一面是月光大片铺开，可供潜行者无所遮掩地袒露他的鬼祟。皮克林先生沿着大路健步而行，临近农场时脚步缓了下来。一带灌木恰逢其时，从紧挨大路的栅门一路延展到离宅子不远的地方，给了他正需要的掩体。他闪身钻进自己那带灌木，开始在里面择路穿行。

===Original===

Like generals, authors, artists and others who, after planning broad effects, have to get down to the detail work, he found that this was where his troubles began. He had conceived the journey through the shrubbery in rather an airy mood. He thought he would just go through the shrubbery. He had not taken into account the branches, the thorns, the occasional unexpected holes, and he was both warm and disheveled when he reached the end of it and found himself out in the open within a short distance of what he recognized as beehives. It was not for some time that he was able to give that selfless attention to exterior objects which is the prowler’s chief asset. For quite a while the only thought of which he was conscious was that what he needed most was a cold drink and a cold bath. Then, with a return to clearheadedness, he realized that he was standing out in the open, visible from three sides to anyone who might be in the vicinity, and he withdrew into the shrubbery. He was not fond of the shrubbery, but it was a splendid place to withdraw into. It swallowed you up.

This was the last move of the first part of Mr. Pickering’s active campaign. He stayed where he was, in the middle of a bush, and waited for the enemy to do something. What he expected him to do he did not know. The subconscious thought that animated him was that on a night like this something was bound to happen sooner or later. Just such a thought on similarly stimulating nights had animated men of his acquaintance thirty years ago, men who were as elderly and stolid and unadventurous now as Mr. Pickering had been then. He would have resented the suggestion profoundly, but the truth of the matter was that Dudley Pickering, after a late start, had begun to play Indians.

Nothing happened for a long time﻿—for such a long time that, in spite of the ferment within him, Mr. Pickering almost began to believe that nothing would happen. The moon shone with unutterable calm. The crickets and the tree frogs performed their interminable duet, apparently unconscious that they were attacking it in different keys﻿—a fact that, after a while, began to infuriate Mr. Pickering. Mosquitoes added their reedy tenor to the concert. A twig on which he was standing snapped with a report like a pistol. The moon went on shining.

===Chinese===

同那些规划罢宏大效果便不得不俯身细部活计的将领、作家、画家诸公一样，他发现麻烦正是从这里开的头。穿越灌木丛这一程，他原本设想得相当潇洒。他只当穿过去便是。树枝、荆棘、间或出其不意的坑洞，全都未曾计入；等他捱到灌木丛尽头，发现自己已置身旷地，离他认出来的那几只蜂箱不远，早已浑身发热、衣冠不整。过了好一阵，他才能重新聚拢那份潜行者赖为本钱的、对身外之物的无私注意。相当一段时间里，他心中唯一的念头便是：眼下最要紧的，是一杯冷饮，一场冷水浴。随后，头脑恢复了清明，他才意识到自己正站在旷地里，左近但凡有人，三面都望得见他，于是他退回了灌木丛。他并不喜欢这片灌木丛，可它实在是个退守的妙处。它能把你整个儿吞下去。

这是皮克林先生积极战役第一阶段最后一次移动。他守在原处——一丛灌木的正中——等敌人有所动作。他指望敌人干什么，他自己也说不上。暗中给他打气的那个下意识念头是：这样的夜晚，早晚必定要出点事。三十年前，同样撩人的夜晚，同样这个念头也曾推着他相识的那些人；那些人如今同当年的皮克林先生一个样，老态龙钟、四平八稳、与冒险无缘。倘若有人向他点破这一层，他必定深以为忤；然而实情是：达德利·皮克林起步虽晚，到底玩起印第安人游戏来了。

好长时间什么也没有发生——长得竟叫皮克林先生尽管胸中情绪翻腾，也几乎开始相信什么都不会发生了。月亮照着，恬静得难以言宣。蟋蟀和树蛙上演着它们没完没了的二重奏，显然并不自知两下用的调门不合——这一层，过不多时便开始激怒皮克林先生。蚊子给这场音乐会添上了芦笛似的男高音。他站立其上的一根细枝咔的一声断了，响得活像放了一枪。月亮兀自照着。

===Original===

Away in the distance a dog began to howl. An automobile passed in the road. For a few moments Mr. Pickering was able to occupy himself pleasantly with speculations as to its make; and then he became aware that something was walking down the back of his neck just beyond the point where his fingers could reach it. Discomfort enveloped Mr. Pickering. At various times by day he had seen long-winged black creatures with slim waists and unpleasant faces. Could it be one of these? Or a caterpillar? Or﻿—and the maddening thing was that he did not dare to slap at it, for who knew what desperate characters the sound might not attract.

Well. It wasn’t stinging him, that was something.

A second howling dog joined the first one. A wave of sadness was apparently afflicting the canine population of the district tonight. Mr. Pickering’s vitality began to ebb. He was aging, and imagination slackened its grip. And then, just as he had begun to contemplate the possibility of abandoning the whole adventure and returning home, he was jerked back to boyhood again by the sound of voices.

===Chinese===

远处，一条狗嚎了起来。大路上驶过一辆汽车。有那么一会儿，皮克林先生得以饶有兴致地捉摸它的牌子，自遣良辰；跟着他便觉出有样东西正沿着他的后脖颈往下爬，恰在手指将将够不着的地方。不适裹住了皮克林先生。白天他不止一回见过那种翅膀长长、腰身纤细、面目可憎的黑色飞虫。会不会正是此君之一？还是一条毛毛虫？抑或是——最磨人的是，他不敢抬手去拍，谁知道这一声响会招来什么样的亡命之徒。

罢了。它没有蜇他，这也算一桩安慰。

第二条狗也应和着嚎了起来。看来今晚有一股悲伤的浪潮，正折磨着这一带的犬类居民。皮克林先生的元气开始退潮。他在见老，想象力也松了钳制。就在他开始盘算要不干脆放弃这整场冒险、打道回府的当口，一串人声把他猛地拽回了童年。

===Original===

He shrank farther into the bushes. A man﻿—The Man﻿—was approaching, accompanied by his female associate. They passed so close to him that he could have stretched out a hand and touched them.

The female associate was speaking, and her first words set all Mr. Pickering’s suspicions dancing a dance of triumph. As has been said, he was a man who rarely thought in slang, but if he had been, he would have told himself at that moment that he had had the right dope. The girl gave herself away with her opening sentence.

“You can’t think how nervous I was this afternoon,” he heard her say. She had a soft pleasant voice; but soft pleasant voices may be the vehicles for conveying criminal thoughts. “I thought every moment one of those newspaper men would look in here.”

===Chinese===

他往灌木深处缩了缩。一个男人——正是那个人——走了过来，身边带着他的女同伙。两人打他身边擦过，近得他只消伸出手去便能碰到他们。

女同伙正在说话，她开口头一句，便叫皮克林先生满腹的疑窦跳起了一支凯旋之舞。前文说过，他这人即使在思想里也很少用俚语；可他若是用，此刻准会告诉自己：消息果然千真万确。这姑娘头一句话就露了馅。

“你想不到我今天下午有多紧张，”他听见她说。她的嗓音轻柔悦耳；可轻柔悦耳的嗓音，也一样可以充当运载犯罪念头的工具。“我一刻不停地担心，那些新闻记者里头保不齐哪一个就会朝这儿张望。”

===Original===

Where was here? Ah, that outhouse! Mr. Pickering had had his suspicions of that outhouse already. It was one of those structures that look at you furtively as if something were hiding in them.

“James! James! I thought I heard James in those bushes.”

The girl was looking straight at the spot occupied by Mr. Pickering, and it had been the start caused by her first words, and the resultant rustle of branches, that had directed her attention to him. He froze. The danger passed. She went on speaking. Mr. Pickering pondered on James. Who was James? Another of the gang, of course. How many of them were there?

“Once I thought it was all up. One of them was about a yard from the window, just going to look in.”

Mr. Pickering thrilled. There was something hidden in the outhouse, then! Swag?

===Chinese===

“这儿”？这儿是哪儿？啊，那座棚屋！皮克林先生对那座棚屋早有疑心。世上有一种建筑，总是贼头贼脑地望着你，仿佛里头藏着什么东西；那棚屋便属于此列。

“詹姆斯！詹姆斯！我刚才好像听见詹姆斯在那些灌木丛里。”

姑娘正直勾勾地望着皮克林先生占据的那一处；正是她头一句话惹得他猛一惊乍、树枝随之簌簌作响，才把她的注意力引到他身上来。他顿时僵住。危机过去了。她继续说着。皮克林先生捉摸起詹姆斯来。詹姆斯是谁？自然是这伙人里的又一个。他们这伙人究竟有多少？

“有一回我还当全完了。他们有一个离窗口不到一码，正要朝里看。”

皮克林先生浑身一震。这么说，那棚屋里当真藏着东西！是赃物？

===Original===

“Thank goodness, a bee stung him at the psychological moment, and﻿—Oh!”

She stopped and The Man spoke:

“What’s the matter?”

It interested Mr. Pickering that The Man retained his English accent even when talking privately with his associates. For practice, no doubt.

“Come and get a banana,” said the girl. And they went off together in the direction of the house, leaving Mr. Pickering bewildered. Why a banana? Was it a slang term of the underworld for a pistol? It must be that.

===Chinese===

“谢天谢地，一只蜜蜂在节骨眼上蜇了他一下，跟着——哎呀！”

她戛然收声，那个人开了口：

“怎么了？”

叫皮克林先生颇为留意的是，那个人即便私下里同同伙讲话，也依旧保留着他的英国口音。无疑是为了练熟。

“过来吃香蕉，”姑娘说。两人一道朝宅子的方向去了，撇下皮克林先生满心茫然。为什么是香蕉？这莫非是黑社会中管手枪的黑话？必定是了。

===Original===

But he had no time for speculation. Now was his chance, the only chance he would ever get of looking into that outhouse and finding out its mysterious contents. He had seen the girl unlock the door. A few steps would take him there. All it needed was nerve. With a strong effort Mr. Pickering succeeded in obtaining the nerve. He burst from his bush and trotted to the outhouse door, opened it and looked in. And at that moment something touched his leg.

At the right time and in the right frame of mind man is capable of stoic endurances that excite wonder and admiration. Mr. Pickering was no weakling. He had once upset his automobile in a ditch, and had waited for twenty minutes until help came to relieve a broken arm, and he had done it without a murmur. But on the present occasion there was a difference. His mind was not adjusted for the occurrence. There are times when it is unseasonable to touch a man on the leg. This was a moment when it was unseasonable in the case of Mr. Pickering. He bounded silently into the air, his whole being rent asunder as by a cataclysm.

He had been holding his revolver in his hand, as a protection against nameless terrors, and as he leaped he pulled the trigger.
