You couldn’t fight against the evidence, when once your instinct﻿—or whatever it is that tells you that I am speaking the truth﻿—had begun to weaken. And it would weaken. Think what it would have to be fighting all the time. Think of the case your intelligence would be making out, day after day, till it crushed you. It’s impossible that you could keep yourself from docketing the evidence and arranging it and absorbing it. Think! Consider what you know are actual facts! Nutty invites you down here, knowing that you are Lord Dawlish. All you know about my attitude toward Lord Dawlish is what I told you on the first morning of your visit. I told you I hated him. Yet, knowing you are Lord Dawlish, I become engaged to you. Directly afterward you hear Nutty consoling me, as if I were marrying you against my will. Isn’t that an absolutely fair statement of what has happened? How could you go on believing me with all that against you?”

===Chinese===
“证据摆在这儿，你没法信。”

“我就是信。”

“不行。你可以一时三刻说服自己信，可过不多久你就得按证据办事，由不得你自己。你还没领教证据是何等碾人的东西。违背心愿，你也得依着它。你要明白，证据是唯一的准绳。你并不确知我说的句句是真，你只是觉着是这样。你靠的是心，不是脑。到头来赢的必然是脑。你兴许还能信上些日子，可早晚你得犯疑、发愁、自己折磨自己。一旦你的直觉——或者随便什么告诉你我说的是实话的那点东西——开始松劲，你就敌不过证据了。而它准会松劲。想想看，它得时时刻刻迎战的是什么。想想你的头脑会一天接一天替对方垒一份什么样的状子，垒到把你压垮为止。你躲不开的，你一定会把证据一件件归档、整理、咽下去。想想吧！把你知道的铁的事实摆一摆！纳蒂明明知道你是达利什勋爵，还请你到这儿来。你对我的态度的全部了解，只是你来的头一天早上我自己说的那句话。我告诉你我恨达利什勋爵。可是明明知道你是达利什勋爵，我却同你订了婚。紧接着你又听见纳蒂安慰我，仿佛我是身不由己才嫁你。这一桩桩一件件，难道不是原原本本、公公正正？证据这样压着你，你怎么还能信得下去？”

===Original===
“I know you’re straight. You couldn’t do anything crooked.”

“The evidence proves that I did.”

“I don’t care.”

“Not now.”

“Never.”

===Chinese===
“我知道你行得正。你干不出歪的事。”

“证据证明我干得出。”

“我不理会。”

“眼下不理会。”

“永远不理会。”

===Original===
She shook her head.

“It’s dear of you, Bill, but you’re promising an impossibility. And just because it’s impossible, and because I love you too much to face what would be bound to happen, I’m going to send you away.”

“Send me away!”

“Yes. It’s going to hurt, Bill; but it’s the only thing to do. I love you too much to live with you for the rest of my life wondering all the time whether you still believed or whether the weight of the evidence had crushed out that tiny little spark of intuition which is all that makes you believe me now. You could never know the truth for certain, you see﻿—that’s the horror of it; and sometimes you would be able to make yourself believe, but more often, in spite of all you could do, you would doubt. It would poison both our lives. Little things would happen, insignificant in themselves, which would become tremendously important just because they added a little bit more to the doubt which you would never be able to get rid of.

===Chinese===
她摇了摇头。

“你这份心意叫人心疼，比尔，可你许的是一个做不到的愿。正因为它做不到，正因为我爱你爱得受不了那迟早要来的事，我才要把你送走。”

“送我走！”

“是的。会疼的，比尔；可这是唯一可行的路。我爱你，爱得没法后半辈子同你过活还时时刻刻犯嘀咕：不知你信没信到底，还是证据那副重担早把那一点直觉的火星压灭了——你如今肯信我，全凭那点火星。你永远不会确切知道真相，你瞧，这就是可怕的地方；有时你兴许能说服自己信，可更多的时候，任凭你怎么挣扎，你还是疑。它会毒害我们两个人的一生。些小的事会发生的，本来无足轻重，只因往那团你一辈子甩不脱的疑心上面又添了一星星，就变得天一样大。”

===Original===
“When we had quarrels﻿—which we should, as we are both human﻿—they wouldn’t be over and done with in an hour. They would stick in your mind and rankle, because, you see, they might be proofs that I didn’t really love you. And then when I seemed happy with you, you would wonder if I was acting. I know all this sounds morbid and exaggerated, but it isn’t. What have you got to go on, as regards me? What do you really know of me? If something like this had happened after we had been married half a dozen years and really knew each other, we could laugh at it. But we are strangers. We came together and loved each other because there was something in each of us which attracted the other. We took that little something as a foundation and built on it. But what has happened has knocked away our poor little foundation. That’s all. We don’t really know anything at all about each other for certain. It’s just guesswork.”

She broke off and looked at the clock.

“I had better be packing your bag if you’re to catch the train.”

===Chinese===
“我们吵起架来——我们免不了要吵的，人都不是神仙——也不会一个钟头就烟消云散。它们会扎在你心里溃烂，因为你要明白，它们没准儿就是我并非真心爱你的凭证。往后我同你相处得快活，你又要犯疑，不知我是不是在做戏。我知道这些话说出来又病态又夸张，其实不然。论到我这个人，你手里有什么凭据？你对我真正知道些什么？倘若这类事出在我们成婚六七年、彼此知根知底之后，我们一笑也就置之了。可我们原是陌生人。我们走到一处，彼此相爱，只因为我们身上各自有一点东西吸引着对方。我们拿那一点点东西做了地基，往上盖房子。可眼下这场事，把我们这可怜的小地基砸了个精光。就是这么回事。我们其实对彼此的底细一无所知。一切不过是瞎猜。”

她忽然收住话头，看了看钟。

“你要赶火车的话，我该去给你收拾行李了。”

===Original===
He gave a rueful laugh.

“You’re throwing me out!”

“Yes, I am. I want you to go while I am strong enough to let you go.”

“If you really feel like that, why in Heaven’s name send me away?”

“How do you know I really feel like that? How do you know that I am not pretending to feel like that as part of a carefully prepared plan?”

===Chinese===
他惨然一笑。

“你这是要把我撵出去！”

“是的。我要趁我的狠劲还撑得住放你走，赶紧放你走。”

“你既然真是这般心思，那看在老天份上，到底为什么还要赶我走？”

“你怎么知道我真是一番这般心思？你怎么知道我不是装出这副心肠，好演足一盘精心筹划的棋局里的一步？”

===Original===
He made an impatient gesture.

“Yes, I know,” she said. “You think I am going out of my way to manufacture unnecessary complications. I’m not, I’m simply looking ahead. If I were trying to trap you for the sake of your money, could I play a stronger card than by seeming anxious to give you up? If I were to give in now sooner or later that suspicion would come to you. You would drive it away. You might drive it away a hundred times. But you couldn’t kill it. In the end it would beat you.”

He shrugged his shoulders helplessly.

“I can’t argue.”

===Chinese===
他烦躁地做了个手势。

“是，我知道。”她说，“你当我这是没事找事，专会节外生枝。我不是，我不过替往后看了一步。假如我当真在打你钱袋的主意设圈套，我还能打出比装出一副巴不得同你分手的样子更有力的一张牌吗？我这会儿若是让了步，那份疑心迟早找上门来。你会赶它走。你能赶它走一百回。可你杀不死它。到头来，赢的是它。”

他无可奈何地耸了耸肩。

“我说不过你。”

===Original===
“Nor can I. I can only put very badly things which I know are true. Come and pack.”

“I’ll do it. Don’t you bother.”

“Nonsense. No man knows how to pack properly.”

He followed her to his rooms, pulled out his suitcase, the symbol of the end of all things, watched her as she flitted about, the sun shining on her fair hair as she passed and repassed the window. She was picking things up, folding them, packing them. Bill looked on with an aching sense of desolation. It was all so friendly, so intimate, so exactly as it would have been if she were his wife. It seemed to him needlessly cruel that she should be playing on this note of domesticity at the moment when she was barring forever the door between him and happiness. He rebelled helplessly against the attitude she had taken. He had not thought it all out, as she had done. It was folly, insanity, ruining their two lives like this for a scruple.

Once again he was to encounter that practical strain in the feminine mind which jars upon a man in trouble. She was holding something in her hand and looking at it with concern.

===Chinese===
“我也说不过你。我心里明明白白的事，叫我讲出来偏偏笨嘴拙舌。来收拾行李吧。”

“我自己来。你别费心。”

“胡说。天底下没有哪个男人会装箱。”

他跟她回到房里，拉出他的手提箱——那是万事俱终的信物——眼看她轻盈地转来转去，她在窗前来来回回，阳光照在她的金发上。她拾起一件件东西，叠好，装箱。比尔在旁边看着，心里一片隐隐作痛的荒凉。这一切多么亲热，多么家常，同她果真做了他妻子的光景一般无二。她一面把他与幸福之间那扇门永远闩死，一面偏又弹起这种家居过日子的调子，在他看来未免残忍得多余。她抱定的这个主意，他只得无可奈何地在心里反抗。她那番道理，他并不能像她一样想得头头是道。为了一道心结，就这样断送两个人的终身，这是糊涂，是发疯。

他又一次领教了女人心性里那股实际的劲儿——人正遭难的时候，这股劲儿最扎人心。她手里捏着一样东西，满面愁容地端详着。

===Original===
“Why didn’t you tell me!” she said. “Your socks are in an awful state, poor boy!”

He had the feeling of having been hit by something. A man has not a woman’s gift of being able to transfer his mind at will from sorrow to socks.

“Like sieves!” She sighed. A troubled frown wrinkled her forehead. “Men are so helpless! Oh, dear, I’m sure you don’t pay any attention to anything important. I don’t believe you ever bother your head about keeping warm in winter and not getting your feet wet. And now I shan’t be able to look after you!”

===Chinese===
“怎么不早告诉我！”她说，“你的袜子都糟成什么样了，可怜的孩子！”

他像被什么东西迎面打了一下。把心思说挪就挪、从悲哀一下挪到袜子上，这本事是女人的，男人没有。

“全是筛子眼儿！”她叹了口气，愁眉在额上拧起一道纹。“男人真是一点不能自助！哎呀，我想你准是什么要紧的事都不放在心上。冬天保暖不保暖、脚潮不潮，你这辈子就没为此费过一点神，我不信。可往后我照料不着你了！”

===Original===
Bill’s voice broke. He felt himself trembling.

“Elizabeth!”

She was kneeling on the floor, her head bent over the suitcase. She looked up and met his eyes.

“It’s no use, Bill, dear. I must. It’s the only way.”

===Chinese===
比尔的声音哑了。他觉出自己在发抖。

“伊丽莎白！”

她跪在地板上，头俯在手提箱上。她抬起头，迎住他的目光。

“没有用的，比尔，亲爱的。我非这样不可。这是唯一的路。”

===Original===
The sense of the nearness of the end broke down the numbness which held him.

“Elizabeth! It’s so utterly absurd. It’s just﻿—chucking everything away!”

She was silent for a moment.

===Chinese===
末日在即的实感，冲垮了钳住他的那层麻木。

“伊丽莎白！这太荒唐了，简直荒唐透顶。这不过是——把什么都白白扔掉！”

她沉默了片刻。

===Original===
“Bill, dear, I haven’t said anything about it before, but don’t you see that there’s my side to be considered too? I only showed you that you could never possibly know that I loved you. How am I to know that you really love me?”

He had moved a step toward her. He drew back, chilled.

“I can’t do more than tell you,” he said.

===Chinese===
“比尔，亲爱的，这话我先前一直没提，可你难道看不出，我这一头也有要顾的地方？我方才只是叫你明白，你永远没法确知我是爱你的。那我又怎么知道你是不是真心爱我呢？”

他已朝她挪近一步。他浑身一凉，退了回去。

“我除了告诉你，没有别的法子。”他说。

===Original===
“You can’t. And there you have put in two words just what I’ve been trying to make clear all the time. Don’t you see that that’s the terrible thing about life, that nobody can do more than tell anybody anything? Life’s nothing but words, words, words, and how are we to know when words are true? How am I to know that you didn’t ask me to marry you out of sheer pity and an exaggerated sense of justice?”

He stared at her.

“That,” he said, “is absolutely ridiculous!”

===Chinese===
“你是没法。而你简简单单两个字，恰恰把我一直竭力想讲明白的事说尽了。你难道看不出，人生可怕就可怕在这里：谁对谁，至多也只能说说而已？人生除了话，话，话，什么都没有，我们又怎么知道哪些话当得了真？我怎么知道你不是出于纯粹的怜悯、出于一股过了头的正义感，才向我求婚的呢？”

他瞪着她。

“这话，”他说，“简直荒唐到家了！”

===Original===
“Why? Look at it as I should look at it later on, when whatever it is inside me that tells me it’s ridiculous now had died. Just at this moment, while we’re talking here, there’s something stronger than reason which tells me you really do love me. But can’t you understand that that won’t last? It’s like a candle burning on a rock with the tide coming up all round it. It’s burning brightly enough now, and we can see the truth by the light of it. But the tide will put it out, and then we shall have nothing left to see by. There’s a great black sea of suspicion and doubt creeping up to swamp the little spark of intuition inside us.

“I will tell you what would happen to me if I didn’t send you away. Remember I heard what that girl was saying last night. Remember that you hated the thought of depriving me of Uncle Ira’s money so much that your first act was to try to get me to accept half of it. The quixotic thing is the first that it occurs to you to do, because you’re like that, because you’re the straightest, whitest man I’ve ever known or shall know. Could anything be more likely, looking at it as I should later on, than that you should have hit on the idea of marrying me as the only way of undoing the wrong you thought you had done me? I’ve been foolish about obligations all my life. I’ve a sort of morbid pride that hates the thought of owing anything to anybody, of getting anything that I have not earned. By and by, if I were to marry you, a little rotten speck of doubt would begin to eat its way farther and farther into me. It would be the same with you. We should react on each other. We should be watching each other, testing each other, trying each other out all the time. It would be horrible, horrible!”

===Chinese===
“为什么荒唐？你得照我往后会有的眼光看——等我心里头那个此刻还告诉我这话荒唐的东西死了以后看。就在这会儿，就在我们俩这么说着话的当口，有一种比道理更强的东西告诉我，你是真心爱我的。可你难道不明白，这东西撑不久？它像一支蜡烛，点在礁石上，四面潮水正围拢上来。眼下它烧得正旺，我们还能借它的光看清真相。可潮水会把它淹灭，到那时我们再没有一点光可借。疑心与猜忌那片黑沉沉的大海，正悄悄漫上来，要吞掉我们心里的那点直觉的火星。”

“我来告诉你，若是我不送你走，往后在我身上会出什么事。别忘了，昨儿夜里那姑娘的话我全听见了。别忘了，你一想到要夺走我舅舅艾拉的钱就难受不堪，所以你的头一件事就是劝我把那一半收下。堂吉诃德式的义举总是头一个跳进你的脑子，因为你就是那种人，因为你是我认识过的、往后要认识的人里头最方正、最清白的一个。照我往后的眼光看，还有什么比这更顺理成章：你会想到娶我，把它当成弥补你自以为亏欠了我的唯一办法。欠人什么这种事，我一辈子都犯傻。我有一种病态的自尊心，见不得欠谁一丝一毫，见不得收下不是自己挣来的东西。渐渐地，倘若我嫁了你，一星霉烂的疑斑就会开始在我心里越蛀越深。你那头也一样。我们会互相牵动。我们会时时刻刻彼此窥察，彼此试探，彼此考校。那可就可怕了，可怕极了！”

===Original===
He started to speak, then, borne down by the hopelessness of it, stopped. Elizabeth stood up. They did not look at each other. He strapped the suitcase and picked it up. The end of all things was at hand.

“Better to end it all cleanly, Bill,” she said in a low voice. “It will hurt less.”

He did not speak.

“I’ll come down to the gate with you.”

===Chinese===
他开口要说话，随即被那种全然无望的分量压住，住了口。伊丽莎白站起身。两人谁也不看谁。他扣好手提箱的皮带，提了起来。万事俱终，就在眼前。

“索性一刀两断的好，比尔。”她低声说，“疼还能疼得轻些。”

他没有说话。

“我送你到大门口。”

===Original===
They walked in silence down the drive. The air was heavy with the torpor of late summer. The sun beat down on them, turning her hair to burnished gold. They reached the gate.

“Goodbye, Bill, dear.”

He took her hand dully.

“Goodbye,” he said.

===Chinese===
两人默默顺着车道走下去。空气沉滞，满是夏末的倦意。太阳直晒在两人身上，把她的头发照成磨亮的金子。大门到了。

“再见，比尔，亲爱的。”

他木然握住她的手。

“再见。”他说。

===Original===
Elizabeth stood at the gate, watching. He swung down the road with long strides. At the bend he turned and for a moment stood there, as if waiting for her to make some sign. Then he fell into his stride again and was gone. Elizabeth leaned on the gate. Her face was twisted, and she clutched the warm wood as if it gave her strength.

The grounds were very empty. The spirit of loneliness brooded on them. Elizabeth walked slowly back to the house. Nutty was coming toward her from the orchard.

“Hello!” said Nutty.

He was cheerful and debonair. His little eyes were alight with the glow of contentment. He hummed a tune.

===Chinese===
伊丽莎白站在大门口望着。他迈开长步，沿着大路去了。到了拐弯的地方他回过身，在那儿立了片刻，仿佛在等她给个什么示意。随后他重新迈开步子，走没了。伊丽莎白倚在大门上。她的脸扭歪了，两只手死死攥着晒暖的木头，仿佛那木头能给她一点力气。

庭园里空落落的。寂寞的魂灵在草木间盘踞。伊丽莎白慢慢走回屋子。纳蒂正从果园那边迎面走来。

“喂！”纳蒂说。

他兴高采烈，一派潇洒。一双小眼睛闪着心满意足的光。他哼着小曲。

===Original===
“Where’s Dawlish?” he said.

“He has gone.”

Nutty’s tune failed in the middle of a bar. Something in his sister’s voice startled him. The glow of contentment gave way to a look of alarm.

“Gone? How do you mean﻿—gone? You don’t mean﻿—gone?”

===Chinese===
“达利什呢？”他说。

“他走了。”

纳蒂的曲子哼到半小节上哑了火。姐姐的声音里有什么东西叫他心里一凛。心满意足的光换成了惊惶的神色。

“走了？你什么意思——走了？你不是说——走了吧？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

“Gone away?”

“Gone away.”

They had reached the house before he spoke again.

===Chinese===
“走了。”

“离开了？”

“离开了。”

两人走到屋前，他才重新开得出口。

===Original===
“You don’t mean﻿—gone away?”

“Yes.”

“Do you mean﻿—gone away?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“你不是说——离开了？”

“是的。”

“你是说——离开了？”

“是的。”

===Original===
“You aren’t going to marry him?”

“No.”

The world stood still. The noise of the crickets and all the little sounds of summer smote on Nutty’s ear in one discordant shriek.

“Oh, gosh!” he exclaimed faintly, and collapsed on the front steps like a boned fish.

===Chinese===
“你不嫁给他了？”

“不。”

天地霎时凝住。蟋蟀的聒噪和夏天一切细碎的声响，汇成一声刺耳的不谐和音，直捣进纳蒂的耳朵。

“哎呀，我的妈呀！”他有气无力地叫了一声，整个人像一条剔净了骨的鱼，瘫软在门前台阶上。

## XXIII / 第二十三章

===Original===

The spectacle of Nutty in his anguish did not touch Elizabeth. Normally a kindhearted girl, she was not in the least sorry for him. She had even taken a bitter pleasure and found a momentary relief in loosing the thunderbolt which had smitten him down. Even if it has to manufacture it, misery loves company. She watched Nutty with a cold and uninterested eye as he opened his mouth feebly, shut it again and reopened it; and then when it became apparent that these maneuvers were about to result in speech, she left him and walked quickly down the drive again. She had the feeling that if Nutty were to begin to ask her questions﻿—and he had the aspect of one who is about to ask a thousand﻿—she would break down. She wanted solitude and movement, so she left Nutty sitting and started for the gate. Presently she would go and do things among the beehives; and after that, if that brought no solace, she would go in and turn the house upside down and get dusty and tired. Anything to occupy herself.

Reaction had set in. She had known it would come, and had made ready to fight against it, but she had underestimated the strength of the enemy. It seemed to her, in those first minutes, that she had done a mad thing, that all those arguments which she had used were far fetched and ridiculous. It was useless to tell herself that she had thought the whole thing out clearly and had taken the only course that could have been taken. With Bill’s departure the power to face the situation steadily had left her. All she could think of was that she loved him and that she had sent him away.

Why had he listened to her? Why hadn’t he taken her in his arms and told her not to be a little fool? Why did men ever listen to women? If he had really loved her would he have gone away? She tormented herself with this last question for a while. She was still tormenting herself with it when a melancholy voice broke in on her meditations.

===Chinese===

纳蒂痛苦万分的那副样子，并没有触动伊丽莎白。她平素是个心地善良的姑娘，此刻却丝毫不同情他。方才放出那记把他劈倒的霹雳时，她甚至咂摸出一种苦涩的快意，得了片刻的宽解。悲苦这东西总爱找伴作陪，哪怕这个伴得现造一个出来。她冷眼旁观、意兴阑珊地看着纳蒂有气无力地张开嘴，又合上，再张开；等到看出这一套动作眼看就要酿出话来，她便撇下他，快步又顺着车道朝大门走去。她心里明白，纳蒂若是开口盘问起来——看那架势，少说也备着一千个问题——她准会垮下来。她要的是清静和走动，便留下纳蒂坐在原地，自己朝大门出发。过一会儿，她要到蜂箱中间去干点活儿；干完之后，若还不得宽解，她就进屋把家里翻个底朝天，弄得灰头土脸、筋疲力尽。什么都行，只要能让自己有事可做。

事后的反劲上来了。她早知道它要来，也预先备好要同它斗，却低估了这敌人的气力。头几分钟里，她只觉得自己干了一件疯事，搬出来的那一整套道理，统统牵强得可笑。再对自己说，当初早把整件事想得清清楚楚，走的已是唯一可走的路，也是枉然。比尔一走，她那份正视现实的定力也随之而去。她满心只剩一件事：她爱他，而她把他打发走了。

他干吗要听她的？干吗不把她一把搂进怀里，叫她别做小傻瓜？男人到底为什么要听女人的话？倘若他当真爱她，还会走么？末一问熬煎了她好一阵。她还在拿它熬煎自己，一声愁苦的嗓音闯进了她的沉思。

===Original===

“I can’t believe it,” said the voice. She turned, to perceive Nutty drooping beside her. “I simply can’t believe it!”

Elizabeth clenched her teeth. She was not in the mood for Nutty.

“It will gradually sink in,” she said unsympathetically.

“Did you really send him away?”

“I did.”

“But what on earth for?”

===Chinese===

“我简直不能相信，”那嗓音说。她回头一望，只见纳蒂蔫头耷脑地立在身边。“我就是不能相信！”

伊丽莎白咬紧了牙。她这会儿没心思搭理纳蒂。

“你会慢慢想通的，”她说，语气里没有半分体恤。

“他真是你打发走的？”

“是我。”

“可这到底是为的什么？”

===Original===

“Because it was the only thing to do.”

A light shone on Nutty’s darkness.

“Oh, I say, did he hear what I said last night?”

“He did hear what you said last night.”

Nutty’s mouth opened slowly.

“Oh!”

===Chinese===

“因为只有这么办。”

一线光亮照进了纳蒂的黑暗。

“哦，我说，我昨晚说的话他听见了？”

“你昨晚说的话，他听见了。”

纳蒂的嘴慢慢地张开了。

“哦！”

===Original===

Elizabeth said nothing.

“But you could have explained that.”

“How?”

“Oh, I don’t know﻿—somehow or other.” He appeared to think. “But you said it was you who sent him away.”

“I did.”

“Well, this beats me!”

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白没有作声。

“可你本可以解释一下嘛。”

“怎么解释？”

“哦，我说不上来——总归有法子。”他像是在思索。“可你说，是你打发他走的。”

“是我。”

“这就奇了，我实在想不通！”

===Original===

Elizabeth’s strained patience reached its limit.

“Nutty, please!” she said. “Don’t let’s talk about it. It’s all over now.”

“Yes, but﻿—”

“Nutty, don’t! I can’t stand it. I’m raw all over. I’m hating myself. Please don’t make it worse.”

Nutty looked at her face, and decided not to make it worse. But his anguish demanded some outlet. He found it in soliloquy.

“Just like this for the rest of our lives!” he murmured, taking in the farm grounds and all that in them stood with one glassy stare of misery. “Nothing but ghastly bees and sweeping floors and carrying water till we die of old age! That is, if those blighters don’t put me in the coop for getting that money out of them. How was I to know that it was obtaining money under false pretenses? It simply seemed to me a darned good way of collecting a small roll. I don’t see how I’m ever going to pay them back, so I suppose it’s the cooler for me, all right.”

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白强撑的耐性到了头。

“纳蒂，求你！”她说，“咱们别谈这件事了。现在全都已经完了。”

“是，可是——”

“纳蒂，别说了！我受不了。我浑身都是伤口，一碰就疼。我恨我自己。求你别把事情弄得更糟。”

纳蒂看了看她的脸，决定不再把事情弄得更糟。可他满腹的苦楚总得有个出口。他在自言自语里找到了。

“往后一辈子都得这样！”他喃喃道，一双呆滞的眼睛满是悲苦，把农场场院连同院中的一切扫视了一遍。“除了讨厌死了的蜜蜂、擦地板、拎水，什么都没有，一直干到老死！这还得算那帮家伙没把我关进大牢——谁叫我从他们兜里把钱哄出来的。我哪知道这就叫以欺瞒手段骗取钱财？我只当那是个攒一小卷钞票的再好不过的法子。我看这钱是永远还不上了，所以横竖我是要蹲班房的。”

===Original===

Elizabeth had been trying not to listen to him, but without success.

“I’ll look after that, Nutty. I have a little money saved up, enough to pay off what you owe. I was saving it for something else, but never mind.”

“Awfully good of you,” said Nutty, but his voice sounded almost disappointed. He was in the frame of mind which resents alleviation of its gloom. He would have preferred at that moment to be allowed to round off the picture of the future which he was constructing in his mind with a reel or two showing himself doing the lockstep or brooding in a cell. After all, what difference did it make to a man of spacious tastes whether he languished for the rest of his life in a jail or on a farm in the country. Jail, indeed, was almost preferable. You knew where you were when you were in jail. They didn’t spring things on you. Whereas life on a farm was nothing but one long succession of things sprung on you. Now that Lord Dawlish had gone he supposed that Elizabeth would make him help her with the bees again. At this thought he groaned aloud. When he contemplated a lifetime of Flack’s, a lifetime of bee dodging and carpet beating and water lugging, and reflected that but for a few innocent words﻿—words spoken, mark you, in a pure spirit of kindliness and brotherly love with the object of putting a bit of optimistic pep into sister﻿—he might have been in a position to touch a millionaire brother-in-law for the needful whenever he felt disposed, the iron entered into Nutty’s soul. A rotten, rotten world!

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白一直竭力不听他，却没能做到。

“这事我来料理，纳蒂。我攒下了一点钱，够还清你欠的那笔。本来是攒着办别的用的，不过不要紧。”

“你真是太好了，”纳蒂说，可那声音听着几乎像是失望。他正处在这么一种心境：见不得自己的阴郁有谁来化解。此刻他宁愿人家由着他，好给心里正搭建的未来图景补上一两盘胶片，演一演他自己迈着囚犯的锁步，或是关在牢房里枯坐。说到底，对一个要过阔气日子的人来说，后半辈子是熬在监牢里，还是熬在乡下农场上，又有什么两样？监牢甚而还要可取些。蹲监狱，你起码知道自己在哪儿。牢里没人冷不丁地给你来一手。农场上的日子却纯粹是一长串没完没了的冷不丁。达利什勋爵既已走了，他料想伊丽莎白又要拉他去帮她侍弄蜜蜂。想到这里，他放声呻吟。一想到要在弗拉克家熬一辈子，熬一辈子躲蜂、拍地毯、拎水，再想到若不是那几句无心的话——你记着，那话是本着一片纯粹的仁厚与手足之情说的，为的是给妹妹打一点乐观的气——他本可以随时高兴了就跟百万富翁的妹夫开口告帮，弄几个急用的钱，一块寒铁便直扎进纳蒂的灵魂。世界真是烂，烂透了！

===Original===

Nutty had the sort of mind that moves in circles. After contemplating for a time the rottenness of the world, he came back to the point from which he had started.

“I can’t understand it,” he said, “I can’t believe it.”

He kicked a small pebble that lay convenient to his foot.

“You say you sent him away. If he had legged it on his own account, because what he heard me say got his goat, I could understand that. But why should you﻿—”

===Chinese===

纳蒂的脑子是兜圈子的一路。对着这世界的腐烂哀叹了一阵之后，他又绕回了出发点。

“我实在弄不明白，”他说，“我简直不能相信。”

他抬脚踢了踢一颗正巧在脚边的小石子。

“你说是你打发他走的。要是他自己脚底抹油溜了，因为听见我的话气到了家，那我还能明白。可你为什么要——”

===Original===

It became evident to Elizabeth that, until some explanation of this point was offered to him, Nutty would drift about in her vicinity, moaning and shuffling his feet indefinitely.

“I sent him away because I loved him,” she said, “and because, after what had happened, he could never be certain that I loved him. Can you understand that?”

“No,” said Nutty frankly, “I’m darned if I can. It sounds looney to me.”

===Chinese===

伊丽莎白看明白了：这一点不给他个解释，纳蒂就会没完没了地在她的近旁转悠，哼哼唧唧，蹭着鞋底。

“我打发他走，是因为我爱他，”她说，“也因为，经过这一遭，他再也没法确信我爱他。这个你能明白吗？”

“不明白，”纳蒂坦白道，“我死活也明白不了。听着简直是疯话。”

===Original===

“You can’t see that it wouldn’t have been fair to him to marry him?”

“No.”

The doubts which she was trying to crush increased the violence of their attack. It was not that she respected Nutty’s judgment in itself. It was that his view of what she had done chimed in so neatly with her own. She longed for someone to tell her that she had done right, someone who would bring back that feeling of certainty which she had had during her talk with Bill. And in these circumstances Nutty’s attitude had more weight than on its merits it deserved. She wished she could cry. She had a feeling that if she once did that the right outlook would come back to her.

===Chinese===

“你看不出来，那样嫁给他，对他不公平？”

“看不出来。”

她极力想压下去的那些疑虑，反扑得愈发凶猛。倒不是她敬重纳蒂的判断力本身，而是他对这件事的看法，同她自己心底的看法合拍合得那样严丝合缝。她渴望有个人来告诉她，她做得对；渴望有个人能把她同比尔谈话时的那种笃定带回来。在这样的情形下，纳蒂的态度便有了超出其实际分量的分量。她真想大哭一场。她觉得，只要哭出来一次，那正确的心境便会回到她身上。

===Original===

Nutty, meanwhile, had found another pebble and was kicking it somberly. He was beginning to perceive something of the intricate and unfathomable workings of the feminine mind. He had always looked on Elizabeth as an ordinary good fellow, a girl whose mind worked in a more or less understandable way. She was not one of those hysterical women you read about in the works of the highbrow novelists, she was just a regular girl. And yet now, at the one moment of her life when everything depended on her acting sensibly, she had behaved in a way that made his head swim when he thought of it. What it amounted to was that you simply couldn’t understand women.

Nobody home! There you had Woman in a nutshell.

Into this tangle of silent sorrow came a honking automobile. It drew up at the gate and a man jumped out.

===Chinese===

这当口，纳蒂又寻到一颗石子，阴沉着脸踢着。他开始多少领略到一点女人心思的曲折幽深、不可究诘。他一向把伊丽莎白看作一个平平常常的好伙计，一个脑瓜子转起来大体可以理解的姑娘。她不是高雅派小说家笔下那种歇斯底里的女人，她不过是个再正常不过的普通姑娘。可如今，偏偏在她一生中样样都指着她明理办事的这一刻，她的行事竟叫他想起来就头晕。归结起来无非一句话：女人这东西，压根儿没法懂。

没人在家！女人这东西，这一句就说尽了。

就在这一团无声的悲绪里，闯来一辆喇叭声大作的汽车。汽车在大门口停住，一个男人跳了下来。

## XXIV / 第二十四章

===Original===
The man who had alighted from the automobile was young and cheerful. He wore a flannel suit of a gay blue and a straw hat with a colored ribbon, and he looked upon a world, which, his manner seemed to indicate, had been constructed according to his own specifications, through a single eyeglass. When he spoke it became plain that his nationality was English.

Nutty regarded his beaming countenance with a lowering hostility. The indecency of anyone’s being cheerful at such a time struck him forcibly, He would have liked mankind to have preserved till further notice a hushed gloom. He glared at the young man.

Elizabeth, such was her absorption in her thoughts, was not even aware of his presence till he spoke to her.

“I beg your pardon, is this Flack’s?”

She looked up and met that sunny eyeglass.

===Chinese===
从汽车上下来的那个人年轻而快活。他穿一套艳蓝色的法兰绒西装，戴一顶缀着彩带的草帽，隔着一枚单片眼镜打量世界——瞧他那副神气，仿佛这世界正是照他自家的规格营造出来的。一开口，他那英国人的国籍便一望而知。

纳蒂（Nutty）满脸阴沉地敌视着那张春风满面的脸。这种时候居然有人高兴，简直不成体统——这层感想狠狠击中了他。他恨不得全人类把那种屏息敛声的阴郁保持下去，直至另行通知为止。他恶狠狠地瞪着年轻人。

伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）一心沉在自己的思绪里，直到他开口同她讲话，竟还没察觉有人到场。

“对不起，请问这里是弗拉克家（Flack’s）吗？”

她抬起头，正迎上那枚晴光熠熠的单片眼镜。

===Original===
“This is Flack’s,” she said.

“Thank you,” said the young man.

The automobile, a stout, silent man at the helm, throbbed in the nervous way automobiles have when standing still, suggesting somehow that it were best to talk quick, as they can give you only a few minutes before dashing on to keep some other appointment. Either this or a natural volatility lent a breezy rapidity to the visitor’s speech. He looked at Elizabeth across the gate, which it had not occurred to her to open, as if she were just what he had expected her to be and a delight to his eyes, and burst into speech.

===Chinese===
“这里是弗拉克家，”她说。

“谢谢，”年轻人说。

那辆汽车由一个粗壮沉默的汉子把着舵，停在原地突突地颤，带着汽车寸步不移时特有的那种神经质，不知怎么就让人觉得顶好快些讲完——仿佛它们只能拨给你几分钟，回头就得一踩油门，赶赴别的什么约会。不知是这份无声的催促，还是来客天生的浮躁，他的话音里透着一股风风火火的爽利。他隔着那道栅门端详伊丽莎白——她压根还没想起去开门——那眼神仿佛在说：她正是他意料中的模样，看着就叫眼睛受用。跟着他滔滔不绝开了腔。

===Original===
“My name’s Nichols﻿—J. Nichols. I expect you remember getting a letter from me a week or two ago?”

The name struck Elizabeth as familiar. But he had gone on to identify himself before she could place it in her mind.

“Lawyer, don’t you know? Wrote you a letter telling you that your Uncle Ira Nutcombe had left all his money to Lord Dawlish.”

“Oh, yes,” said Elizabeth, and was about to invite him to pass the barrier when he began to speak again.

===Chinese===
“我姓尼科尔斯——杰里·尼科尔斯（Jerry Nichols）。想必你还记得，一两个礼拜前接到过我一封信？”

这姓氏伊丽莎白听着耳熟。可还没等她在心里对上号，他已经自报了家门。

“律师，你要知道。写过一封信给你，说你舅舅艾拉·纳特科姆（Uncle Ira Nutcombe）把钱全数留给了达利什勋爵（Lord Dawlish）。”

“哦，不错，”伊丽莎白说。她正要请他进来，越过这道栅栏，他却又开了口。

===Original===
“You know, I want to explain that letter. Wrote it on a sudden impulse, don’t you know. The more I have to do with the law, the more it seems to hit me that a lawyer oughtn’t to act on impulse. At the moment, you see, it seemed to me the decent thing to do﻿—put you out of your misery, and so forth﻿—stop you entertaining hopes never to be realized, what, and all that sort of thing. You see, it was like this: Bill﻿—I mean Lord Dawlish﻿—is a great pal of mine, a dear old chap. You ought to know him. Well, being in the know, you understand, through your uncle having deposited the will with us, I gave Bill the tip directly I heard of Mr. Nutcombe’s death. I sent him a telephone message to come to the office, and I said: ‘Bill, old man, this old buster﻿—I beg your pardon﻿—this old gentleman has left you all his money.’ Quite informal, don’t you know. And at the same time, in the same informal spirit, I wrote you the letter.” He dammed the torrent for a moment. “By the way, of course you are Miss Elizabeth Boyd, what?”

“Yes.”

The young man seemed relieved.

“I’m glad of that,” he said. “Funny if you hadn’t been. You’d have wondered what on earth I was talking about.”

===Chinese===
“是这样的，我想把那封信解释一下。一时冲动写下来的，你要知道。同法律打的交道越多，我越觉得，做律师的不该凭一时冲动行事。你瞧，当时在我想来，那么办才够体面——叫你早些脱离苦海，诸如此类——免得你空抱着永不兑现的指望，是不是，反正是这一路的意思。事情是这样：比尔（Bill）——我是说达利什勋爵——是我顶要好的朋友，一个可爱的老伙计。你真该认识认识他。呃，你明白，你舅舅把遗嘱存放在我们行里，这内情我是知道的；纳特科姆先生的死讯一传到，我立刻给比尔递了信儿。我打电话叫他到事务所来，对他说：‘比尔，老伙计，这位老小子——对不起——这位老先生把钱全留给你了。’极不拘礼节的，你要知道。同一股不拘礼节的劲头，我又给你写了那封信。”他往这股急流上拦了一道闸，止了片刻。“对了，想必你就是伊丽莎白·博伊德小姐，是不是？”

“是的。”

年轻人似乎松了一口气。

“那我就放心了，”他说。“你要不是，才叫滑稽呢。你准得纳闷，我究竟在说些什么。”

===Original===
In spite of her identity this was precisely what Elizabeth was doing. Her mind, still under a cloud, had been unable to understand one word of Mr. Nichols’ discourse. Judging from his appearance, which was that of a bewildered hosepipe or a snake whose brain is being momentarily overtaxed, Nutty was in the same difficulty. He had joined the group at the gate, abandoning the pebble which he had been kicking in the background, and was now leaning on the top bar, a picture of silent perplexity.

“You see, the trouble is,” resumed the young man, “my governor, who’s the head of the firm, is all for doing things according to precedent. He loves red tape﻿—wears it wrapped round him in winter instead of flannel. He’s all for doing things in the proper legal way, which, as I dare say you know, takes months. And, meanwhile, everybody’s wondering what’s happened and who has got the money, and so on and so forth. I thought I would skip all that and let you know right away exactly where you stood, so I wrote you that letter. I don’t think my temperament’s quite suited to the law, don’t you know, and if he ever hears that I wrote you that letter I have a notion that the governor will think so too. So I came over here to ask you, if you don’t mind, not to mention it when you get in touch with the governor. I frankly admit that letter, written with the best intentions, was a bloomer.”

With which manly admission the young man paused, and allowed the rays of his eyeglass to play upon Elizabeth in silence. Elizabeth tried to piece together what little she understood of his monologue.

===Chinese===
虽说她身份无误，伊丽莎白做的恰恰正是这件事。她的脑子仍在一团云雾里，尼科尔斯先生这一篇高论，她一个字也没听懂。看纳蒂那副模样——活像一条没回过神的水龙带，又像一条脑子一时不胜负荷的蛇——他分明陷在同样的困境里。他早撇下了在后头踢着玩的那颗卵石，加入了栅门口这一小群人，此刻正倚着门顶横杠，活脱一幅无言的茫然图。

“毛病出在这儿，”年轻人接着说，“我家老爷子（the governor）是行里的主事人，干什么都非按先例来不可。他爱繁文缛节爱得入骨——冬天拿它一层层裹在身上，连法兰绒都省了。凡事他都要走正经法律手续，你大概也知道，那一套得拖上好几个月。这当口，人人都在纳闷出了什么事、钱落进了谁的腰包，等等等等。我想干脆把这些一概跳过，马上叫你明明白白知道自己的处境，就给你写了那封信。你要知道，我看我这脾气实在不大适合干法律这一行；老爷子万一听说我写了那封信，我料他自己也会这么看。所以我特地渡海过来求你，你不见怪的话，往后你同老爷子接洽时，这事千万提也别提。我坦白承认，那封信虽出于一片至诚，却是个不折不扣的昏招。”

下了这番有男子气概的招认，年轻人打住话头，由得那枚单片眼镜的反光在伊丽莎白身上无声地游移把玩。伊丽莎白竭力把他那篇独白里自己听懂了的那一丁点，拼凑到一块儿。

===Original===
“You mean that you want me not to tell your father that I got a letter from you?”

“Exactly that. And thanks very much for not saying ‘without prejudice,’ or anything of that kind. The governor would have done it.”

“But I don’t understand. Why should you think that I should ever mention anything to your father?”

“Might slip out, you know, without your meaning it.”

===Chinese===
“你是说，你要我不告诉你父亲，说我接到过你的信？”

“正是这个意思。还要多多感谢你没说什么‘无损权益’（without prejudice）之类的话。老爷子就干得出来。”

“可我不明白。你怎么会以为我会同你父亲提起什么呢？”

“说不准一不留神就溜出来了，你要知道。”

===Original===
“But when? I shall never meet your father.”

“You might quite easily. He might want to see you about the money.”

“The money?”

The eyebrow above the eyeglass rose, surprised.

===Chinese===
“可哪有那种时候？我根本不会见到你父亲。”

“那可容易得很。他没准要为那笔钱的事来见你。”

“钱？”

单片眼镜上边那道眉毛诧异地扬了起来。

===Original===
“Haven’t you had a letter from the governor?”

“No.”

The young man made a despairing gesture.

“I took it for granted that it had come on the same boat that I did. There you have the governor’s methods! Couldn’t want a better example! I suppose some legal formality or other has cropped up and laid him a stymie, and he’s waiting to get round it. You really mean he hasn’t written? Why, dash it,” said the young man, as one to whom all is revealed, “then you can’t have understood a word of what I’ve been saying!”

===Chinese===
“老爷子的信你没收到？”

“没有。”

年轻人做了个绝望的手势。

“我还只当那封信跟我乘的是同一条船呢。老爷子的办事章程，这就全领教了！再好的例证都求不来！多半是哪道法律手续冷不防冒了出来，给他摆下了一记障碍球（stymie），他正候着绕球呢。你真说他一直没写信？嗐，见鬼，”年轻人一副恍然大悟的神气，“那你对我方才这一篇话，准是一个字也没听懂！”

===Original===
For the first time Elizabeth found herself capable of smiling. She liked this incoherent young man.

“I haven’t,” she said.

“You don’t know about the will?”

“Only what you told me in your letter.”

===Chinese===
伊丽莎白头一回发觉自己还笑得出来。这个说话颠三倒四的年轻人，她喜欢。

“是没有，”她说。

“遗嘱的事你不知道？”

“只知道你信里讲的那些。”

===Original===
“Well, I’m hanged! Tell me﻿—I hadn’t the honor of knowing him personally﻿—was the late Mr. Nutcombe’s whole life as eccentric as his will-making? It seems to me﻿—”

Nutty spoke.

“Uncle Ira’s middle name,” he said, “was Bloomingdale. That,” he proceeded bitterly, “is the frightful injustice of it all. I had to suffer from it right along, and all I get, when it comes to a showdown, is a dinky hundred bones. Uncle Ira insisted on father’s and mother’s calling me Nutcombe, which ought to have brought the Gerry Society down on him; and whenever he got a new craze I was always the one he worked it off on. You remember the time he became a vegetarian, Elizabeth? Gosh!” Nutty brooded coldly on the past. “You remember the time he had it all doped out that the end of the world was to come at five in the morning one February? Made me sit up all night with him, reading Marcus Aurelius! And the steam heat turned off at twelve-thirty! I could tell you a dozen things just as bad as that. He always picked on me. And now I’ve gone through it all he leaves me a hundred dollars!”

===Chinese===
“嘿，这就见鬼了！我问你——我没那份荣幸当面结识过他——已故的纳特科姆先生这一辈子为人行事，是不是同他立遗嘱一样地古怪？照我看——”

纳蒂开了口。

“艾拉舅舅的中间名，”他说，“是布鲁明代尔（Bloomingdale，纽约一所疯人院的名字）。这，”他恨恨地往下说，“便是这一切当中骇人听闻的不公。这些年我一路受着它的罪，可挨到摊牌的一天，我到手的不过区区一百块大洋。艾拉舅舅定要爸爸妈妈管我叫纳特科姆，单这一条照理就该把杰里协会（Gerry Society，纽约防止虐待儿童会）的人招到他头上去；再说他每迷上一样新名堂，拿来过瘾作践的总归是我。你还记得他吃素那阵子吗，伊丽莎白？我的天！”纳蒂冷冷地凭吊着往事。“你还记得那回他掐算停当，说二月里哪天清早五点钟世界就要完了吗？他叫我陪他守一整夜，读马可·奥勒留（Marcus Aurelius）！暖气到十二点半便关了！这样的事我还能给你数出十来桩。他向来单拣我作践。如今我把这一切都熬过来了，他就留给我一百块钱！”

===Original===
Mr. Nichols nodded sympathetically.

“I should have imagined that he was rather like that. You know, of course, why he made that will I wrote to you about, leaving all his money to Bill Dawlish? Simply because Bill, who met him golfing at a place in Cornwall in the off season, cured him of slicing his approach shots! I give you my word that was the only reason. I’m sorry for old Bill, poor old chap. Such a good sort.”

“He’s all right,” said Nutty. “But why you should be sorry for him gets past me. A fellow who gets five million﻿—”

===Chinese===
尼科尔斯先生同情地点了点头。

“我本来就想他多半是这路人。我写信提过的那份遗嘱——把钱全数留给比尔·达利什的那份——他为什么立它，你当然知道喽？就因为比尔在康沃尔一处地方、趁着淡季同他打高尔夫，治好了他逼近球一味右曲的毛病！我拿人格担保，缘故仅此一条。我真替老比尔难受，可怜的老伙计。人是那么一个好人。”

“他人不坏，”纳蒂说。“可你要替他难过，这就叫我摸不着头脑了。一个到手五百万的人——”

===Original===
“But he doesn’t, don’t you see?”

“How do you mean?”

“Why, this other will puts him out of the running.”

“Which other will?”

===Chinese===
“可他得不到呀，你还不明白？”

“这话怎么讲？”

“嗨，那另一份遗嘱把他刷出局了呀。”

“什么另一份遗嘱？”

===Original===
“Why, the one I’m telling you about.”

He looked from one to the other, apparently astonished at their slowness of understanding. Then an idea occurred to him.

“Why, now that I think of it, I never told you, did I? Yes, your uncle made another will at the very last moment, leaving all he possessed to Miss Boyd.”

The dead silence in which his words were received stimulated him to further speech. It occurred to him that, after that letter of his, perhaps these people were wary about believing anything he said.

===Chinese===
“嗨，就是我在对你们讲的这一份呀。”

他把两人挨个望了一遍，显然对他们开窍之迟钝大感诧异。随后他想起一件事来。

“嗨，如今想起来，这一节我压根没告诉过你们，是不是？对了，你舅舅在最后关头又立了一份遗嘱，把他名下的一切统统留给了博伊德小姐。”

他这话迎来的是一片死寂，这片死寂又刺激他往下说。他转念想到，上回有过那么一封信，这一家人对他的话，兴许说什么也不敢信了。

===Original===
“It’s absolutely true. It’s the real, stable information this time. I had it direct from the governor, who was there when he made the will. He and the governor had had a row about something, you know, and they made it up during those last days, and﻿—well, apparently your uncle thought he had better celebrate it somehow, so he made a new will. From what little I know of him that was the way he celebrated most things. I took it for granted the governor would have written to you by this time. I expect you’ll hear by the next mail. You see, what brought me over was the idea that when he wrote you might possibly take it into your heads to mention having heard from me. You don’t know my governor. If he found out I had done that I should never hear the last of it. So I said to him: ‘Gov’nor, I’m feeling a bit jaded. Been working too hard, or something. I’ll take a week or so off, if you can spare me.’ He didn’t object, so I whizzed over. Well, of course, I’m awfully sorry for old Bill, but I congratulate you, Miss Boyd.”

“What’s the time?” said Elizabeth.

Mr. Nichols was surprised. He could not detect the connection of ideas.

“It’s about five to eleven,” he said, consulting his watch.

===Chinese===
“千真万确。这一回可是货真价实、板上钉钉的消息。消息是老爷子亲口告诉我的，立遗嘱那天他就在场。你知道，他同老爷子先前为什么事拌过一场嘴，最后那几天里两人言归于好，于是——呃，明摆着你舅舅觉得总得拿什么庆贺庆贺，就立了份新遗嘱。据我对他那点有限的了解，他庆贺大多数事情，用的都是这一路法子。我只当到这会儿老爷子总该给你去过信了。想来下一班邮件你就该接到信儿了。是这样的，我所以要跑这一趟，是怕他写信的时候，你们兴许会心血来潮，提起接到过我的信。你们不了解我家老爷子。叫他查出我干过这等事，我这辈子休想让他念叨完。于是我对他说：‘老爷子，我觉着有点乏了。工作太累，还是怎么的。你那边抽得开人，我就歇个把礼拜去。’他没拦，我就一溜烟渡海过来了。呃，自然，老比尔我是难过得很，可是博伊德小姐，我给你道喜。”

“几点钟了？”伊丽莎白说。

尼科尔斯先生一怔。他捉摸不出这念头的来路。

“十一点差五分，”他看了看表说。

===Original===
The next moment he was even more surprised, for Elizabeth, making nothing of the barrier of the gate, had rushed past him and was even now climbing into his automobile.

“Take me to the station,” she was crying to the stout, silent man, whom not even these surprising happenings had shaken from his attitude of well-fed detachment.

The stout man, ceasing to be silent, became interrogative.

“Uh?”

===Chinese===
紧接着他越发吃惊了：伊丽莎白全不把那道栅门放在眼里，从他身边一阵风冲了过去，这会儿已经在往他的汽车里爬了。

“送我去车站，”她朝那个粗壮沉默的汉子连声嚷道——纵是这许多惊人变故，也没能撼动那人养尊处优的超然姿态半分。

粗壮汉子不再沉默，改作疑问。

“啊？”

===Original===
“Take me to the station. I must catch the eleven o’clock train.”

The stout man was not a rapid thinker. He enveloped her in a stodgy gaze. It was only too plain to Elizabeth that he was a man who liked to digest one idea slowly before going on to absorb the next. Jerry Nichols had told him to drive to Flack’s. He had driven to Flack’s. Here he was at Flack’s. Now this young woman was telling him to drive to the station. It was a new idea, and he bent himself to the Fletcherizing of it.

“I’ll give you ten dollars if you get me there by eleven,” shouted Elizabeth.

The car started as if it were some living thing that had had a sharp instrument jabbed into it. Once or twice in his life it had happened to the stout man to encounter an idea which he could swallow at a gulp. This was one of them.

===Chinese===
“送我去车站。我非赶十一点那班火车不可。”

粗壮汉子不是个转得快念头的人。他用一副迟钝呆滞的目光把她整个罩住。伊丽莎白心里雪亮：这人是喜欢把一个念头慢慢消化停当，再去接纳下一个的。杰里·尼科尔斯吩咐他开到弗拉克家。他把车开到了弗拉克家。他这会儿人在弗拉克家。如今这个年轻姑娘却叫他开车去车站。这是个新念头，他便凝神贯注，对它做起弗莱彻式细嚼慢咽（Fletcherizing）的功夫来。

“你十一点以前把我送到，我给你十块钱，”伊丽莎白高声嚷道。

车子猛地蹿了出去，活像一头生灵叫什么锐器狠狠扎了一记。粗壮汉子一辈子也碰上过一两回能一口囫囵吞下的念头，这一个便是。

===Original===
Mr. Nichols, following the car with a wondering eye, found that Nutty was addressing him.

“Is this really true?” said Nutty.

“Absolute gospel.”

A wild cry, a piercing whoop of pure joy, broke the summer stillness.

===Chinese===
尼科尔斯先生满眼惊奇地目送汽车，发觉纳蒂在同他讲话。

“这话当真？”纳蒂说。

“字字是福音。”

一声狂呼，一嗓子透着纯粹欢喜的尖啸，划破了夏日的静谧。

===Original===
“Come and have a drink, old man!” babbled Nutty. “This needs celebrating!” His face fell. “Oh, Lord, I was forgetting, I’m on the wagon.”

“On the wagon?”

“Sworn off, you know. I’m never going to touch another drop as long as I live. I began to see things﻿—monkeys!”

“I had a pal,” said Mr. Nichols sympathetically, “who used to see kangaroos.”

===Chinese===
“来喝一杯，老伙计！”纳蒂吹里呱啦地说。“这事得庆祝庆祝！”他陡然垮下脸来。“哦，天哪，瞧我，倒忘了，我在戒酒呢。”

“戒酒？”

“发过誓的，你要知道。我这辈子再不沾一滴了。我都开始看见东西了——猴子！”

“我从前有个朋友，”尼科尔斯先生深表同情地说，“看见的是袋鼠。”

===Original===
Nutty seized him by the arm, hospitable though handicapped.

“Come and have a bit of bread and butter, or a slice of cake or something, and a glass of water. I want to tell you a lot more about Uncle Ira, and I want to hear all about your end of it. Gee, what a day!”

“The maddest, merriest of all the glad New Year,” assented Mr. Nichols. “A slice of that old ’87 cake. Just the thing!”

===Chinese===
纳蒂一把攥住他的胳膊，处境虽窘，待客的礼数不能缺。

“来吃点黄油面包，或是来一片蛋糕什么的，再喝杯水。艾拉舅舅的事，我还有一大堆要讲给你听；你那头的来龙去脉，我也想统统听听。天哪，这个日子！”

“新年里顶疯顶乐的一天，”尼科尔斯先生随声附和。“那块八七年的老蛋糕来上一片，再合适不过！”

## XXV / 第二十五章

===Original===
Bill made his way along the swaying train to the smoking car. It had come upon him overwhelmingly that he needed tobacco. He was in the mood when a man must either smoke or give up altogether the struggle with Fate. He lit his pipe, and looked out of the window at Long Island racing past him. It was only a blur to him.

The smoking car was almost empty. Across the aisle a warm man, who had the air of having run for the train, was fanning himself with a newspaper. In the seat behind him two men had begun an earnest and wordy discussion of automobiles. An official in uniform stood beside a seatful of papers and packages. Soon, perhaps because he was an unquenchable optimist but more probably because it was his job, he would patrol the train offering for sale the peanut brittle and the road maps of Long Island which nobody ever bought. In the far corner there was something shapeless which closer inspection would have revealed as a sleeping Irishman.

The conductor was asking for tickets. Bill showed his mechanically, and the conductor passed on. Then he settled down once more to his thoughts. He could not think coherently yet. His walk to the station had been like a walk in a dream. He was conscious of a great dull pain that weighed on his mind, smothering it. The trees and houses still moved past him in the same indistinguishable blur.

===Chinese===
比尔沿着摇摇晃晃的列车一路走去，进了吸烟车厢。他猛然被一种排山倒海的感觉攫住：他非抽口烟不可。人正是到了这种心境，要么点上烟，要么索性彻底放弃同命运的搏斗。他点着烟斗，望着窗外长岛（Long Island）飞驰而过。在他眼里，那只是一片模糊。

吸烟车厢里几乎空无一人。过道对面，一位热得够呛的先生正拿一张报纸给自己扇风，看那架势像是刚跑着赶上车的。他身后的座位上，两个男子已就汽车问题展开了一场热烈而冗长的讨论。一名穿制服的铁路职员站在堆满报纸和包裹的座位旁边。要不了多久，他就要沿着车厢巡走叫卖了，兜售花生脆糖和长岛公路图——多半因为他是个打不垮的乐天派，但更大的可能是，这本就是他的差事——那些货色从来无人问津。远处的角落里有一团不成形状的东西，凑近了细看，便会发现那是一个睡着的爱尔兰人。

列车长开始查票。比尔机械地出示了车票，列车长便走了过去。他重新沉入自己的思绪。他的思绪此刻还理不出条理。走到车站的一路，恍如梦中行走。他只觉一股沉甸甸的钝痛压在心头，闷得它透不过气来。树木和房屋依旧从窗外掠过，依旧是一片分辨不清的模糊。

===Original===
He became aware that the conductor was standing beside him, saying something about a ticket. He produced his once more, but this did not seem to satisfy the conductor. To get rid of the man, who was becoming a nuisance, he gave him his whole attention, as far as that smothering weight would allow him to give his whole attention to anything, and found that the man was saying strange things. He thought that he could not have heard him correctly.

“What?” he said.

“Lady back there told me to collect her fare from you,” repeated the conductor. “Said you would pay.”

Bill blinked. Either there was some mistake or trouble had turned his brain.

===Chinese===
他发觉列车长站在身旁，说着什么车票的事。他又掏出一次车票，可这似乎仍不能令列车长满意。这家伙渐渐成了件麻烦，为了打发他走，比尔把全副注意力都给了他——就那股压得人透不过气的分量所容许的程度而言——这才发现，这人说的净是些怪话。他觉得自己八成是听错了。

“什么？”他说。

“后头那位小姐吩咐我来找您收她的车钱，”列车长又说了一遍，“说您肯付。”

比尔眨了眨眼。要么是哪里出了岔子，要么是这场伤心事搅昏了他的头脑。

===Original===
“It’s to New York﻿—one seventy-nine.”

Bill pushed himself together with a supreme effort.

“A lady said I would pay her fare?”

“Yup.”

“But﻿—but why?” demanded Bill feebly.

===Chinese===
“到纽约——一块七毛九。”

比尔使出浑身力气，把自己振作起来。

“一位小姐说，她的车钱由我来付？”

“没错。”

“可——可是为什么呢？”比尔有气无力地追问。

===Original===
The conductor seemed unwilling to go into first causes.

“Search me,” he replied. “It’s what she said.”

“Pay her fare!”

“Told me to collect it off the gentleman in the gray suit in the smoking car. You’re the only one that’s got a gray suit.”

“There’s some mistake.”

“Not mine.”

===Chinese===
列车长似乎不打算深究事情的缘起。

“你问我，我问谁去，”他答道，“她就是这么说的。”

“替她付车钱！”

“她吩咐我，找吸烟车厢里穿灰西装的那位先生收。灰西装就您一位。”

“准是弄错了。”

“错不在我。”

===Original===
“What does she look like?”

The conductor delved in his mind for adjectives.

“Small,” he said, collecting them slowly. “Fair hair. Brown eyes﻿—”

He desisted from his cataloguing at this point, for with a loud exclamation that woke the slumbering Irishman and caused the two automobile experts to lose temporarily the thread of their remarks, Bill had dashed from the car. The man with the newspaper sought information.

===Chinese===
“她长什么模样？”

列车长在心里翻检起形容词来。

“个头儿小，”他一边慢慢搜罗，一边说，“金发。棕色的眼睛——”

形容词编目到此告一段落，因为比尔大叫一声，冲出了车厢——这一声惊醒了酣睡的爱尔兰人，也叫两位汽车专家一时接不上话头。拿报纸的先生忙不迭打听起原委来。

===Original===
“What’s the trouble?”

“Search me,” said the conductor, a man of a slim vocabulary.

A solution occurred to him. He offered it.

“Dippy,” he suggested, and went to talk about peanut brittle and road maps to the official in uniform.

Two cars farther back Bill had dropped into the seat by Elizabeth, and was gurgling wordlessly. A massive lady, who had entered the train at East Moriches in company with three children and a cat in a basket, eyed him with a curiosity that she made no attempt to conceal. Two girls in a neighboring seat leaned forward eagerly to hear all. This was because one of them had told the other that Elizabeth was Mary Pickford. Her companion was skeptical, but nevertheless obviously impressed.

===Chinese===
“出什么事了？”

“你问我，我问谁去，”列车长说。此君的词汇向来精简。

他灵光一闪，想出一个答案，便慷慨贡献出来。

“疯了，”他给出断语，随即找那位穿制服的职员搭话去了，兜售他的花生脆糖和公路图。

隔开两节车厢的后面，比尔已一屁股坐进伊丽莎白（Elizabeth）身旁的座位，喉咙里咕咕直响，一个字也吐不出来。一位大块头太太正拿好奇的眼光打量他，丝毫不想掩饰；她是在东莫里奇斯（East Moriches）上的车，随行还有三个孩子和一只装在篮子里的猫。邻座上两个姑娘热切地探着身子，恨不得一字不漏全听进去。原因在于：其中一个告诉过另一个，伊丽莎白就是玛丽·碧克馥（Mary Pickford）。她的同伴将信将疑，却分明也大为折服。

===Original===
“My God!” said Bill.

The massive lady told the three children sharply to look at their picture book.

“Well, I’m damned!”

The mother of three said that if her offspring did not go right along to the end of the car and look at the pretty trees trouble must infallibly ensue.

===Chinese===
“我的老天！”比尔说。

大块头太太疾言厉色，吩咐三个孩子去看他们的图画书。

“这可真见鬼了！”

这位三个孩子的母亲宣告：她的宝贝们若不立刻到车厢那头去看那些好看的树，祸事必定接踵而至。

===Original===
“Elizabeth!”

At the sound of the name the two girls leaned back, taking no further interest in the proceedings.

“What are you doing here?”

Elizabeth smiled, a shaky but encouraging smile.

===Chinese===
“伊丽莎白！”

一听这名字，两个姑娘便缩了回去，对眼前这出戏再无兴趣。

“你在这儿做什么？”

伊丽莎白笑了笑，笑得发颤，却透着令人宽心的意味。

===Original===
“I came after you, Bill.”

“You’ve got no hat!”

“I was in too much of a hurry to get one, and I gave all my money to the man who drove the car. That’s why I had to ask you to pay my fare. You see, I’m not too proud to use your money after all.”

“Then﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我是跟着你来的，比尔。”

“你帽子都没戴！”

“我急得来不及去弄一顶，再说我把钱全给了开汽车的那个人。所以我只好请你替我付车票钱。你瞧，我到底还不至于矜持到不肯花你的钱。”

“这么说——”

===Original===
“Tickets, please. One seventy-nine.”

It was the indefatigable conductor, sensible of his duty to the company and resolved that nothing should stand in the way of its performance. Bill gave him five dollars and told him to keep the change. The conductor saw eye to eye with him in this.

“Bill! You gave him﻿—” She gave a little shrug of her shoulders. “Well, it’s lucky you’re going to marry a rich girl.”

A look of the utmost determination overspread Bill’s face.

“I don’t know what you’re talking about. I’m going to marry you. Now that I’ve got you again I’m not going to let you go. You can use all the arguments you like, but it won’t matter. I was a fool ever to listen. If you try the same sort of thing again I’m just going to pick you up and carry you off. I’ve been thinking it over since I left you. My mind has been working absolutely clearly. I’ve gone into the whole thing. It’s perfect rot to take the attitude you did. We know we love each other, and I’m not going to listen to any talk about time making us doubt it. Time will only make us love each other all the more.”

===Chinese===
“请出示车票。一块七毛九。”

来的是那位不知疲倦的列车长。他深知自己对铁路公司所负的职责，并下定决心：任何东西都休想拦在他履行职责的路上。比尔递给他五块钱，叫他不用找了。在这一桩上，列车长与他所见完全相同。

“比尔！你居然给了他——”她微微一耸肩，“唉，幸好你快要娶的是位阔小姐。”

一副无比坚决的神色漫上了比尔的脸。

“我不知道你在说什么。我要娶的就是你。既然重新得到了你，我就不会再放你走。你爱搬出多少套道理都随你，可全都无济于事。当初我居然听得进去，真是个傻子。你要是再来一回那套把戏，我就一把抱起你，把你扛走。从离开你那一刻起，我一直在把这件事想过来想过去。我的脑子运转得绝对清楚。整件事我都从头到尾推演过了。你那种态度，纯属胡闹透顶。咱们明明白白彼此相爱，谁也别想拿时间会叫咱们生疑那一套来说服我。时间只会叫咱们爱得更深。”

===Original===
“Why, Bill, this is eloquence!”

“I feel eloquent.”

The stout lady ceased to listen. They had lowered their voices and she was hard of hearing. She consoled herself by taking up her copy of *Gingery Stories* and burying herself in the hectic adventures of a young millionaire and an artist’s model. Elizabeth caught a fleeting glimpse of the cover.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，比尔，好一篇雄辩！”

“我自觉雄辩得很。”

胖太太不再听了。他们压低了嗓音，而她耳朵又背。她拿起自己那本《姜味故事集》（Gingery Stories），埋头扎进一位年轻百万富翁和一位画家模特儿的火辣历险里，聊以自慰。那封面只从伊丽莎白眼前一晃而过。

===Original===
“I bet there’s a story in there of a man named Harold who was too proud to marry a girl, though he loved her, because she was rich and he wasn’t. You wouldn’t be so silly as that, Bill, would you?”

“It’s the other way about with me.”

“No, it’s not. Bill, do you know a man named Nichols?”

===Chinese===
“我敢打赌，那里头准有个故事，说一个名叫哈罗德（Harold）的男人，明明爱着一位姑娘，却因为姑娘有钱、自己没钱，放不下架子不肯娶她。你总不至于蠢成那样吧，比尔，会不会？”

“我这儿情形正相反。”

“才不相反呢。比尔，你认不认识一位姓尼科尔斯（Nichols）的先生？”

===Original===
“Nichols?”

“J. Nichols. He said he knew you. He said he had told you about Uncle Ira leaving you his money.”

“Jerry Nichols! How on earth﻿—Oh, I remember. He wrote to you, didn’t he?”

===Chinese===
“尼科尔斯？”

“杰·尼科尔斯。他说他认识你。他说，舅舅艾拉（Uncle Ira）把钱留给你的事，他早就告诉过你了。”

“杰里·尼科尔斯（Jerry Nichols）！这怎么可能——哦，我想起来了。他给你写过信，是不是？”

===Original===
“He did. And this morning, just after you had left, he called.”

“Jerry Nichols called?”

“To tell me that Uncle Ira had made another will before he died, leaving the money to me.”

Their eyes met.

===Chinese===
“写过。就在今天早上，你前脚刚出门，他后脚就到了。”

“杰里·尼科尔斯登门了？”

“来告诉我，舅舅艾拉临终之前又立了一份新遗嘱，把钱留给了我。”

两人的目光碰在了一处。

===Original===
“So I stole his car and caught the train,” said Elizabeth simply.

Bill was recovering slowly from the news.

“But﻿—this makes rather a difference, you know,” he said.

“In what way?”

“Well, what I mean to say is you’ve got five million dollars and I’ve got two thousand a year, don’t you know, and so﻿—”

===Chinese===
“所以我偷了他的汽车，赶上了这趟火车，”伊丽莎白简简单单地说。

比尔叫这个消息震得慢慢回过神来。

“可是——你要知道，这一来可就大不相同了，”他说。

“哪方面不相同？”

“呃，我的意思是说，你如今有五百万美元，我一年只有两千镑，你懂的，所以——”

===Original===
Elizabeth tapped him on the knee.

“Bill, do you see what this is in my hand?”

“Eh? What?”

“It’s a pin. And I’m going to dig it right into you wherever I think it will hurt most unless you stop being Harold at once. I’ll tell you exactly what you’ve got to do, and you needn’t think you’re going to do anything else. When we get to New York we take the subway down to Brooklyn Bridge. We then walk to the City Hall, where you go to the window marked Marriage Licenses and buy one. It will set you back one dollar. You will give your correct age and name and you will hear mine. It will come as a shock to you to know that my second name is something awful! I’ve kept it concealed all my life. After we’ve done that we shall go to the only church that anybody could possibly be married at. It’s on Twenty-ninth Street, just round the corner from Fifth Avenue. It’s got a fountain playing in front of it, and it’s a little bit of Heaven dumped right down in the middle of New York. And after that﻿—well, we might start looking about for that farm we’ve talked of. We can get a good farm for five million dollars, and leave something over to be doled out﻿—cautiously﻿—to Nutty. And then all we have to do is to live happily ever after.”

===Chinese===
伊丽莎白在他的膝盖上轻轻敲了敲。

“比尔，你看见我手里拿的是什么了吗？”

“嗯？什么？”

“这是别针。你要是不立刻收起哈罗德那一套，我就照准我觉得最疼的地方，把它扎进你肉里去。你该做的事，我一五一十讲给你听，你也休想再干别的。到了纽约，咱们坐地铁下到布鲁克林大桥（Brooklyn Bridge），再走到市政厅（City Hall），你到标着‘婚姻登记（Marriage Licenses）’的窗口买一份许可证，这要花掉你一块钱。你要报上自己真实的年龄和姓名，也会听到我的——等你晓得我的中间名有多可怕，准会吓一大跳！这名字我瞒了一辈子。办完这桩事，咱们就去全天下独一无二、人人只该在那儿完婚的教堂。它坐落在二十九街（Twenty-ninth Street）上，从第五大道（Fifth Avenue）拐过街角便是。教堂门前有一座喷泉喷着水，那真是一小块天堂，直挺挺卸在了纽约的正中央。再往后嘛——咱们就可以动手物色咱们念叨过的那座农场了。五百万美元尽够买一座好农场，还能剩下一笔，一点一点——慎之又慎地——贴补给纳蒂（Nutty）。到那时候，咱们要做的就只剩一件事：从此以后幸福地生活在一起。”

===Original===
Something small and soft slipped itself into his hand, just as it had done ages and ages ago in Lady Wetherby’s wood.

It stimulated Bill’s conscience to one last remonstrance.

“But, I say, you know﻿—”

===Chinese===
一个小小的、软软的东西滑进他的手心，正如许久许久以前，在韦瑟比夫人（Lady Wetherby）的树林里那样。

这东西刺激得比尔的良心发出最后一次抗议。

“可是，我说，你瞧——”

===Original===
“Well?”

“This business of the money, you know. What I mean to say is﻿—Ow!”

He broke off, as a sharp pain manifested itself in the fleshy part of his leg. Elizabeth was looking at him reprovingly, her weapon poised for another onslaught.

===Chinese===
“怎么？”

“就是那笔钱的事，你懂的。我的意思是说——哎哟！”

他话到半截戛然而止，因为一阵剧痛在他腿上的厚肉里发作起来。伊丽莎白正用责备的眼神望着他，武器已然架好，只待再度出击。

===Original===
“I told you!” she said.

“All right, I won’t do it again.”

“That’s a good child. Bill, listen. Come closer and tell me all sorts of nice things about myself till we get to Jamaica, and then I’ll tell you what I think of you. We’ve just passed Islip, so you’ve plenty of time.”

===Chinese===
“我警告过你！”她说。

“好，好，不敢了。”

“这才乖呢。比尔，听着。凑近些，把我的种种好处一样一样讲给我听，一直讲到牙买加（Jamaica）；到那时，我再把我怎么想你讲给你听。咱们刚过艾斯利普（Islip），你有的是时间。”
